# DICOM de-identification design

This document specifies the DICOM de-identification engine that will replace `pymedphys.dicom.anonymise` and the experimental pseudonymisation module, and records the decisions behind it. A run now de-identifies, checks, and reports a collection, but no preset is enabled until the first supported release; the register records what is implemented. The current tools share one engine in `lib/pymedphys/_dicom/anonymise/`, do not implement a DICOM confidentiality profile, and have the limitations listed in [DICOM de-identification](../../users/background/dicom-deidentification.md).

Pull requests for this work are listed in the [tracking issue](https://github.com/pymedphys/pymedphys/issues/2075). A pull request that changes a decision updates this document, the user documentation, and any affected code in the same change.

## Scope

The engine de-identifies a collection of DICOM instances as a unit and preserves the references between them. The library API, the command-line interface, and the Streamlit app all call the same engine.

The target coverage is:

- **Objects:** CT, MR, PET, RT Structure Set, RT Plan, RT Dose, RT Beams Treatment Record, RT Image, spatial registration, and segmentation.
- **Profile and options:** the Basic Application Level Confidentiality Profile with each option of Table E.1-1, as the maintainer decided on 12 October 2026: Retain Safe Private, Retain UIDs, Retain Device Identity, Retain Institution Identity, Retain Patient Characteristics, Retain Longitudinal Temporal Information with Full Dates or with Modified Dates, Clean Descriptors, Clean Structured Content, and Clean Graphics.
- **Evidence:** a generated conformance statement, a requirements register traced to tests, and published benchmark results (D-018).

The first supported release covers the Basic Profile, alone (`basic`) and with the Clean Descriptors Option (`basic-clean-descriptors`), with a run-scoped key, for instances of the CT, MR, and PET image IODs, including their Enhanced, Legacy Converted Enhanced, and colour variants and the For Processing SOP Classes, of MR Spectroscopy, and of RT Structure Set, RT Plan, and RT Dose, uncompressed or in a transfer syntax that encapsulates Pixel Data that pydicom's decoding plugins decode (D-010). Some instances of these IODs are still sequestered under both presets: every Enhanced PET Image instance that conforms to its IOD (D-020), and any instance whose Referenced Performed Procedure Step Sequence has no UID to replace, or whose required ICC Profile is not an RGB profile (D-021). A compressed instance is also sequestered where the plugin pinned to its transfer syntax is not installed, or where its frames or codestreams fail the engine's checks (Architecture items 2 and 7). Clean Descriptors is included because, under the Basic Profile alone, RT Structure Sets would keep no usable structure names (D-009). Later releases extend it to the target coverage, each with its own evidence.

The first supported release ships the library API and the command line, which is a thin layer over the library: batch users need the command line, and the surface to validate stays small. A library alone was rejected because most users will not script it. The command prints a summary of counts, statuses, and reason names, never a source value or source path (the only directories it names are the release directory, its staging area, and the QC destination), and its exit status tells a script the outcome: 0 when every input was released or is a duplicate of one released, 1 when the release was published but an input was refused, sequestered, or held for review, 2 when its arguments cannot be parsed, 3 when the run did not start, its first pass stopped it, or its QC pack could not be written, 4 when its staging area was left behind, which outranks every other status since the staging area may still identify people and must be deleted by hand, and 70 for any other failure, reported by the exception's type alone. The app comes in a later release, because `pymedphys gui` has no login and can serve its apps to other computers, so a de-identification app needs its own design for keys and QC material. Until the new app ships, the legacy pseudonymisation app stays, with its limitation banner (D-019).

The following are not supported. Input that needs them is rejected or sequestered, without a conformance claim:

- second-generation RT objects, RT Ion objects, and brachytherapy treatment records;
- the Clean Pixel Data Option (D-015), which can be composed into a policy, and each option of Table E.1-1 whose actions the engine does not yet apply, all of which the engine refuses by name (D-011), except Clean Pixel Data, under which it blacks out the text it finds and holds each instance it cleans for review, claiming the option only for an instance without top-level pixel data or whose regions a reviewer approved on a later run (D-037, D-048), and the Clean Recognizable Visual Features Option for an instance that can show a face and that a reviewer has not found free of one, which the engine holds for review (D-046);
- Structured Reports, Key Object Selection documents, Presentation States, and Private SOP Classes (D-010);
- legal determinations, since whether data are anonymous depends on the release context, not on the software.

## Terminology and claims

The engine performs **de-identification**, the term used by DICOM PS3.15 and the MIDI Task Group report. Anonymisation and pseudonymisation are not separate features: both apply the same transformation. They differ in whether anyone retains a means of re-identification, such as a project key or a crosswalk, and in which indirect identifiers the selected options keep.

Whether data are anonymous is a legal conclusion about the data in their release context (for example GDPR Recital 26), which software cannot establish. The Australian Privacy Act 1988 (Cth) s 6(1) and the HIPAA Privacy Rule (45 CFR 164.514) use "de-identified" for such a legal status.

In code, command-line output, reports, and documentation:

- Describe output only as "de-identified in accordance with the DICOM PS3.15 *edition* Basic Application Level Confidentiality Profile with *options*", and only after validating its effective policy and content against that profile and those options (D-011). Never use "de-identified" without that qualification.
- Never describe output as "anonymised".
- Where a key or crosswalk is retained, state that the data remain pseudonymised personal data for whoever holds it, and that any other recipient needs its own assessment.

An instance is **sequestered** when the engine does not write it to the release output, whether it is set aside before de-identification or fails a check during or after it: the run report lists it by opaque identifiers only, it is excluded from the conformance claim, and its source is left unchanged.

## Standards basis

| Role | Source |
| --- | --- |
| Normative rules | [DICOM PS3.15 Annex E](https://dicom.nema.org/medical/dicom/current/output/chtml/part15/chapter_E.html), edition 2026d: E.1.1 (de-identifier), E.1.3 (conformance statement), E.2 (Basic Profile), E.3 (Options), and Tables E.1-1, E.1-1a, E.3.4-1, and E.3.10-1 |
| Supporting DICOM parts | PS3.3 (attribute Types per IOD), PS3.4 (Table B.5-1, the IOD of each Storage SOP Class), PS3.5 (UID encoding, registration, and UUID-derived UIDs), PS3.6 (data dictionary and well-known UIDs), PS3.7 (Implementation Class UIDs), PS3.10 (File Meta Information), PS3.16 (Tables 8-1 and 8-2 coding schemes and their UIDs, CID 7050 de-identification methods, and CID 7005 contributing equipment purposes) |
| Best practice | Clunie DA et al., *Report of the Medical Image De-Identification (MIDI) Task Group: Best Practices and Recommendations*, 7 February 2025, [arXiv:2303.10473](https://arxiv.org/abs/2303.10473) |
| Validation | NCI MIDI synthetic-identifier datasets, answer keys, and validation script (D-018); `dciodvfy` and `dcentvfy` from dicom3tools, and dicom-validator, which check object validity, not privacy, by comparing each output with its input (D-030) |
| Governance context (documentation only) | GDPR Article 4(5) and Recital 26; CJEU C-413/23 P *EDPS v SRB* (4 September 2025); UK ICO anonymisation guidance; Privacy Act 1988 (Cth) and OAIC de-identification guidance; ISO 25237:2017 |

Where this document relies on an informative Note in PS3.15 or on a MIDI recommendation, the choice it supports is a design decision, not a conformance requirement. Browsing links follow the current edition; the table generator uses the pinned publication (D-001).

## Requirements register

The requirements register traces the standard's "shall" statements and the MIDI best practices to the decisions, code, and tests that satisfy them. It is `lib/pymedphys/_dicom/deidentify/requirements.toml`, curated by hand, and records:

- every paragraph of PS3.15 Annex E in the pinned edition that contains "shall", outside Notes and tables, verbatim with any list it introduces and a link to the paragraph;
- the 18 best practices of MIDI §1.6, summarised in PyMedPhys's words.

Like the generated tables, the file carries the attribution "DICOM PS3.15 2026d, © NEMA" (D-001).

Identifiers are stable and never reused. `PS3.15-E.1.1-01` numbers the paragraphs of a section in the standard's order, and `MIDI-BP-01` to `MIDI-BP-18` follow the report's numbering. Each entry cites the decisions that address it and has one of these statuses:

| Status | Meaning | Entry also records |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `planned` | In scope, not yet implemented | The milestone that completes it |
| `partial` | Partly implemented | Modules so far, what remains, and the milestone that completes it; tests so far cite it |
| `implemented` | Met for the supported scope | Modules; the tests that show it cite it |
| `out-of-scope` | Not supported, such as an Option that Scope excludes | A note saying why |
| `not-applicable` | Outside PyMedPhys's role, such as re-identification | A note saying why |

- A pull request that implements part of a requirement updates its entry in the same change.
- A test cites the requirements it shows are met with a marker on the test function or its `Test` class, `@pytest.mark.deid_requirement("PS3.15-E.1.1-01")`, and the register does not list tests. The register's tests for a requirement are those that cite it, read from the tests' source without importing them, in the order of their files and then of their definitions. A note may be written one sentence per line, and its lines are joined with spaces. Both keep pull requests that add tests or sentences to the same requirement from editing the same lines of the register.
- `pymedphys._dicom.deidentify.requirements.load_requirements` rejects malformed or inconsistent entries, such as an implemented requirement without tests or an exclusion without a note, and a test that cites a requirement the register does not record. Tests check that every cited decision and module exists, that pytest collects every citing test and sees the same citations as the source, and that the register follows the edition of the generated tables.
- A new edition updates the register with the tables, after comparing it with every Annex E page of that edition: add paragraphs, update changed text, and remove deleted paragraphs without reusing their identifiers.
- Normative text without "shall", such as the paragraphs after Table E.1-1a on actions for Sequences and on Options overriding the Profile, gets entries when the engine implements it.
- The traceability matrix for each release (D-018) is generated from the register by `pymedphys dev deid-matrix`, as Markdown, and is not committed. It summarises the entries by status and source, and lists each requirement with its milestone, decisions, modules, and traced tests, the exclusions with the reason for each, and the remaining work with its milestone. Given pytest JUnit XML reports with `--junit`, one for each tested environment, it gives each traced test's outcome. A requirement's traced tests pass only when every case of every test that traces it passed in every report: a skipped case, or a test or case that one report lacks although another ran it, leaves them incomplete; passing tests do not make a partial requirement met. `--check` exits with status 1 when a traced test failed, was skipped, or did not run. Each release renders the matrix from the reports of every environment in the unit-test matrix, checks it, and attaches it to the GitHub release.

## Architecture

The engine will live in `lib/pymedphys/_dicom/deidentify/`, with its public API in `pymedphys.dicom`. Its components are listed below; they are not a pull request sequence.

1. **Generated standard tables.** Table E.1-1 with every option column; Tables E.1-1a and E.3.10-1; the rows of Table E.3.4-1 whose concept names are coded by DICOM or SNOMED CT; the PS3.6 data dictionary (Table 6-1), with each attribute's keyword, VR, VM, and retirement; the PS3.6 Annex A registries of UIDs, well-known frames of reference, and the UIDs of context groups and templates (Tables A-1 to A-4); the PS3.16 coding schemes (Tables 8-1 and 8-2) and context groups CID 7050 and CID 7005; the PS3.4 Storage SOP Classes with the IOD of each (Table B.5-1); and, from PS3.3, the modules of every composite IOD that the pin does not leave out, each table of PS3.3 Annex A titled with an IOD's name and "IOD Modules", with the IOD's Functional Group Macros where its modules include them, and the attribute tables of those modules and Functional Group Macros and of the macros they include, which give each attribute's Type (D-001).
2. **Rule layers.** Rules apply in this order: generated tables (L1), reviewed supplementary rules (L2), then validated user rules (L3).
   - L1 gives each attribute of Table E.1-1 the action of the selected options that give one, and otherwise the Profile's, since "a requirement for an Option, when implemented, overrides any requirement for the underlying Profile" (E.1.1). E.3.6 specifies Retain Longitudinal Temporal Information with Full Dates and with Modified Dates as mutually exclusive, so a selection of both is rejected. Otherwise PS3.15 defines no precedence between options, so an attribute to which two selected options give different actions has no L1 action and is reported as a conflict for policy validation (D-007, D-011). In 2026d, the only such options are Retain Device Identity and Modified Dates, on eleven attributes. Policy validation rejects every conflict except these, which only the `tps-import` preset resolves (D-011).
   - L2 covers attributes that Table E.1-1 omits but the de-identifier remains responsible for (E.1.1, Note 1 after Table E.1-1a). These are person names, and dates and times (D-023), by VR; text attributes, such as Beam Name, Dose Comment, and Wedge ID, by a reviewed list, with removal by Type for any it does not cover (D-022); URI and URL attributes, Series Number, and some sequences and binary attributes, by reviewed rules (D-022); the role of every UI attribute (D-003); and the role of every temporal attribute (D-007). A test fails if the pinned dictionary contains a date, time, datetime, person name, or UI attribute with no rule.
   - The L2 file `supplementary_actions.toml` gives an action of Table E.1-1a, with a note saying why, to attributes that Table E.1-1 omits, other than UI attributes, which have roles instead (D-003). Every person name that the table omits has one: in 2026d, Secondary Reviewer Name and Evaluator Name, which PS3.6 marks DICONDE and no supported IOD uses, get X/Z/D, and the retired Document Author (Trial) gets X, as the table gives the other (Trial) person names. Burned In Annotation and Recognizable Visual Features are kept (K), as D-015 requires. So does every text attribute that the table omits and a supported IOD uses (D-022). URN Code Value and Coding Scheme URL are kept (K) (D-022). Every date, time, and datetime that the table omits has one too, besides its role (D-007, D-023). Like a row of the table, a rule can also give an action under an option, which overrides its Basic Profile action when that option is selected: for a text attribute, only C under Clean Descriptors and K under Retain Device Identity; for a date, time, or datetime, only K under Retain Longitudinal Temporal Information with Full Dates and C under Modified Dates; and only over an action that removes or replaces the value. Policy composition resolves these option actions as it does the table's, and rejects a rule to which two selected options give different actions (D-011). The file never covers an attribute that Table E.1-1 lists.
   - L2 only strengthens L1. It never retains a value that L1 removes, replaces, or cleans, unless a selected option permits it.
   - Each element is decoded with the VR that the pinned dictionary gives its attribute, not with pydicom's dictionary, and then handled by its rule. A value of VR UN is decoded as Implicit VR Little Endian, whatever the transfer syntax (PS3.5 Section 6.2.2), and as a sequence only if items fill it, since pydicom reads malformed items without complaint. The element decoder, the first pass's reference records, the removal of private attributes, and the pixel data risk indicators pass each sequence value that pydicom holds undecoded through `pymedphys._dicom.deidentify.sequences.decode_items` before they read its items, which has pydicom decode it only once `file_layout.reads_as_items` finds that items fill it, and each refuses one that is not only items by the sequence's path, without quoting a value; a test keeps any other module of the engine from having pydicom decode items itself. A VR in the file must be one that the dictionary gives, and must not contradict the one that the standard decides of alternatives. Of alternatives, US or SS follows Pixel Representation (0028,0103) in the nearest data set that has it (PS3.3 Sections C.7.5.1, C.7.6.3, and C.11.1.1.1), but for LUT Descriptor (0028,3002) in VOI LUT Sequence (0028,3010), PS3.3 Section C.11.2.1.1 has the input to the VOI LUT decide it: Pixel Representation "if there is no Modality LUT or Rescale Slope and Intercept specified", SS "if the possible output range after application of the Rescale Slope and Intercept may be signed", as Hounsfield Units always can, and "US otherwise", which includes the output of a Modality LUT, "always unsigned" (Section C.11.1.1.1). There, in explicit VR, either is accepted, since "the explicit VR actually used is dictated by the VR needed to represent the second Value". Section C.7.6.3.1.5 says the same of the Red, Green, and Blue Palette Color Lookup Table Descriptors (0028,1101-1103), adding that it "will be consistent with Pixel Representation", so in explicit VR either is accepted there too, even where it is not, as in an icon image (0088,0200) whose own Pixel Representation, or its image's, says otherwise; Pixel Representation decides it only without a VR in the file. In Presentation LUT Sequence (2050,0010), "the Value Representation of the second Value is always US" (Section C.11.4.1). Whatever the VR, "the first and third values are always by definition interpreted as unsigned" (Sections C.7.6.3.1.5, C.11.1.1.1, and C.11.2.1.1), so they are decoded and written as unsigned where the VR is SS, and a table of 40000 entries keeps its count. Real World Value First Value Mapped (0040,9216) and Last Value Mapped (0040,9211) are SS for floating point pixel data (Section C.7.6.16.2.11.1.1), which the first supported release's IODs do not have, so without a VR in the file they cannot be decoded where Pixel Representation is absent. Without a VR in the file, OB or OW, and US or OW, are OW, as PS3.5 Section A.1 gives Pixel Data, Overlay Data, and Waveform Data, whose bytes are the same either way; and in explicit VR, Pixel Data is OB if encapsulated and OW where Bits Allocated (0028,0100) is more than 8 (Sections A.2 and A.4). An element whose VR is not one the dictionary gives or contradicts the one the standard decides, whose alternatives nothing decides, or whose value does not decode exactly with its VR, such as a DS that is not a number, cannot be decoded. Elements are decoded from data sets as `dcmread` returned them: once pydicom has decoded an element read from a file, as indexing, attribute access, `get`, and iteration do, it holds no encoded value to check, and pydicom has chosen any VR in doubt itself and read a UN sequence's items unchecked, so such an element cannot be decoded either. Specific Character Set (0008,0005), which `dcmread` decodes itself, and a sequence of undefined length, which it reads as items, are the exceptions. Building the first pass's records decodes elements in this way, so the second pass reads each file again rather than reuse those data sets. What happens to an instance with an element that cannot be decoded depends on that element's rule. As the maintainer decided on 1 October 2026, text with bytes outside the Default Character Repertoire where no Specific Character Set applies sequesters its instance only where the text would be kept or cleaned; where its rule removes or replaces it, the instance is de-identified. As the maintainer clarified on 2 October 2026, that transformation proceeds, but whether the output may be released depends on the coverage of the residual search (D-010, D-027). Decoding, reading the first pass's records, adding the de-identification markers, and writing output redact pydicom's warnings and log records, including those of loggers below `pydicom` and debug records, with the engine's own `redacted_diagnostics`, only in the thread that calls pydicom and only while it does, and without adding to Python's process-wide warning filters. Attributes absent from the pinned dictionary and without an L2 rule are removed. Retired attributes follow Table E.1-1 where listed and L2 otherwise.
   - As the maintainer decided on 1 October 2026, the engine admits sources strictly, decodes selectively, and verifies what it preserves (B+); it is not a general DICOM normaliser. A source file is admitted only once its whole structure is read from its own bytes, independently of pydicom: the preamble, the File Meta Information, every element's header and length, rising tags in each data set and item, so that no tag repeats, each item and delimiter, a bound on nesting, and Data Set Trailing Padding, with nothing after the last element, in Implicit or Explicit VR Little Endian, or in a transfer syntax that encapsulates Pixel Data in an Explicit VR Little Endian data set and that pydicom 3.0.2 can decode: JPEG, JPEG-LS, JPEG 2000 Part 1, HTJ2K, or RLE Lossless (PS3.5 Section A.4). Which of them a release de-identifies is for its scope to decide, and the first supported release de-identifies instances in each of them (D-010); one that is sequestered is still read, so that its values reach its subject's residual search (D-027). Before one that it de-identifies is written, every frame of its Pixel Data is decoded once, one frame at a time, by the pydicom decoding plugin pinned to its transfer syntax, whatever else is installed: GDCM, through python-gdcm, for JPEG and JPEG-LS, pylibjpeg, with pylibjpeg-openjpeg, for JPEG 2000 and HTJ2K, and pydicom's own for RLE Lossless. GDCM and pylibjpeg decode in one child process, kept for the life of the process that runs the engine, since a malformed codestream can make a native library end the process that calls it, which no exception handler can prevent: GDCM 3.2 aborts whenever libjpeg warns while reading a JPEG frame's header, other than that it must scale the image down, as for a JFIF segment of a version other than 1. A frame whose decoder ends the child is `undecodable-pixel-data`, or, in the comparison of a written frame with its source's, `cut-changed-pixels`, and the next decode starts a new child. Where the child cannot be started, the instance is `no-decoder`, and the engine logs a warning saying so, once per process. A decode refused because the engine's process is exiting, as one in a daemon thread, is `no-decoder`, or `cut-changed-pixels` in the comparison, with no warning. The engine reads what the child sends back as JSON of a checked form, never as a pickle, so that a decoder that a codestream subverts cannot, through what it sends back, make the engine's process run code, import a module, or change its objects. The QC pack's previews still decode in process, since they decode only released or held files, whose frames this check has already decoded with the same plugin. As the maintainer decided on 7 October 2026, each is licensed at least as permissively as PyMedPhys's Apache License 2.0, so pylibjpeg-libjpeg, under the GNU General Public License version 3, is not used. pydicom 3.0.2's GDCM plugin refuses some frames that pylibjpeg-libjpeg decoded, JPEG Extended at a precision of 12 bits among them, and also refuses JPEG-LS at 6 or 7 bits and signed JPEG-LS Near-Lossless below 8 bits, so an instance with such a frame is sequestered as `undecodable-pixel-data`. A Basic Offset Table that is not empty must give one offset for each frame, from 0 and rising, each where a fragment's Item Tag starts; an Extended Offset Table (7FE0,0001) must come with Extended Offset Table Lengths (7FE0,0002) and an empty Basic Offset Table and give each fragment in turn; and the frames must decode and match Number of Frames, or one where it is absent, but never for a value of zero or an empty one, Rows, Columns, and Samples per Pixel. Since a decoder shapes a frame by those attributes, each frame's codestream header is also read before decoding, the JPEG or JPEG-LS frame header or the JPEG 2000 or HTJ2K SIZ marker segment, and must give the same Rows, Columns, and Samples per Pixel, a sample precision equal to Bits Stored, and, for JPEG 2000 and HTJ2K, no subsampled component, signedness as Pixel Representation gives it, and a multiple component transformation as Photometric Interpretation gives it (PS3.5 Sections 8.2.1, 8.2.3, 8.2.4, and 8.2.14). The codestream must also be of the coding process that its transfer syntax names (PS3.5 Section A.4): the JPEG frame header SOF0 for JPEG Baseline, SOF0 or SOF1 for JPEG Extended, and SOF3 with every scan's point transform 0 for JPEG Lossless; every scan lossless, with NEAR and the point transform 0, for JPEG-LS Lossless; and only the reversible wavelet, in the main header and every tile-part header, for the JPEG 2000 and HTJ2K syntaxes that are lossless only. The reversible wavelet is needed for lossless coding but does not show it: a codestream that uses it can be truncated, a lossy process (PS3.5 Section A.4.4), and whether every coding pass was kept is recorded only in packet headers. As the maintainer decided on 7 October 2026, for those syntaxes every packet header of every tile is therefore read too, in its progression order, and each code-block that the packets include must hold every coding pass to its least significant bit-plane: `3 (M_b - P) - 2` passes for ITU-T T.800 coding, and an HT cleanup pass to bit-plane 0, alone or with both refinement passes from bit-plane 1, for ITU-T T.814 coding. A frame that lacks a pass is `frame-mismatch`; one whose packets do not hold together, or that uses packed packet headers, progression order changes, a region of interest, Part 2 extensions, mixed HT code-blocks, or an HT code-block in more than one packet, is `undecodable-pixel-data`. A code-block that no packet includes is decoded as all zero, so a codestream truncated so far that it drops only whole code-blocks is not detected. A JPEG or JPEG-LS codestream must reach its EOI marker, and each scan must hold entropy-coded data, since GDCM decodes a frame whose scan is empty, or that lacks only its EOI, without an error, and pylibjpeg also one cut short within its scan; damaged entropy-coded data still followed by EOI is not detected. Otherwise the instance is sequestered, as `no-decoder` where the plugin is not installed or cannot be run, `offset-table-mismatch`, `undecodable-pixel-data`, or `frame-mismatch`, so compressed pixel data are never released unread. The `user` extra declares the plugins; the `dicom` extra does not, so with it alone only RLE Lossless can be decoded. Every Value Length and Item Length but the undefined length must be even (PS3.5 Sections 7.1.1 and 7.5), in the File Meta Information and at every depth of the data set, although the layout reader used to search written files for residual values reads odd lengths, so that nothing in such a file escapes the search. The engine keeps an immutable copy of those bytes as the source evidence, with where each element's Value Field is and its VR as written; a pydicom data set is only a mutable view of it, read afresh when needed. An element read for a decision is decoded from the evidence's Value Field, so pydicom decoding it on access changes neither the evidence nor what the engine reads. An element still encoded whose bytes or VR differ from the evidence, an element set in memory, and a decoded sequence whose items differ in number from the source's cannot be read as source. An element that pydicom has already decoded is read from the evidence whatever value the view now holds, since pydicom keeps no encoded form to compare, so the view is never the authority on a source value. Source text is decoded in the character set that the source declares, whatever the view's Specific Character Set now holds. Everything else is refused with a reason code and the offset where reading stopped, never a value.
   - The engine verifies what it preserves from the bytes of each written file's data set, including Data Set Trailing Padding, read as a source is, against the plan for its instance: which elements, by path, were kept, which emptied, replaced, or introduced, and which removed, with everything inside a removed sequence. Paths number items as the source does, so an item is never removed on its own. The preamble and File Meta Information are replaced (D-025) and not compared. An unchanged transfer syntax is necessary but not sufficient. The written file must have its source's transfer syntax, since the first supported release does not transcode; every source element must be planned once; nothing outside the plan may be written; every kept element must be in both files; and every emptied, replaced, or introduced element must be written. A kept element must have the VR as written in its source, which is absent in implicit VR, and, unless it holds items, the same header length and length form and the same Value Field, byte for byte; a kept sequence must have as many items, and its items' elements are verified by their own paths, so its length may be defined in one file and undefined in the other. A kept private element, including a private sequence or a private UN that holds items, must have a Private Creator with the same bytes in its own data set, or none in either file. Kept text of SH, LO, ST, LT, UT, UC, or PN, or of a VR not known, must also be in a Specific Character Set with the same bytes, that of its own data set or of the nearest item or data set that holds it and has one (PS3.5 Section 7.5.3); an empty one counts as absent, since both give the Default Character Repertoire. Nothing is decoded to compare: two encodings that decode to the same value, such as `1 ` and `01` in an IS, differ. Encapsulated Pixel Data of the top-level data set, of VR OB and starting with its Basic Offset Table item, in a transfer syntax that encapsulates it, has no single Value Field, so it is copied and compared whole, byte for byte: its Basic Offset Table, every fragment with its item header, and its delimiter. Any other kept value of undefined length without items, such as fragments elsewhere, cannot be verified. Each failure names a reason and an element path, never a value, and its instance is to be sequestered. Emptied, replaced, and introduced values are validated before they are written.
   - The engine therefore writes each data set from its source evidence rather than through pydicom's data set writer, which resolves an ambiguous VR, such as US or SS, from the data set it writes wherever it holds the VR as ambiguous, as for an element read from implicit VR or from the items of a UN sequence. Once a data set has been walked, pydicom writes a VOI LUT Sequence (0028,3010) that the source holds as UN as SQ, with the LUT Descriptor (0028,3002) of a CT image in Hounsfield Units as US rather than SS, which verification refuses. The engine's writer copies each kept element, header and value, from the source's bytes, including a sequence or other element holding items when nothing in it changes, so that an opaque value of defined length that happens to read as items is never rewritten as a sequence; it leaves out each removed element with everything in it. It writes each kept sequence with a change in it with its tag and VR as written and an undefined length, and each of its items with an undefined length, ended by the Item and Sequence Delimitation Items (PS3.5 Sections 6.2.2, 7.5.1, and 7.5.2), so that no length is worked out again when an element in an item changes; every item is written, even one whose elements are all removed. Only replacements and introduced elements are encoded, each by pydicom's writer for one element, with the VR it is given and the Specific Character Set in force at its place in the output, and placed in ascending order of tag; an introduced sequence's items are encoded in the same way, from their elements as held, without pydicom resolving any VR. A replacement is refused if it is still encoded, has an ambiguous VR, is a File Meta, command, or group length element or a delimiter, would be written with another VR, as pydicom writes UN for a value too long for its VR, or has text that cannot be written in the character set in force, which must be one the element decoder accepts (item 5), or, for a VR of the Default Character Repertoire alone, outside ISO 646. A plan is refused where it keeps an element inside one that is removed or replaced, keeps a group length, which could go stale, or changes the character set in force over kept text. A kept sequence stored as UN stays UN, with its items in implicit VR (PS3.5 Section 6.2.2), even where an element in them changes: its kept elements stay byte for byte as the source has them, and a reader resolves each VR in its items from its dictionary, as it does for the source. Rewriting it as SQ with explicit VR items would give every kept element in it a new header, and needs a VR for each, which a private attribute may not have. Where no VR is written, in implicit VR or in such items, a replacement is refused unless the pinned dictionary gives its VR alone, since an ambiguous VR such as US or SS, or another VR, could be read otherwise. Encapsulated Pixel Data of the top-level data set is copied byte for byte, or, where item 7 cuts metadata from its codestreams, written anew with VR OB, an undefined length, its items, each of an even, defined length, and the Sequence Delimitation Item; any other kept value of fragments is refused, since verification could not show it preserved. The File Meta Information and preamble come from D-025.
   - An attribute of the pinned dictionary that no rule covers, and that the engine does not itself remove or write (D-012, D-013), is kept if its VR is CS, AT, or a numeric VR, and a sequence or a binary value is kept only where the IOD defines the attribute at that place, with the attributes in a kept sequence's items handled by the same rules. Such an attribute of any other VR is removed (D-022).
   - Content that cannot be decoded inside a sequence that is removed is removed with it, and its values are listed as not collected in the confidential QC material, which makes the collection for the residual search incomplete (D-027). An instance is sequestered only where content that cannot be decoded would be kept or cleaned.
   - Each data set is planned before any of its values is read, from the source evidence's structure alone: each element's rule and action at its place, with compound actions resolved by Type, and what must read its value. The readers are UID replacement, the comparison of a dummy value with the source's, cleaning, and residual collection, which takes every value that is removed or replaced, those of a removed sequence's descendants included, whatever its VR, leaving what is searched to the residual search's own rules (D-027). A sequence is descended only under K or U, and its items' elements take their own rules; under any other action, its descendants go with it and take no rule of their own. The plan sequesters the instance where a dummy value is to be written but neither the VR nor a reviewed rule gives one (D-021), and where an attribute that is not removed is written with a VR, other than UN, that the pinned dictionary does not give it. Only the values that a reader needs are then decoded.
   - Compound actions such as X/Z/D are resolved from the strictest Type that the IOD gives the attribute at its place in the data set, as are plain actions where the IOD requires the attribute there or does not define it there (D-020). Z and D write the values that D-021 sets.
   - L3 rules are validated as D-011 requires.
   - Each element takes the rule of the first of these that covers it: the engine's own removals, of groups 0000, 0002, and 0004 within a data set, group lengths (gggg,0000), and Data Set Trailing Padding (item 7, D-016, D-025), and of Encrypted Attributes Sequence (D-013); Table E.1-1's Private Attributes row, for an odd group other than 0001, 0003, 0005, 0007, and FFFF, which PS3.5 Section 7.8.1 does not allow for private use; the table's other rows, by exact tag and then by masked tag, where (50xx,eeee) and (60xx,eeee) match only the repeating groups 5000 to 501E and 6000 to 601E (PS3.5 Section 7.6); the L2 rules; U by the role of a UI attribute (D-003); removal by Type of a text attribute (D-022); the rule for attributes that no rule covers (D-022); and, for an attribute absent from the pinned dictionary, removal. A sequence to which the policy gives C takes its Basic Profile action (D-009). A test fails if a person name, date, time, datetime, or UI attribute of the pinned dictionary reaches the rule for attributes that no rule covers.
3. **Identifiers.** Keyed replacement of UIDs (D-003) and patient identifiers (D-005), using keys and subject profiles held by a custodian (D-004). Under Retain Device Identity, the AE titles that Table E.1-1 gives C become keyed tokens, by the first of the reviewed cleaners that settle C (D-034).
4. **Dates and times.** Per-subject shifts (D-006), handling of each temporal attribute by its role (D-007), and stable synthetic birth dates for `tps-import` (D-008).
5. **Free text.** Descriptor cleaning (D-009) and the reviewed rules for text attributes that Table E.1-1 omits (D-022). Text is decoded according to the Specific Character Set (0008,0005) of its data set, or of the sequence item that has its own (PS3.5 Section 7.5.3), before matching, cleaning, or hashing. A data set whose Specific Character Set is not supported or is invalid is read in the Default Character Repertoire, as one without a value is, and an instance whose Specific Character Set cannot be read is sequestered (D-010). Specific Character Set is supported only as the Defined Terms of PS3.3 Section C.12.1.1.2 that pydicom can decode, combined as that section and PS3.5 Section 6.1.2.4 allow; in 2026d this leaves out ISO_IR 203 and ISO 2022 IR 203. ISO_IR 6 is also accepted alone: that section gives the Default Character Repertoire by the absence of a value, not by ISO_IR 6, but real data commonly holds ISO_IR 6, which names the same repertoire, so it is read as an absent value is, and decoded just as strictly. With other values it is not supported, since ISO 2022 IR 6 is the Defined Term for that repertoire with code extensions. Text that does not decode exactly in its character set cannot be decoded; where the Default Character Repertoire is the only character set, that includes any character outside ISO 646 (PS3.5 Section 6.1.2.1), although pydicom reads that repertoire as ISO 8859-1, and item 2 sets out when such text sequesters its instance. Nor is reading more lenient than writing: text that D-011's check of a written value finds could not be written back in its character set cannot be decoded either, such as a kanji in Shift JIS where Specific Character Set is ISO_IR 13, which is single-byte. That check encodes each whole value as pydicom's writer does, with the codec it chooses, and reads it back: a value that does not read back as the same single value, such as Latin letters and katakana together in ISO_IR 13, which pydicom writes with replacement characters, or a yen sign there, which JIS X 0201 places at the backslash that separates values (PS3.5 Section 6.1.2.3), cannot be written. Replacement values are encoded in the dataset's character set, and the dataset is converted to UTF-8 (ISO_IR 192) when a value cannot otherwise be encoded. The residual search (item 8) encodes each decoded source value again, in its source character set, UTF-8, ISO 8859-1, and UTF-16LE. Text with bytes outside the Default Character Repertoire that no Specific Character Set applies to is read for the search as ISO 8859-1, in which every byte is a character, so encoding it again in ISO 8859-1 gives its own bytes, and a copy of it left in the output is still found. That byte search supplements the coverage but does not stand in for the decoded text, whose copies in other character sets are not searched, so the value counts as read only as bytes and its collection as incomplete (D-027).
6. **Private attributes.** Removed unless the Retain Safe Private Option is selected.
   - Without that option, every element of an odd group is removed, at the top level and in the items of every standard sequence, and a private sequence is removed with everything in it (D-028).
   - With that option, a private attribute is retained only when shown to be safe by a route E.3.10 allows. It is matched by private creator, whichever block it occupies (MIDI §1.17.3), and the conformance statement lists each rule that can retain one, with its basis (D-035).
   - The L2 file `safe_private.toml` holds the reviewed rules: one for every row of Table E.3.10-1, and for each reviewed addition with its public source, each giving how a retained value is handled (D-035).
   - TCIA's Private Tag Dictionary ships unchanged in `_tcia/`, pinned by its SHA-256, as a second list that a run may choose instead of the reviewed rules; the method digest, the release report, and the conformance statement record which list a run used (D-035).
   - Table E.1-1 gives retained private attributes C, which each rule's handling settles: numbers and code strings are checked and kept, UIDs are replaced by D-003, and text is cleaned as descriptors are under Clean Descriptors and removed otherwise (D-009, D-035).
   - An instance's own Private Data Element Characteristics Sequence (0008,0300) is also a route: an attribute in a block that it marks SAFE in Block Identifying Information Status (0008,0303), or that it lists in Nonidentifying Private Elements (0008,0304), and that no rule lists, takes the handling of its VR, and one that it says may identify is removed, even where a rule would keep it (D-035).
   - Every other private attribute is removed, which E.3.10 allows in place of processing it as Deidentification Action (0008,0307) recommends, and (0008,0300) itself is removed by its supplementary rule (D-035).
   - A private sequence is parsed, and each nested attribute is handled on its own merits, as E.3.10 requires. One that no rule lists is kept, with its private creator, only where something in its items is kept or replaced (D-035).
7. **File container.**
   - The engine replaces the File Meta Information and the preamble, as E.1.1 requires: it builds the File Meta Information itself, describing the de-identifying application as D-025 sets, and writes a zeroed preamble (D-002).
   - It removes group 0004 from every file other than a DICOMDIR, and removes Data Set Trailing Padding. It also removes every element of group 0000, the DIMSE command set (PS3.7 Section 6.3), wherever it appears in a data set, as the maintainer decided on 1 October 2026: a command element has no place in a stored instance, and one such as Requested SOP Instance UID (0000,1001) would otherwise carry a source UID that no UID role replaces. A source DICOMDIR is never passed through, and none is written, since the output is a plain directory tree rather than a File-set (D-016); E.1.1 would allow one only if regenerated from the de-identified files.
   - Encapsulated Document (0042,0011), which Table E.1-1 gives D, is replaced, or the instance is sequestered, where the IOD defines it at that place. Elsewhere, as in every IOD of the first supported release, it is removed, since D acts as X on an attribute that the IOD does not define at that place (D-020).
   - Metadata in compressed pixel data bitstreams, such as JPEG APPn segments, is removed without recompression. Each frame's codestream of encapsulated Pixel Data is parsed, and every byte of it accounted for: the frame is found from the Basic Offset Table, as one fragment, or by parsing the fragments one codestream after another, each ending where a fragment ends. Every comment (COM) and application (APPn) marker segment of JPEG and JPEG-LS is cut, except a JFIF APP0 without a thumbnail, an Adobe APP14, and, in JPEG-LS, an APP8 colour transform, each of fixed format, which say how to decode colour and are kept once parsed; so is every comment (COM) in the main header of a JPEG 2000 or HTJ2K codestream. An RLE Lossless frame has no metadata segments; its header and segments are parsed, and each segment must decode to one byte plane. A frame that cannot be found or parsed, a marker that its transfer syntax does not define, a JPEG 2000 codestream inside a JP2 file, and any byte after a codestream's end other than one padding byte of 0x00 sequester the instance (`unparsable-codestream`), since bytes that no decoder reads could carry anything; so do a comment in a JPEG 2000 tile-part header, which could be cut only by rewriting the tile-part's length, and a cut where an Extended Offset Table is present, whose values would have to change too (`uncuttable-metadata`). Where something is cut, Pixel Data is written anew, one fragment for each frame, with a Basic Offset Table where the source had one, and every frame of the written file must decode to the same pixels as its source's frame, by the same pinned plugin (item 2), or the instance is sequestered (`cut-changed-pixels`). Where nothing is cut, Pixel Data is kept byte for byte.
   - A run lists every entry below its source directory, in an order that depends only on their paths relative to it, and names each input by its position in that order, its run position. It follows no symbolic link or other link, opens no file that is not a regular file, and reads a file only while it is still the one that it listed.
   - Output file and directory names are built only from replacement identifiers, never from source paths, file names, or attribute values. Each instance is written to `<Patient ID>/<Study Instance UID>/<Series Instance UID>/<SOP Instance UID>.dcm` below the output directory, from its replacement values, or from the keyed replacements of the UIDs that the Retain UIDs Option keeps (D-016, D-040).
   - De-identification markers follow D-012.
8. **Referential integrity.**
   - A first pass builds a graph of instances and references. It reports dangling references, frame of reference mismatches, and inconsistent hierarchy using opaque identifiers, before anything is written. It follows the reference attributes that the PS3.3 tables define inside a sequence of the instance's IOD, and resolves each against the inputs' SOP Instance, Series Instance, or Study Instance UIDs, by the level of what it names. The attribute gives the level, except that Referenced SOP Instance UID names a study in the items of Referenced Study Sequence and RT Referenced Study Sequence, whose Referenced SOP Class UID is the study's own class, such as the retired Detached Study Management (PS3.3 10.6.1 and C.8.8.5.4). An empty value where the IOD makes the attribute Type 3 means the same as an absent one (PS3.5 7.4.5), so it is no reference; an empty Type 1 value is invalid and an empty Type 2 value is unknown (PS3.5 7.4.1 and 7.4.3), so each names nothing, and is dangling unless the class rule below applies. Where the IOD's definitions give an attribute several Types at one place, the strictest applies, with 1C as 1 and 2C as 2. A reference to an instance whose Referenced SOP Class UID is not a Standard Storage SOP Class of PS3.4 Table B.5-1, such as a Private or retired SOP Class, resolves if it names an input; otherwise it is not reported, because such a class may name something that is not a stored instance, such as a performed procedure step, or a patient through a retired or private management class. The class rule does not apply to a reference to a study, which is dangling unless an input has that Study Instance UID. A UID that the pinned tables register is never dangling. Findings name inputs by their position in the run and attributes by their tags, and contain no values.
   - The first pass also reports an inconsistent hierarchy: instances that share a SOP Instance UID, as identical duplicates where their source bytes are the same and as conflicting copies otherwise; a series whose instances name several Study Instance UIDs; and a study whose instances name several patients, each the identity of a Patient ID with its issuer (D-005). The instances without a Patient ID together count as one more patient, apart from every identity (D-026), and an instance without the UID that a check compares is left out of that check, since it is already reported as missing that identifier. The first pass also reports a frame of reference mismatch: a series whose instances name several Frame of Reference UIDs (0020,0052), though "each Series shall have a single Frame of Reference UID" (PS3.3 C.7.4.1.1.1); an RT Structure Set ROI whose Referenced Frame of Reference UID (3006,0024) has no value or is not listed "once and only once" in the structure set's Referenced Frame of Reference Sequence (3006,0010) (PS3.3 C.8.8.5.1); and an input image that an item of that sequence lists in its Contour Image Sequences under a frame of reference other than the image's own, since the frame of reference of a set of ROIs is "obtained from the source images" (PS3.3 C.8.8.5.1, Note). An input or item without a Frame of Reference UID is left out of the comparisons it would take part in. A run reports each such mismatch only, as it does a dangling reference (D-026). An instance's source bytes are its Transfer Syntax UID (0002,0010) and the bytes of its data set from the end of the File Meta Information to the end of the file, leaving out each group length (gggg,0000), which PS3.5 7.2 retires, and the Data Set Trailing Padding (FFFC,FFFC) at the top level of the data set; the preamble and the File Meta Information are not part of the data set (PS3.10 7.1). A group length or padding inside an item is compared as it is. Copies are identical only when their source bytes are the same (D-026). The first pass finds the bytes with PyMedPhys's own reader of a file's structure, which decodes no value, so no decoder or guessed VR takes part in the comparison. Copies that differ only in their encoding therefore conflict, which sequesters them: copies in Implicit VR and in Explicit VR Little Endian, with sequences or items of defined and of undefined length, with values padded differently, or with compressed and uncompressed Pixel Data. A copy whose bytes cannot be shown to be sound conflicts with every other copy: one whose structure cannot be read to the end of the file, one without a Transfer Syntax UID, one whose transfer syntax deflates the data set or is the retired Explicit VR Big Endian, and one with a top-level group length or Data Set Trailing Padding that holds items, whose elements would otherwise be compared as if they were at the top level. A file in which a sequence that the first pass follows does not hold only items, including any such sequence in Explicit VR Big Endian, which only pydicom can read, has no record, since its references cannot be followed; the first pass refuses it by the sequence's path. The first pass reads each file in full, and compares SHA-256 digests of the source bytes, which it keeps only for the run.
   - After writing, the engine verifies one-to-one UID mappings; consistent references; consistent values for each entity above the instance level, such as Patient ID, Study ID, and Series Number (E.1.1 step 2, Note 3); absence of original UIDs where replacement or removal is required; and unchanged values where retention is required. A run checks the UIDs and references of the files that its gate releases against the first pass before it publishes anything. A finding there is a fault of the engine, so the run sequesters each instance that it names and checks the rest again; a reference to an instance that was withheld is reported only, as a dangling reference is (D-026); and a check that fails, or a finding that no sequestration isolates, publishes nothing.
   - It searches every byte of each written file for the source values that had to be removed or replaced: the instance's own, and its subject's from the run's other instances. It reports each **residual**, a source value found in a form the search derives, by the value's kind (person name, UID, date, datetime, or other text), its source attribute path, the form and encoding found, and its place: the preamble, a File Meta Information element, a data set element by its path, with sequence items numbered from 0, Data Set Trailing Padding, or the bytes after the last readable element. It also lists each form it did not search, and why. Neither list contains a source value (D-016). The search itself decides nothing: output is written to a staging area, and the release gate (`pymedphys._dicom.deidentify.release_gate`) runs the search of each written file itself, for exactly the values collected across the file's subject, and decides from the search and the coverage of that collection whether the file is released, goes to QC review, or is withheld, with the consequences that D-027 sets. A search that finds nothing is not on its own a release.
     - **Forms.** Each value is searched as given, composed (NFC), decomposed (NFD), with compatibility characters replaced (NFKC and NFKD), and with each character that has a half-width or full-width form written in its other width, such as `ﾔﾏﾀﾞ` for `ヤマダ` or `ＹＡＭＡＤＡ` for `YAMADA`. It is split at backslashes unless its VR is LT, ST, UR, or UT, and stripped of spaces, NULs, and whitespace at either end. A person name is searched whole, by each component group, by its family, given, and middle names, by each word of those names between spaces or hyphens, and with its family and given names run together in either order, as Chinese, Japanese, and Korean names are usually written. Where the family or given name has fewer than four characters, they are also searched in either order with a space or an ideographic space (U+3000) between them, and as "family, given". A name with an apostrophe, whether ASCII's or U+2018, U+2019, or U+02BC, is also searched with ASCII's, with U+2019, and without it. A date, including one written as YYYY.MM.DD, as ACR-NEMA wrote dates, is also searched as YYYYMMDD, YYYY-MM-DD, YYYY:MM:DD (as EXIF writes dates in bitstream metadata), DD/MM/YYYY, MM/DD/YYYY, and DD.MM.YYYY, and a datetime by its date in each of these. A UID or other text is searched as it is, by up to its first 256 characters.
     - **Not searched.** Forms of fewer than four characters, counted once composed (NFKC); binary values; values that cannot be decoded, which are removed with the sequence that contains them (item 2), apart from text with bytes outside the Default Character Repertoire that no Specific Character Set applies to, which is read as ISO 8859-1 (item 5); datetimes without a full date, such as `2024`, which would match any number that contains it; and values of VR AS, AT, CS, DS, FD, FL, IS, SL, SS, SV, TM, UL, US, and UV, whose codes, numbers, ages, times, and tags occur throughout files and are checked attribute by attribute instead. Each is listed as not searched (D-027).
     - **Not collected.** As the maintainer clarified on 2 October 2026, a value that cannot be decoded is never collected, so it is also listed as not collected, as text read only as ISO 8859-1 (item 5) is listed as read as bytes, and both make the collection incomplete (D-027).
     - **Constants.** A value that equals a constant the engine writes whatever the source held is not searched, since finding it would reveal nothing about the source. The constants are D's constants and second constants of the VRs that are searched, the values of the item that D writes in Person Identification Code Sequence (D-021), and the markers' fixed values: Patient Identity Removed, Longitudinal Temporal Information Modified, Manufacturer, and the Code Value, Coding Scheme Designator, and Code Meaning of each code that the markers can write (D-012). Keyed values, such as replacement UIDs and pseudonyms, and the markers' versions and method digest are not constants. A value is compared with each constant as D compares a source value with its constant, as text of the source value's VR (D-021), so that `Deidentified` and `1900.01.01` are skipped; a value of several values is compared value by value, and only those equal to a constant are skipped. Each form that the search derives from a value, such as a person name's family name or a datetime's date, is compared in the same way, and a form equal to a constant is skipped while the value's other forms are still searched. Each source attribute skipped in whole or in part is recorded once by its attribute, never by its value (D-027). A value of a VR that is never searched is listed as not searched for that reason instead.
     - **Encodings and case.** Each form is searched in UTF-8, ISO 8859-1, UTF-16LE, and its source character set (item 5). In the source character set, a form can also be spelt with ISO 2022 escape sequences, whose bytes depend on the text around them: where the character set's codec writes them itself, or, for a form that is not ASCII, where Specific Character Set has more than one value, so that a writer puts one before each component in another character set (PS3.5 Section 6.1.2.5.3). Such a form is still searched as the codec encodes it, which finds a single component after its escape sequence, or text written without them, as pydicom writes ISO 2022 IR 58 text; it is also listed, since its spellings with other escape sequences are not searched. Where the codec designates a character set before the form and resets after it, as ISO 2022 JP does, the bytes between them are also searched, as a longer run in that character set, such as the form followed by an honorific, writes them. ASCII letters match in either case, and text with other letters is also searched in upper, lower, and title case; a capital inside a word on a letter outside ASCII, as in a McDonald-style spelling in Cyrillic, is found only where the whole form is in one of those cases. Case is folded byte by byte, so a byte of an ASCII capital inside a character of several bytes, as in UTF-16LE, Shift_JIS, or GBK, is folded too, and some text outside ASCII matches a form by chance; this errs towards a finding.
     - **Matches.** Letters and digits are those of ASCII, and in UTF-16LE each neighbouring character is two bytes. A match does not count where a digit precedes a form that starts with a digit, as in a 39-digit component of a `2.25.` UID; where a digit follows a form that ends with one, unless the form is a date or datetime, which a time may follow, or the first 256 characters of a longer value; where a letter adjoins a person name form at an edge that is a letter, as "MARY" in "PRIMARY"; for a form of digits alone, inside a DS or IS value, such as contour data; or, for a form of digits alone that a digit follows, inside the integer of a UI value under the `2.25.` root (PS3.5 B.2), which the engine uses for keyed replacement UIDs, so that a date at the start of a keyed replacement UID is not a finding. That rule recognises the root, not who wrote the UID, so it applies to a source UID kept under that root too, and should be reassessed before a preset keeps source UIDs; digits that make up the whole integer, and UIDs under other roots, are still found. These rules are for text: a match wholly in a value that holds numbers, below, or in the fragments of encapsulated Pixel Data whose codestreams all parse counts whatever bytes adjoin it, since they are samples and coding parameters, not letters or digits of a longer word or number.
     - **Values that hold numbers.** Native Pixel Data, Float Pixel Data, and Double Float Pixel Data of the top-level data set, and values of VR OD, OF, OL, OV, and OW, are searched only for forms of at least 8 bytes that are not UTF-16LE, since UTF-16LE text and shorter forms match sample values by chance. The entropy-coded data of encapsulated Pixel Data of the top-level data set, with its Basic Offset Table, where every codestream of its fragments parses, are searched only for forms of at least 6 bytes that are not UTF-16LE, except in RLE Lossless, below: compressed samples match short forms by chance as often as random bytes do, or more. Measured on 7 October 2026 over synthetic phantoms of 12 bits, 256 by 256, in JPEG Lossless, JPEG 2000 Lossless, RLE Lossless, and JPEG Baseline, each 4-letter form matched by chance about once in every 30 to 200 MB, and each 4-digit form up to once in every 40 MB, against about once in every 40 TB or more for an 8-letter form. A run of a few GB with tens of short forms would therefore hold hundreds of files for review for nothing. Text in a codestream lies in its marker segments, which item 7 cuts or parses. Its entropy-coded data hold samples as native Pixel Data does, but also bytes that no decoder reads: after the data a decoder needs, at the end of a JPEG or JPEG-LS scan or restart interval, of a JPEG 2000 tile-part, or of a JPEG 2000 code-block's codeword segments, a writer can put anything, and the frame still decodes identically. Finding those bytes would take a full decode in JPEG 2000, so, as the maintainer decided on 10 October 2026, the entropy-coded data are searched for forms of 6 bytes or more, not 8 as sample values are, and names and other forms of 4 and 5 bytes there are not found. RLE Lossless has no such bytes, since item 7 parses each of its segments, which must decode to exactly one byte plane, so its fragments are searched as values that hold numbers. Searching them for forms of 6 bytes would find nothing hidden and would cost more than elsewhere, since RLE keeps each byte of the samples in its own segment: the low bytes of soft tissue in CT, such as stored values 1072 to 1081 (48 to 57 HU with a rescale intercept of -1024), are the ASCII digits, so a numeric identifier of 6 digits matches by chance far more often than a 6-letter form. Estimated on 10 October 2026 from the runs of letters in the entropy-coded data of synthetic CT phantoms, 512 by 512, each 6-letter form would match by chance about once in every 0.2 TB in JPEG Baseline and once in every 1 to 6 TB in JPEG 2000 Lossless, JPEG 2000 at 10:1, and JPEG Lossless SV1; searching 1.5 GB of those phantoms, 300 MB in each, for a patient's invented values, of whose forms 6 are of 6 or 7 bytes, found nothing. Every other byte, including the marker segments of those codestreams, fragments whose codestreams do not parse, OB and UN values, and bytes that cannot be read as elements, is searched for every form.
     - **Reports.** Each source attribute is reported once for each place it is found, by its widest form there, with the encoding and byte offset of that form's first match there. A file whose structure cannot be read to its end is still searched in full, and reported as not fully readable.
   - It reads each written file's structure independently of the library that wrote it, so that anything verification finds is located by element path, or in the preamble, the File Meta Information, Data Set Trailing Padding, or the bytes after the last readable element.
   - The first pass's findings have graded consequences, from reporting a dangling reference to stopping the run for a study with several patients (D-026).
9. **Risk detection and quality control.** Indicators for burned-in text and reconstructable faces (D-015), a restricted QC pack for human review (D-016), and statistical disclosure control (D-017).
   - A text detector, `pymedphys._dicom.deidentify.text_detection`, proposes boxes around burned-in text for the Clean Pixel Data Option (E.3.1), whose regions the engine blacks out (D-037). It runs PaddleOCR's PP-OCRv6 small text detection model, licensed under Apache-2.0, which it downloads on first use from RapidOCR's wheel on PyPI, pinned with the model by SHA-256, on ONNX Runtime from the `text-detection` extra, single-threaded, with ONNX Runtime's telemetry disabled before it is imported; on Windows it runs only the official `onnxruntime` wheel from 1.31, the first whose telemetry that setting turns off there. It searches each frame's full stored range and its white and black top-hats, and drops regions too wide or too sparse to be lines of text, which the network otherwise marks on smooth anatomy. It reads no text and can miss faint or small text, so it guides review rather than replacing it.
10. **Reports.**
    - Each run writes a machine-readable and human-readable release report, which contains no source values or original paths (D-016) and names released instances only by their output names and sequestered instances only by labels (D-026). It records the method digest with its components (D-024) and the runtime environment that ran (D-029). Its human-readable form, `release-report.md` beside `release-report.json`, is CommonMark, with GitHub Flavored Markdown tables, generated from the JSON text alone, which shows each of its values as a code span and draws nothing else from it, so it can hold no value or path that the JSON lacks. Its machine-readable form is a JSON document labelled with its format, `pymedphys-deid-release-report/22`, whose `policy` section names the PS3.15 edition, the preset (none for a custom option set), the selected options, and whether the policy can claim conformance, and whose `method` and `runtime` sections hold the fields that D-024 and D-029 list. Its `qc_review` section gives the run's QC pack by its opaque reference, with the outcome of its attestation (attested, rejected, or not attested) and the releaser's two confirmations in it, that the output was checked for its intended use and that its residual risk was accepted, each true, false, or null where not stated and null without an attestation, or is null for a run that wrote no QC pack (D-016). Its `face_review` section gives, under the Clean Recognizable Visual Features Option, the opaque reference of the earlier run's QC pack whose face verdicts the run followed, and how many series of CT Image instances the run defaced and how many it did not, by reason (D-050), and is null under any other policy (D-046). Its `redaction_review` section gives, under the Clean Pixel Data Option, the opaque reference of the earlier run's QC pack whose redaction verdicts the run followed, and is null where the run was given none (D-048). Its `released` section lists the output name of each released instance, sorted (D-026). Its `sequestered` section names each sequestered instance by a random label for the run, such as `S-0007`, with each reason: the stage that sequestered it (scope, admission, which refuses a source file and sets it aside, the first pass's references, the walker, the run's own checks, the transform, including descriptor cleaning, the writer, the check that the source is preserved, or the release gate), the stage's reason code, the attribute's tags without items from the walker, and from the release gate where its reason names one, and, from the walker only, the action and the VR; for an instance of an unsupported IOD, the reason also gives the IOD as PS3.4 Table B.5-1 names it without "IOD", such as `Comprehensive SR`, never a value from the instance (D-010, D-026). Its `held_for_review` section counts the instances held for review by stage (descriptor cleaning's ROI names, Clean Pixel Data's pixel data (D-037), the face review (D-046), including a CT series with a target in its face block or an eye ROI that disagrees with its globes and an RT Structure Set, RT Dose, or PET instance beside a defaced CT series that the run does not clean (D-050), a reviewed RT Structure Set whose contours the run writes otherwise (D-052), or an RT Dose with too much dose in a defaced series' face block to set to zero there (D-051), or the release gate) and reason code, each instance once for each reason however many of its names or attributes have it, and names none of them (D-009). The labels are `S-0001` to `S-n` for `n` sequestered instances, in an order drawn at random. Its `roi_names` section counts the ROI Names of the structure sets that descriptor cleaning cleaned in the run, each name by its outcome (renamed, empty, kept, mapped, emptied, held, or emptied unreviewed), and the distinct names sent for review, whether then held or emptied unreviewed, each once for each of its reasons, as the confidential QC pack lists them, and names none of them (D-009). Its `descriptors` section counts the values of the free-text descriptors that descriptor cleaning read in the run, each value by its outcome (empty, kept, kept by vocabulary, mapped, emptied, held, or emptied unreviewed), the distinct values, each of one attribute, that it did not clean, each once for each of its reasons, and the elements that took the action that the policy gives them without Clean Descriptors, as the confidential QC pack lists them, and names none of them (D-009). Its `reference_findings` section counts the instances that have each kind of first-pass finding that the run reports without acting on it, such as a dangling reference, each instance once for each kind, an identical copy counting as the instance it copies, and names none of them (D-026). Its `pixel_risks` section counts the released instances, and those held for review, that show each risk in their pixel data (burned-in text, a reconstructable face, or a possibly disclosed body weight), and each indicator of it, among them a CT, MR, PET, or RT Dose volume, a localizer, the head or neck, values scaled or mapped to an SUV, and unreadable evidence, each instance once for each, an identical copy counting as the instance it copies, as the confidential QC pack lists them, and names none of them (D-015). Its `search_coverage` section counts the source values that the residual search did not search, in full or in part, by attribute and reason, each value once for each reason however many of its forms that reason left out, at the instance that holds it, although each search of its subject's files looks for it (D-027). Its `source_gaps` section counts, by attribute and Type, the instances whose source lacks an attribute that their IOD unconditionally requires, which the run reports and never acts on (MIDI-BP-03); the QC pack lists each by instance and place. Its `structural_checks` section summarises the engine's checks that the release is well formed and refers to itself as the source did, as the maintainer decided on 6 October 2026: for the first pass's references (`references`), the transform's check that each written instance keeps the attributes its IOD requires where its source had them, with the source's own gaps (`iod-requirements`), and the second pass's check of what was written (`written-references`), always in that order, how many instances the check sequestered, which the report refuses to put above those that its `sequestered` section lists with the check's reasons, and how many it reported without acting on them, each instance once, an identical copy counting as the instance it copies, naming none; the QC pack lists each reported finding by instance, including a written reference to an instance that was not written (D-026). Every field is built from the policy, the engine's own files, the versions that run it, the keyed digests of the reviewed-names and reviewed descriptors lists, the technique vocabulary's digest, the digest of the text finder's class and description of its method, and codes and tags that the engine defines, never from a DICOM value directly, and as a backstop, writing the report refuses any field that is not a digest, a version, a known edition, preset, or option, a file name or path within the engine's package or, for an outside module, within PyMedPhys, a QC pack's reference, an attestation outcome, or a confirmation given only with an attestation, an output name listed once, one of the run's labels, a path of tags, a code that the engine defines, an IOD that Table B.5-1 names and the release does not support, a positive count, the descriptors' count of elements that fell back, from 0, or a structural check's count from 0. A change to its fields takes a new format label.
    - Each run also writes a conformance statement generated from the rule tables and the effective policy, beside the release report at the root of the release, since it holds no instance value. It covers everything E.1.3 requires, the descriptions that E.3.5, E.3.6, E.3.7, and E.3.10 require for selected options, and each interpretation recorded in this document.
      - It is generated as CommonMark from the policy, the pinned tables, and the engine's parameters, so it is regenerated whenever they change and never edited by hand, and the same inputs always give the same text. It names tags, actions, and parameters, never a value from an instance. It lists the action that the engine applies to each attribute, with each compound action, and each plain X, Z, and D, resolved by Type at every place where a supported IOD defines the attribute, other than within a sequence that is removed with its contents (D-020), and says why where that is not the policy's action, as for the engine's own removals and a sequence to which the policy gives C. It describes the rules that give every other element its action (D-022), the values that Z, D, and U write (D-003, D-005, D-021), how dates and times are handled without a Retain Longitudinal Temporal Information Option (D-006, D-023), and how Modified Dates modifies them (D-007, D-039), what the residual search covers and lists as not searched (D-027), and how the release report names sequestered instances by labels and counts the values that the residual search does not search by attribute and reason, with per-instance detail only in the confidential QC pack (D-016, D-026, D-027), how ROI Names are cleaned where the policy gives ROI Name C (D-009), gives the policy's method digest (D-024), and describes the markers that each instance gains as the engine computes them for the policy (D-012).
      - Where the engine does not yet decide something that the statement must describe, such as the manner of cleaning an attribute to which the policy gives C other than under Clean Descriptors alone, as under Modified Dates, or a run under a policy whose other options cannot be composed without Clean Descriptors, the statement lists it under "Not yet described" and makes no conformance claim. Where an IOD requires an attribute to which the policy gives a plain X, the statement names the enclosing sequence or overlay group that D-020 removes with it, or the sequestration that it decides instead, as the walker plans them. A preset is enabled only once its statement lists nothing there.
    - Each release publishes a traceability matrix from requirements to tests (D-018). The documentation shows the register's part of it, without test results, as the [requirements matrix](deidentification-requirements.md), which every documentation build generates.

## Presets

| Preset | Intended use | Profile and options |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `basic` (default) | Baseline transformation | Basic Profile, with no options |
| `basic-clean-descriptors` | Baseline transformation that keeps cleaned descriptors, such as structure names that match TG-263 | Basic Profile; Clean Descriptors |
| `tps-import` | Copies for non-clinical treatment planning system databases, such as research copies of patient data, or duplicates of phantom data that must coexist without UID conflicts | Basic Profile; Retain Longitudinal Temporal Information with Modified Dates; Retain Patient Characteristics; Retain Device Identity; Clean Descriptors |
| `public-release` | Collections being assessed for unrestricted public sharing | Basic Profile; Retain Longitudinal Temporal Information with Modified Dates; Clean Descriptors; Retain Safe Private |

- These are the target option sets. Each preset's policy is composed from its options and validated before processing (D-011). A preset is enabled only once its behaviour is implemented and validated for the documented input scope. No preset is enabled yet; the first supported release is to enable `basic` and `basic-clean-descriptors`. A run can select a custom option set in place of a preset (D-033), which is validated in the same way, except that every conflict between its options is rejected, so its policy can claim conformance, unlike that of `tps-import`. A custom option set is enabled only while `basic` and each of its options are enabled, an option being enabled once its behaviour is implemented and validated; no option is enabled yet. Selecting a preset or options is not evidence of conformance (D-011).
- Profile implementations strictly match DICOM definitions. A preset named after a profile, such as `basic` or `basic-clean-descriptors`, applies exactly the profile and options its name gives, and any claim a preset makes names exactly the profile and options it applies (D-011). `basic` therefore stays exactly the Basic Profile, with no options, and the Clean Descriptors Option that the first supported release includes has a preset of its own rather than joining `basic`.
- `tps-import` makes no PS3.15 conformance claim, because its Retain Device Identity and Modified Dates options conflict (D-007). Its output is marked as D-012 describes.
- `tps-import` writes synthetic birth dates (D-008), cleans ROI names as `basic-clean-descriptors` does, keeping no name that has not been reviewed apart from those that automatic cleaning writes in the vocabulary's spelling, and retains other cleaned descriptors only after pooled human review, otherwise applying the Basic Profile's actions (D-009). Each run with a new key gives UIDs and patient pseudonyms unrelated to earlier copies, so several copies of one source, such as a phantom, can coexist in one planning system (D-003).
- `public-release` omits Retain Device Identity, because device serial numbers and machine names can identify an institution and, with dates, individual treatments.
