- **Open question.** Whether the naming principle above should also rename `tps-import` and `public-release`, which are named after their purposes rather than after a profile, is not yet decided.
- Profile conformance and readiness to release are reported separately. `basic` and `basic-clean-descriptors` output is not thereby ready to share. `tps-import` output is for non-clinical databases only. `public-release` output also needs a statistical assessment, pixel and face review, and a human QC attestation (D-016 and D-017).

## Decisions

These are the active design decisions, not statements that the code implements them. Decision identifiers are stable and never reused. A number reserved for a decision still being drafted is marked by an HTML comment where its section goes, such as `<!-- D-040 -->`, which the pull request that records it replaces, so that decisions recorded in parallel change no line in common and the sections stay in numeric order.

### D-001: Tables generated from a pinned edition

- **Decision.**
  - A development command generates the L1 tables from the pinned edition of the standard, currently 2026d. It downloads the published HTML pages, verifies each page's SHA-256 against the pinned digest, and parses them with `html.parser`. Most pages are NEMA's chtml pages, one per section. PS3.3's module and macro tables span dozens of those, so PS3.3 is read from its single-page HTML, which marks up tables in the same way. It records the edition, the source digests, and a digest of the generated content. The PS3.6 data dictionary and Annex A tables, and the PS3.16 coding schemes and context groups, parse from the HTML as reliably as the Annex E tables, so no DocBook XML parser is needed.
  - The repository and the package keep the generated tables, not the standard's source files. Each generated file carries the copyright attribution `DICOM PS3.x <edition>, © NEMA`, such as "DICOM PS3.15 2026d, © NEMA".
  - A loader reads each table, checks each row's fields, types, and actions, and rejects a table whose rows no longer match the digest recorded in the file. This catches a table edited without updating that digest; it does not authenticate the file.
  - Table E.1-1a must define exactly the action codes the engine implements, so a new edition that adds or removes a code fails generation until the engine handles it.
  - PS3.3 Types are generated only for the composite IODs, ahead of the releases that support them: each table of PS3.3 Annex A titled with an IOD's name and "IOD Modules", such as "CT Image IOD Modules", 174 tables in 2026d. The first supported release supports 14 of these IODs: CT Image, Enhanced CT Image, Legacy Converted Enhanced CT Image, MR Image, Enhanced MR Image, Enhanced MR Color Image, Legacy Converted Enhanced MR Image, MR Spectroscopy, Positron Emission Tomography Image, Enhanced PET Image, Legacy Converted Enhanced PET Image, RT Structure Set, RT Plan, and RT Dose. Generated Types do not make an IOD supported: its instances are sequestered until a release supports it (D-010). PS3.3 publishes 18 other tables titled "IOD Modules" in 2026d, for the Normalized IODs of Annex B and the Basic Directory IOD of Annex F, and their Types are deliberately not generated. No instance of them is de-identified: Normalized IODs describe the attributes of DIMSE-N services, so their module tables give no Types apart from the SOP Common Module's, since PS3.4 sets their attributes' requirements for each service, and the Basic Directory IOD is a DICOMDIR, which is never passed through (D-016). For each IOD, its modules table gives each module's information entity, section, and usage, with any condition; a section number can end in a letter, as C.7.6.4b does. Each module's attribute table is the one in that section titled with the module's name and "Module Attributes", such as "Patient Module Attributes". Attribute tables, and every macro table they include, are kept as published, without descriptions: each row's nesting depth, name, tag, and Type, or the macro an "Include" row adds. Generation checks every tag against the data dictionary and allows nesting only below a sequence, or one level below an "Include" of a macro whose only top-level attribute is a sequence, whose items the nested rows extend, as Table C.18.4-1 "Image Reference Macro Attributes" extends Referenced SOP Sequence (0008,1199) of Table C.18.3-1 "Composite Object Reference Macro Attributes". A table may include itself below one of its own sequences, as Table C.17-6 "Document Relationship Macro Attributes" does in Content Sequence (0040,A730) and Table C.38.2-3 "Inventory Reference Macro Attributes" does in Incorporated Inventory Instance Sequence (0008,0422), so that content nests to any depth; generation rejects any other cycle of includes.
  - Errors in the published tables are corrected by named corrections in the pin. Each replaces text that must occur in exactly one row of its table, or in a stated number of rows where identical cells need it, counting its title and header as rows, so a correction fails generation once an edition changes that text. In 2026d there are 18: usages separated from their conditions by an en dash, a hyphen without a space, or nothing; module tables titled differently from their modules, and a macro table titled differently from its section, "Frame VOI LUT With LUT Macro"; two modules listed with the wrong section; a name column headed "Attribute name"; an "Include" row with a space after its ">" characters; and the X-Ray Grid Macro's Include of the X-Ray Grid Description Macro, published outside the macro's only sequence, X-Ray Grid Sequence (0018,9555), whose Grid (0018,1166) it describes, which the pin nests in that sequence, as the X-Ray Filter Macro nests its description in its own.
  - The Multi-frame Functional Groups Module (C.7.6.16), and three other modules like it, include the IOD's Functional Group Macros in the items of their Functional Groups Sequences: of the Multi-frame Functional Groups Module, the one item of Shared Functional Groups Sequence (5200,9229) and the item for each frame of Per-Frame Functional Groups Sequence (5200,9230). Each IOD whose modules include them lists its macros, each with a usage, in a table of Annex A titled with its name and "Functional Group Macros", such as Table A.38-2 for Enhanced CT Image, except that the text gives the Enhanced MR Color Image IOD the Enhanced MR Image IOD's table, which the pin records by the two IODs' names. Generation keeps the row that includes the macros as an "Include" of them, records the IOD's macros with their usage and condition, and keeps each macro's attribute table, the one in its section titled with its name and "Macro Attributes" or "Functional Group Macro Attributes". It fails if an IOD's modules include Functional Group Macros and no table lists them, or a table lists them and no module includes them; if such a table names no IOD of Annex A, or an IOD has two; if rows are nested below the row that includes them; and if a macro does not define exactly one top-level attribute, a sequence, or includes Functional Group Macros itself, since each Functional Group is one sequence (C.7.6.16.1.1).
  - The three real-time IODs, Real-Time Video Endoscopic Image, Real-Time Video Photographic Image, and Real-Time Audio Waveform, are left out: their Current Frame Functional Groups Module gives Current Frame Functional Groups Sequence (0006,0001), which PS3.6 defines in Table 9-1, not in the data dictionary of Table 6-1. The pin names each by its table and name, since table numbers can change between editions, and records the attribute it lacks, (0006,0001). Generation fails if a named table is not the modules table of Annex A of the IOD named with it, if an IOD it names can be generated or fails for any reason other than the attribute recorded with it, or if an IOD it does not name cannot be generated. In 2026d, Types are generated for 171 of the 174 composite IODs, including 30 of the 33 whose modules include Functional Group Macros, such as Enhanced CT Image and Segmentation.
  - The loader checks both PS3.3 files when it reads them, and expands an IOD's modules when its Types are first needed, following every "Include", into each attribute's Type in each module and within each enclosing sequence, and keeps the result. An Include by which a table includes itself is kept as a reference back to that table rather than expanded again, so the attributes below that sequence have the same Types at every level of nesting, and the loader finds them at any depth. An Include of Functional Group Macros expands the IOD's macros there, so in both Functional Groups Sequences of the Multi-frame Functional Groups Module, and each attribute defined in a macro names it. A macro's usage applies to the macro as a module's does to the module: whether an instance must, under its condition must, or may include the macro's sequence, in the Shared item or in each Per-Frame item, and the Types of the macro's attributes apply wherever it is included. A condition, such as Frame Content's "May not be used as a Shared Functional Group.", is kept as text, and the macro is expanded in both sequences, as C.7.6.16 defines them. An attribute of a repeating group, such as Overlay Data (6002,3000), matches its group's definition, (60xx,3000). Different modules can give an attribute different Types, and an attribute the IOD does not define has none.
  - An instance names its SOP Class in SOP Class UID (0008,0016), not its IOD, so PS3.4 Table B.5-1, which gives the IOD of each Standard Storage SOP Class, is generated whole from its chtml page. The engine finds an instance's IOD, and with it the Types, from its SOP Class UID without trailing NUL and space padding, as it does before replacing a UID (D-003), since PS3.5 Section 9.1 pads a UID of odd length, such as each of the first release's SOP Class UIDs, with a NUL. PS3.3 Annex A publishes a modules table for the IOD of every Storage SOP Class in Table B.5-1, and none of them is left out, so each of those SOP Classes has Types. Ten generated IODs in 2026d, such as Hanging Protocol and Color Palette, have SOP Classes that other parts of PS3.4 define, so no SOP Class of Table B.5-1 finds them. Any other SOP Class, including one the table does not list, such as a retired or Private SOP Class, has none and is outside the supported scope (D-010).
  - Table E.3.4-1, the concept names of the content items that the Clean Structured Content Option cleans, is generated from its own chtml page. Each row gives a code, the value type of the content items it names, whether PS3.16 retires the code and uses it in a template, and the actions of the Basic Profile and of the seven options the table has columns for, as Table E.1-1 gives them for an attribute. A code can have a row for each of several value types with different actions, so a row is identified by its coding scheme designator, code value, and value type. The table gives a Coding Scheme Version in brackets after the designator where it gives one, as in "NCDR [2.0b]", and generation records the designator and the version separately. Only rows whose coding scheme the pin keeps are generated, DCM and SCT in 2026d (200 of 214 rows); the pin names the schemes it leaves out, LN, NCDR, NCIt, and UMLS, and generation fails if a row's scheme is in neither list or a scheme in them has no row. A content item whose concept name is coded in a scheme that the pin leaves out cannot be matched, so under the option its sequence takes its Basic Profile action (D-047).
  - PS3.3's Enumerated Values and Defined Terms of the attributes of VR CS are generated from the same page as its module tables, and the Defined Terms of Body Part Examined (0018,0015), for which PS3.3 refers to PS3.16 Annex L, from that annex's chtml page, for the residual search's exemptions (D-032).
  - `pymedphys dev deid-tables --check-current` downloads the pinned pages from NEMA's unpinned `current/` directory, generates each table from them with the same development-only parsers and the pin's corrections, and compares the digest of each table's rows with the one recorded in the committed table, without writing the tables. It names the edition the pages give, each table that would change, each page that could not be fetched, and each table that could not be generated, with the pages it read and the type of error, never quoting a page. It exits with status 1 when a table would change and 3 when a page could not be fetched or a table generated, and `--json` also writes the result as JSON. A monthly workflow runs it and opens or updates one issue when a table would change, a page fails, or the check stops without a result. The workflow is defined in `.github/workflows/deid-edition-check.yml`. Its check job can only read the repository, and only the job that opens the issue, which runs no repository code, can write issues. The check compares generated tables only, so it does not see a change to the Annex E paragraphs that the requirements register transcribes by hand: when the pin moves, the register is compared by hand with every Annex E page of the new edition, and an edition that changes only such a paragraph is not noticed until the pin next moves. Generated files are never edited by hand.
- **Open question.** Whether the generator should also extract each Annex E paragraph that contains "shall", outside Notes and tables, with any list it introduces and its anchor, as generated content is not yet decided. A test would then check that the register's PS3.15 entries are exactly those paragraphs, and the monthly check would open an issue when an edition adds, changes, or removes one.
- **Rationale.** The legacy keyword list was transcribed by hand and has drifted: it has 217 entries, drawn from Supplement 142, while Table E.1-1 in 2026d has 657 rows. The standard is revised about five times a year. NEMA holds the copyright in the standard, and each table records that attribution. Table E.3.4-1 also contains SNOMED CT, LOINC, NCDR, NCIt, and UMLS codes, which carry their own terms. Its SNOMED CT codes belong to the subset of SNOMED CT that DICOM publishes, which the reviewed head and neck regions also use, so their rows are generated. The rows of the other schemes are left out until their terms are checked against the policy for bundled data; leaving them out keeps nothing identifying, because a content item coded in one of those schemes makes its sequence take its Basic Profile action (D-047), and only the options that would retain such an item lose it. The standard library parsers add no dependency and only ever read verified publications, in a development-only tool. PS3.3 tables are kept as published because expanding each macro in each IOD would repeat shared modules and macros: the 171 IODs generated in 2026d expand to about 709,000 definitions, and the first supported release's 14 to about 79,400, but the two files that hold them and their 664 tables take 2.0 MB. Expanding each IOD only when its Types are first needed keeps loading the tables quick however many IODs they hold. For the same reason each IOD records its Functional Group Macros, and the module that includes them keeps the row that does, rather than each IOD having a copy of that module with its macros expanded: 29 IODs share the Multi-frame Functional Groups Module, each with its own macros. Table B.5-1 is small (173 rows in 2026d), so it is generated whole. Generating the Types of every composite IOD means that every stored object type's Types and reference sites are known, so no later release, the reference graph, or the resolution of compound actions is limited by which tables were generated, and instances that are sequestered can still have their references recorded. The costs are larger generated data, review load, and edition-check issues on most editions, since almost every edition changes some IOD. The monthly check compares generated content rather than source pages, so it raises an issue only when the tables would actually change; comparing source digests alone was rejected because it would raise one for every edition, about five a year, even when no generated table would change, which trains people to ignore it. The parser executes nothing from the page, and a page that cannot be parsed opens an issue itself, as does a check that stops without a result, so the check never fails silently. The report quotes no page, so the issue reproduces none of the standard's text. Errors in the published tables are corrected by name, rather than by a parser that accepts variants, so that an unexpected layout in a later edition still fails generation. The real-time IODs are left out until Table 9-1 is generated too, rather than generated without Current Frame Functional Groups Sequence, so that no consumer can read incomplete Types; for the same reason the loader rejects an IOD whose modules include Functional Group Macros that it does not list.
- **Tests.** Regeneration reproduces the committed tables and digests; a source file with a different digest is rejected; a table whose rows differ from their recorded digest, or whose rows have the wrong fields, types, or actions, is rejected; and every generated file carries the copyright attribution. For Table E.3.4-1, columns are matched by header text; a code with rows for two value types, and a designator with a version, are generated; a row with a malformed code, designator, value type, flag, or action, a repeated designator, code value, and value type, or a coding scheme that the pin neither keeps nor leaves out fails generation, as does a scheme the pin names without a row; counts and spot rows match the published table; every scheme the pin names is in PS3.16 Table 8-1; and the table's value types are the Enumerated Values of Value Type (0040,A040) other than NUMERIC, which the Content Item Macro uses where the table, following PS3.3 Section C.17.3, gives NUM. For PS3.3, only the IOD Modules tables of Annex A are selected, and each is generated or named in the pin; each IOD whose modules include Functional Group Macros has exactly one table of them, its own or the one the pin gives it, and every such table belongs to an IOD of Annex A; a row that includes them becomes an Include of them, while one in other words is rejected, as are rows nested below it, a macro without a table in its section, and a macro that does not define exactly one sequence or that includes Functional Group Macros; the pin leaves out exactly the IODs that cannot be generated because of an attribute the data dictionary lacks, which a test also lists with the attribute each lacks, and generation fails if one of them first lacks an attribute other than the one recorded with it; every other composite IOD is generated, including the enhanced multi-frame IODs; each correction applies to its stated number of rows, and fails generation otherwise; merged cells are expanded; modules are found by section and title, including a section that ends in a letter; macros are reached through nested includes; rows nested below an "Include" extend the included macro's only sequence, and nesting below an Include of any other table is rejected; spot Types match the published tables, including different Types in different modules and sequences, for IODs other than CT Image and the RT IODs, such as MR Image, Positron Emission Tomography Image, RT Image, and RT Beams Treatment Record; spot Types inside both Functional Groups Sequences, such as Pixel Spacing (0028,0030), Type 1C, in Pixel Measures Sequence (0028,9110) of Enhanced CT Image and Segmentation, each macro's usage and condition, and each macro in every sequence that includes the macros; an IOD is expanded only when its Types are first needed; Types are found at every level of a table that includes itself, such as Relationship Type (0040,A010), Type 1, in Content Sequence items nested three deep in an Encapsulated PDF; and malformed nesting, missing tables, and any other cycle of includes, including a table that includes itself outside its own sequences, are rejected. For PS3.4, every Storage SOP Class is a current SOP Class in PS3.6 Table A-1; the IOD of every Storage SOP Class is generated, and every Storage SOP Class finds it; the generated IODs that no SOP Class of Table B.5-1 names are listed; and the SOP Classes of CT Image, RT Structure Set, RT Plan, and RT Dose find their IODs, with or without padding and when read from a file, as do the SOP Classes of other generated IODs, such as MR Image, RT Beams Treatment Record, Enhanced CT Image, and Segmentation, while SOP Classes that the table does not list find none, and so does one that it lists whose IOD has no generated Types, as when a later edition lists an IOD that the pin leaves out. The edition check downloads each pinned page from `current/`; reports no change for the pinned edition's pages, or for a page that changes without changing a table; reports exactly the table that a changed cell changes; reports a page that cannot be fetched or parsed, and the tables it prevents, without quoting the page, as a failure even when another table would change; and writes nothing but its report.

### D-002: pydicom 3.0.2 minimum

- **Decision.** Require pydicom 3.0.2 or later, with no compatibility layer for pydicom 2, as the maintainer decided on 1 October 2026.
- **Rationale.** The package declared `pydicom>=2.0.0`, but continuous integration tested only the locked 3.0.2. The engine uses `dcmwrite(..., enforce_file_format=True)`, added in pydicom 3.0. That call updates the Media Storage SOP Class and Instance UIDs but keeps other File Meta elements and any existing preamble, so the engine builds both itself. The engine also reads each element without pydicom decoding it, through `Dataset.get_item(..., keep_deferred=True)`, which pydicom 3.0 added too. pydicom 3.0.0 and 3.0.1 are affected by CVE-2026-32711, a path-traversal flaw in reading DICOMDIR files that 3.0.2 fixes. The engine writes no DICOMDIR and never passes one through (D-016), but other PyMedPhys code and users' own code read files with the same pydicom, so the minimum excludes the affected releases rather than leaving them installable.
- **Tests.** Continuous integration runs the de-identification tests with pydicom 3.0.2, the lowest release that this minimum allows, as well as with the locked version and the latest release (D-029).

### D-003: UID replacement

- **Decision.**
  - Resolve each UID attribute's effective action from the L1, L2, and validated L3 rules before transforming its value.
  - Semantic identifiers need explicit L2 rules. SOP Class UIDs, transfer syntaxes, and well-known coding-scheme and context-group UIDs are retained. Local coding-scheme identifiers and private codes are retained only when a reviewed L2 rule classifies them as non-identifying; this is stricter than Table E.1-1, which gives C only to the Code Sequences it lists (E.1.1, Notes 7 and 10 after Table E.1-1a). A UI attribute with no rule is rejected, never hashed merely because of its VR.
  - The L2 file `uid_roles.toml` gives every UI attribute of the pinned dictionary a role. An `instance` attribute identifies an instance, event, entity, device, or organisation; every UI attribute that Table E.1-1 replaces has this role, as do instance UIDs the table omits, such as Target Frame of Reference UID (0018,991E). A `definition` attribute identifies something a standard or vendor publishes: a SOP Class, transfer syntax, coding scheme, context group, mapping resource, or template.
  - A value that the pinned tables register (PS3.6 Annex A and PS3.16 Tables 8-1 and 8-2) is retained whatever the attribute's role, because it names a public definition rather than an instance. Examples are a well-known frame of reference (PS3.6 Table A-2) and the Universal Coordinated Time synchronization frame of reference. This interprets U for such values, and the conformance statement records it. Any other value is replaced; in a `definition` attribute, where it may be a local definition under an institution's root, the replacement is also reported.
  - Where replacement is required, remove trailing NUL and space padding, then compute a 32-byte token with HMAC-SHA256 under the run's key (D-004) and a UID-specific domain label. The replacement is the name-based SHA-1 (version 5) UUID of that token in a fixed namespace defined by PyMedPhys, written as a `2.25.` UID of at most 44 characters. The namespace is the version 5 UUID of `https://docs.pymedphys.com/deidentify/uid-namespace` in the URL namespace, and never changes.
  - Use the same derivation for every occurrence, including nested references, File Meta Information, and retained private elements. The same key always gives the same replacement, within a run and across runs. A new key gives unrelated replacements, for example to import another copy of a phantom alongside earlier copies.
  - Replacement UIDs never use an organisation root.
  - Device UID (0018,1002) is replaced under the Basic Profile (U) and retained under Retain Device Identity (K).
  - Under the Retain UIDs Option, the UIDs to which Table E.1-1 gives K under it are retained, and every other UID is replaced as above (D-040).
- **Rationale.**
  - **Standards basis.** PS3.5 B.2 derives `2.25.` UIDs from UUIDs as defined by ISO/IEC 9834-8 and ITU-T X.667. X.667 defines the name-based SHA-1 UUID as version 5 (clause 14.3). Its Table 3 reserves versions 0 and 6 to 15, and a note in clause 12.2 expects any new format to use different variant bits, so the version 8 layout that RFC 9562 adds is not an X.667 UUID. X.667 does not limit names to text (Annex B), so the 32-byte token is a valid name. Using the token as the name keeps the standard version 5 construction; replacing its SHA-1 step with HMAC-SHA256 would not. A `2.25.` UID needs no registered root, whereas an organisation root can identify the institution (E.3.9 Note 2).
  - **Re-linking.** The UUID's name is a keyed token, so without the key, knowing a source UID does not reveal its replacement. Unkeyed hashes, as in the legacy pseudonymisation, and version 5 UUIDs of the source UIDs themselves both reveal it. Published SHA-1 collision attacks need inputs the attacker chooses, and without the key nobody can choose the HMAC outputs that form the names. A compromised key reveals the replacements of known source UIDs (D-004).
  - **Uniqueness.** A version 5 UUID has 122 variable bits, so the probability of any collision among $10^9$ replacement UIDs is about $10^{-19}$.
  - **State.** Replacements are computed from the key alone, so no UID map is needed: parallel workers need no coordination, output does not depend on the number of workers, and incremental export needs only the key. Random version 4 UUIDs would instead need a map equivalent to a crosswalk, protected, shared between workers, and kept for incremental export.
- **Tests.** Known-answer vectors for the derivation; version and variant bits; length at most 44 characters; a role for every UI attribute, and the instance role for every UI attribute Table E.1-1 replaces; registered values retained under either role; consistent replacements across workers, nested references, File Meta Information, retained private elements, and incremental runs; unrelated replacements under different keys; Device UID under the Basic Profile and under Retain Device Identity; retained class, coding-scheme, and context-group identifiers; rejection of unclassified UIDs; and, after writing, both replacements and required retentions.

### D-004: Keys and subject profiles

- **Decision.**
  - Keys are 256 bits from a cryptographically secure generator. An ephemeral key and its run-only state are discarded after the run, so generated values are consistent only within that run. A project key and its subject profiles are held by a custodian, so generated values stay consistent across runs, as incremental RT collections need.
  - Use a separate key for each project and recipient, with subject profiles isolated per key. Releases under one key are linkable by design. Separate keys separate only the generated values: pixel data, geometry, and retained attributes can still link releases, and each release's assessment covers them (D-017).
  - Derivations for UIDs, patient identifiers, date offsets, and the keyed digest of the reviewed-names list (D-024) are domain-separated. Each is an HMAC-SHA256 under the key of the version label `pymedphys-deid/1`, the domain, and each input, every one preceded by its length, so no two domains or splits of the input share a message. A new derivation takes a new version label.
  - Each key has a non-secret identifier derived from it. Reports may record the identifier, never the key.
  - Project keys and subject profiles are stored only in custodian-controlled locations outside output and QC directories, and never in logs, reports, or the PyMedPhys user configuration. A key file is JSON (`pymedphys-deid-key/1`) holding the key and its identifier. It is created exclusively, so an existing file or symbolic link is never overwritten or followed, and with owner-only permissions where the platform enforces file modes; elsewhere writing it warns.
  - The subject profile is the single source of truth for a subject's date offset and synthetic values. They are derived at first export, persisted, and read back afterwards; a change of derivation never silently changes an exported subject.
  - A profile store is JSON (`pymedphys-deid-profiles/1`) that records its key's identifier and refuses any other key. It holds each subject under the key's `subject` derivation of the identity, never under an identifier, with the values and the derivation version that produced them. It is replaced atomically, created with owner-only permissions where the platform enforces file modes, and kept out of the configuration directory and any protected directory, like a key file. Under an ephemeral key, profiles are kept for the run only.
  - Incremental export needs both the key and the subject profile. Recipients receive neither.
  - Rotating a key starts a new domain of generated values. If a key or profile is lost, earlier exports cannot be extended consistently. A compromised key is treated as disclosure of the crosswalk, and affected releases are reassessed.
- **Rationale.** Medical record numbers and similar identifiers have little entropy, so anyone holding the key can enumerate candidates and re-identify every subject: a project key is equivalent to a crosswalk. Profiles shared between keys would give every recipient the same shifted dates and synthetic values.
- **Tests.** Key-file creation and permissions; refusal to write keys or profiles into output, QC, or configuration locations; stable key identifiers; offsets read from an existing profile after a derivation change; profiles isolated per key; consistent generated values across incremental releases; and a demonstration that a key enumerates low-entropy identifiers.

### D-005: Keyed patient pseudonyms

- **Decision.** Every preset replaces Patient ID and Patient's Name with values derived from the subject's identity (identifier and issuer, or a curator-supplied identity) under the key (D-004), rather than with empty values. One format serves every preset. It is conspicuously synthetic, deterministic under the key, valid for the VR (64 characters for LO and for each PN component group), identical for every instance of a subject, and does not embed the source identifier. The conformance statement describes it, as E.1.3 requires for dummy values.
- **Format.** The subject's code is the base32 form (RFC 4648) of the first 80 bits of the key's `patient` derivation of the identity. Patient ID becomes `DEID-` and the code (21 characters), and Patient's Name becomes `DEIDENTIFIED^` and the code. An identity is either a Patient ID with its issuer, ignoring LO padding, or a curated subject identifier; the two kinds are framed differently, so the same text in each is two subjects. An empty Patient ID names no subject and needs a curated identity. Among a million subjects the chance that any two share a code is below $10^{-12}$.
- **Rationale.** Patient's Name is Z and Patient ID is Z/D in the Basic Profile, both of which permit a dummy value. Empty values would merge every patient in a multi-patient collection, whereas keyed pseudonyms keep subjects separate and, under a project key, consistent across runs. Conspicuous values stop a research copy from being mistaken for a clinical record.
- **Tests.** Determinism under one key and difference across keys; VR validity; distinct pseudonyms for distinct subjects, including the same identifier from different issuers; and consistency across a subject's instances.

### D-006: Date shifting

- **Decision.** Where Retain Longitudinal Temporal Information with Modified Dates is selected, shift each subject's subject-event and radiation-source dates (D-007) backwards by a whole number of weeks between 52 and 520, never zero. With a project key, the offset is read from the subject profile (D-004); with an ephemeral key, it is derived for the run. Times are unchanged. Dates found in free text are removed, not shifted. Without the option, the Basic Profile's actions apply.
- **Derivation.** A new subject's offset is 52 weeks plus the first 64 bits of the key's `date-offset` derivation of the identity, modulo 469, so each of the 469 offsets is equally likely to within one part in $10^{16}$. A DA value moves by the offset; a DT value moves its date and keeps its time, and any UTC offset it carries becomes the nominal offset once the value is in the instance's local time (D-007). A DT value without a full date, such as a year alone, cannot move by whole weeks, so it is not shifted, and its instance is sequestered (D-039). A DT value at a leap second, with a second of 60, is neither shifted nor converted to local time, because PS3.5 allows 60 only at a real leap second, which the moved value would not be; its instance is sequestered.
- **Rationale.** Whole weeks preserve intervals, times of day, and weekdays, which fractionation analyses need. Shifting backwards avoids future-dated plans, which planning systems may reject. Excluding zero ensures every date moves (MIDI §1.17.9). Shifting a free-text string wrongly recognised as a date can disclose the offset (MIDI §1.17.9).
- **Tests.** Offset range and exclusion of zero; preservation of intervals, times, and weekdays; rejection of leap seconds; consistency across the studies and runs of one subject; and removal of dates in free text.

### D-007: Temporal attributes by role

- **Decision.** L2 gives every date, time, and datetime attribute a role. Where Modified Dates is selected, each role is handled as follows:
  - **Subject event**, such as Study Date, Treatment Date, and Beam Hold Transition DateTime (300C,0127): shifted by the subject's offset (D-006).
  - **Radiation source**, Source Strength Reference Date (300A,022C) and Time (300A,022E): shifted by the same offset, so that decay intervals to treatment remain correct.
  - **Device**, such as the calibration, installation, and manufacture attributes listed below; **vocabulary version**, published or local, such as Context Group Version (0008,0106) and Context Group Local Version (0008,0107); and **other**: replaced with a fixed, conspicuous dummy value that differs from the original.
  - **Time zone**, Timezone Offset From UTC (0008,0201): replaced with the nominal offset `+0000`, after every DT value in the instance that carries its own offset is converted to the instance's local time and given the same nominal offset. The instance's dates and times are then all local times under one offset, so their intervals are kept, including across midnight, and no offset reveals the season of the original date through daylight saving, such as +1100 rather than +1000, or the location. The local time is the instance's Timezone Offset From UTC where present, which PS3.3 C.12.1.1.8 makes the offset of every value without its own, and otherwise the offset of its earliest DT value that has one, compared to the microsecond. The replacement is a valid offset for the instance's times, as C requires, and the conformance statement records that it is nominal. Intervals between instances at different offsets, such as either side of a daylight-saving change, move by the difference between their offsets. An instance with a value whose precision cannot express its conversion exactly, such as a date alone, is sequestered.

  Without Modified Dates, Table E.1-1 applies unchanged. The conformance statement describes these modifications, as E.3.6 requires.

  The L2 file `temporal_roles.toml` gives each DA, DT, and TM attribute of the pinned dictionary a role, including attributes that Table E.1-1 omits, such as DICONDE and DICOS attributes. It also gives a role to every attribute of another VR that Table E.1-1 cleans under Modified Dates, so a new edition that cleans another fails to load until that attribute has a reviewed rule. In 2026d it has 187 rules: 152 subject event, 2 radiation source, 12 device, 4 vocabulary version, 1 time zone, and 16 other. An attribute that PS3.3 requires to follow a subject event, such as Item Inventory DateTime (0008,0404), which must be at or after Content Date (0008,0023), is itself a subject event, so shifting keeps the order. The UI and temporal roles files share one format and loader.

  Besides Timezone Offset From UTC, Table E.1-1 cleans two attributes of other VRs under Modified Dates. Frame Origin Timestamp (0034,0007), each frame's capture time as an IEEE 1588 timestamp in TAI (PS3.3 C.7.6.27.1.1), is a subject event; its seconds move by the subject's offset, which keeps the timing between frames exactly. Certified Timestamp (0400,0310) occurs only inside the Digital Signatures Sequence (FFFA,FFFA), which Table E.1-1 removes under every option, so its role, other, never applies.

  In 2026d, eleven attributes are K under Retain Device Identity and C under Modified Dates. Ten are device attributes: Date of Last Calibration (0018,1200), Time of Last Calibration (0018,1201), DateTime of Last Calibration (0018,1202), Calibration DateTime (0018,1203), Date of Manufacture (0018,1204), Date of Installation (0018,1205), Calibration Time (0014,407C), Calibration Date (0014,407E), Date of Last Detector Calibration (0018,700C), and Time of Last Detector Calibration (0018,700E). The eleventh is Beam Hold Transition DateTime. K keeps a non-Sequence value unchanged (Table E.1-1a) and E.3.8 requires retention, whereas E.3.6 requires modification, and PS3.15 defines no precedence between options. No output can satisfy both options while one of these attributes has a value, so a policy that selects both makes no conformance claim. `tps-import` does this deliberately and handles these attributes by role, as above; any other such policy is rejected (D-011). A test fails if a regenerated Table E.1-1 adds a conflict.
- **Rationale.** E.3.6 requires every date and time in a Table E.1-1 attribute to be modified, in a manner that reduces the possibility of matching and preserves temporal relationships only to the extent the application needs. Neither removal nor retention is modification. Shifting a device or vocabulary date by the subject's offset would disclose that offset whenever the original is known or guessable, such as a published version date or a known calibration date; MIDI §1.17.9 describes the same mechanism for free text. It would also give one device different dates in different subjects' output, revealing the differences between their offsets. No preset needs these dates' relationships to the images, so a fixed dummy value satisfies E.3.6 and C (a value of similar meaning that contains no identifying information) and discloses nothing.
- **Consequences.** Dose and decay calculations stay consistent; device calibration history and vocabulary versions are lost. The first supported release is unaffected, since neither of its presets selects Modified Dates. Under `tps-import`, Retain Device Identity keeps Source Serial Number (3008,0105), so subjects treated with the same source can be linked and the differences between their offsets disclosed; this residual risk is documented for non-clinical use. `public-release` does not retain source serial numbers.
- **Tests.** Every temporal attribute in the pinned dictionary has a role; every date and datetime value in a Table E.1-1 attribute is modified, including one equal to the dummy value, and every time of a subject event or radiation source is kept (D-006); known device and vocabulary dates alongside shifted subject dates do not expose the offset; source-to-treatment intervals are preserved; a custom policy selecting both conflicting options is rejected; and a regenerated Table E.1-1 with a new option conflict fails the tests.

### D-008: Synthetic birth dates for TPS import

- **Decision.**
  - Resolve a stable subject identity, as D-005 does.
  - Before first export, designate one reference study with a validated date and an age in completed years. The synthetic birth date is that study's shifted date minus the age in calendar years, with 29 February mapped to 28 February.
  - Persist the reference, offset, derivation version, and chosen date in the subject profile (D-004; run-only under an ephemeral key), and reuse the date for every instance and later run. A study that arrives later, whatever its date, never changes the reference.
- **Failure handling.** Unresolved subject identity stops the affected objects for curator resolution. For an identified subject, missing or invalid reference dates or ages, or inconsistent source ages, must be resolved before synthesis. Otherwise an empty birth date is written consistently for that subject, and that choice is persisted too. A conflict with an existing profile stops the subject for review. Changing an exported choice requires an explicit migration of the related collection.
- **Rationale.** Patient's Birth Date is Z in the Basic Profile, and no option retains or cleans it, so a synthetic value is a permitted dummy value. Planning systems may reject records with empty or invalid values. Recomputing the date from each study's date and rounded age could give one subject several birth dates. The synthetic date expresses approximate age at the reference study, not the true birthday; the retained Patient's Age remains the source for age analyses.
- **Tests.** Several studies at one age; birthdays between studies; leap years; independence from processing order; earlier studies arriving later; missing or inconsistent ages; missing subject state; and identical birth dates across a subject's instances and incremental runs.

### D-009: Descriptor cleaning

- **Decision.**
  - Start with a vocabulary-based cleaner, combined with context-aware checks and checks for echoes of known patient and other person identifiers, including reordered names and reformatted dates (MIDI §1.17.2).
  - Treat unresolved ambiguous text conservatively, subject to D-011. Remove optional attributes, use permitted empty or dummy values for required ones, or hold the output for confidential review.
  - Other validated methods, including removing optional descriptors, may satisfy the option. Describe the method and its limits in the conformance statement, as E.3.5 requires.
  - Claim Clean Descriptors (DCM 113105) only for output that retains no descriptor string that has not passed pooled human review. A descriptor that is not cleaned takes its action under the policy without the option instead, and where that action removes or replaces it, the output still meets the option, since E.3.5 specifies what the option removes, not what it retains, and E.1.1 makes Table E.1-1 the minimum actions; where that action keeps it, the output does not claim the option. A ROI Name that automatic cleaning, below, writes in the spelling of the published TG-263 vocabulary is a cleaned value (C), not a retained string, so it needs no review before the claim.
  - The first supported release includes the Clean Descriptors Option, in the `basic-clean-descriptors` preset. The maintainer decided on 1 October 2026 that it cleans ROI Name (3006,0026), using a vocabulary made with the TG-263 converter below, in two tiers:
    - **Automatic.** A ROI Name that matches a vocabulary name once case, padding, and the separators `_`, `-`, and space are disregarded is written in the vocabulary's own spelling: `lung l`, `LUNG-L`, and `Lung L` all become `Lung_L`. TG-263 writes `-` for a subtraction, as in `Lungs-PTV`, so a vocabulary name that contains `-` matches only a name with `-` in the same place: `lungs - ptv` becomes `Lungs-PTV`, but `Lungs PTV` and `Lungs_PTV` go to review. A name that starts with `_` or `-` also goes to review, since TG-263 marks a structure not used for dose evaluation, such as an optimisation contour, with a leading `_`, so `_Heart` is not `Heart`; separators elsewhere in a name are still disregarded. Only names of printable ASCII are matched, since TG-263 names are ASCII and Unicode case folding and compatibility forms would match look-alike characters. Each such rename is recorded for audit in the confidential QC material (D-016), and needs no review before the option is claimed. This applies only to the list that AAPM publishes, whose entries are generic names of anatomy and targets: a vocabulary is accepted for automatic renaming only where the digest of its entries is that of a published edition of the TG-263 spreadsheet, as PyMedPhys records it, since the converter writes AAPM's attribution for any workbook with the TG-263 columns, including one extended with local names. A vocabulary converted from an institutional list can hold an entry that names a site or a person, such as `ClinicX_Lung`, and its source record and digest show which list was used, not that its entries are safe to write, so every match against such a vocabulary goes to review, in the second tier, instead. As the maintainer decided on 6 October 2026, a run can be given such a list, converted as below. It renames nothing and changes no value written: a ROI Name held for review that matches one of its names, once case, padding, and separators are disregarded as above, but with any leading `_` or `-` the same in both, keeps the reason it was held for, and the confidential QC material shows its reviewer the list's names it matched (D-016). Since the list changes no value written, the method digest does not cover it (D-024). A name also goes to review where its normalised form matches more than one vocabulary name; where the checks for echoes above find that it echoes a known patient or other person identifier; or where a different ROI Name of the same structure set would be written as the same vocabulary name, as `Lung_L` and `LUNG-L` would, since duplicate ROI Names can make a planning system reject the import.
    - **Reviewed and remembered.** Every other distinct ROI Name is listed in the confidential QC material for review. A reviewer keeps it, maps it to another name, including by a bulk rename across a collection, or has it emptied. The decisions are kept in a reviewed-names list for the site or project, held by the custodian with the key and subject profiles (D-016), and reused on later runs; this is the pooled human review that the claim above requires. A reviewer records decisions by writing them into a CSV file with the columns `roi_name`, `decision` (`keep`, `map`, or `empty`), and `to`, which a command records in the list, starting it on first use, as the maintainer decided on 6 October 2026. Recording is all or nothing: a name given different decisions in the file, or a decision that differs from the list's without an explicit choice to replace it, is refused and nothing is recorded, and neither its summary nor its messages quote a name or path. An instance with a ROI Name that the list does not cover and automatic cleaning does not rename is held for review (D-027), and counted by reason, without its name or any label, in the release report's `held_for_review` section, rather than emptied silently, unless the user chooses explicitly to empty such names so that the run proceeds. As the maintainer decided on 6 October 2026, a held instance's file is deleted with the staging area when the run ends, and a later run writes it once a reviewer's decision for the name is in the list; since the current command gives each run an ephemeral key (D-004), that run's release replaces the earlier one rather than adding to it. A decision applies to every ROI Name with exactly its spelling, once LO padding is removed, and only where automatic cleaning does not rename the name; a name sent to review only because another name of the structure set would be written the same takes the list's decision, so mapping one of two such names lets the other be renamed. What a kept or mapped name would write is checked, as an automatic rename is, for echoes of the instance's patient and other person identifiers and for duplicates within the structure set, ignoring case, since some planning systems compare structure names without case, and the name is held for review if either check fails, since a reviewer may have decided on it for another patient; the user's choice to empty held names covers such a name too, so that the run proceeds. The list changes what the engine writes, so its keyed digest is an input to the method digest (D-024).
  - `tps-import` cleans ROI names in the same way and keeps no name that has not been reviewed, apart from those that automatic cleaning writes in the vocabulary's spelling, since its output carries Patient Identity Removed YES only when every deviation from its profile and options removes or modifies more than they require (D-012).
  - Recognising well-formed TG-263 target names by their grammar, such as `PTV_High` or `CTVp_5040`, which TG-263 defines by pattern rather than by list, is future development.
  - Under Clean Descriptors, a sequence that Table E.1-1 gives C takes its Basic Profile action rather than being cleaned item by item, since rules for cleaning its contents are not designed, as the maintainer decided on 1 October 2026. In 2026d there are nine: Admitting Diagnoses Code Sequence (0008,1084), Principal Diagnosis Code Sequence (0008,1301), Primary Diagnosis Code Sequence (0008,1302), Secondary Diagnoses Code Sequence (0008,1303), Histological Diagnoses Code Sequence (0008,1304), Reason for Visit Code Sequence (0032,1067), Reason for Requested Procedure Code Sequence (0040,100A), and Request Attributes Sequence (0040,0275), which the Basic Profile removes (X), and Prescription Notes Sequence (3010,0081), which it empties (Z) and which no IOD of the first supported release uses.
  - Under Clean Descriptors, every other attribute to which the policy gives C, such as Study Description, Series Description, and Beam Name, takes the action that the policy gives it without that option, unless descriptor cleaning writes it as the next bullet describes, and an instance in which that action removes or replaces each such attribute still claims the option, since E.3.5 specifies what the option removes and Table E.1-1 gives the minimum actions, as the maintainer decided on 6 October 2026, over leaving the option unclaimed for such output or cleaning short labels too. An instance in which that action keeps such an attribute does not claim the option. The conformance statement gives that action for each such attribute, at each place.
  - Reviewers' decisions on the values of those other descriptors are kept in a reviewed descriptors list for the site or project, beside the reviewed-names list and held by the custodian in the same way. A decision keeps, maps, or empties one value of one attribute, by its tag and exact spelling once padding is removed, wherever that attribute holds it; each value of an attribute whose VR allows several is decided on alone. The attributes are those that Table E.1-1, or a reviewed rule for text that it omits (D-022), gives C under Clean Descriptors, of VR SH, LO, ST, LT, UC, or UT, other than ROI Name; in 2026d there are 190. Under Retain Safe Private, the list also decides on the private text that a safe private rule retains, each attribute named by its private creator, group, element within the block, and VR, written `(gggg,"CREATOR",ee) VR`, as `(0019,"GEMS_ACQU_01",9E) LO`, since its tag depends on the block that its creator reserves (D-035); the list's format is `pymedphys-deid-reviewed-descriptors/2`, which a list of format 1, without private text, is read as. A value to map to is printable ASCII that the attribute's VR can hold. What a kept or mapped value would write is checked for echoes of the instance's patient and other person identifiers, as for ROI names, and also for a whole identifier or a word of one, of at least four letters and digits, among the value's letters and digits, since free text can run an identifier into other text or reformat it, as `MRN 99-1234` does `991234`; a value that echoes one, or that the list does not cover and the automatic tier below does not keep, is not cleaned. A reviewer records decisions by writing them into a CSV file with the columns `attribute` (a keyword, a tag, or a private attribute's key, as the QC pack gives it), `value`, `decision`, and `to`, which a command records in the list as the ROI-name command does, run as `python -m pymedphys._dicom.deidentify.reviewed_descriptors_command`. A run is given the list as the reviewed-names list is, and applies it to each such descriptor whose decoded value the edits collected in the VR that the data dictionary gives it, each value of a VR that allows several split at its backslashes. It writes an element with what cleaning gives its values only where every value is cleaned, at least one is written with text, and no kept value is written beside one that a reviewer mapped or emptied, since the residual search leaves out the element of a kept value as a retained value (D-027), which would leave out that value's source too. An instance withheld before its descriptors are cleaned is still edited so that its values are collected for its subject's search (D-027), and each descriptor that cleaning would write with a kept value is left out of that collection in the same way, so that the same value written in the rest of its series or study is not found from it; a value that a reviewer maps or empties, that is not cleaned, or that echoes an identifier stays in the search, and so does a kept value in a sequence that also holds an attribute given C that cleaning would not write, since that attribute's action without the option may remove the sequence with both (D-020), which only an instance that is written settles. A descriptor that another selected option's cleaner cleans, such as Allergies under Retain Patient Characteristics, is left to that cleaner, and a value that the cleaner leaves pending is not cleaned, even where the list has a decision on it (D-038). Every other such element, including one whose sequence another attribute's action removes, takes the action that the policy gives it without the option, as the previous bullet describes, so a value that is not cleaned is removed or replaced rather than released, and the instance is not held for it. The maintainer decided on 13 October 2026 that such a value is removed or replaced in this way, over holding the instance until a reviewer decides on the value, which would hold nearly every instance of a first run, since Study Description and other free text are on almost every file and the automatic tier covers only the technique descriptors (D-036); under both, a value that is not cleaned is never released. The confidential QC pack lists each element's values, what cleaning decided for each, and whether it was written, so that a reviewer can record decisions for later runs (D-016), and the release report counts the values by outcome, the distinct values not cleaned by reason, and the elements that took the action without the option, naming none. The list changes what the engine writes, so its keyed digest, in a derivation domain of its own, `reviewed-descriptors`, is an input to the method digest (D-024).
  - For technique descriptors, an automatic tier also keeps, without review, a value made only of reviewed vocabulary words, where the list has no decision on it (D-036). This is the one departure from the rule above that a retained descriptor string has passed pooled human review.
  - PyMedPhys downloads the published TG-263 spreadsheet itself and uses it as the default vocabulary, as the maintainer decided on 2 October 2026; it bundles no copy. The edition it uses is pinned in PyMedPhys by its URL on AAPM's Radiation Oncology Nomenclature Resource Page, the SHA-256 of the file, and the digest of its entries; the pin is the 2017-08-15 edition. The download is cached in the PyMedPhys data directory and used only if it has the pinned SHA-256, and a file that does not is refused. A user without internet access can convert a copy obtained elsewhere, which is checked against the pin in the same way. A monthly check, like the check for a new DICOM edition, opens an issue when AAPM's page links to a download that maintainers have not reviewed, no longer links to the pinned spreadsheet, or the pinned file has changed, so that moving to a new edition is a reviewed change to the pin, never automatic.
  - A vocabulary is a UTF-8 JSON file that holds its entries together with their source, version, source-file SHA-256, and required attribution. A converter command produces it from the TG-263 spreadsheet, or from an institutional list exported as CSV with a `Name` column, as the maintainer decided on 1 October 2026, and converted files are never edited by hand. The converter commands are kept out of the `pymedphys` command line until the tool is released, as the maintainer decided on 2 October 2026, as is every other command of the tool, and until then run as `python -m pymedphys._nomenclature`, the command that records reviewers' decisions as `python -m pymedphys._dicom.deidentify.reviewed_names_command`, and the command that records a QC reviewer's attestation as `python -m pymedphys._dicom.deidentify.attestation_command`; a test fails if a `pymedphys` command outside the maintainers' own `pymedphys dev` commands belongs to the tool. Releasing the tool makes them public commands with user documentation, in the pull request of the first supported release (M6), as the maintainer decided on 6 October 2026. Without a vocabulary, no ROI Name is renamed automatically: each needs a reviewer's decision from the reviewed-names list, or is held for review.
  - `pymedphys._nomenclature.tg263` converts the TG-263 spreadsheet. It reads the downloaded or a local copy, maps its columns by header text, and writes JSON with the spreadsheet's file name, worksheet version, SHA-256, AAPM's attribution, and a digest of its entries; its loader rejects a file whose entries no longer match that digest. Entries are kept as published, except that surrounding whitespace is removed, the derived "N Characters" column is dropped, and unlabelled columns are ignored. The module sits outside the de-identification package so that other tools, such as a check of structure names against TG-263, can use it.
- **Rationale.** E.3.5 specifies what to remove, not an algorithm (Note 4). A vocabulary can contain words that are also names, such as "Hand" (MIDI §1.17.2), so a word's presence in a vocabulary is not evidence that its use within free text is safe. JSON keeps the provenance, attribution, and value types in the same file as the entries, which CSV cannot, and matches the repository's existing data files. The TG-263 journal article is licensed CC BY-NC-ND 4.0, which forbids the commercial use and adaptation that Apache-2.0 permits, and no licence compatible with Apache-2.0 has been found for its nomenclature spreadsheet, so, under the packaging rule for bundled data in `AGENTS.md`, PyMedPhys downloads the published file from AAPM when it is needed rather than bundling one. Pinning the file's SHA-256 and the digest of its entries makes the default vocabulary reproducible and detects a changed or substituted file; a hash taken from a third-party mirror was rejected, since it would show only what the mirror holds. AAPM publishes the spreadsheet in the Excel 97-2003 format, which `xlrd` reads. Under the Basic Profile alone, ROI Name is Z, ROI Observation Label and Beam Description are X, and RT Plan Label is D, so a first release with the Basic Profile alone would produce RT Structure Sets without usable structure names, which are of limited use for radiotherapy research. Including the option is a real increase in scope, with review risk, because Clean Descriptors applies to every descriptor, such as Study Description and Series Description, not only to ROI names. Writing the vocabulary's spelling of a ROI name that matches it once case, whitespace, and separators are disregarded is cleaning as Table E.1-1a defines C, replacing the value "with values of similar meaning known not to contain identifying information", since the whole value written is a vocabulary entry. The caution about "Hand" (MIDI §1.17.2; PS3.15 E.3.5 Note 2) concerns words found inside free text, such as a Study Description, and matching a whole ROI Name never yields such a word: `Dr Hand` never normalises to `Hand`. Echoes of known patient and other person identifiers are still checked, and the safety of the vocabulary's own entries rests on its source, which is why only a published edition's entries are renamed automatically. Matching only exact spellings was rejected because it would empty structure names that differ from TG-263 only in case or separators. A name whose normalised form matches several vocabulary names cannot be renamed without a choice, so a reviewer makes it. Remembering reviewed decisions for a site or project means that each distinct name is reviewed once, and holding an instance whose name nobody has reviewed, rather than emptying the name silently, keeps names that a reviewer would keep; a user who cannot wait for review can choose to empty them. TG-263 target names are defined by pattern, so no list holds them all. Cleaning a sequence means changing the values within it where the meaning of the sequence in the IOD is specified, or applying the Profile's rules to each of its items otherwise (Table E.1-1a); neither is designed for the codes of diagnoses and reasons or for the contents of Request Attributes Sequence, so their Basic Profile action applies.
- **Tests.** A ROI name that matches a vocabulary name once case, whitespace, and the separators `_`, `-`, and space are disregarded is written in the vocabulary's spelling, and the rename is recorded in the confidential QC material; a name whose normalised form matches more than one vocabulary name, a name that echoes a known patient or other person identifier, and different ROI names of one structure set that would be written as the same vocabulary name, such as `Lung_L` and `LUNG-L`, go to review, as does every match against a vocabulary converted from an institutional list, which, given to a run, writes no name and shows the reviewer of a held name the list's names it matched; reviewed decisions to keep, map, or empty a name, including a bulk rename across a collection, are reused on later runs, apply only to the exact spelling, and are held where what they would write echoes an identifier or duplicates another name of the structure set; decisions recorded from a reviewer's CSV file start the list on first use and are added to it later, a decision that differs from the list's is refused unless replaced explicitly, and a malformed file, a name given different decisions, or a list in a protected location records nothing, with messages that quote no name or path; the list's keyed digest is the same for the same decisions under the same key, whatever order they were recorded in, and changes with any decision, name, or key; an instance with a ROI name that the reviewed-names list does not cover and automatic cleaning does not rename is held in the staging area, or written with that name emptied where the user has chosen so explicitly; `tps-import` keeps no name that has not been reviewed, apart from those that automatic cleaning writes in the vocabulary's spelling; each sequence that Table E.1-1 gives C under Clean Descriptors takes its Basic Profile action; names that overlap anatomical terms; clinician names; mixed descriptive and identifying text; non-English text; missing source identifiers; converting the AAPM spreadsheet and an institutional list; downloading the pinned edition, and refusing a file that does not match the pin; refusing automatic renaming with any vocabulary whose entries are not those of a published edition; matching a hyphen in a vocabulary name only with a hyphen; sending a name with a leading `_` or `-` to review; rejecting a vocabulary without its source record; and processing without one. Output that retains a descriptor string that has not passed pooled human review never claims the option, and output whose other descriptors take their Basic Profile actions, which remove or replace them, claims it, as an RT Plan whose Type 1 RT Plan Label is given a dummy value does; a vocabulary word found within free text never on its own lets a descriptor be retained; and output whose ROI names are all automatic renames claims the option without review. Reviewed decisions on other descriptor values apply to one attribute and one exact value, each value of a multi-valued attribute alone, and are reused on later runs; the list holds only the descriptors that Clean Descriptors cleans and mappings that their VRs can hold, and is refused in a protected location, malformed, or with a repeated value, with messages that quote no value or path; a value that the list does not cover, or whose kept or mapped value echoes an identifier by word, as a whole, or within its letters and digits, is held, or emptied where the library's caller asks, which no run does; a run writes a descriptor whose values are all kept, mapped, or emptied by the list or kept by the technique vocabulary, leaves its kept values out of the residual search, and lists them as retained strings, while a descriptor with a value not cleaned, with only empty values, with a kept value beside a mapped or emptied one, or read in another VR takes the action without the option and still claims the option where that action removes or replaces it; each value of a multi-valued descriptor is cleaned alone; the QC pack lists each descriptor's values, outcomes, and whether it was written, and the release report counts them without values; and the list's keyed digest, in a derivation domain of its own, is the same for the same decisions under the same key and changes with any decision, value, attribute, or key. An instance withheld before cleaning leaves out of its collected values a Series Description that the vocabulary keeps, but not a Study Description that echoes the patient, a Protocol Name that a reviewer empties, or an Admitting Diagnoses Description that a reviewer maps, and under the Basic Profile alone leaves out none; nor does it leave out a value that is not cleaned, a vocabulary value that echoes the patient's name, or a kept value beside a mapped one in one element, while it leaves out, whole, an LT value with a backslash that a reviewer keeps, listing it among the QC pack's drops as retained; a kept Series Description in an RT Structure Set's Source Series Information Sequence stays in its collected values where another item's Series Description is not cleaned, as it is removed with the sequence from an instance that is written, and is left out where every item's is kept; and with Retain Patient Characteristics, a reviewer's decision to keep or map a value of Allergies still leaves it pending and holds its instance, which still collects the value for its subject's search; and its kept Series Description does not hold another slice of its series.

### D-010: Unsupported object types

- **Decision.** Sequester Structured Reports (including dose reports), Key Object Selection documents, Presentation States, instances of Private SOP Classes, and objects outside the supported scope. List them in the report and exclude them from the conformance claim. An instance is classified from its SOP Class UID, through PS3.4 Table B.5-1, and from its file's transfer syntax. Support is decided by IOD, so every Storage SOP Class that Table B.5-1 maps to a supported IOD is supported. The first supported release de-identifies instances of 14 IODs in the Implicit VR Little Endian and Explicit VR Little Endian transfer syntaxes and in the twelve transfer syntaxes that encapsulate Pixel Data that pydicom 3.0.2's decoding plugins decode (Architecture item 2): CT Image, Enhanced CT Image, Legacy Converted Enhanced CT Image, MR Image, Enhanced MR Image, Enhanced MR Color Image, Legacy Converted Enhanced MR Image, MR Spectroscopy, Positron Emission Tomography Image, Enhanced PET Image, Legacy Converted Enhanced PET Image, RT Structure Set, RT Plan, and RT Dose. The For Processing SOP Classes, such as CT Image Storage - For Processing (1.2.840.10008.5.1.4.1.1.2.3) and Enhanced CT Image Storage - For Processing (1.2.840.10008.5.1.4.1.1.2.4), are among them, since Table B.5-1 maps each to its IOD. The maintainer decided on 7 October 2026 to bring MR and PET, including their enhanced variants, and Enhanced CT into the first supported release, which had covered CT Image, RT Structure Set, RT Plan, and RT Dose. That adds ten IODs: every IOD of a CT, MR, or PET image Storage SOP Class in Table B.5-1, and MR Spectroscopy. The maintainer also decided on 7 October 2026 that the first supported release takes compressed input, which it had sequestered. An instance in one of the twelve compressed transfer syntaxes, lossy ones included, keeps its transfer syntax and encapsulated pixel data, without decoding and re-encoding, once the comment and application segments of its codestreams are cut and every frame is shown to decode to the same pixels as its source's (Architecture item 7); one whose pinned decoding plugin is not installed, or whose frames or codestreams fail those checks, is sequestered with its reason. A JPEG 2000 or HTJ2K frame under a lossless-only syntax that was truncated so far that it drops only whole code-blocks passes (Architecture item 2) and is written as its source labels it; since every byte of its codestream is still accounted for and its metadata segments cut, that limit concerns how its pixel data are labelled, not what they could disclose. Protocol objects, such as CT Performed Procedure Protocol, and derived objects that are not specific to a modality, such as segmentations, parametric maps, and spatial registrations, stay out of scope. Within the supported IODs, the rules still sequester every Enhanced PET Image instance that conforms to its IOD under both presets (D-020), and any instance whose Referenced Performed Procedure Step Sequence has no UID to replace, or whose ICC Profile, where Enhanced CT Image, Enhanced MR Image, or Enhanced MR Color Image requires it, is not an RGB profile (D-021). The report says why any other instance was sequestered: it had no SOP Class UID or one that is not text, its SOP Class is not a Standard Storage SOP Class of Table B.5-1, its IOD is not supported (the report names the IOD), or its transfer syntax is missing or not supported. An instance whose Specific Character Set (0008,0005) cannot be read, such as one stored with a VR other than CS, is also sequestered, with that reason. As the maintainer decided on 12 October 2026, a data set whose Specific Character Set is not supported or is invalid, such as a misspelt Defined Term, is read in the Default Character Repertoire, as one without a value is: text in ISO 646, with no byte from 0x80 and no escape, is read as the same characters by the pydicom codec of every Defined Term of PS3.3 Section C.12.1.1.2 that the engine supports, so it is read without guessing a character set, and text outside ISO 646 is handled as below. That is a property of those codecs, not of every character set: JIS X 0201 Roman, the G0 set of `ISO_IR 13`, places a yen sign and an overline at 0x5C and 0x7E (PS3.5 Section 6.1.2.3), which pydicom's codec, like the engine under a supported `ISO_IR 13`, reads as a backslash and a tilde. Specific Character Set is invalid where its values break the rules of PS3.3 Section C.12.1.1.2. `ISO_IR 6` as a single value, which is not a Defined Term, since Table C.12-2 gives the Default Character Repertoire no Defined Term, is read as that repertoire. A value of VR SH, LO, ST, PN, LT, UC, or UT that has bytes outside the Default Character Repertoire, in a data set or item to which no Specific Character Set applies, cannot be decoded reliably, since PS3.3 Table C.12-1 makes Specific Character Set "Required if an expanded or replacement character set is used" and nothing says which was used. The maintainer decided on 1 October 2026 that such text sequesters its instance only where the text would be kept or cleaned; where it is removed or replaced, the instance is de-identified, as for other content that cannot be decoded (Architecture item 2, D-027). The residual search reads such text as ISO 8859-1, in which every byte is a character (Architecture item 5). **Clarification, 2 October 2026.** The maintainer clarified that "the instance is de-identified" means that the transformation proceeds, not that the output may be released: a value read only as ISO 8859-1 is incomplete coverage for the residual search, and the release gate withholds the file or sends it to QC review by the value's kind (D-027 sets out the examples).
- **Rationale.** Keeping the coded pixel data, rather than decoding and re-encoding them, changes no pixel value and degrades no lossy data further, while decoding every frame lets the engine check what it releases and show it in the QC previews. The Basic Profile gives Content Sequence D, and E.3.4 Note 2 warns of significant risk in de-identifying Structured Reports without the Clean Structured Content Option. Presentation States can carry graphic and text annotations (MIDI §1.22.3). De-identification of Private SOP Classes is not defined (E.1.1, Note 6 after Table E.1-1a). Supporting every Storage SOP Class of a supported IOD, including CT Image Storage - For Processing, is consistent with deciding support by IOD, and the benchmark data should include such instances. Text decoded with the wrong character set would defeat descriptor matching (D-009), the check of a source value against the dummy value (D-021), and the residual search (D-027). Text with bytes outside the Default Character Repertoire that no Specific Character Set applies to is handled as other content that cannot be decoded is, for consistency: removing or replacing it writes nothing of it into the output, whereas keeping or cleaning it would write or match text whose characters are unknown. The residual search still finds a copy of such text left in the output, because it encodes each source value again in several character sets, including ISO 8859-1, which gives the text's own bytes back; like every form of fewer than four characters, a shorter one is listed as not searched instead (D-027), so a copy of it is not found.
- **Tests.** Each type is sequestered and reported, and never written to the release output. The Storage SOP Classes of the first release's 14 IODs, including the For Processing SOP Classes, are supported in each supported transfer syntax, protocol objects, segmentations, parametric maps, spatial registrations, and nuclear medicine and ultrasound images are not, and each other kind of SOP Class, and each other transfer syntax, is classified with its reason. Compressed instances of a synthetic collection, with identifying text planted in their codestreams' metadata segments, are released with their transfer syntax and pixels kept and none of that text. An instance whose Specific Character Set cannot be read is sequestered with that reason; a data set whose Specific Character Set is not supported, or is invalid, is read in the Default Character Repertoire, its text in ISO 646 collected and its removed text outside ISO 646 read as ISO 8859-1; and `ISO_IR 6` alone is read as the Default Character Repertoire. Text with bytes outside the Default Character Repertoire, in a data set or in an item to which no Specific Character Set applies, sequesters its instance where the text would be kept or cleaned; where it is removed or replaced, the transformation proceeds, the release gate withholds the file where the text is a person name and sends it to QC review where it is a description, and a copy of the text's bytes of at least four characters left in the output, such as in a private element, is found by the residual search, while a shorter one is listed as not searched.

### D-011: Validated overrides and derived claims

- **Decision.**
  - Validate the effective policy across L1, L2, and L3 before processing. Reject unresolved conflicts between selected options; a documented deviation never authorises a conformance claim.
  - A policy is a preset's options, or a custom option set, with the resulting action of each attribute of Table E.1-1. Any option of Table E.1-1 can be composed into a policy, so that its actions can be described, and so can the Clean Pixel Data and Clean Recognizable Visual Features Options, which PS3.15 E.1.1 Note 11 leaves out of the table and which give no attribute an action: a policy lists them after the table's options, with their CID 7050 codes, 113101 and 113102. The engine applies only a policy whose options it implements, and refuses any other before transforming anything, naming each option it does not yet apply; it applies an option once it implements the option's actions. Validation rejects both Retain Longitudinal Temporal Information Options together, which E.3.6 makes mutually exclusive; a Table E.1-1 of another edition than the data dictionary, against which the L2 rules were reviewed; and every conflict between selected options except those `tps-import` resolves.
  - That exemption belongs to the `tps-import` preset. Where Retain Device Identity gives an attribute K and Modified Dates gives it C, and the attribute has a temporal role, the preset applies C by that role (D-007) and records Retain Device Identity as unmet, so its policy claims no conformance. The same options chosen as a custom set are rejected, as is any other conflict, including one that a regenerated table adds.
  - The engine accepts only an enabled policy, composed from the pinned tables, so a policy composed from another table is never enabled: that of an enabled preset, or that of a custom option set while `basic` and each of its options are enabled (D-033).
  - Validate every L3 removal, replacement, and retention against attribute Type, VR and VM, conditional requirements, referential integrity, and the effective profile and options. Reject a change that invalidates a supported IOD.
  - Check every value the engine writes, whether a replacement, a dummy value, or an L3 value, against its VR's definition in PS3.5 Table 6.2-1 and its attribute's VM in the pinned dictionary before writing it; a value of LT, ST, UR, UT, OB, OD, OF, OL, OV, OW, or UN is single whatever the dictionary gives, as PS3.5 Section 6.4 requires. A text value is checked as the engine sets it, before encoding adds a padding byte.
