Where PS3.6 gives an attribute alternative VRs, such as "US or SS" or "OB or OW", the value is checked against the VR that applies in the data set, as the standard determines it from other attributes and the transfer syntax: for example Pixel Representation (0028,0103) decides US or SS for Pixel Padding Value (PS3.3 C.7.5.1), and Bits Allocated (0028,0100) and the transfer syntax determine which of OB and OW Pixel Data may have (PS3.5 Annex A). The checks of a value on its own accept any alternative it fits. As a text value is written, the engine also checks that the data set's Specific Character Set can encode it, in which the Default Character Repertoire, given by an absent value or by ISO_IR 6 alone (Architecture item 5), is ISO 646 (PS3.5 Section 6.1.2.1) and a single-byte character set takes one byte for each character, and where that repertoire is Value 1 with code extensions, no character of ISO 8859-1 outside ISO 646 is accepted, since pydicom would write it in ISO 8859-1 without the escape sequence of the code extension that holds it (PS3.5 Section 6.1.2.5.3); and that the first component group of a Person Name uses only the characters PS3.5 Section 6.2.1.2 allows there. What is wrong is reported without quoting the value.
  - A change incompatible with a selected option requires an explicitly revised policy that is revalidated in full; an option is never dropped silently.
  - Derive all report wording and De-identification Method and Code Sequence content from each instance's validated result. A run with mixed results cannot claim that every output conforms.
  - `tps-import` is the only policy that produces nonconformant output, marked as D-012 describes. Any other policy that cannot conform, such as one retaining attributes its options do not keep, is rejected, and instances of Private SOP Classes are sequestered (D-010).
- **Rationale.** E.1.1 requires that each attribute specified to be retained "shall be retained", and an option's requirements override the Profile's. Removing Patient's Weight therefore contradicts Retain Patient Characteristics, even though removal discloses less. Maintaining IOD integrity is the de-identifier's responsibility (E.1.1 step 2, Note 1).
- **Tests.** The presets' option sets; the eleven conflicts that `tps-import` resolves, each modified by its role, and their rejection in a custom option set; every option of Table E.1-1 composed, and refused by the engine until it applies it; each pixel option composed with any preset's options after them, leaving every attribute's action unchanged, and refused by the engine until it applies it; rejection of both Retain Longitudinal Temporal Information Options together, of a table of another edition, and of a conflict that a regenerated table adds; only enabled presets, and custom option sets of enabled options, reach the engine; removal of Type 1 and Type 2 attributes; removal of retained patient characteristics; retention the profile forbids; conditional attributes; revised option sets; and agreement between report claims and inserted markers.

### D-012: De-identification markers

- **Decision.** Markers are derived from each instance's validated result (D-011): its policy, the method digest, and the options the instance satisfies. They are added after every other action, and change nothing else in the data set.
  - Every preset adds two values to De-identification Method (0012,0063), an LO attribute of VM 1-n, after the values already present: first a value that is exactly the 64 hexadecimal digits of the method digest (D-024), and then a readable value of at most 63 characters. No other digest, such as one of the runtime environment or of the run, is added. Output that already carries markers gains them only as set out below.
  - For conformant output, set Patient Identity Removed (0012,0062) to YES; add the CID 7050 codes for the profile and each satisfied option to De-identification Method Code Sequence (0012,0064), after the items already present and apart from any already among them; and write the readable De-identification Method value `PyMedPhys <version>; PS3.15 <edition>; <preset>`, such as `PyMedPhys 0.42.0; PS3.15 2026d; basic`. A custom option set has no preset, so its readable value names it `custom option set` instead.
  - The satisfied options are the policy's options that the instance's validated result meets. Under a policy that claims conformance, every selected option is satisfied except Clean Descriptors, which goes unsatisfied where an instance retains a descriptor string that has not passed pooled human review (D-009), and Clean Pixel Data, which an instance satisfies only where it holds no top-level pixel data or a reviewer approved its regions (D-048); such an instance carries the profile's code but not the option's. A descriptor that its Basic Profile action removes or replaces instead still meets the option. Clean Recognizable Visual Features goes unsatisfied likewise where an instance is held for its face review, and an instance that satisfies it also has Recognizable Visual Features (0028,0302) set to NO, whether or not its IOD defines the attribute (D-046). Markers whose satisfied options leave out any other selected option are refused, with an error that names each option left out. The profile's code, DCM 113100, comes first, then each satisfied option's, in Table E.1-1's order of options. Every option of Table E.1-1 has its code, so the markers can be computed for any policy that can be composed. Each item holds the Code Value, Coding Scheme Designator, and Code Meaning of its row of the pinned CID 7050, and no Coding Scheme Version, which the Basic Code Sequence Macro (PS3.3 Table 8.8-1a) requires only where the designator does not identify the code unambiguously, as DCM does.
  - For `tps-import` output, which makes no conformance claim (D-007), set Patient Identity Removed to YES; add no CID 7050 codes, keeping existing items; and write the readable value `PyMedPhys <version>; no PS3.15 claim; tps-import`, which names PyMedPhys, its version, and the preset, and states that no PS3.15 conformance is claimed. Where no item was present, De-identification Method Code Sequence is left out, because the Patient Module (PS3.3 Table C.7-1) makes it Type 1C, so that where present it needs an item.
  - For both, set Longitudinal Temporal Information Modified (0028,0303) to REMOVED, to MODIFIED where Modified Dates is applied, or to UNMODIFIED where Full Dates is applied (E.3.6), unless a stricter value is already present, and add a Contributing Equipment Sequence (0018,A001) item whose purpose of reference is DCM 109104 (De-identifying Equipment, CID 7005), after the items already present unless an equal one is among them.
  - Every de-identified instance names PyMedPhys in these markers, never in dummy values, apart from the Coding Scheme Designator of the dummy item of Person Identification Code Sequence (D-021). The Contributing Equipment Sequence item names PyMedPhys as its Manufacturer (0008,0070), and the readable De-identification Method value names PyMedPhys too. In the SOP Common Module (PS3.3 Table C.12-1), which each of the first supported release's IODs includes as mandatory, the item's only Type 1 attributes are Manufacturer and Purpose of Reference Code Sequence (0040,A170), whose item carries the code: Code Value `109104`, Coding Scheme Designator `DCM`, and Code Meaning `De-identifying Equipment`. Every other attribute of the item, including Software Versions (0018,1020), is Type 3. The item holds only Manufacturer, Software Versions, and Purpose of Reference Code Sequence, so it has no Contribution DateTime (0018,A002).
  - Software Versions of the item, an LO attribute of VM 1-n, records the software that ran as four values, in this order: the PyMedPhys version, such as `0.42.0`; the Python implementation, as `platform.python_implementation()` gives it, a space, and the Python version, such as `CPython 3.14.0`; `pydicom`, a space, and its version, such as `pydicom 3.0.2`; and `tomlkit`, a space, and its version, such as `tomlkit 0.15.1`. Each value is checked as an LO value, like every value the engine writes (D-011). These values are provenance of the run, not inputs to the method digest (D-024), and the release report records the same values (D-029). They are read from the running process each time the markers are computed.
  - `<version>` is PyMedPhys's full version, as in the first value of Software Versions, and `<edition>` is the edition of the policy's Table E.1-1. Every marker value is checked against its VR and VM in the pinned data dictionary before it is written (D-011). A value that does not fit, such as a Software Versions value longer than the 64 characters of LO or a readable value longer than 63 characters, is never shortened or written without the version: the markers are refused with an error that names each attribute that does not fit. They are refused in the same way, before anything is written, where an attribute that they write would have an odd length in all and end in a value of its VR's maximum length, as Software Versions could, or De-identification Method where such a value from another de-identifier follows a pair already present. With edition 2026d, every version of up to 14 characters fits every preset's readable value; `basic-clean-descriptors` gives the longest.
  - Never set Patient Identity Removed to NO. Nonconformant output carries YES only when every deviation from the selected profile and options removes or modifies more than they require, as `tps-import`'s modification of device dates does, so a policy that resolves a conflict between options by keeping the value gets no markers.
  - On output that already carries de-identification markers, PyMedPhys's own or another de-identifier's, such as output de-identified a second time, the markers are added as the maintainer decided on 1 October 2026:
    - De-identification Method gains this run's digest and readable value only where that pair is not already present as two consecutive values, the digest first, and otherwise gains nothing. It never gains only one of the pair, so a digest is never separated from its readable value.
    - De-identification Method Code Sequence gains each code only where no item already present has the same Code Value and Coding Scheme Designator, and the same Coding Scheme Version where either item has one. Items already present keep their order, and new ones follow in this run's order.
    - Contributing Equipment Sequence gains this run's item only where no item already present is equal to it: the same Manufacturer, the same Software Versions values in the same order, and the same purpose of reference code, by its Code Value and Coding Scheme Designator.
    - Longitudinal Temporal Information Modified becomes the stricter of the value already present and this run's, in the order UNMODIFIED, MODIFIED, REMOVED, so that it never returns towards a less strict state. A value already present that is not one of these three, or more than one value, is refused rather than guessed at, as an earlier marker that cannot be read as its VR is, and the markers are not written. An empty value counts as absent.
    - Patient Identity Removed is YES, replacing any value already present.
- **Rationale.** E.1.1, E.2, and E.3.6 require these markers in their text, not in Table E.1-1, so the table generator does not produce them. E.1.1 says codes and text are "added to" these attributes, so earlier markers are kept. The CID 7050 codes carry the profile claim, so output without them claims none, and each code comes from a satisfied option rather than a selected one because D-009 claims Clean Descriptors only after review. PS3.3 notes that De-identification Method may describe the extent of de-identification, for example a HIPAA Limited Data Set, so YES with a descriptive value is an established way to record de-identification that stops short of a profile. NO would be false for `tps-import`, whose patient identity is removed, and E.1.2 has a re-identifier set NO while removing the method attributes. MIDI §1.14.3 recommends recording the de-identifying equipment in Contributing Equipment Sequence. The full SHA-256 digest fits one value exactly, since LO allows 64 characters, and the attribute is multi-valued. A shortened digest inside the readable value was rejected because a truncated digest proves less, and recording the digest in the report only was rejected because a file on its own could not be traced to its method. Software Versions takes one value for each piece of software, so each stays readable and within the 64 characters of LO, and the PyMedPhys version stands alone because the Manufacturer already names PyMedPhys. Recording the runtime there, rather than in the digest, shows what ran without changing the method's identity (D-024). The markers' fixed values, such as the Manufacturer `PyMedPhys`, the name `custom option set`, the text `no PS3.15 claim`, and the codes, are not parameters of generated values, but the method digest covers the engine's source files, which define them, so a change to any of them changes the digest (D-024).
  - The digest comes first and the readable value second so that output reads cleanly with pydicom's strict reading. An attribute whose values have an odd length in all is padded with one trailing space, which follows its last value. PS3.5 Section 6.4 allows the last value to exceed its VR's maximum length by that space, but pydicom checks each value's length before it removes the padding, so it reads a last value of the maximum length as too long: with a warning, or with an error under its strict reading. The readable value is last, and has at most 63 characters, so the padding never makes it too long. Putting the digest last was rejected because output whose two values have an odd length in all, which depends on the length of the version and the preset, could then not be read strictly. For the same reason, any other attribute that the markers would write ending in a value of its VR's maximum length with an odd length in all is refused rather than written in a form that pydicom reads as invalid.
  - Every selected option other than Clean Descriptors, Clean Pixel Data, and Clean Recognizable Visual Features must be satisfied, because an option's code must be present wherever the instance carries any of the option's actions. A Retain option keeps or modifies what the Basic Profile removes, so leaving out its code would claim more removal than was done: `public-release` output without the code of Modified Dates would claim that the Basic Profile removed the dates that were shifted. Longitudinal Temporal Information Modified, set from the selected options, then agrees with the codes. Clean Descriptors may go unsatisfied because the descriptors then receive the Basic Profile's actions, which the profile's code claims. Clean Pixel Data may, because leaving out its code claims no cleaning of the pixel data, which the profile's code does not claim either, and an instance whose regions no reviewer has approved is held for review and never released (D-048). Clean Recognizable Visual Features may, because an instance that does not satisfy it is held for review and never released (D-046).
  - The `tps-import` readable value keeps the conformant value's three fields, so that the same reading finds the software, the claim, and the preset, and says in the claim's place that none is made. It names no edition, which could be read as a claim, and is short enough to limit the version no more than `basic-clean-descriptors` does.
  - A custom option set is named by the term this document uses for it. No preset's name contains a space, so the two cannot be confused, and the codes and the method digest identify its options. Codes follow Table E.1-1's order of options, the order in which every policy holds them.
  - A value that does not fit is refused rather than shortened, because a shortened value would misstate the version or the preset. Dropping the version where it does not fit, as D-025 does for Implementation Version Name, was rejected because the readable value must name it.
  - The Contributing Equipment Sequence item holds only what this decision lists. Contribution DateTime would record when each instance was de-identified, a date that Table E.1-1 removes under the Basic Profile.
  - Software Versions is read each time the markers are computed, rather than once when the module is imported, so that it gives the versions of the software that computes them, and importing the module loads neither pydicom nor tomlkit, which PyMedPhys imports only where they are used.
  - An empty De-identification Method Code Sequence would make the output invalid, so it is left out rather than written.
  - E.1.1 has codes and text "added to" these attributes but does not address a second run, and a value, code, or item equal to one already present adds no information, so it is not added again. A run with another release or policy has a different digest, so its readable value and digest are still recorded, and a run in another runtime environment, which has the same digest unless the run is under Clean Pixel Data and its text finder's runtime differs, still adds its Contributing Equipment Sequence item, whose Software Versions differ (D-024).
  - Longitudinal Temporal Information Modified must stay true of the instance. PS3.3's SOP Common Module defines it as indicating "whether or not the date and time Attributes in the Instance have been modified during de-identification", which describes the instance's dates as they are now, and a later run cannot restore what an earlier one removed or shifted. Replacing the value would make `basic` output given Modified Dates in a second run say MODIFIED for dates that were removed and may now be shifted dummy values, and Modified Dates output given Full Dates by another de-identifier say UNMODIFIED for shifted dates. The codes stay a flat set across runs, so this attribute, not the codes, carries the state of the dates.
- **Tests.** Markers for each preset, including `tps-import`, for a custom option set, including each option of Table E.1-1 alone with its code and Full Dates with UNMODIFIED, and for input that already carries markers, whose values and items are kept; the same release and policy applied twice giving exactly the markers of one run; a different digest adding its pair, and a pair that is not present as two consecutive values added; a code already present, from another de-identifier, not added again, with new codes after it; an equal Contributing Equipment Sequence item not added again, and one with other Software Versions added; the stricter Longitudinal Temporal Information Modified for every pair of present and new values; a present value that is not one of the three refused without quoting it; values within their VR and VM limits, every version of up to 14 characters fitting every readable value within 63 characters, and a longer one refused rather than shortened; the markers of every preset, with Clean Descriptors satisfied and not, and of a custom option set, written at two versions that give their De-identification Method values an odd and an even length in all, in Implicit and Explicit VR Little Endian, read back by pydicom's strict reading without a warning, and gaining nothing when added again to what was read back; Software Versions, and De-identification Method with another de-identifier's value of LO's maximum length after a pair already present, refused where they would have an odd length in all and written where they would have an even one; the markers' pair following another de-identifier's value of LO's maximum length, which then reads strictly; a single value of its VR's maximum length written; markers changed after they are computed, such as to NO or to a value too long for its VR, refused before anything is written; both De-identification Method values from every preset, the first exactly the method digest and the second the readable value, and no other digest; a `tps-import` readable value that claims no PS3.15 conformance; codes that are rows of the pinned CID 7050, in order, for the profile and the satisfied options only, with Clean Descriptors claimed only where satisfied; refusal of satisfied options that leave out a selected option other than Clean Descriptors, Clean Pixel Data, and Clean Recognizable Visual Features, naming each option left out, except under `tps-import`, which needs none satisfied; a Contributing Equipment Sequence item that names PyMedPhys, has every Type 1 attribute of the SOP Common Module, and records in Software Versions the PyMedPhys version, the Python implementation and version, and the versions of pydicom and tomlkit, in that order, each a valid LO value, with a value too long for LO refused; each of the four values read from the running process when the markers are computed, so that changing its source changes it; changing the Python implementation and version and the versions of pydicom and tomlkit changing Software Versions but not the method digest in De-identification Method, so that output marked again in another runtime gains only that runtime's Contributing Equipment Sequence item; `tps-import` output without new CID 7050 codes or an empty code sequence; no output with Patient Identity Removed set to NO; markers that quote no source value and leave every other attribute unchanged; and agreement between markers and report claims.

### D-013: No Encrypted Attributes Sequence

- **Decision.**
  - The engine does not write the Encrypted Attributes Sequence and stores no recovery or consistency data in its output. Consistency comes from custodian-held keys and subject profiles outside the output (D-004). If the sequence is added later, it is for controlled sharing only and is never enabled by `public-release`. The conformance statement records this interpretation.
  - Any Encrypted Attributes Sequence (0400,0500) in the input is removed with its items, whatever its Type, before the rule for attributes that no rule covers applies (D-022). Each IOD of the first supported release defines it in the SOP Common Module as Type 1C: "Required if application level confidentiality is needed and certain recipients are allowed to decrypt all or portions of the Encrypted Attributes Data Set" (PS3.3 Table C.12-1). That condition never holds in the output, since no recipient of the output may decrypt anything in it, just as ROI Interpreter Sequence's condition lapses once ROI Creator Sequence is removed (D-020).
- **Rationale.** E.1.1 step 1, Note 3, deprecates other mechanisms in the data for identity recovery or longitudinal consistency "in favor of the Encrypted Attributes Data Set mechanism". MIDI §1.17.11 calls encrypting original values into de-identified images "generally inadvisable", especially for publicly released data. Table E.1-1 omits the sequence and both attributes of its items, Encrypted Content Transfer Syntax UID (0400,0510) and Encrypted Content (0400,0520), so, without the removal above, the rule for attributes that no rule covers would keep the sequence, keep the UID by its `definition` role (D-003), and keep Encrypted Content as a binary value that the IOD defines there (D-022). An input's sequence can hold original values encrypted for recipients who may re-identify them (E.1.1 and E.1.2), which this decision keeps out of the output. Giving the sequence X/Z/D, as D-022 gives the sequences it reviews, was rejected: at its Type 1C place X/Z/D resolves to D, for which D-021 gives SQ no dummy value, so every instance that carries one would be sequestered.
- **Tests.** No output contains an Encrypted Attributes Sequence or other recovery data; an instance whose input carries one is written without it, with its items removed.

### D-014: No preset named after a regulation

- **Decision.** No preset carries a regulatory name. The documentation maps DICOM profile behaviour to regulatory frameworks without claiming compliance.
- **Rationale.** The HIPAA Safe Harbor method is not a DICOM profile. It permits dates reduced to a year, which a DA value cannot hold, and its "comparable images" provision is open to interpretation for head imaging (MIDI §1.18.3.3).

### D-015: Pixel data: detect and warn

- **Decision.**
  - Detect and report indicators of risk for review: burned-in text; reconstructable faces, including RT Structure Set contours of the head (MIDI §1.20.4); and PET series from which body weight can be recovered, such as SUV-scaled series released with attenuation-corrected images (MIDI §1.20.2). The absence of an indicator is not proof of absence.
  - Each instance's attributes give its own indicators, whatever its IOD. Of burned-in text: Burned In Annotation (0028,0301) of YES; Image Type (0008,0008) whose second value is SECONDARY, since screen captures, such as dose screens, are made after the examination and usually carry text (MIDI §1.19.1); Conversion Type (0008,0064), which describes how an image was converted or captured, such as from a workstation's screen or a scanned document (PS3.3 C.8.6.1); and an overlay group without Overlay Data (60xx,3000), whose overlay PS3.3 before 2004 let lie in unused bits of the pixel data (PS3.3 C.9.2), where it stays when the Basic Profile removes the overlay's attributes. Of a reconstructable face: Recognizable Visual Features (0028,0302) of YES, and the contours of an RT Structure Set ROI whose RT ROI Interpreted Type (3006,00A4) or ROI Name (3006,0026) is EXTERNAL, BODY, or SKIN, the patient's outline, or BOLUS or FIXATION, the Defined Terms of a bolus and of an immobilization device (PS3.3 C.8.8.8), which lie on the outline and can be moulded to the face, comparing the whole value whatever its case. Of a possibly disclosed body weight: Units (0054,1001) of GML or CM2ML, values scaled to a standardised uptake value (SUV), and a Real World Value Mapping Sequence (0040,9096) item, at the top level or in a functional group of an enhanced image, whose Measurement Units Code Sequence (0040,08EA) gives a Code Value or Long Code Value that contains `{SUV`, as every SUV unit of PS3.16 CID 85 does; the Basic Profile removes Patient's Weight (0010,1030) but keeps these and Radionuclide Total Dose (0018,1074), and Retain Patient Characteristics keeps the weight itself, so that under that option these indicators say no more than the written instance does (D-038). These indicators mean possible disclosure, not demonstrated recovery: recovering the weight depends on the SUV normalisation, of which SUVibw uses height and sex rather than weight, on the activity calibration, and on compatible dose timing, possibly using an activity concentration mapping of the same values (PS3.3 C.7.6.16.2.11) or SUV-scaled images released with attenuation-corrected images of the same acquisition. An attribute that cannot be read as its VR in the pinned data dictionary is reported as unreadable evidence of the risk it bears on. A received NO counts for nothing, as an absent attribute does. Each CT, MR, PET, or RT Dose series that is a volume, whose instances of that modality, single-frame or enhanced, hold at least two frames that are not localizers, an RT Dose counting only where it holds at least two frames itself, is reported as one from which a face may be reconstructed, whatever its slice spacing: how finely it samples the face changes how readily the face is recognised (MIDI §1.18.3.2), but no spacing makes it safe, and only the pixel data could show whether it covers the face. An RT Dose grid shows the patient's outline wherever the planning system computed dose only within it, and its build-up at the skin shows it otherwise; this is an inference, not a published result. The CT, MR, and PET localizers of a series, whose Image Type has a third value of LOCALIZER, are reported apart, since a lateral localizer can show the face in profile, although no study has measured recognition from one. A volume or localizer whose Body Part Examined (0018,0015) or Anatomic Region Sequence (0008,2218), at the top level or in the Frame Anatomy functional group of an enhanced image, names a region of the head or neck, or one containing it, in a reviewed list from PS3.16 Annex L, is also reported as showing the head or neck.
  - Claim the Clean Pixel Data Option only for an instance without top-level pixel data, or whose regions a reviewer accepted or added to (D-048). Claim the Clean Recognizable Visual Features Option only for an instance that cannot show a face, or in which a reviewer found no recognisable face (D-046).
  - Keep Burned In Annotation (0028,0301) and Recognizable Visual Features (0028,0302) as received, except that an instance that satisfies the Clean Pixel Data Option has Burned In Annotation set to NO (D-048), and one that satisfies the Clean Recognizable Visual Features Option has Recognizable Visual Features set to NO (D-046); the engine removes a face from pixel data only under the Clean Recognizable Visual Features Option (D-050). Never change either to NO otherwise, and do not treat a received NO as evidence of safety.
  - Report the indicators of each released or held instance, its own and its series', in the confidential QC pack by instance (D-016), and in the release report as counts of the instances with each risk and each indicator, by disposition, naming none.
  - A reported risk does not waive the `public-release` gates (D-017). Optical character recognition and defacing may later be added as optional plugins.
- **Rationale.** PS3.15 sets these attributes to NO only as part of the Clean Pixel Data and Clean Recognizable Visual Features Options (E.3.1 and E.3.2). The engine claims each only by review, apart from instances that hold nothing to clean (D-048, D-046). Neither attribute is in Table E.1-1, so L2 needs an explicit rule. RT Dose grids, localizers, and bolus and fixation ROIs became indicators when the maintainer chose on 12 October 2026 to implement the Clean Recognizable Visual Features Option by attestation and then CT defacing, since every object that can carry the face must be found before any of them can be cleaned or attested.
- **Tests.** The L2 rules keep both attributes; received values pass through unchanged, no output changes either attribute to NO except Burned In Annotation under D-048 and Recognizable Visual Features under D-046, and detection results appear in the report.

### D-016: Confidential QC separate from release reports

- **Decision.**
  - Logs, standard output and standard error, warnings, exception messages, output file and directory names, and release reports contain no source attribute values, original paths, or keys. They use attribute paths, opaque object identifiers, rule identifiers, and aggregate results.
  - Each instance is written to `<Patient ID>/<Study Instance UID>/<Series Instance UID>/<SOP Instance UID>.dcm` below the output directory, from its replacement Patient ID (D-005) and replacement UIDs (D-003). Names therefore hold only ASCII digits, upper-case letters, `.` and `-`, and the `.dcm` suffix, and a path below the output directory has at most 160 characters. Where the policy keeps a Study, Series, or SOP Instance UID, as the Retain UIDs Option does, the file is named by that UID's keyed replacement, the name that the Basic Profile gives the same instance under the same key (D-040); any other value that is not a replacement names nothing. Instances that share a file name, and so a SOP Instance UID, are never renamed: D-026 sets what happens to them, and their run positions appear only in the confidential QC pack. The output is a plain directory tree, not a PS3.10 File-set: a File ID component holds at most eight characters from A to Z, 0 to 9, and underscore (PS3.10 8.2 and 8.5), which a UID cannot meet, so no DICOMDIR describes it.
  - A QC pack contains only the retained strings, contextual excerpts, and image previews needed for review. It is written to an explicitly designated location restricted to authorised reviewers, treated as potentially identifying, excluded from release directories and archives and kept out of the source directory, and has documented retention and deletion.
  - A run's QC pack is the JSON document `qc-pack.json`, labelled with its format, `pymedphys-deid-qc-pack/12`. It lists each input by its run position and source path, with the SHA-256 of the source file as the run's first pass read it, with the SHA-256 of the pixel data of the file written of each released or held input, as the gate read it, which binds a face verdict to the pixel data that the reviewer saw, recorded only where previews that could show a face, which are not its contour sheets, draw every frame of them from the first, as a volume's projection and surface render draw each of its slices, taking a slice into the pixel or voxel nearest it by its maximum where slices are closer than those, and for an RT Dose only where its dose-levels previews do, since its cine strip is windowed to the highest doses and an Otsu render of a series of its planes shows one level (D-017), nor for an instance listed as not previewed, whose native Pixel Data hold more bytes than its frames and one byte of padding, or with a frame that holds more than one of the stored values that Pixel Padding Value and Pixel Padding Range Limit name, which previews draw as one black or as no patient (D-017, D-046), and with the SHA-256 of the contours of each released or held RT Structure Set as written, which binds the verdict on its series to the contours that the reviewed run wrote, recorded only where the pack's contour sheet shows them (D-017, D-046, D-052), and with what the run did with it: its output path if released or a duplicate of one released; the opaque per-run label by which the release report refers to it, S-0001 onwards at one width, and why, if sequestered, as the maintainer decided on 2 October 2026 (D-026); and why, if held for review or refused; each residual by instance and place, with an excerpt of up to 48 bytes before its offset and 96 from it, decoded with the residual's encoding, or ISO 8859-1 where Python cannot decode with it, and with each undecodable byte and each backslash written as a backslash escape; each value left out of the search, because the policy retains it, it equals a constant that the engine always writes, it cannot be decoded inside a removed sequence, or it is a UID that the pinned tables register, and each form not searched, by instance and place, as the maintainer decided on 2 October 2026 (D-027); each distinct retained string with every place it was retained, a retained string being a value that the instance's plan keeps as it is, in an element with a text VR other than UI, outside any removed sequence, and other than Specific Character Set, an instance whose retained string cannot be decoded for review, such as text outside ISO 646 where no Specific Character Set applies, being sequestered rather than released unreviewed (D-017); and what descriptor cleaning wrote for each ROI Name, renamed, empty, kept, mapped, emptied, held for review, or emptied unreviewed, with why a held name was held and the names of the run's institutional list that it matched (D-009); what descriptor cleaning decided for each value of each free-text descriptor that it read, kept, kept by vocabulary, mapped, emptied, or held, with why a held value was held and what each would write, and whether the element was written with those values or took the action without Clean Descriptors, each by its tag or, for private text, by its private creator, group, element within the block, and VR (D-009, D-035, D-036); each first-pass finding that the run reports without acting on it, at each instance it names, with its kind, the attribute's tags, and its count (D-026); each instance in a high-risk category, with the indicators of risk in its pixel data that put it there (D-015, D-017); each series of released or held instances that is a CT, MR, or PET volume, with the instances that make it one, and the attributes that name the head or neck and the instances that hold them, and each attribute that its series assessment could not read, the series being grouped by the source's Series Instance UID, which the pack does not write (D-015); each series of released or held instances, by the run positions of its instances, grouped the same way, by which a reviewer gives the series a face verdict (D-017); each released or held instance whose Pixel Data the Clean Pixel Data Option cleaned, with why it is held, its number of frames, the Rows and Columns of each, and the regions blacked out in each frame, by frame index from 0, each region as `[top, left, bottom, right]` in pixels from the frame's top left corner, with the bottom row and right column excluded (D-037); and each image preview for D-017's and D-037's review, by its file in a `previews` directory beside the pack, with the frames it shows, by run position and frame index, how many frames its series or instance has, and the file's SHA-256, and each instance whose pixel data could not be previewed, or whose contours could not be shown, with why; a cleaned instance has one preview of each frame, in order, unless it is listed as not previewed. Its opaque reference is random, `A-` and 32 hexadecimal digits, not derived from its content. Under Clean Pixel Data the pack is more sensitive than the release, and than packs before format 5, since each frame with regions is previewed as received, with the burned-in text that the release lacks. It is also larger: each cleaned frame is a PNG at full resolution, before compression two bytes a pixel, plus the gutter, for a monochrome frame without regions, and nine bytes a pixel, plus the gutters, for one with them, so a 300-slice CT of 512 by 512 pixels with regions in every slice adds about 715 MB before compression, and the run holds the PNGs, not decoded frames, until it writes the pack. A run that reads an attested pack back takes only the current format, so a pack written in an earlier format, before a change such as this one, is written and attested again. Each run writes its QC pack before it publishes the release, from each input's outcome and what its transform and gate found, so a release never exists without one; a pack that cannot be written publishes nothing, and the pack of a release that cannot then be published is removed, or named in the error if it cannot be. The location has no default, and a run checks it before it creates anything. It must be neither inside the source, release, or staging directory nor contain any of them, compared by resolved path, without case on Windows and macOS, and by the device and inode of each existing directory, which finds a directory spelt otherwise on a case-insensitive share or reached through a bind mount; the checks are repeated once the location is created. It must be new or an empty directory; on POSIX an existing one must belong to the current user and grant its group and others no access, and the pack's directory is opened without following a symbolic link and given mode 0700, as is its `previews` directory, which holds a copy of the marker, and its files 0600, while on Windows access rests on the location's access control lists. A marker file, `.pymedphys-deid-qc-pack`, written first, lets a release step refuse any tree that holds QC material, or a symbolic link to some, and a tree that cannot be read is reported rather than passed. A handling notice beside the pack states that it may identify people, is for authorised reviewers only, is never copied into a release, and is deleted once the review, the attestation, and the custodian's documented retention period no longer need it. No file is overwritten, and errors name the check that failed and the operating system's reason, never a path or value.
  - Key and subject-profile stores are separate custodian-controlled state and are included in neither. The reviewed-names list that ROI name cleaning reuses (D-009) is confidential state too, since it holds source ROI names verbatim and persists across runs. The maintainer decided on 1 October 2026 that it is held in the custodian's store with the key and subject profiles, protected as they are (D-004), and included in neither; only its keyed digest enters the method digest (D-024).
  - Release reports record an opaque attestation reference and outcome, not the review material.
  - A reviewer attests to a QC pack once, in `attestation.json` beside it, labelled `pymedphys-deid-qc-attestation/3`, naming themselves and the time in UTC, stating whether they reviewed every distinct retained string, every series, and every instance in high-risk categories (D-017), and giving the outcome, attested or rejected; attested needs all three reviews. It may also give a face verdict for any of the pack's series, named by the run position of its first instance: absent where the reviewer saw no recognisable face in its previews, and present where they saw one. The previews show each instance as the run wrote it, and a verdict covers only the pixel data that the run wrote, by their SHA-256 in the pack, so a CT series judged as received is not released defaced by a later run under the option (D-050), and a run under the option previews it defaced. A verdict is the reviewer's judgement, the outcome does not depend on it, and the release report does not record it; a later run of the same files under the Clean Recognizable Visual Features Option follows it (D-046). It may likewise give a redaction verdict for any instance whose Pixel Data the pack lists as cleaned and previewed, named by its run position: accepted, where the pack's regions cover every burned-in text that the reviewer saw in its previews; revised, with regions, by frame, that the reviewer adds to the pack's, so that together they cover every burned-in text that the reviewer saw, each in the form that the pack gives its own, in a frame that the instance holds, and within the Rows and Columns that the pack records for it; or rejected. A later run of the same files under the Clean Pixel Data Option follows them, and the release report does not record them (D-048). An outcome of rejected takes no verdict of either kind, since a rejected attestation's verdicts would release nothing: attesting refuses one, and a later run refuses a rejected attestation as it does a pack that is not attested (D-046, D-048). The attestation records the SHA-256 of the pack's file, which records the SHA-256 of each of its previews, so a pack or preview changed after it was attested is refused, and a pack whose previews do not match it cannot be attested. The attestation is confidential with the pack, since it names the reviewer, and the release report records only the pack's reference, the outcome (attested, rejected, or not attested where no attestation has been written), and the releaser's two confirmations described below, each true, false, or not stated. As the maintainer decided on 6 October 2026, a public command records the attestation in the QC pack's directory, through the same library function, naming the pack's directory, the reviewer, the outcome, each review done, and any face and redaction verdicts, with each region that a revised verdict adds in its summary, beside the file in the pack that previews its frame, for the reviewer to check, and kept out of the `pymedphys` command line until the first supported release, like the tool's other commands. It refuses a directory that holds no QC pack, and neither its summary nor its messages quote the reviewer or a path. It does not change the release report published with the run, which records the outcome when the run wrote it, not attested, since the design does not provide for rewriting a published report; the attestation stays with the pack, from which the record a report takes is read. The person releasing the data may also confirm in the attestation, each yes or no and neither required, that the output was checked for its intended use and that its residual risk was accepted under the institution's governance; the confirmations hold no text, are bound to the pack with the rest of the attestation, and reach the record that a release report takes as their outcomes alone, since fitness for a use and an acceptable residual risk depend on the purpose and the recipient, which only the releaser knows. This was decided on 6 October 2026 for the first release, which assigns both confirmations to whoever releases the data and has each run's release report also summarise the engine's structural checks of its output.
- **Rationale.** Human review needs material that may still identify people; routine diagnostics and distributable reports do not. Names built from replacement values are the same under one key in every run and processing order, whereas renaming instances that share a name would make each name depend on the other instances, and so rename earlier output when an incremental export adds instances. Naming a file by its replacement SOP Instance UID lets a reviewer find output from the identifiers in reports. The 160-character bound leaves 98 characters for the output directory within the 259 that Windows allows a path without long-path support.
- **Tests.** Captured logs and errors contain no source values or paths; residual search results and errors contain no source value in any form; output names contain no source path, file name, or value, are portable and within the length bound, and are refused for any value that is not a replacement; instances that share a file name are found by their run positions, not renamed; release archives contain no QC or state artefacts; the QC destination and access requirements are enforced; and a reviewer can locate affected output from opaque identifiers.

### D-017: Public-release risk assessment

- **Decision.** A `public-release` run is marked ready for release only when three gates pass: statistical disclosure control, pixel and face review, and a human QC attestation.
  - **Statistical gate.** Accept a maximum per-subject re-identification probability of 0.09 by default, or 0.05 when selected, under an explicitly selected and validated model.
    - The assessment names and versions the model and its implementation. It defines the attacker's assumed knowledge, including knowledge of membership, and identifies the reference population and sampling assumptions, or justifies assessment within the release cohort. It records matching variables, generalisation, missing-data handling, uncertainty, and exclusions.
    - It counts distinct subjects, not images, frames, or visits, and accounts for linked longitudinal records and earlier linked releases. A sample-based `1/k` calculation is permissible only with a documented known-membership or equivalence-class model.
    - The assessment is bound to the exact collection and policy versions, and any change requires reassessment. A missing model, incomplete subject coverage, an unavailable estimate, or a failed threshold fails the gate; no default score substitutes.
    - A documented external assessment may supply the evidence, recorded and bound as D-053 sets. An automated estimator is enabled only after it has been selected and validated.
  - **Human review.** Review every distinct retained string, every series (by maximum intensity projection or cine strip), and every instance in high-risk categories. Potentially reconstructable facial information blocks release unless a risk assessment reference is recorded. For each series, the reviewer may record whether its previews, such as the renders of its skin surface, show a recognisable face; the pack records the SHA-256 of each source file, so the verdict is bound, through the attestation's digest of the pack, to the files the reviewer saw (D-016). Review material follows D-016.
    - The QC pack previews the files that the run released or held for review, so that a reviewer sees the pixel data as written, with any region that the Clean Pixel Data Option blacked out (D-037). A series, the instances that share a Series Instance UID, ordered along the normal where they share an orientation and every one has a position, or else by Instance Number where every one has one, or else by run position, is shown as a cine strip of up to 16 frames, evenly spaced from its first frame to its last, each scaled to fit 256 pixels. A series of at least three single-frame instances of one size, orientation, and pixel spacing, at distinct positions stacked along the normal, and with axes along the patient's three directions, each with its other two direction cosines at most $10^{-4}$, is also shown as a frontal maximum intensity projection, projected along the anterior-posterior axis, head up and with the patient's right on the viewer's left, sampled to square pixels, each slice also taken into the pixel nearest it by the maximum, so that every slice is drawn where slices are closer than the pixels. An oblique stack has a cine strip only, since a projection along an oblique axis would superimpose what a frontal view keeps apart. Each such volume is also shown as six renders of its skin surface, head up, from the front, at 45 degrees to either side, in both profiles, and from behind: the surface of the voxels at or above -300 HU for CT, or Otsu's threshold for any other modality, such as MR or PET, with pixel padding counted below every other value, sampled by nearest neighbour to cubic voxels of 2 mm, or of its smallest spacing where that is larger, however long the volume, so that a long volume's renders are taller rather than coarser, each slice also taken into the voxels nearest it along the slice axis by their maximum, so that every slice is drawn where slices are closer than the voxels, seen at the first such voxel along each ray, and shaded by Lambert's law with the light at the viewer. A volume more than 2,000 mm long, or more than 1,000 mm from left to right or from front to back, as for a contour sheet's frame, is neither rendered nor projected, so that its slices beyond its cine strip are held. Pixel padding is shown black, and lies below every surface's threshold. An RT Dose is shown by its dose levels: each RT Dose instance of two or more frames whose axes lie along the patient's and whose Grid Frame Offset Vector is evenly spaced, and each such volume of RT Dose planes, as rows of those six renders, one of its support, the voxels of any dose above 0 after Dose Grid Scaling, and one at each fraction of its greatest dose of 0.5, 0.2, 0.1, 0.05, and so on down, whose dose is above its smallest dose above 0, each plane of a volume scaled by its own Dose Grid Scaling; and each RT Dose plane of one frame in no volume as a map at full resolution, grey by how many of those levels each pixel reaches, so that each level's outline is an edge. Each level's row of a grid or volume is followed, where a ray of the frontal view or of a profile crosses its surface more than twice, by a row of its cavities and a row for each further crossing, as a contour sheet draws an ROI that hides its own surface, so that a lower dose enclosed by a higher one is shown; a sheet that would be taller than 32,768 pixels is not drawn, so its grid is held, and a level's rows of further crossings are counted before any is drawn, so that rows that would not fit are neither drawn nor found. Only an RT Dose by its SOP Class UID whose stored values are unsigned, not padded, not rescaled, and scaled by a finite Dose Grid Scaling above 0 to doses that float32 holds, none above 0 taken to 0 or below a normal float32 and none to infinity, is shown so, since a signed grid can hold doses below 0 that no level shows, a padded one has stored values that are read as no dose, and a scaling that float32 loses would leave a dose out of every level; a grid of no dose shows an empty support. A planning system either sets dose to zero outside the body, so that the support outlines the skin wherever the grid reaches past it, or computes a dose in air, so that dose falls steeply across the skin. The region at or above a level lies inside the region at or above any lower one, and neighbouring levels, and the lowest level and the smallest dose, are at most a factor of 2.5 apart, so an outline that holds over a band of dose wider than that shows in at least one row; one that holds only over a narrower band may show in none, which is the dose levels' residual risk, and the factor is a placeholder, to be set by validation. The band is that at each point of an outline, so an outline whose dose varies along it, as a dose in air may, can show in parts across rows rather than whole in one. The cine strip of an RT Dose is windowed to its highest doses, which can leave a low dose outlining the skin black, and an Otsu render of a series of its planes shows one level, so only its dose levels count as showing an RT Dose's frames for the SHA-256 of its pixel data (D-016).
    - Each released or held instance with contours, such as an RT Structure Set, is shown as a contour sheet after the series' previews, so that the reviewer sees a face that its contours, as the run wrote them, trace. The sheet renders the region that the contours bound as the skin-surface renders do, from the same six directions. For each Frame of Reference of its ROIs, in the order of the first ROI in each, it shows every ROI of that frame united, on cubic voxels of 2 mm however long the frame, so that a long frame's row is taller rather than coarser, and then, where the run wrote a volume in that frame, preferring a series that the structure set references, that volume's surface render in grey, with the edge of the united region's silhouette in each view drawn over it in magenta. Then each item of the ROI Contour Sequence has a row of its own, in order, its ROI alone on the finer of the voxels that a united region of its own extent would have and those that keep that extent within 128 a side, but none under 0.5 mm, so that an ROI far from another is sampled as if alone, since the united region hides an ROI wholly inside another, such as a face-shaped ROI inside a box; an item without contours has a black strip, so that each row stays that of its item, and an item the same as an earlier one is rendered once. Where a ray of the frontal view or of a profile, which look along the axes of the voxels and so between them meet every voxel of a region's surface, crosses an ROI's surface more than twice, the ROI hides some of its surface behind its own, as one does with a cavity within it, such as a face-shaped cavity that `CLOSEDPLANAR_XOR` or keyhole contours bound within a box, or with one part behind another. Its row is then followed by a row of its cavities, the voxels that each slice of its region encloses but does not hold, rendered as a region of their own, where it has any, and by a row for each further crossing, in which each ray of those three views, and of the view from behind, shows the surface where it crosses it, into or out of the region, that many times from the viewer, so that every surface that those rays cross is shown; one surface can be split across those rows, where rays cross different numbers of surfaces before it, so a reviewer reads an ROI's rows together. The oblique views have no rows of crossings, since their rays, which step between voxels, cross a surface falsely where they graze it. In each plane, an ROI's region is the even-odd fill of its closed contours there, whether `CLOSED_PLANAR` or `CLOSEDPLANAR_XOR`, so that a contour within another is a hole; a plane, the mean height of contours within 0.15 mm of one another, covers the slices of voxels within half the distance to its ROI's next plane above and below, or half the median distance between its ROI's planes, or half a voxel where that is more, where the next is farther than twice that median or there is none. The voxel nearest each point of a `POINT` contour, or of a closed contour of fewer than three points, and nearest each point along an open or closed contour at most half a voxel apart, is filled as a mark, so that a closed contour that bounds no area is drawn as its line; and each region's voxels extend up to a voxel below its lowest point, so that it is drawn. A mark is hidden where the ROI's own closed contours fill its voxel, if it is a mark of a point, of an open contour, or of a closed contour that bounds no voxel, as a curve drawn within a box is; and any mark is hidden where what its slice of voxels shows, the region and every mark there, holds every voxel within two of it in the slice, as around a contour that retraces a loop within its region, one that bounds a hole that its own marks or others fill, or one in a plane that covers no slice. Those marks, with every mark of the first kinds, then have rows of their own after the ROI's, in layers: each contour's hidden marks lie in the first layer that holds none of their voxels and none beside one along its row or its column of voxels, so that a layer draws each contour's marks as they would be drawn alone, and a contour's marks the same as an earlier one's are drawn once. Each layer has a row for each further crossing of its surface however few times a ray crosses it, but none of cavities, so that every mark is shown, in those rows or within two voxels, at most 4 mm, of the edge of what its slice shows, which the ROI's rows draw from the front, the back, and the sides. A layer's frontal view and profiles draw every voxel of it that has a neighbour along its row or its column of voxels that is not in the layer, and no other, and a set is not shown where any voxel of a layer has no such neighbour, even where an oblique view would draw it. Since a layer draws each contour's marks as they would be drawn alone, that holds a set only where one contour's own marks lie side by side within its region: where a line drawn back and forth over a patch, with no gap a voxel wide between its passes, covers a curve within it, where one contour retraces a cross along a row and a column of voxels, near the tip of an open contour that turns back sharply, as a V whose arms meet at 45° or less can, or where a ring only a few voxels across lies within its region, as one can at the very end of a rounded ROI. Lines that fill a hole, cross one another, or run side by side, and the outlines of planes finer than the voxels that cross one another in a slice, as a body's and an arm's do, or lie within one another, as near a rounded end, are each drawn in a layer of their own. A sheet shows a structure set only where every contour is of one of those five types, of finite coordinates, in threes, none above 1,000,000 mm in magnitude, without a Contour Offset Vector or Contour Slab Thickness, and, for a closed contour of three or more points, with each point within 0.15 mm of one axial plane, and where the ROIs of each frame span at most 2,000 mm along each axis and at most 1,000 mm from left to right and from front to back, wider than any scanner's bore, so that each row of 2 mm voxels is at most about 1,000 pixels tall and 4,300 wide, and the sheet is at most 32,768 pixels tall, at least about 120 rows in one frame up to 510 mm long, about 103 in ten frames each with the render of a volume up to 510 mm long, about 2 fewer for each further frame with one, and about 32 rows of an ROI, or a volume's render, 2,000 mm long, so that no viewer need scale down or refuse it; a taller sheet is drawn only until it is too tall. Any other is listed as not previewed, as `contours-not-shown`, and the pack records no SHA-256 of its contours; the pack is refused where it records a digest of contours, other than of none, without a contour sheet of that instance, or lists one as `contours-not-shown` with either, and a face review reads it the same way, so that no verdict releases contours that the reviewer did not see (D-046, D-052).
    - An instance is in a high-risk category where its source's attributes show an indicator of risk in its pixel data (D-015). It is listed with its indicators and previewed at full resolution, every frame or 16 evenly spaced, since burned-in text may be small. The indicators of a whole series, of a CT, MR, PET, or RT Dose volume, of a localizer, and of the head or neck, are assessed over the series' released and held instances, since only they reach a recipient, listed with the series, and reviewed through its series' previews; an RT Structure Set with the patient's outline has no pixel data, so its outline's face is reviewed through its contour sheet and the images it is drawn on; where a run under the Clean Recognizable Visual Features Option defaces those images, the structure set is cut by the same face block, or held where it cannot be (D-050, D-052).
    - Monochrome pixel data are rescaled by Rescale Slope and Intercept and windowed from the 0.5th to the 99.5th percentile of what each preview shows, other than a redaction preview's full-range image (D-037), leaving out the values that Pixel Padding Value and Pixel Padding Range Limit name, which are shown black, and as no patient in a surface render, and MONOCHROME1 is inverted. Since padding is drawn as one value, a frame whose padding holds more than one stored value, which could hold an image that every preview draws black, leaves its instance's pixel data without a digest in the pack, so that no face verdict releases them; padding of one value shows only where it lies, which the previews draw. Colour pixel data of 8 bits are shown as RGB: RGB as it is, YBR_FULL and YBR_FULL_422 as pydicom converts them, and YBR_ICT and YBR_RCT as pylibjpeg-openjpeg decodes them. Compressed pixel data are decoded by the plugin pinned to their transfer syntax (Architecture item 2). Pixel data in a compressed transfer syntax that no installed plugin is pinned to decode, of another photometric interpretation, as float values, or that cannot be decoded are listed as not previewed, with why, rather than left out, and so is a released or held instance whose written file the run did not hand to the pack. These are the defaults that the QC pack thread chose on 6 October 2026.
- **Open question.** A ROI Name that automatic cleaning writes in the vocabulary's spelling is a cleaned value, not a retained string (D-009), so the human review gate's review of every distinct retained string does not cover these renames. Whether `public-release` should review them too is not yet decided.
- **Rationale.** MIDI §1.15.2.2 describes 0.09 as a common choice and 0.05 for more sensitive collections. Sections 1.15.1.1 and 1.15.1.2 show that such a number is meaningful only with a group size, reference population, threat model, and choice of maximum or average risk, and that public releases are assessed on maximum risk with attack assumed certain. The statistic covers only the modelled disclosure channels, so it cannot replace pixel review or human QC, or establish legal anonymity. The surface renders were added when the maintainer chose on 12 October 2026 to implement the Clean Recognizable Visual Features Option by attestation and then by CT defacing, since a recognition system matches a reconstructed skin surface, which neither a cine strip nor a frontal maximum intensity projection shows. The view from behind was added so that a surface facing the back is seen as one facing the front is, and not only edge on from the sides, as a face would be in a volume whose stated orientation puts it at the back, and so that the edge of a contour sheet's slice that faces the back is drawn. The renders were first sampled to keep each axis within 256 voxels, so a volume more than 510 mm in extent was rendered coarser than 2 mm, about 7 mm for a whole-body CT 1,800 mm long, coarser than the render of a head that a reviewer's verdict on a face relies on; they now keep 2 mm, within the bounds of a contour sheet's frame, and a volume beyond them is held beyond its cine strip, failing safe, since no scanner's bore is 1,000 mm across. The per-series face verdicts were added for the same option, so that a reviewer's judgement of each series is recorded with the exact source files it covers. The contour sheets were added for D-052's condition that the option not be enabled until the reviewer sees each structure set's contours as written, drawn with its images: a row for each ROI item as well as each frame's union, since a union hides an ROI wholly inside another, which a verdict would otherwise release unseen, and points and open contours drawn as marks rather than leaving their sets unshown, since clinical sets commonly carry markers such as an isocentre's point. An ROI also hides from its own row any surface behind its own, as a box hides a face-shaped cavity that its own contours bound, so such an ROI has rows of its cavities and of each further crossing of its surface; listing such sets as not shown instead would hold every ROI of paired organs, a body with its arms, or a vertebra with its canal, and holding only contours nested in a plane would miss a keyhole contour's cavity and a part hidden behind another. Its region likewise absorbs a mark on a voxel that it fills, such as a curve traced within it, which would otherwise bind its digest to lines that the reviewer did not see, so such marks have rows of their own; the marks of a closed contour that bounds a voxel lie on the edges of what its slice shows, except where it runs within that region, bounds a hole that marks fill, or lies in a plane that covers no slice, so only its marks two voxels or more within what the slice shows join those rows, which would otherwise draw every ROI's outline again. Those rows draw their marks as regions, which cannot show a mark that they hold within themselves, so they show every crossing that the rays of the frontal view and the profiles make, and a set is held rather than shown where a voxel of a layer has no neighbour along its row or its column that is not in it, since no ray of the frontal view or a profile then crosses into or out of the layer there; a test of the marks' depth within their own rows, as for those within the region, would miss marks that cover a curve while leaving gaps a voxel wide between them. With all of an ROI's hidden marks in one layer, as first made, a contour's marks merged with those beside them, so that test also held sets whose rows would draw each contour alone: three or more lines side by side, two lines that cross along a row and a column of voxels, a body whose outline crosses its arms' in planes finer than its voxels, and a rounded end that is blunt for its voxels in planes about 0.3 of its voxel apart or finer, where the rings of several planes lie within one another in a slice, such as a body 400 by 250 by 600 mm, of 2.35 mm voxels, at all of 16 offsets in planes 0.7 mm apart, and ellipsoids the size of a liver (220 by 160 by 180 mm) and the lungs (300 by 180 by 260 mm) in planes 0.5 mm apart. Each contour's marks in a layer where no other's lie beside them are drawn as they would be alone, and a curve that lines cover, or a hole's outline that they fill, is shown in a layer of its own rather than held. Every one of those sets is now shown: the body in planes 0.5 to 1.25 mm apart, and the liver, the lungs, and an ellipsoid 300 by 200 by 480 mm, each at four offsets; the body in planes 0.625 mm apart has 2 layers of hidden marks, and its sheet is 4,232 pixels tall; of two draws of 108 rounded ROIs 40 to 300 mm across, 27 in each of planes 0.5, 0.625, 1, and 1.25 mm apart, one is held: in planes 0.5 mm apart, an ellipsoid 200 by 140 by 160 mm whose lowest plane lies 0.023 mm above its end, so that the ring there, about 3 by 2 voxels across, lies side by side with itself within the slice's region. A set whose hidden marks fit one layer is drawn as before. Counting the oblique views, which can draw a voxel that the frontal view and profiles do not, was measured instead and is left out: with each contour drawn as alone, it would add only where one contour lies side by side with itself, as at the tip of a sharp V. A V within its region whose arms meet at 45° was held at 2 of 24 placements over voxels of 0.5 mm, at 30° at 9, and at 20° at 14, and at 60° and 90° at none; counting the obliques' first hits would show the 2 at 45° and leave 5 held at 30° and 9 at 20°, and would draw a cross that one contour retraces, but not a zigzag two voxels tall. Holding such sets fails safe, and splitting a contour into pieces that each draw would show a face's outline only in pieces. A mark up to two voxels within is left to the edge, since the contours of planes that cover no slice, between those of a smooth outline, lie there without showing anything that the edge does not; so a part of a contour that runs within two voxels of its slice's edge, at most 4 mm, is shown only as that edge, which a view looks at from the front, the back, or a side. Near a rounded end, such as the top of a head or the ends of a body, an outline can shrink by more than two voxels from one such plane to the next, so planes finer than the voxels there give rows of hidden marks: in planes 1 mm apart over voxels near 2 mm, a head to its top or an ellipsoidal body 480 mm long has 11 rows, 4 of them of hidden marks, where planes 3 mm apart give only its own row. No fixed depth avoids those rows, and a narrower test, such as of a mark's depth within its own plane's region, would miss a notch that such a plane cuts into its outline, where a face could lie, so the rows are kept. The contours as received are not drawn, since a sheet of them would put back in the pack the face that the run removed, and a verdict covers only the contours written. On synthetic sets of an elliptical outline 180 by 216 mm across and about 200 mm long, in planes 2.5 mm apart, and ROIs of random elliptical sections 6 to 96 mm across and 5 to 60 mm long, centred within 50 mm of its axis, none of whose ROIs hides its own surface, and no volume to draw them on, a sheet of 60 ROIs is about 4,400 pixels tall by 870 wide and 0.3 MB as a PNG, made in about 2 s, and one of 150 ROIs about 10,600 by 870 pixels and 0.8 MB, made in about 5 s. A sheet is drawn only until it is too tall, and its height without rows of cavities or further crossings is found before any of it is drawn, so most sets over the bound are held without drawing it. A closed contour in another plane is not drawn, since the slices of voxels would cut its region edge on, so its set is held rather than shown in part.
- **Tests.** Subject versus instance counts; longitudinal and prior-release linkage; missing model, assumptions, or coverage; threshold boundaries; failed or stale assessments; and independence of the three gates. Contour sheets: closed, open, and point contours are read with the frames and series that the set references, each ROI taking its own frame or else the set's only one; a sagittal or tilted closed contour, an unknown or missing type, a value that is not finite or too far, a Contour Offset Vector, a Contour Slab Thickness, Contour Data not in threes or absent, or ROIs of one frame spanning more than 2,000 mm, or more than 1,000 mm from left to right or from front to back, leaves a set unshown, while ROIs spanning exactly 1,000 mm across are shown; a contour within another of its ROI is a hole for either closed type; one ROI's planes never empty another's slices; a gap of more than twice the planes' spacing is left empty; points and lines are drawn as marks; an ROI alone is sampled finely, and no coarser for an ROI far from it; a frame's united row, and an ROI more than 510 mm long, are drawn on 2 mm voxels, and a sheet is the same however many slices are rendered at once; every point of an ROI, the lowest of many planes, and an open contour's lower end are drawn; a closed contour that bounds no area is drawn as its line, and marks are taken along a segment a whole number of steps long; a head's nose shows in the renders of its contours and not of contours cut, and the same contours always give the same PNG; an ROI enclosed by another has its own row; a face-shaped cavity that one ROI's `CLOSEDPLANAR_XOR` or keyhole contours bound within a box, which the box's own row hides, has a row of its own and shows in the rows of further crossings, and two parts of one ROI, one behind the other, give a row for each further crossing; open curves, a point, a point just inside an edge, a closed contour that bounds no area, and two identical closed contours within their own ROI's box, which the box's rows hide, have rows of their own that tell different curves apart, and a line above the box has none; a box contour that also traces a loop within the box twice, or a spur into the box and back, has rows that tell a square loop from a circle and a straight spur from a bent one, and so do, in a slab of planes finer than its slices, a slit a voxel wide and a hole in a plane that covers no slice; lines that fill a box's hole, or that cover a curve within it with gaps a voxel wide between them or in bands four voxels wide, are drawn in layers that tell a square hole from a circle and one curve from another, and so are lines that cross along a row and a column of voxels or lie three side by side, and the rings near the ends of an ellipsoid the size of a liver in planes 0.5 mm apart, while contours whose voxels touch only diagonally, and a contour repeated, share a layer; a box whose curve one line drawn back and forth over it covers, or within which one open contour retraces a cross, has no sheet; over random ROIs with random points, lines, and retraced loops, the frontal view and profiles cross into or out of each region at every voxel of its edge in its slice and no other, some layer holds each contour's hidden marks and draws them as alone, and a set is shown exactly where its layers' rows draw every voxel of them; a head to its top in planes 1 mm apart has rows of its hidden marks, and a sheet; ordinary outlines, of a head, a box, and that slab, hide no marks; each crossing of a surface is found in order along a ray, up to the ray's end; a volume 1,000 mm long is rendered on 2 mm voxels, and one more than 2,000 mm long or 1,000 mm across, from left to right or from front to back, is neither sampled nor rendered, and a CT series that wide has only its cine strip, and pixel data's digests only for the slices that it shows; the view from behind shows a head's ear on the viewer's left, mirrors the frontal view of a head symmetric from front to back, and hides its nose, and only it and the profiles show a bump on a box's back; views and crossings are the same however many slices are rendered at once; a dose level's rows of further crossings that would not fit are neither drawn nor found; each frame has its own united row and each item its own, with a black strip for an item without contours; a sheet is drawn with the CT series that the set references, not another in its frame, with the outline in magenta in that row alone, and without a CT row where none is in its frame; every structure set with contours has a sheet, whatever its risk; a sheet over its tallest is not given, and is drawn only until it is too tall, and an ROI whose layers would take more of its height than is left has none drawn, while one whose own rows would has neither its rows drawn nor its crossings or hidden marks found; a set with a contour not drawn, or whose sheet is over the height bound, even by the rows of a cavity alone, is listed as `contours-not-shown` with no contours' digest, while a set without contours keeps the digest of none; the contours' digest changes with a Contour Offset Vector or Contour Slab Thickness; a pack, and a face review reading one, refuse a contours' digest without a sheet, and a pack refuses a set listed as not shown with a sheet or a digest; even-odd filling matches an independent polygon library on random polygons, holes, vertices, and horizontal edges; and in two runs of a structure set alone, a verdict of absent releases one whose contours were shown, and holds one with a sagittal contour, or whose sheet is over the height bound, as `no-face-verdict`; and the pack records the SHA-256 of pixel data only where its previews show every frame: an instance with more frames than its cine strip or its full-resolution preview shows, a series that forms no volume beyond the frames of its cine strip, and Float Pixel Data, listed as not previewed, have none, while every slice of a volume, shown by its projection and surface render, and every frame of an instance whose frames are all shown, have theirs; a file without pixel data keeps the digest of none; Pixel Data holding more than their frames have none; an RT Dose's dose levels list every frame of grids of 30 and 12 frames, of a series of planes, of two planes, and of one, and of a grid labelled CT, and a grid without them, unscaled, scaled by 0 or below, signed, rescaled, oblique, with uneven offsets, or longer than its frames, or a series of planes one of which is unscaled, has none though its cine strip or Otsu render lists every frame; the dose levels run from the support to half the greatest dose, no two neighbours, nor the lowest and the smallest dose, more than 2.5 apart; the support of a grid is the head that a CT render shows, and a hot spot alone is above it; a nose at 12 mGy beside a 1 Gy core shows in the support where air is set to zero, and in the 5 and 10 mGy levels where air has 3 mGy, and never in the 5% level; planes with their own scaling render as the grid of the same doses; a lone plane is mapped grey by its levels; a grid that cannot be placed or levelled has a cine strip only; a block in a slice of a volume whose slices are closer than its projection's pixels and its render's voxels, which nearest-neighbour sampling alone would skip, changes both; and the pack, and a face review reading one, refuse a pixel data's digest unless previews that could show a face list its frames from the first with none missing, or for an instance listed as not previewed; a face review holds pixel data with more frames than its pack showed, and a run given one holds such an instance; a 12-frame CT instance labelled RTDOSE is not shown; an instance is an RT Dose by its SOP Class UID, or by a Modality of RTDOSE in any case, of several values (so `CT\MR` is one too), unreadable, not text, or with a character outside printable ASCII such as a leading NUL or a letter outside ASCII, and not by a missing or empty one; a face review holds an RT Dose with pixel data unless its dose levels showed every frame, and so always where it gives none, while one of dose-volume histograms alone needs none, and a run given a review that binds every frame of a dose grid, or of a CT instance labelled `RTDOSE`, `rtdose`, `RTDOSE\CT`, or `RTDOSE` after a NUL, through other previews, or through dose levels of fewer frames, holds it, and releases it through dose levels of every frame; and in two runs, a verdict of absent holds as `face-verdict-on-other-pixel-data` an instance beyond its cine strip, one longer than its frames, an unscaled grid, a grid longer than its frames, and a series of planes one of which is unscaled, and releases an instance whose every frame was shown, dose grids of 30 and 12 frames, a series of single-frame RT Dose planes, and a lone plane.

### D-018: Published benchmark results

- **Decision.**
  - For each release of the engine, publish results against the NCI MIDI validation resources as separate metrics rather than a single score, recording versions and supported coverage. Each implementation pull request adds its own tests and traceability, and M6 consolidates the evidence for the first supported release.
