Releases that change only documentation or legacy code claim no engine results.
  - Use the MIDI-B collection, with its synthetic and curated datasets, answer keys, and mapping files ([doi:10.7937/cf2p-aw56](https://doi.org/10.7937/cf2p-aw56)), and the Pseudo-PHI-DICOM-Data collection ([doi:10.7937/s17z-r072](https://doi.org/10.7937/s17z-r072)), both licensed CC BY 4.0, with the [NCI validation script](https://github.com/CBIIT/midi_validation_script) (Apache-2.0).
  - Benchmark runs download the collections. Tests may use small subsets cached through the PyMedPhys Zenodo data store, with attribution, the licence, and a note of changes. The validation script's repository is not copied into PyMedPhys, because it also bundles third-party executables.
  - The benchmark harness is run on the TCIA collections only on the maintainer's own machine, not in automated sessions, as the maintainer decided on 6 October 2026.
  - Report results per answer-key category, and document each category where PyMedPhys's policy deliberately differs from the answer key.
- **Rationale.** Separate, versioned metrics let users judge performance for their own data and coverage. The validation manual states that many answers follow TCIA's original curation of each source collection rather than strict DICOM requirements, so an aggregate score would penalise deliberate, conformant differences. CC BY 4.0 permits redistribution with attribution, and the collections are too large to keep in the repository.
- **Tests.** The benchmark workflow reproduces the published results, and cached subsets carry their attribution.

### D-019: No deprecation before a released replacement

- **Decision.**
  - Disclose the legacy tools' limitations now with warnings that are not deprecations and promise no removal. For experimental pseudonymisation, these are a `UserWarning` subclass in the library, a standard-error notice on the command line, and a banner in the app. For `pymedphys.dicom.anonymise`, they are corrected documentation and docstrings, a `UserWarning` subclass from the library function, and a standard-error notice on the command line.
  - Deprecate a legacy interface (library, command line, or app) only once a released replacement covers its use and migration guidance is published, which is no earlier than the first supported release (M6). That release replaces the library and the command line, not the app (Scope), so the legacy pseudonymisation app stays, with its limitation banner, until the new app ships.
  - Keep each legacy interface for at least one full minor release with deprecation warnings, then remove both in the same release. Record exact versions in the release notes when they are scheduled.
  - Until removal, change the legacy tools only to stop them disclosing identifying information or to prevent harm to data, such as silently altering or losing source files. List other defects among the limitations in the user guidance instead of fixing them.
- **Rationale.** Deprecating before a replacement exists would leave users nowhere to go and, if the work stalled, would make the warning permanent noise. If the replacement is not delivered, both legacy interfaces remain available with their documented limitations. Other fixes to the legacy tools are spent on code with a limited life, and can change behaviour that users rely on, as a new pseudonym format would; disclosure and data-safety fixes protect people and data until the replacement is available, which justifies that cost.
- **Tests.** Limitation warnings are not `DeprecationWarning`s and mention no removal; each public call warns once; and the command-line and app notices appear. Once deprecation starts, the deprecation warnings are tested and the migration guide is published.

### D-020: Compound actions from the strictest Type

- **Decision.**
  - A compound action of Table E.1-1a (X/Z, X/D, Z/D, X/Z/D, or X/Z/U*) is resolved from the strictest Type that the instance's IOD gives the attribute at that place in the data set, across every definition of it there (D-001). Type 1C counts as 1 and 2C as 2, and modules of conditional (C) and user-optional (U) usage are included, so no condition is evaluated.
  - The action is then D for Type 1, Z for Type 2, and X for Type 3. Where the compound action does not offer that action, the next of X, Z, and D that it offers applies, as the definitions in Table E.1-1a imply: X/D gives D for Type 2, as for RT Plan Date (300A,0006) in the RT Plan IOD, and Z/D gives Z for Type 3. In X/Z/U*, U, the replacement of the instance UIDs that the sequence contains, takes the place of D.
  - X/Z on an attribute that is Type 1 or 1C at its place writes D's dummy value, as a plain Z there does, which the maintainer decided on 1 October 2026. Table E.1-1a defines Z as "replace with a zero length value, or a non-zero length value that may be a dummy value and consistent with the VR", so this is the Z that X/Z offers, with a non-zero-length dummy value, and the conformance statement describes it so. In 2026d, no attribute with X/Z is Type 1 or 1C anywhere in the first supported release's 14 IODs. Of the other generated IODs, only Referenced Study Sequence (0008,1110) is, in three sequences of the RT Patient Position Acquisition Instruction and RT Physician Intent IODs; it is a sequence, which has no generic dummy value, so it needs a reviewed rule, and until one exists its instance is sequestered (D-021).
  - An attribute that the IOD does not define at that place is treated as Type 3, so it is removed, or emptied under Z/D.
  - The Type also decides how a plain X, D, or Z is applied; the maintainer decided these rules on 1 October 2026:
    - Where Table E.1-1 gives a plain X, the attribute is removed, as Table E.1-1a defines X, whatever its Type. Where the IOD requires it at that place, by its strictest Type as above, the engine keeps the output valid by also removing the innermost sequence that encloses it and that the IOD makes Type 3 at its own place, with everything in that sequence, and sequesters the instance where no such sequence encloses it. This includes an X that applies under Clean Descriptors where descriptor cleaning applies the Basic Profile's action (D-009). Two attributes need neither. Where Overlay Data (60xx,3000) is removed, every attribute of its repeating group is removed with it, since, of the first supported release's IODs, only CT Image, MR Image, and Positron Emission Tomography Image include the Overlay Plane Module, each as user-optional (U). ROI Interpreter Sequence (3006,004E) is removed, as the table says. PS3.3 Table C.8-44 makes it Type 1C: "Required if ROI Creator Sequence (3006,004D) is present in the Structure Set Module, and the person or device performing the interpretation differs from ROI Creator Sequence (3006,004D). May be present otherwise." Table E.1-1 removes ROI Creator Sequence too, so that condition never holds in the output.
    - The basis is Table E.1-1a, which defines X as removal and gives an alternative by Type only through its compound actions, such as X/Z and X/D, and E.1.1's Note, which makes it "the responsibility of the de-identifier to ensure that this process does not negatively affect the integrity of the Information Object Definition". Removing an optional sequence that encloses the attribute does both: the attribute is removed, as the table says, and the IOD stays valid, so the instance keeps an exact Basic Profile claim. For example, Responsible Person (0010,2297) is Type 2C in the Patient Module, "Required if the Patient is a non-human organism. May be present otherwise." Since conditions are not evaluated, it counts as required there, and no sequence encloses it, so an instance that has it is sequestered, as is one with Responsible Organization (0010,2299); both are rare outside veterinary data.
    - In 2026d, the first supported release's IODs have eight attributes with a plain X that the IOD requires and that no removed sequence encloses: Series Description (0008,103E), Type 1 in Source Series Information Sequence (3006,004C) of the RT Structure Set IOD; ROI Interpreter Sequence, Type 1C in RT ROI Observations Sequence (3006,0080) of the RT Structure Set IOD; Responsible Person (0010,2297) and Responsible Organization (0010,2299), Type 2C in the Patient Module of each IOD; Patient Treatment Preparation Procedure Parameter Description (300A,078E), Type 2 in Patient Treatment Preparation Procedure Sequence (300A,0790), and Patient Setup Photo Description (300A,0794), Type 2 in Referenced Patient Setup Photo Sequence (300A,078C), both sequences within Patient Treatment Preparation Sequence (300A,079F), at the top level of each image IOD other than MR Spectroscopy and within Patient Setup Sequence (300A,0180) of the RT Plan IOD; Overlay Data, Type 1 in the Overlay Plane Module of the CT Image, MR Image, and Positron Emission Tomography Image IODs; and Radiopharmaceutical Start DateTime (0018,1078), Type 1 in the Type 1 Radiopharmaceutical Information Sequence (0054,0016) of the Enhanced PET Image IOD. Source Series Information Sequence and Referenced Patient Setup Photo Sequence are Type 3 wherever these IODs define them, and Patient Treatment Preparation Sequence is Type 3 at the top level of those image IODs and within Patient Setup Sequence, so Series Description and the two descriptions are removed with the sequence that holds them; Overlay Data and ROI Interpreter Sequence are removed as above; and an instance with Responsible Person or Responsible Organization is sequestered. So is every Enhanced PET Image instance that conforms to its IOD, under both presets of the first supported release, since no Type 3 sequence encloses Radiopharmaceutical Start DateTime there; Table E.1-1 gives it C under Retain Longitudinal Temporal Information with Modified Dates, which those presets do not select.
    - A plain D on an attribute that the IOD does not define at that place acts as X there, and a plain Z there stays Z, following Note 13 after Table E.1-1a: Verifying Observer Sequence (0040,A073) "is only defined in structured report IODs and hence is described in Table E.1-1 as D since it is Type 1C; if encountered in an image instance, it should simply be removed (treated as X)".
    - A plain Z on an attribute that is Type 1 or 1C at that place writes D's dummy value, which Table E.1-1a's definition of Z allows: "replace with a zero length value, or a non-zero length value that may be a dummy value and consistent with the VR". In 2026d, no IOD of the first supported release has such a place.
    - Clinical Trial Protocol Ethics Committee Name (0012,0081) is removed, although Table E.1-1 gives it D, or K under Retain Institution Identity, which the maintainer decided on 10 October 2026. PS3.3 makes it Type 1C in the Clinical Trial Subject Module of each IOD of the first supported release: "Required if Clinical Trial Protocol Ethics Committee Approval Number (0012,0082) is present". Table E.1-1 gives the approval number X under the Basic Profile and every option, so that condition never holds in the output, and PS3.5 Section 7.4.2 then does not allow the name, since its condition does not say that it may be present otherwise. With ROI Interpreter Sequence, it is one of the two attributes whose condition lapses in this way in every instance; each is removed alone, whatever its action. The conditions of the Common Instance Reference Module's two sequences lapse only in an instance that de-identification leaves with no other reference to an instance, which depends on its content, so D-022 removes them only there.
  - Z and D write the values that D-021 sets.
- **Rationale.** The strictest Type never removes an attribute that the IOD may require, so compound actions never make the output invalid, and it never retains more, because Z and D still replace the value. The cost is occasionally writing an empty or dummy value where removal was allowed. Evaluating conditions was rejected: PS3.3 writes them as free text, so an interpreter would be hand-built and error-prone, and it would gain almost no privacy. Removing every attribute that is not Type 1 was rejected because removing a Type 2 attribute makes the object invalid, which breaks import into planning systems. A plain X is applied differently: Table E.1-1a defines X as removal, so where the IOD requires the attribute, removing an optional sequence that encloses it keeps both the table's action and the IOD's integrity, and the rare instance where no such sequence exists is sequestered. Writing an empty or dummy value in place of a plain X was rejected because it is not the removal that the table specifies, so the exact Basic Profile claim would rest on reading E.1.1's general permission to replace a value as overriding the table, which the standard does not state. Leaving such instances out of the conformance claim was rejected for the same reason: removal keeps the claim for every instance that it can keep valid. Removing the whole overlay group leaves a valid CT, MR, or PET image, since the Overlay Plane Module is optional in each, and removing ROI Interpreter Sequence leaves a valid RT Structure Set once ROI Creator Sequence is gone. Clinical Trial Protocol Ethics Committee Name is removed because D, or K, would make every instance that has it invalid once the approval number is gone, as `dciodvfy` reports (D-030), and removing it discards only a dummy value, or a name that the Basic Profile does not keep; keeping D was rejected because no instance with the name could then conform to its IOD. The Common Instance Reference Module's two sequences are removed only where their conditions can be seen to have lapsed from the data set's structure: an instance whose remaining references all name instances of other studies keeps a Referenced Series Sequence whose condition does not hold, since telling which study a reference names needs the UIDs' values, which the engine's plan does not use, and D-022 accepts that trade-off. Instances keep the exact Basic Profile claim: removing the name keeps less than D does, E.1.1's Note makes keeping the IOD intact the de-identifier's responsibility, and Note 13 after Table E.1-1a already treats a D given to a Type 1C attribute as X where the attribute is not allowed. An attribute that the IOD does not define at that place needs no value, so D removes it, as Note 13 describes, and Z, which writes nothing identifying, is kept as the table gives it. A plain Z on a Type 1 attribute writes a dummy value because an empty value would make the output invalid. Table E.1-1a defines X/Z as "X unless Z is required to maintain IOD conformance (Type 3 versus Type 2)", so it does not say what X/Z does at a Type 1 place; the Z it offers allows a dummy value, as a plain Z does, so X/Z there writes one too. Sequestering the instance instead was rejected, where the attribute's VR has a dummy value (D-021), because it would discard an instance that the profile can de-identify.
- **Tests.** Each compound action gives the specified action for Types 1, 1C, 2, 2C, and 3; an attribute with different Types in different modules, including modules of conditional or user-optional usage, gets the action of its strictest Type; the Type depends on the enclosing sequence; an attribute that the IOD does not define at that place is removed, or emptied under Z/D; X/Z on a Type 1 or 1C attribute writes the dummy value, and does so in the generated IODs only for Referenced Study Sequence in the three sequences named above; and no Type 1 or Type 2 attribute with a compound action is removed. A plain X removes the attribute, and where the IOD requires it at that place, also the innermost enclosing sequence that is Type 3 at its own place, as for Series Description in Source Series Information Sequence and the two Patient Treatment Preparation descriptions, or sequesters the instance where there is none, as for Responsible Person and Responsible Organization, and for Radiopharmaceutical Start DateTime in Enhanced PET Image; removing Overlay Data removes its whole repeating group, and ROI Interpreter Sequence is removed; every plain action on Clinical Trial Protocol Ethics Committee Name removes it, and an instance that loses it with its approval number keeps every attribute its IOD requires; a plain D on an attribute that the IOD does not define at that place, such as Verifying Observer Sequence in a CT image, removes it, and a plain Z there empties it; and a plain Z on a Type 1 or 1C attribute writes the dummy value.

### D-021: Dummy values

- **Decision.**
  - Z writes a zero-length value, except where a dummy value is already decided: the keyed pseudonyms for Patient ID and Patient's Name (D-005), the synthetic birth dates of `tps-import` (D-008), and D's dummy value where a plain Z, or the Z that X/Z offers, applies to an attribute that is Type 1 or 1C at its place (D-020).
  - D writes one conspicuous constant for each VR, valid for that VR: `DEIDENTIFIED` for the text VRs (LO, SH, LT, ST, UC, and UT) and PN; `19000101` for DA; `000000` for TM; `19000101000000` for DT; zero for the numeric string VRs (DS and IS) and the binary number VRs (FL, FD, SL, SS, SV, UL, US, and UV); and the keyed replacement for UI (D-003). Where the constant equals the source value, a second constant is used, so the value always changes.
  - The second constants are `DE-IDENTIFIED`, `19000102`, `000001`, `19000101000001`, and one. A source value equals a constant when the two match as their VR defines them: text and PN ignoring case and padding, and a PN's trailing `^` and `=` delimiters; DA, TM, and DT as the same date or time, ignoring a DT's UTC offset and taking absent components at their least values; and the numeric VRs as the same number, so `0.0` equals zero. Equality is deliberately broad, since a value treated as equal still changes. Where any of an attribute's source values equals the constant, the second is used. D writes the fewest values that the attribute's VM allows, each the constant, and for UI the replacement of each source UID, even one that the pinned tables register, since D must replace the value.
  - CS, SQ, and every other VR have no generic dummy value, nor has a UI attribute without a source UID to replace. A D on an attribute of such a VR needs a reviewed rule for that attribute; without one, the instance is sequestered rather than given an invalid value.
  - Person Identification Code Sequence (0040,1101), which Table E.1-1 gives D, has such a rule, which the maintainer decided on 1 October 2026. D writes one item, with Code Value (0008,0100) `DEIDENTIFIED`, Coding Scheme Designator (0008,0102) `99PYMEDPHYS`, and Code Meaning (0008,0104) `DEIDENTIFIED^DEIDENTIFIED`. Where a source item's Code Value or Code Meaning equals its constant, compared as above, the item takes the second constants, `DE-IDENTIFIED` and `DE-IDENTIFIED^DE-IDENTIFIED`, with the same designator. In the first supported release's IODs, the only place where Table E.1-1 removes no sequence that encloses it is Asserter Identification Sequence (0044,0103) in RT Assertions Sequence (0044,0110) of the RT Plan IOD, where it is Type 2C.
  - In 2026d, two other attributes of the first supported release's IODs resolve to D on a VR without a generic dummy value. Referenced Performed Procedure Step Sequence (0008,1111), which Table E.1-1 gives X/Z/D, is Type 1C in the series module of each of the eight multi-frame IODs, "Required if a Performed Procedure Step SOP Class was involved in the creation of this Series": the CT Series Module (PS3.3 Section C.8.15.1) of Enhanced CT Image and Legacy Converted Enhanced CT Image, the MR Series Module (Section C.8.13.6) of Enhanced MR Image, Enhanced MR Color Image, Legacy Converted Enhanced MR Image, and MR Spectroscopy, and the Enhanced PET Series Module (Section C.8.22.1) of Enhanced PET Image and Legacy Converted Enhanced PET Image. ICC Profile (0028,2000), which D-022 gives X/Z/D, is Type 1 in the ICC Profile Module of the Enhanced CT Image, Enhanced MR Image, and Enhanced MR Color Image IODs, which is mandatory in Enhanced MR Color Image and user-optional in the other two. Each has a reviewed rule, which the maintainer chose on 7 October 2026:
    - In Referenced Performed Procedure Step Sequence, D writes one item for each source item, with Referenced SOP Class UID (0008,1150) `1.2.840.10008.3.1.2.3.3`, the Modality Performed Procedure Step SOP Class, and Referenced SOP Instance UID (0008,1155) the keyed replacement of the source item's (D-003). Where the sequence has no item, or an item has no Referenced SOP Instance UID, there is nothing to replace, and the instance is sequestered.
    - In place of ICC Profile, D writes a fixed ICC version 2.4 input profile (ICC.1:2001-04) of the sRGB colour space of IEC 61966-2.1, a matrix and tone curve profile. PS3.3 Section C.11.15.1.1 requires ICC Profile to be an input device profile whose input colour space is RGB, whatever the image's Photometric Interpretation, so only the source profile's header is read, for its data colour space, and only an RGB profile is replaced. Its description is `DEIDENTIFIED sRGB`, or `DE-IDENTIFIED sRGB` where the source has the same bytes, and its copyright is `No copyright, use freely`; its header names no device, platform, or creator, and gives 1 January 1900 as its date, and its media white point is D50. Where the source is not an ICC profile, or is one of another colour space, such as a grey profile, which does not conform there, the instance is sequestered. Color Space (0028,2002), which names the colour space of the profile, is removed under every option (D-022), so it cannot contradict the profile written. Pixel values are preserved, but colour rendering is not: replacing the source profile's colorants and tone curves can substantially change colour and brightness in a colour-managed viewer. For example, a source profile with sRGB primaries and a linear tone curve shows a stored neutral value of 128 as about 188 on an sRGB display, and the fixed profile shows it as 128. Keeping the source profile's colorimetric tags while removing its text tags, private tags, and the identifying fields and date of its header would keep that fidelity; it needs a parser of version 2 and version 4 profiles, and is future development, which the maintainer asked on 7 October 2026 to be recorded.
  - Dummy values never name PyMedPhys; the markers do (D-012). The one exception is the Coding Scheme Designator `99PYMEDPHYS` of the item above, which the maintainer chose on 1 October 2026: it names who defines the code, not the person, and the item's Code Value and Code Meaning are the constants. The conformance statement describes each dummy value, as E.1.3 requires.
- **Rationale.** Constants are obviously synthetic, reveal nothing, are deterministic, and are always valid for their VR, and a zero-length value carries the least information and is always valid for Type 2. Plausible random values were rejected because they can be mistaken for real data, may match real values, and are not reproducible. Keyed values derived from the source were rejected because equal source values would give equal dummy values, which leaks equality and links records. Dummy values never name PyMedPhys because they sit in clinical fields, where they read as data: `DEIDENTIFIED` means the same to any reader, and the markers are where the de-identifier describes itself. The Coding Scheme Designator of the dummy code is a narrow exception, because a private coding scheme's designator conventionally identifies who defines its codes. PS3.16 Chapter 8 requires a local or private coding scheme to be identified by an alphanumeric designator beginning with "99", as HL7 V2 Table 0396 specifies, and PS3.3 Section 8.2 notes that HL7 V2 defines such designators as private or local; the rest of a private designator conventionally names the scheme's owner. `99PYMEDPHYS`, 11 of the 16 characters that SH allows, therefore says which organisation defined the code. It is metadata about the code, not content that reads as data about the person, and the Code Value and Code Meaning stay the conspicuous constants. The item follows PS3.3 Table 10-1, the Person Identification Macro, which requires "One or more Items" in the sequence and lets Code Meaning follow the rules of PN, "except that a single component (i.e., the whole name unseparated by caret delimiters) is not permitted", so the constant has two components. At the place above, in Asserter Identification Sequence, the Assertion Macro (PS3.3 Table 10.30-1) defines the sequence instead through Table C.17-3b, the Identified Person or Device Macro, as Type 2C with "Zero or one Item shall be included in this Sequence"; the one item satisfies both macros. The procedure step's items take the keyed replacement rather than a constant, the deliberate exception to rejecting keyed values that applies to every UID, as for U, because Referenced SOP Instance UID is a reference, which Table E.1-1 replaces (U) wherever else it occurs, so the replacement is the same as any other reference to that step would get, and no constant UID is shared between unrelated series; the UID is the only value read, and the class is fixed to the Modality Performed Procedure Step SOP Class, which a modality creates for the series it acquires, so nothing else of the source item is written. An ICC profile is a file in another format whose text, such as its description, copyright, and device and creator fields, the engine does not parse, so none of it is kept; the fixed profile keeps the instance valid, since ICC Profile is Type 1 where D applies, and sRGB, a standard RGB colour space in wide use, keeps the colours plausible. A grey source profile does not conform to PS3.3 Section C.11.15.1.1, and a grey replacement would not either, so it is sequestered rather than given a profile that would be invalid there, as are other colour spaces. Copying the source profile's colorants and curves was rejected because they can identify a device, and would need the profile to be parsed.
- **Tests.** Z writes a zero-length value except for the decided pseudonyms and synthetic birth dates, and D's dummy value where the attribute is Type 1 or 1C; D writes its VR's constant, or the second constant where the source value equals the first in any form, such as padded, in another case, or `0.0` for DS; every dummy value is valid for its VR and its attribute's VM; different source values give the same dummy value; a D on an attribute of another VR without a reviewed rule sequesters the instance; D on Person Identification Code Sequence writes one item with the constants above, or with the second constants where a source item's Code Value or Code Meaning equals the first, each valid for its VR, and the written file holds that item; D on Referenced Performed Procedure Step Sequence writes one item for each source item, with the keyed replacement of its UID, and sequesters the instance where there is no UID to replace; and D on ICC Profile writes the fixed profile of the source's colour space, which LittleCMS reads, whose colorants sum to its white point, and whose tone curve is that of sRGB, the second description where the source has the same bytes, and sequesters the instance for any other colour space.

### D-022: Text and other attributes that Table E.1-1 omits

- **Decision.**
  - A reviewed L2 list gives an action, attribute by attribute, to each text attribute (VR LO, SH, LT, ST, UC, or UT) that Table E.1-1 omits and a supported IOD uses. In 2026d, the first supported release's 14 IODs use 141. They range from coded vocabulary, such as Code Value, Coding Scheme Designator, and Code Meaning; through equipment descriptions, such as Manufacturer, Software Versions, and Convolution Kernel; to operator-typed RT text, such as Beam Name, Block Name, Patient Setup Label, Dose Comment, Setup Image Comment, and Text Value (0040,A160); accessory identifiers, such as Wedge ID, Block Tray ID, Applicator ID, Compensator ID, and General Accessory ID; and accessory codes that a device reads, such as Accessory Code.
  - The list puts each attribute in one of three groups, apart from the stricter rules below:
    - operator-typed labels and text: X/Z/D, resolved by Type (D-020), and C under Clean Descriptors (D-009). In 2026d, there are 62.
    - accessory and equipment identifiers, treated as device identity: X/Z/D, and K under Retain Device Identity. In 2026d, there are 24.
    - coded and technical values: K. In 2026d, there are 37: codes and the vocabulary that qualifies them, Code Value, Long Code Value, Coding Scheme Designator, Coding Scheme Version, Coding Scheme Registry, Coding Scheme External ID, and Code Meaning; values that the standard constrains, Strain Nomenclature, Genetic Modifications Nomenclature, Rescale Type, Overlay Subtype, Fraction Pattern, and Device Alternate Identifier Format; values that equipment or software writes, Manufacturer, Manufacturer's Model Name, Manufacturer's Model Version, Software Versions, Algorithm Version, Convolution Kernel, Filter Type, Fluence Mode ID, and Source Isotope Name; and the identifiers of private data elements that software writes, Private Creator Reference, Private Data Element Name, Private Data Element Keyword, Selector Sequence Pointer Private Creator, and Selector Attribute Private Creator; and ten that only the CT, MR, and PET IODs use, below. Free text that a person types, such as a comment, label, or description, is never in this group.
  - Names and identifiers of people, organisations, records, and networks, and identifiers that a device reads, have a rule stricter than that of operator-typed text: X/Z/D, resolved by Type, under every option, with no action under Clean Descriptors or Retain Device Identity. It is chosen because these values name people, organisations, records, or networks, or, read by a device such as a bar-code reader, identify accessories often made for one patient, rather than describe content as descriptors do. In 2026d, there are 13: Coding Scheme Responsible Organization (0008,0116), Mapping Resource Name (0008,0122), Strain Stock Number (0010,0214), Strain Source (0010,0217), Breed Registration Number (0010,2295), NTP Source Address (0018,1803), Algorithm Source (0024,0202), Local Namespace Entity ID (0040,0031), Universal Entity ID (0040,0032), HL7 Instance Identifier (0040,E001), Storage Media File-set ID (0088,0130), and the accessory identifiers that a device reads, Accessory Code (300A,00F9) and Tray Accessory Code (300A,0355).
  - The text of overlays, Overlay Description (60xx,0022) and Overlay Label (60xx,1500), has the same stricter rule, because Table E.1-1 cleans overlay text only under Clean Graphics, under which the engine removes it until a cleaner of overlays exists (D-041). With the two alternative calendar dates and De-identification Method, below, the three groups (62, 24, and 37) and these 15 make the 141.
  - Every rule carries a note giving the reason for its action.
  - Of the 141, 31 are used only by the ten CT, MR, and PET IODs that the maintainer added to the first supported release on 7 October 2026 (D-010), and not by CT Image or the RT IODs. These IODs are Enhanced CT Image, Legacy Converted Enhanced CT Image, MR Image, Enhanced MR Image, Enhanced MR Color Image, Legacy Converted Enhanced MR Image, MR Spectroscopy, Positron Emission Tomography Image, Enhanced PET Image, and Legacy Converted Enhanced PET Image. Sixteen are operator-typed labels and text, among them Sequence Name, which PS3.3 calls user-defined, the free-text names of radiopharmaceuticals and intervention drugs and their routes, whose code sequences carry them as codes, the arterial spin labelling descriptions, Dimension Description Label, and the PET correction and reconstruction methods, which the scanner usually writes but PS3.3 defines as free text. Five are device identity: the names of the receive and transmit coils, which a site can choose for its own coils, the coils' manufacturers, which can be a site that builds its own, and the names of a multi-element coil's elements, which a site can choose in the same way. Ten are coded and technical values: Imaged Nucleus, Cardiac Framing Type, Collimator/Grid Name, Pulse Sequence Name, which PS3.3 says may be specific to the vendor, unlike the user-defined Sequence Name, Applicable Safety Standard Description, which names a published standard, Image Filter, written like Convolution Kernel, Stack ID and Frame Label, which name groups of frames, which Dimension Index Pointer can name, and of which Stack ID is required in Enhanced PET, so that changing them would break how frames are organised, and the private creators that Dimension Index Private Creator and Functional Group Private Creator name. Keeping Pulse Sequence Name, Stack ID, and Frame Label accepts the residual risk that a site writes a name in them, since PS3.3 does not say who writes them.
  - Patient's Birth Date in Alternative Calendar (0010,0033) and Patient's Death Date in Alternative Calendar (0010,0034) are among the 141: their VR is LO, but they hold dates. They are removed (X), whatever group rule would otherwise apply. Each is Type 3 in the Patient Module of the first supported release's IODs.
  - De-identification Method (0012,0063), which Table E.1-1 also omits, is among the 141 too. The engine writes it itself: it keeps the existing values and adds its own, as D-012 sets.
  - A text attribute that the list does not cover is removed by default: X/Z/D, resolved by Type.
  - The maintainer decided on 1 October 2026 the rules for the other attributes that Table E.1-1 omits:
    - Of the 12 URI and URL attributes, of VR UR, that the table omits, URN Code Value (0008,0120), which holds a code's value where that value is a URN or URL, and Coding Scheme URL (0008,010E) are kept (K), like Code Value and Coding Scheme Designator, since they name coding concepts and schemes. The maintainer decided this on 1 October 2026. URN Code Value is Type 1C in the Basic Code Sequence Macro (PS3.3 Table 8.8-1a), and Coding Scheme URL is Type 1 in the items of Coding Scheme Resources Sequence (0008,0109), which is Type 3 within the Type 3 Coding Scheme Identification Sequence (0008,0110) of the SOP Common Module.
    - Each of the other ten gets X/Z/D, resolved by Type. The first supported release's IODs use three of them: Retrieve URL (0008,1190), Pixel Data Provider URL (0028,7FE0), and Retrieve URI (0040,E010). The other seven are Stored Instance Base URI (0008,0407), Folder Access URI (0008,0408), File Access URI (0008,0409), Filename in Container (0008,040B), Storage URL (0040,4073), Relative URI Reference Within Encapsulated Document (0068,7005), and Contact URI (0074,100A).
    - Seven sequences whose content can identify an institution or other patients or say something about the patient get X/Z/D, resolved by Type: Requesting Service Code Sequence (0032,1034), the "Institutional department, unit or service where the request initiated" (PS3.3 Table C.7-3, General Study Module); Patient's Size Code Sequence (0010,1021) (Table C.7-4a, Patient Study Module); Issuer of Patient ID Qualifiers Sequence (0010,0024) (Table 10-18, the Issuer of Patient ID Macro, which the Patient Module includes), which holds Assigning Facility Sequence (0040,0036), Assigning Jurisdiction Code Sequence (0040,0039), and Assigning Agency or Department Code Sequence (0040,003A); Issuer of Accession Number Sequence (0008,0051) (Table C.7-3); Reason For Performed Procedure Code Sequence (0040,1012) (Table C.7-3); and Source Patient Group Identification Sequence (0010,0026) and Group of Patients Identification Sequence (0010,0027) (Table C.7-1, Patient Module).
    - ICC Profile (0028,2000) and MAC Parameters Sequence (4FFE,0001) get X/Z/D, resolved by Type, and so does Color Space (0028,2002), which names the colour space of the ICC profile and would otherwise be kept as a code string, since the profile is removed or replaced under every option and a kept Color Space could contradict the profile written.
    - Patient's Body Mass Index (0010,1022), Measured AP Dimension (0010,1023), and Measured Lateral Dimension (0010,1024), measures of the patient's body like Patient's Size (0010,1020) and Patient's Weight (0010,1030), which Table E.1-1 removes (X), get X/Z/D, resolved by Type. They are Type 3 in the Patient Study Module (PS3.3 Table C.7-4a), so they are removed in the first supported release's IODs under every option, including Retain Patient Characteristics, as Patient's Size Code Sequence is.
    - Series Number (0020,0011), a number that identifies a series, gets X/Z/D, resolved by Type, under every option, and wherever Z or D applies, it takes its rank among the distinct Series Numbers of the study that holds the series it numbers, as the maintainer decided on 10 October 2026. The run's first pass records each Series Number that an instance holds where its IOD defines one, with the Series Instance UID of the series it numbers, the instance's own at the top level and the item's own in an item, and finds the study of that series among the run's instances; each Series Number takes the numbering of all the values that number a series of that study. Each distinct number, read as one Integer String (PS3.5 Section 6.2), takes its rank in ascending order from 1, so equal numbers share a rank and the ranks have no gaps. A value that is empty, is not one Integer String, or has no rank, as where the series it numbers has no instance in the run or has instances in several studies, is emptied under Z and given the dummy value of D-021 under D. In every IOD of the first supported release, Series Number is Type 2 in the General Series or RT Series Module, where Z applies, and Type 1 only in the items of Source Series Information Sequence (3006,004C) of the Structure Set Module, where D applies; an item that names a series of the run, of whichever study, takes the rank that the series' own instances take, and one that names a series that the run does not hold takes the dummy value, since a rank in another study's numbering would number that series differently from its own instances. The numbering spans one run, as the values that a run's ephemeral key generates do (D-004), so a study de-identified in two runs can number its series differently. A project key, whose values stay consistent across runs, would need the study's numbering kept with its subject profile, or every series of the study in each run, for a series to keep its number.
    - The seven sequences and MAC Parameters Sequence are Type 3 wherever the first supported release's IODs define them, so each is removed there, as are Retrieve URL and Retrieve URI, which those IODs require only inside sequences that Table E.1-1 removes. ICC Profile is Type 3 there too, except at the top level of the Enhanced CT Image, Enhanced MR Image, and Enhanced MR Color Image IODs, where it is Type 1, so D replaces it there with the fixed profile of D-021's reviewed rule. The third, Pixel Data Provider URL, is Type 1C in the Image Pixel Module of RT Dose and of each image IOD of the first supported release, all but MR Spectroscopy, and the module requires it only for the JPIP Referenced transfer syntaxes, which the first supported release does not read. There X/Z/D resolves to D, for which D-021 gives UR no dummy value, so an instance with such a value there is sequestered unless a reviewed rule gives one.
    - Any other attribute of the pinned dictionary that no rule covers, after Table E.1-1, these rules, the UID roles (D-003), the rule for text above, and the engine's own removals and writes (D-012, D-013), is kept if its VR is CS, AT, or a numeric VR (DS, IS, FL, FD, SL, SS, SV, UL, US, or UV). A sequence, or a binary value of VR OB, OD, OF, OL, OV, OW, or UN, is kept only where the IOD defines the attribute at that place, and removed elsewhere, so that a sequence that no supported IOD defines, such as Discharge Diagnosis Code Sequence (0038,0044), cannot keep the codes in its items. The attributes in a kept sequence's items are handled by the same rules. Such an attribute of any other VR is removed; in 2026d there is none, since every AE and AS attribute is in Table E.1-1. An attribute absent from the pinned dictionary is removed, as Architecture item 2 sets.
  - The maintainer decided on 6 October 2026 how the first supported release treats an institution's name in local codes and private creators. Codes of a local coding scheme, whose Coding Scheme Designator begins with "99" or is "L", which PS3.3 Section 8.2 says HL7 V2 defines as private or local Coding Schemes, keep their Code Value, Coding Scheme Designator, and Code Meaning, like every other code, although they can carry an institution's name or abbreviation. The conformance statement says so, and says that each such value is listed among the QC pack's retained strings (D-017). Private Data Element Characteristics Sequence (0008,0300), whose items hold Private Creator Reference (0008,0302), is removed whenever no private attribute is kept (D-028). Selector Attribute Private Creator and Selector Sequence Pointer Private Creator are kept, as above.
  - The Common Instance Reference Module's two sequences, Referenced Series Sequence (0008,1115) and Studies Containing Other Referenced Instances Sequence (0008,1200), are kept by the rule for sequences above, with their UIDs replaced (D-003), except that both are removed, with their items, where the instance keeps no other reference to an instance: no Referenced SOP Instance UID (0008,1155) or Multi-frame Source SOP Instance UID (0008,1167) with a value, in an item of any sequence but theirs, other than one that names a study in the items of Referenced Study Sequence (0008,1110) or RT Referenced Study Sequence (3006,0012). PS3.3 Section C.12.2 makes both Type 1C, "Required if this Instance references Instances in this Study" and "Required if this Instance references Instances in other Studies", without "May be present otherwise", so PS3.5 Section 7.4.2 does not allow them once the Basic Profile has removed every reference that met those conditions, as X/Z/U* removes Referenced Image Sequence (0008,1140) and Source Image Sequence (0008,2112) where they are Type 3 (D-020). Where a reference remains, both are kept. The dummy item that D writes for Referenced Performed Procedure Step Sequence (0008,1111) counts as one (D-021). Every IOD of the first supported release includes the module as user-optional, so removing both sequences leaves an instance without the module, which its IOD allows. The check of each written instance against its IOD (D-020) allows their removal only where the output holds no such reference with a value.
- **Rationale.** E.1.1 leaves the de-identifier responsible for identifying information in these attributes. The list is auditable and consistent with how Table E.1-1 treats similar attributes: RT Plan Label is D, RT Plan Name and Beam Description X, and ROI Name Z, all C under Clean Descriptors, and Treatment Machine Name is X/Z, and K under Retain Device Identity. Removing every omitted text attribute that is not listed as safe was rejected: it would replace Code Value (0008,0100), which is Type 1C in the Basic Code Sequence Macro (PS3.3 Table 8.8-1a), and so destroy coded meaning, and it would strip technical values that are useful and not identifying, such as Convolution Kernel. Keeping them all was rejected because it fails E.1.1: beam names and comments often contain patient names and dates. Accessory identifiers are not kept in the first supported release, whose presets do not select Retain Device Identity; `tps-import` keeps them as typed, including those of accessories usually made for one patient, such as Compensator ID. Names and identifiers of people, organisations, records, and networks are removed under every option, as Table E.1-1 removes comparable attributes, such as Operators' Name, Clinical Trial Sponsor Name, Issuer of Patient ID, and Container Identifier, under every option, and keeps Institution Name only under Retain Institution Identity, which retains the institution where the acquisition was performed, not the other organisations that these attributes name (D-044); Mapping Resource Name is among them because PS3.3 Section 8.4 says it may denote the institution or organisation that specified the value set. Accessory Code and Tray Accessory Code are removed under every option because PS3.3 describes each as an identifier intended to be read by a device such as a bar-code reader, nearly as it describes Device Alternate Identifier (3010,001B), which Table E.1-1 empties under every option, including Retain Device Identity. Table E.1-1 at most cleans network identities, such as Station AE Title, even under Retain Device Identity, so NTP Source Address is removed under every option; and it cleans Overlay Comments only under Clean Graphics, under which the engine removes it (D-041), so Overlay Description and Overlay Label are removed under every option too. Manufacturer, Manufacturer's Model Name, and Software Versions are kept because they identify products, which every site that uses them shares, rather than devices: Table E.1-1 omits them while listing Device Serial Number, and Manufacturer is Type 1 in Contributing Equipment Sequence (PS3.3 Table C.12-1). A device made in-house can carry the hospital as its Manufacturer. That residual risk is accepted because the value names an institution, not a patient: `public-release` reviews every distinct retained string (D-017), but the first supported release's presets keep such a value without review. A URI or URL can carry host names and file paths, so the UR attributes are removed by Type, as the Basic Profile removes or replaces the three UR attributes that Table E.1-1 lists, such as Display URI (0040,E012). URN Code Value and Coding Scheme URL are the exceptions: they name coding concepts and schemes, as Code Value and Coding Scheme Designator do, and removing them where they are required would sequester an instance, since UR has no dummy value, although Coding Scheme URL is often the public URL of a published coding scheme. A local scheme's URL can name an institution's host; that residual risk is accepted, as for Manufacturer, because it names an institution, not a patient. The first five of the seven sequences hold codes or identifiers like those of attributes that Table E.1-1 removes or empties: it gives Requesting Service (0032,1033), Patient's Size (0010,1020), and Issuer of Patient ID (0010,0021) X, Accession Number (0008,0050) Z, and Reason for Visit Code Sequence (0032,1067) and the other reason and diagnosis code sequences X. Table E.1-1 keeps Patient's Size under Retain Patient Characteristics, which `tps-import` selects, whereas Patient's Size Code Sequence is removed under every option. The difference is deliberate: E.3.7 requires the option's characteristics to be retained "as described in Table E.1-1", which does not list the sequence, so removing it is consistent with the option, and Architecture item 2 lets a rule give an action under an option only to a text attribute or to a date, time, or datetime. The other two, Source Patient Group Identification Sequence and Group of Patients Identification Sequence, hold Patient IDs other than the instance's own, of the subjects in an instance that holds a group of subjects acquired together, and of the group's source instances, from which an instance was extracted (PS3.3 Table C.7-1); removing them avoids deciding pseudonyms for patients outside the export. MAC Parameters Sequence describes the digital signatures that Table E.1-1 removes with Digital Signatures Sequence (FFFA,FFFA), and ICC Profile holds a file in another format, whose contents the engine does not parse. Series Number is a number like those that the rule for attributes that no rule covers keeps, but nothing constrains what is written there beyond its VR: in MIDI-B's synthetic Validation collection, most released instances held a date of eight digits as their whole Series Number, and the residual search does not search numbers (D-027), so keeping it released the date. Table E.1-1 empties Study ID (0020,0010), the study's own number (Z), and Z and D each allow a dummy value consistent with the VR (Table E.1-1a), as the pseudonyms of Patient ID and Patient's Name are (D-005), so the rank replaces the number: it keeps the order of the series within a study, by which viewers sort them and people name them, and carries nothing else of the source value. Emptying it was rejected because it loses that order, and the Basic Profile removes Series Description (0008,103E) too, so the series of a study could then be told apart only by their content and UIDs. Emptying only values that read as dates was rejected: it would be the only action that depends on what a value reads as, and would miss an identifier of another shape. Ranking needs the study's other instances, which the first pass already reads before anything is written; an instance de-identified outside a run ranks only the values that number its own series. Any other attribute is kept because Table E.1-1 "defines the minimum actions required for conformance" (Note 5 after Table E.1-1a), and PS3.15 notes that "it is usually safe to assume that code strings do not contain identifying information" (Note 9), and that the same is usually true of coded sequence entries, except codes for providers and staff (Note 10). Keeping a sequence requires "recursively applying the Profile rules to each Data Set in each Item of the Sequence" (Table E.1-1a), so its items get the same rules. A binary value of an attribute that the IOD does not define at that place has no meaning there that the engine can check, so it is removed. The Common Instance Reference Module's sequences are removed only where no reference remains because that can be decided from the data set's structure, as the engine's plan is made; telling whether a remaining reference names an instance of this study or of another needs the UIDs' values, so where one remains both are kept, and an instance whose remaining references all name instances of other studies keeps a Referenced Series Sequence whose condition does not hold. The dummy item of Referenced Performed Procedure Step Sequence counts as a reference because PS3.3's "references Instances" does not restrict itself to composite instances; the module cannot list a procedure step, so counting it only keeps the module where it may have lapsed, as before this rule, and never removes one that is needed. Removing both wherever dciodvfy reports them was rejected: its condition counts only Source Image, Source Instance, Referenced Image, and Referenced Instance Sequences and a few others, while PS3.3's counts a reference from any module, such as Referenced Image Evidence Sequence (0008,9092) or Referenced Defined Protocol Sequence (0018,990C), so removing them there would make the output invalid.
- **Tests.** Every text attribute that Table E.1-1 omits and a supported IOD uses has a rule with a note, and a regenerated table that adds one fails to load until it has a rule; each of the 31 text attributes that only the CT, MR, and PET IODs added on 7 October 2026 use has a rule in its group; each group gets its actions under the Basic Profile, Clean Descriptors, and Retain Device Identity, and the names and identifiers are removed under every option; a malformed option action, or one on a rule whose group allows none, is rejected; coded values keep their meaning; both alternative calendar dates are removed under every preset; De-identification Method keeps its existing values and gains the engine's; a text attribute without a rule is removed, or emptied or given a dummy value where its Type requires; URN Code Value and Coding Scheme URL are kept under every option, with a note; each other UR attribute that Table E.1-1 omits, each of the seven sequences, ICC Profile, Color Space, and MAC Parameters Sequence has X/Z/D with a note, Series Number has X/Z/D under every option, which resolves to Z where the first supported release's IODs define it but in the items of Source Series Information Sequence, where it is Type 1 and resolves to D, and wherever Z or D applies it takes its rank in the numbering that the run builds for the study that holds the series it numbers, so that an item naming a series of another study takes that series' own rank, while a value without a rank, including one in an item that names a series that the run does not hold, is emptied under Z and given the dummy value under D, and no date written there reaches the output, and each is removed where the first supported release's IODs make it Type 3, under every option, as Patient's Size Code Sequence is under Retain Patient Characteristics, while a UR value that the IOD requires sequesters its instance; an attribute that no rule covers is kept if its VR is CS, AT, or a numeric VR; a sequence or a binary value is kept where the IOD defines the attribute at that place and removed elsewhere, with the attributes in a kept sequence's items handled by their own rules, and an Encrypted Attributes Sequence is removed with its items (D-013); such an attribute of any other VR is removed; and an attribute absent from the pinned dictionary is removed; and the Common Instance Reference Module's two sequences are removed, with their items, where no other reference to an instance with a value is kept, an empty one, one that names a study, and the module's own not counting, and both are kept where one is, including one within a nested Referenced Series Sequence and the dummy item of Referenced Performed Procedure Step Sequence, while the check against the IOD allows their removal only where the output holds no such reference.

### D-023: Dates and times that Table E.1-1 omits

- **Decision.** Every date, time, and datetime attribute of the pinned dictionary that Table E.1-1 omits gets X/Z/D under the Basic Profile, as a reviewed L2 action, besides the role that D-007 gives it. In 2026d there are 15: three general attributes, Item Inventory DateTime (0008,0404), Expiration DateTime (0008,0416), and Study Update DateTime (0008,041F); eight that PS3.6 marks DICONDE; and four that it marks DICOS. As the table does for every date and time it lists except Patient's Birth Date, Patient's Birth Time, and GPS Time Stamp, each rule also gives K under Retain Longitudinal Temporal Information with Full Dates and C under Modified Dates, which modifies the attribute by its role (D-007), and no action under any other option, so Retain Device Identity keeps neither Date of Gain Calibration (0014,3076) nor Time of Gain Calibration (0014,3077) and conflicts with Modified Dates on neither.
- **Rationale.** This is consistent with the Basic Profile removing every date it lists, and none of the 15 appears in the first supported release's IODs, so the choice costs nothing there. Keeping them under the Basic Profile was rejected because dates are quasi-identifiers; they are kept only under Full Dates, as the table keeps the dates it lists. Acting on them by temporal role under the Basic Profile was rejected as more nuance than the risk justifies.
- **Tests.** Each of these attributes has X/Z/D under the Basic Profile, with a note, K under Full Dates, and C under Modified Dates, and every policy gives it C exactly when it selects Modified Dates; and a regenerated dictionary that adds a date, time, or datetime that Table E.1-1 omits fails the tests until the attribute has a rule.

### D-024: De-identification method digest

- **Decision.**
  - The de-identification method digest is a SHA-256 identifier of the policy and the PyMedPhys implementation that determine the de-identification method. It covers the policy (D-011) and everything the engine could apply, whether or not the selected options use it: PyMedPhys's version; the content digest of every generated table; every L2 rule; every L3 rule, or their absence; the digest of the entries of the TG-263 vocabulary that descriptor cleaning matches ROI names against (D-009), or the absence of a vocabulary; the keyed digest of the reviewed-names list whose decisions descriptor cleaning applies (D-009), or the absence of a list; the text finder's class and its description of its method under the Clean Pixel Data Option (D-037), or the absence of a finder; under the Retain Safe Private Option, the list whose rules settle private attributes, the reviewed rules or TCIA's Private Tag Dictionary (D-035), or, without the Option, the absence of one; every parameter of generated values; the source and rule files of the de-identification package; and the source files of PyMedPhys's modules outside the package that it imports. Each instance records it in De-identification Method (D-012) as 64 lowercase hexadecimal digits.
  - A generated table's content digest is computed from its rows and must match the digest the table records, as its loader requires (D-001).
  - The source and rule files are every `.py`, `.toml`, `.json`, `.csv`, and `.txt` file of the `pymedphys._dicom.deidentify` package, at any depth: its modules, its L2 rule files and the requirements register beside them, its generated tables, and TCIA's Private Tag Dictionary with its notice (D-035). Each is read as bytes, with CRLF line endings normalised to LF, so a Windows checkout gives each file the same digest. `__pycache__` and names that start with `.` within the package, such as the caches that tools write, are left out. The digest is refused when none of these files can be found, or when they do not include the module that computes the digest, so that an installation whose files cannot be read never gives a digest that covers none of its code. These files and the generated tables are read once per process, when the first digest is computed, and stand for the engine as read then, not as imported. An edit after the first digest is not seen until the process restarts, so every instance of a run carries the same digest; an edit between importing the engine and the first digest can give the old code the new files' digest. Editing or reloading the engine's modules in a running process, for example with `importlib.reload` or Jupyter's autoreload, is not supported, and the digest is then not guaranteed to match the code that runs. The engine is to compute the digest before it processes anything, so that the digest stands for the code that runs.
  - The outside modules are every module of PyMedPhys outside the package that its modules import, directly or through one another, such as `pymedphys._geometry`, which clips contours, `pymedphys._nomenclature.roi_list`, which matches ROI names, and `pymedphys._version`, listed by their paths within PyMedPhys; a package in the list stands for each of its files of a covered type at any depth. Each is read in the same way and at the same time as the package's files, and named in the serialisation by `pymedphys/` and its path within PyMedPhys, such as `pymedphys/_geometry/polygons.py`. The digest is refused when one cannot be found. A package's `__init__.py` that runs only because one of its modules is imported is left out, as is `pymedphys/__init__.py`, which imports PyMedPhys's public API, none of which the engine calls.
  - The digest is computed over a canonical serialisation labelled with its format, `pymedphys-deid-method-digest/6`: one JSON object encoded as UTF-8, with every object's members sorted by key, no whitespace outside text, every character other than `"`, `\`, and the control characters written as itself, and integers in decimal. Its members are `format`, `engine_version`, `policy`, `tables`, `l2_rules`, `l3_rules`, `vocabulary`, `reviewed_roi_names`, `reviewed_descriptors`, `technique_vocabulary`, `text_finder`, `safe_private_list`, `generated_values`, and `files`. Dataclasses and mappings are objects, sequences are arrays in their own order, and enumerations are their values. The parameters of generated values, and the members of the text finder's description, have different types, so each is a pair of its type and value, such as `["int", 52]`, with floating-point numbers and bytes in hexadecimal, and a set's members are sorted. A value of any other type is rejected. A change to the serialisation takes a new format label.
  - There are no L3 rules yet, so the serialisation records their absence as `null`, as it does an absent vocabulary, and adding them will change every digest.
  - The runtime environment is not an input to the method digest, apart from the text finder's under the Clean Pixel Data Option (next item). The Python implementation and version, and the versions of pydicom and tomlkit, are provenance of the execution rather than identity of the method, and are recorded separately (D-012, D-029). The same method, implemented by the same PyMedPhys code and resources, therefore has the same method digest in every supported runtime environment, except that a run under Clean Pixel Data has one for each runtime of its text finder. Of PyMedPhys's own source tree outside the package, vendored code such as apipkg included, the source files of the modules that the package imports are inputs, as the item on outside modules describes. Of third-party software installed separately, only what a text finder's own description of its method names is an input: for the text detector, the SHA-256 of its model, the version of ONNX Runtime with the names of the distributions that install it, and the versions of NumPy and SciPy.
  - The one runtime that is an input is that of the text finder (D-037). The `text_finder` member is the finder's class, by module and qualified name, with a copy of its own description of its method, both read once when the transform is made, or `null` without a finder. The class keeps a finder that gives another finder's description, such as a wrapper or a subclass of the text detector, from being taken for it, and the copy gives the markers and the release report one description. The text detector describes its method as the SHA-256 of its model; the version of ONNX Runtime that runs it, with the distributions that install it, since `onnxruntime` and `onnxruntime-gpu` give the same version; the versions of NumPy and SciPy, which scale, render, and box each frame; its detection parameters; and its renderings. Its model and parameters cannot be reassigned once it is made. The regions it finds are what the option writes, and neither ONNX Runtime nor those libraries promise the same output across their versions, so a run under Clean Pixel Data has a method digest for each version of each. A finder other than the text detector describes its own method in the same form: a mapping that is not empty, whose values are text, integers, floating-point numbers, bytes, or UUIDs, in mappings, sequences, and sets. A finder without such a description is refused, as is a boolean or `null` in it, as for the parameters of generated values.
  - Runtime software can affect execution. Equality of method digests therefore means equality of the components that the method digest covers; it is not proof of byte-for-byte identical execution or output. Reproducing or auditing an execution also requires its recorded runtime environment and any other relevant external state.
  - Releases and development builds compute the digest in the same way. An unmodified installation of an official release therefore gives the same method digest for a given policy and set of external resources in every supported runtime environment, apart from a text finder's runtime under Clean Pixel Data; an edited or repacked installation that claims the release's version gives another; and two development checkouts that share a `.dev` version give different digests when their de-identification source differs. Each release's conformance statement (Architecture item 10) is to publish the method digest of each enabled preset without a vocabulary, the technique vocabulary, a reviewed-names list, a reviewed descriptors list, or a text finder, and, under Retain Safe Private, with the reviewed safe private rules as its list, naming the PyMedPhys release, the DICOM edition, the preset, and the absence of each, so that anyone can compare a file's digest with it. A run with a site's own vocabulary, reviewed-names list, reviewed descriptors list, or other resource has a digest other than that published baseline, as intended, and so does every run under Clean Pixel Data, which needs a text finder, and so does a run under Clean Descriptors with the technique vocabulary, which such a run uses unless it is given none, and so does a run under Retain Safe Private that chooses TCIA's Private Tag Dictionary as its safe private list. No digest is published yet, since no preset is enabled.
  - De-identifying a file again with the same policy, PyMedPhys files, and resources gives the same method digest under any supported version of Python, pydicom, or tomlkit, so a change of those alone never appears as a new method, whereas a change of the text finder's runtime under Clean Pixel Data does, as intended, and the later run's Contributing Equipment Sequence item records its own runtime (D-012). A change to the policy, the rules, the source, the parameters of generated values, or an external resource changes the digest, so the later processing is identifiable as a different method.
  - A confidential site resource that can change what the engine does contributes to the method digest through a stable keyed digest, never through the resource or an unkeyed digest of it, which anyone could test a guessed resource against. The first is the reviewed-names list of ROI name cleaning (D-009, D-016), which holds source ROI names. Its keyed digest is the HMAC-SHA-256 of the list's canonical form, its file written without whitespace outside text, with every object's members sorted, under the run's key in a derivation domain of its own, `reviewed-roi-names` (D-004), so that no UID, patient identifier, or date offset shares its message. The key is never written to DICOM output or reports. The keyed digest is an input to the method digest, `reviewed_roi_names` in the serialisation, or `null` without a list, and is recorded wherever the digest's components are. The same list under the same key gives the same value in every run, and a change to a decision or a name changes the method digest. The second is the reviewed descriptors list (D-009), whose keyed digest is computed in the same way in the domain `reviewed-descriptors`, as `reviewed_descriptors` in the serialisation. Under an ephemeral key, each run's keyed digest, and so its method digest, differs even for the same list, as intended: without the key, nobody can tell whether two runs used the same list. Resources that are not confidential, such as the TG-263 vocabulary and the technique vocabulary (D-036), keep their unkeyed content digests.
  - Each release report records the method digest with the components it is computed from, as structured fields: `method_digest`; `method_digest_format`; `engine_version`; `table_digests`, the content digest of each generated table by file name; `l2_rules_digest`, the SHA-256 of the canonical bytes of the `l2_rules` member of the serialisation; `l3_rules`, their absence or, once they exist, the SHA-256 of their member; `vocabulary_digest`, or the absence of a vocabulary; `reviewed_roi_names`, the keyed digest of the reviewed-names list, or the absence of a list; `reviewed_descriptors`, the keyed digest of the reviewed descriptors list, or the absence of a list; `technique_vocabulary_digest`, the SHA-256 of the technique vocabulary, or the absence of one; `text_finder_digest`, the SHA-256 of the `text_finder` member, or the absence of a finder; `safe_private_list`, `reviewed` or `tcia` under the Retain Safe Private Option, or the absence of a list without it; `generated_values_digest`, the SHA-256 of the `generated_values` member; and `engine_files`, the SHA-256 of each source and rule file by its path. These show which component differs between two digests. Like the rest of the report, they contain no key, source identifier, source value, or patient data (D-016).
- **Rationale.** Two runs carry the same digest only if they have the same policy, PyMedPhys version, generated tables, rules, vocabulary, text finder, parameters of generated values, package files, and outside modules, with the files and tables as each run's process read them at its first digest. The vocabulary and the L3 rules are not generated tables, but they decide what the engine keeps and writes as much as the tables do, and the engine's version stands for its code in a release. Every call states the vocabulary, or None for its absence, by name: with a default, a caller that forgot the vocabulary would record no vocabulary while descriptor cleaning used one. The digest also changes when an unrelated rule changes, which is harmless. Covering only what the policy uses was rejected: deciding which rules are used would be another dependency calculation that could be wrong, and if it were, behaviour could change silently under the same digest, which is the dangerous failure; a change of digest that proves unnecessary is the safer error. A serialisation that fixes every byte, and states each value's type where JSON alone would not, encodes the same inputs as the same bytes whatever platform and Python version computes it, which hashing Python's own representations of the values would not. Development builds and editable installs share a version, such as `0.42.0.dev0`, across commits, and an edited or repacked installation can claim a release's version, so the engine's files identify their code where the version cannot; limiting the guarantee to released versions was rejected because tests and pilots run such builds, and selecting the files by type, rather than by a list of those that decide behaviour, cannot leave out a new module of the package. Code outside the package decides behaviour too: contour clipping moved to `pymedphys._geometry` so that other parts of PyMedPhys can use it, and ROI name matching is in `pymedphys._nomenclature`, so a digest of the package alone would let either change what is written under the same digest. Covering every module the package imports, directly or through one another, rather than only those judged to decide behaviour, follows the same reasoning as covering the whole package. The outside modules are listed rather than found when the digest is computed, because finding them would mean resolving imports at run time, another dependency calculation that could be wrong; a test that scans the imports instead keeps the list complete. Covering all of PyMedPhys was rejected, because every unrelated change would then give the method a new identity. Reading the files and tables once per process gives every instance of a run the same digest, even when a file of an editable install is edited after the first digest, which would otherwise give later output of the old code the new files' digest. Apart from the text finder's, the runtime environment is left out of the digest for two reasons. The first is comparability: with the versions of Python, pydicom, and tomlkit as inputs, every routine upgrade would give the same method a new identity, so no stable digest could be published for a release, policy, and set of resources, sites could not compare their digests without also matching their environments, and de-identifying a file again after an upgrade would record what looks like a new method. The second is that a few version strings do not identify an execution: it can also depend on how packages were built, on the libraries they load, on details of the platform, and on other external state, so a digest of selected versions would look like a complete fingerprint of behaviour without being one. Recording the runtime as readable provenance shows a custodian what actually ran, which a second, opaque digest in De-identification Method would not, so none is written there, and the effect of the runtime on behaviour is managed by testing the supported environments (D-029). The text finder's runtime is the exception because what it decides is the regions written, from a network's floating-point output, which testing a few environments cannot bound. The finder's class is part of its identity because its description is only what it says of itself: without the class, a wrapper or a subclass that gave the text detector's description would share the detector's digest while finding other regions, which is the dangerous failure above. A plain SHA-256 of a small or guessable confidential list could be reversed by hashing candidate lists offline, which a keyed digest prevents, and a derivation domain of its own keeps the resource's identifier apart from patient pseudonyms, UIDs, and date offsets derived under the same key. The keyed digest, like the vocabulary, must be stated by name with no default, so a caller that forgot the list would be refused rather than record no list while descriptor cleaning used one.
- **Tests.** Changing PyMedPhys's version, any part of the policy, its preset, any generated table, L2 rule, L3 rule, the vocabulary's entries, any parameter of generated values, or any source or rule file of the engine changes the digest, including one that the selected options do not use, as does adding, removing, or renaming a file, adding or removing a vocabulary, or adding, removing, or changing the reviewed-names list's keyed digest, or the text finder's description, each of which shows only in its own component, or choosing TCIA's Private Tag Dictionary in place of the reviewed rules under the Retain Safe Private Option, which shows in `safe_private_list` and is refused without the Option, and a keyed digest that is not 64 lowercase hexadecimal digits, or a call that does not state the list by name, is refused; changing the Python implementation, the Python version, or the version of pydicom or tomlkit, one at a time or all together, leaves the digest unchanged; the serialisation holds exactly the members listed, and while there are no L3 rules, records their absence; the same version, policy, tables, rules, vocabulary, parameters, and files always give the same digest, in whatever order mappings and sets hold them; a small synthetic input gives canonical bytes written out by hand and their independently computed SHA-256, and changing only a value's type changes them; files with CRLF and LF line endings give the same digest, and caches and files of other types are left out; every file of the package other than caches has a type that the digest covers, so a file of a new type fails the tests until the digest covers it; every file is covered when the package is installed below a directory whose name starts with `.`; changing, adding, or removing a file of an outside module, such as one of `pymedphys._geometry`, changes the digest and shows only in its own file's digest, the digest is refused when an outside module cannot be found, an edit to one after the first digest is not seen until it is read again, and a scan of the package's imports finds every outside module listed and no other; the digest is refused when the package's files cannot be found or do not include the module that computes it; an edit to the files or tables after the first digest is not seen until they are read again, as in a new process; a table whose rows do not match its recorded digest is rejected; a reformatted table keeps its content digest, which is computed from its rows, but changes its file's digest; a call that does not state the vocabulary by name is refused; a text finder's class and description are written with each value's type, a finder of another class with the same description gives another digest, a change to a finder after the transform is made changes neither the markers' digest nor the report's, the text detector's description of its default parameters has a canonical form, and a description that is not a mapping, is empty, or holds a value of another type is refused, as is reassigning the text detector's model or parameters; and the digest is 64 lowercase hexadecimal digits, so it reveals no value.

### D-025: File Meta Information

- **Decision.**
  - The File Meta Information that the engine builds (D-002) holds exactly these elements: File Meta Information Group Length (0002,0000), File Meta Information Version (0002,0001), Media Storage SOP Class UID (0002,0002), Media Storage SOP Instance UID (0002,0003), Transfer Syntax UID (0002,0010), Implementation Class UID (0002,0012), and Implementation Version Name (0002,0013).
  - It names the de-identifier by the fixed Implementation Class UID `1.2.826.0.1.3680043.10.188.1.1`, the first UID of the arc `1.2.826.0.1.3680043.10.188.1` that PyMedPhys reserves for UIDs fixed in its design, under its registered root, `1.2.826.0.1.3680043.10.188`. The maintainer decided this on 1 October 2026, in place of a `2.25.` UID derived from a documented name.
  - Implementation Version Name is `PYMEDPHYS`, followed by the version where the result fits the 16 characters of SH. A single space separates them, as in `PYMEDPHYS 0.42.0`, and a development or local version that does not fit, such as `0.42.0.dev1`, gives `PYMEDPHYS` alone.
  - Every other element is omitted, whatever the source file held: Source Application Entity Title (0002,0016), Sending Application Entity Title (0002,0017), and Receiving Application Entity Title (0002,0018); Source Presentation Address (0002,0026), Sending Presentation Address (0002,0027), and Receiving Presentation Address (0002,0028); and private File Meta elements, such as Private Information Creator UID (0002,0100) and Private Information (0002,0102).
- **Rationale.** The elements written are the six that PS3.10 Table 7.1-1 makes Type 1, and Implementation Version Name. The table's other elements are Type 3, except Private Information, which is required only with a Private Information Creator UID, so omitting them keeps the file valid. The titles and addresses of the systems that created, sent, and received the source file can identify the institution, so none is carried into the output. PS3.7 D.3.3.2 requires Implementation Class UIDs to be registered by the implementing organisation per the policies of PS3.5; PS3.5 9.2.2 makes an organisation responsible for registering its UIDs, at least by obtaining a registered root; and PS3.5 B.2, which is informative, advises that a UUID-derived UID is usually not appropriate for an Implementation Class UID. A UID under the registered PyMedPhys root meets all three and leads back to PyMedPhys. Every UID that PyMedPhys generates under the root adds at most one component after it, so none falls under the reserved arc, whose reservation is recorded beside the root in `pymedphys._dicom.uid`. A version that does not fit beside the name is left out rather than shortened, because a shortened version would name another release. PS3.7 D.3.3.2 requires, where there is no Implementation Version Name, that one Implementation Class UID means one version of the implementation, and notes that a version name is expected to include version information; `PYMEDPHYS` alone is therefore still written, since the UID stays the same across versions, and the full version is always in Software Versions (0018,1020) of the Contributing Equipment item (D-012).
