- **Tests.** Every output file's File Meta Information holds exactly the seven elements listed, with the fixed Implementation Class UID `1.2.826.0.1.3680043.10.188.1.1` and an Implementation Version Name of `PYMEDPHYS` with the version where it fits 16 characters; and no output file carries an Application Entity Title, a Presentation Address, or a private element in its File Meta Information, whatever the source file's File Meta Information held.

### D-026: Reference graph findings

- **Decision.** The first pass's findings (Architecture item 8) have graded consequences:
  - A dangling reference is reported only: the release report counts the instances with each kind of finding reported only, and the confidential QC pack lists each such finding at each instance it names, with the attribute's tags and its count.
  - A frame of reference mismatch is reported only, and its instances are still written. The maintainer decided this on 6 October 2026. The mismatches are a series whose instances name several frames of reference, an RT Structure Set ROI whose frame of reference the structure set does not list once and only once, and an image that a structure set lists under another frame of reference than its own (Architecture item 8).
  - Identical duplicate instances are written once. The maintainer decided on 1 October 2026 that instances that share a SOP Instance UID are identical only when their source bytes are the same: the same Transfer Syntax UID, and the same bytes of the data set after the File Meta Information, ignoring the preamble, the File Meta Information, and the top-level group lengths (gggg,0000) and Data Set Trailing Padding (FFFC,FFFC) (Architecture item 8).
  - Instances that share a SOP Instance UID with different source bytes, or whose bytes the first pass cannot show to be sound, are all sequestered.
  - A series whose instances name several studies is sequestered.
  - A study whose instances name several patients stops the run before anything is written. The maintainer decided on 1 October 2026 that a patient is a Patient ID with its Issuer of Patient ID (0010,0021), the identity from which D-005 derives pseudonyms, so a study in which a planning system has dropped the issuer from some instances stops the run. An instance without a Patient ID counts as a patient apart from every identity, which is the conservative reading of that decision: the instances without one together form one patient, so a study that mixes instances with and without a Patient ID stops the run, and a study whose instances all lack one does not. The maintainer decided on 10 October 2026 to keep stopping the run, and to record as an option, should such studies become a problem in practice, a command-line opt-in that sequesters the study, reports it, and lets the rest of the run go ahead. Such an option would still collect the sequestered study's values and search for them in the files of every patient that the study names, and, since the first release's key is made for each run and then discarded (D-004), the corrected study could not be added to that release later, only released again with the rest of its cohort by a new run that replaces it.
  - An instance without its SOP Instance UID, Series Instance UID, or Study Instance UID is sequestered: it cannot be named (D-016), and the first pass cannot check it against its series and study.
  - Release reports name instances by their output names only. Run positions and source paths appear only in the confidential QC pack (D-016).
  - A sequestered instance has no output name. The maintainer decided on 2 October 2026 that release reports name each one by an opaque label for the run, such as `S-0007`, with its reasons, and that only the confidential QC pack maps labels to sources. Labels are assigned in an order drawn at random, so a label says nothing of the instance or its place in the run.
- **Rationale.** Real exports routinely contain dangling references, such as a plan that references a dose that was not exported, so stopping on every finding would make the engine unusable, and reporting every finding without acting on it could release inconsistent collections. A study with several patients is a curation error that no action on individual instances can repair safely. An empty Patient ID needs a curated identity (D-005), and an unresolved identity stops its instances for curator resolution (D-008); a curated identity would give such an instance a different pseudonym from the rest of its study, the outcome that stopping a study with several patients exists to prevent. Comparing source bytes needs no decoder and no resolution of VRs, so no behaviour of pydicom, such as guessing the VR of an element that an Implicit VR file holds without one, can make different values compare equal, and comparing copies cannot change the data set that a caller holds. The same bytes can be different values in different encodings, `ff ff` being -1 as SS and 65535 as US, and `01 00` being 1 in a little endian OW value and 256 in a big endian one, so the transfer syntax, which decides the encoding of the bytes, is part of the comparison. Writing one of two copies that differ would silently drop the other's content, while sequestering both loses nothing that a curator cannot recover. So the cost of comparing bytes, that copies that differ only in their encoding are sequestered for a curator, loses nothing, and a copy whose bytes cannot be shown to be sound counts as a conflict, which fails safe. A frame of reference mismatch is a matter of the data's integrity, not of disclosure: replacing every UID consistently neither creates nor hides one, and contours drawn on an image registered to the planning CT legitimately sit in another frame of reference, so withholding for it would block sound collections.
- **Tests.** A dangling reference is reported and its instance still written; copies with another preamble or File Meta Information, with top-level group lengths, or with top-level Data Set Trailing Padding are identical; the digest is the SHA-256 of the Transfer Syntax UID and the data set's bytes, computed by hand from the file; copies of one data set in Implicit VR and in Explicit VR Little Endian conflict, with or without a private element, as do copies with sequences of defined and of undefined length, with more padding of a value, with a group length or Data Set Trailing Padding inside an item, and with the same data set bytes under another transfer syntax; any changed value or VR, added or removed element, or changed private element makes copies conflict, as do a private element written as SS -1 in one copy and as US 65535 in the other, the same OW bytes in little and big endian, a private or unlisted element read as UN in one copy and with a VR in the other, compressed and uncompressed Pixel Data, and 8-bit Pixel Data in Implicit VR and in Explicit VR; a file in a deflated transfer syntax, without a Transfer Syntax UID, followed by bytes that cannot be read, or with a top-level group length or Data Set Trailing Padding that holds items has no digest, and conflicts with every other copy, even another copy of the same file, while one in Explicit VR Big Endian or truncated within a sequence that the first pass follows, or with such a sequence of Explicit VR, of Implicit VR, or of VR UN whose items run past it, at the top level or nested, has no record; the same Patient ID from another issuer, or without one, is another patient, a study that mixes instances with and without a Patient ID has several patients, and a study whose instances all lack one has one; identical duplicates give one output file; every instance that shares a SOP Instance UID with different source bytes is sequestered; a series that names several studies is sequestered; a study that names several patients stops the run with nothing written; release reports name instances by their output names, never by run positions or source paths; they name sequestered instances by labels that carry nothing of the instance or the run's order, with reasons that hold only codes and tags; and an instance without its SOP Instance, Series Instance, or Study Instance UID is sequestered; and a series in several frames of reference, an ROI whose frame of reference is not listed once, and a contour image in another frame of reference are each reported, and their instances still written.

### D-027: Residual search

- **Decision.** The search of each output file after writing (Architecture item 8) is strict:
  - Any residual person name, UID, or date, and any finding outside the data set, in the preamble, the File Meta Information, or trailing bytes, fails the file, and its instance is sequestered.
  - A residual direct identifier fails the file in the same way, although the search reports it as text. A direct identifier is an attribute that Table E.1-1 lists in a PS3.6 group of the patient, a visit, the study, or a procedure or order (0008, 0010, 0020, 0032, 0038, or 0040), that is neither a sequence nor a UID, that no option retains or cleans, and whose keyword has `ID`, `IDs`, `Number`, `Numbers`, or `Locator` as a word, such as `PatientID` or `PlacerOrderNumberImagingServiceRequest`; it counts wherever it occurs, as in the items of Other Patient IDs Sequence (0010,1002). In 2026d this selects 22 attributes, 15 of them current: Patient ID (0010,0020), Issuer of Patient ID (0010,0021), Patient's Telephone Numbers (0010,2154), Accession Number (0008,0050), Referring Physician's Telephone Numbers (0008,0094), Study ID (0020,0010), Admission ID (0038,0010), Service Episode ID (0038,0060), Scheduled Procedure Step ID (0040,0009), Performed Procedure Step ID (0040,0253), Requested Procedure ID (0040,1001), Person's Telephone Numbers (0040,1103), Order Callback Phone Number (0040,2010), Placer Order Number / Imaging Service Request (0040,2016), and Filler Order Number / Imaging Service Request (0040,2017), and the retired Other Patient IDs (0010,1000), Medical Record Locator (0010,1090), Study ID Issuer (0032,0012), Issuer of Admission ID (0038,0011), Issuer of Service Episode ID (0038,0061), Specimen Accession Number (0040,050A), and Telephone Number (Trial) (0040,A354).
  - Text findings inside the data set go to confidential QC review (D-016), apart from those that kept standard vocabulary or an equipment name explains (D-032), or that a value a selected option keeps explains (D-042).
  - As the maintainer decided on 2 October 2026, transforming a value and collecting it for the search are separate, and release is conditional on both. The release gate enforces exactly this:
    - **Coverage.** The walker reports the paths of the values it planned to collect, those it collected, those it could not collect, with a reason that holds no value, and those it read only as bytes. Collection is complete only when every planned value was collected from its decoded form under a VR that the pinned dictionary gives its attribute. It is incomplete where a planned value was neither collected nor reported as not collected, so an empty collection is never taken as complete; where a value could not be collected; where text outside ISO 646 with no Specific Character Set was read only as ISO 8859-1 (Architecture item 5), since searching its bytes supplements the coverage but does not stand in for the decoded text, whose copies in other attributes or encodings were not searched for; and where a value was collected under another VR, such as a person name collected as CS, which the search would leave out or search in the wrong forms. Coverage is pooled across the subject, as the values searched for are, so a sibling instance's gap counts against every file of the subject, even where another instance collected a value at the same path. An instance out of scope whose IOD the pinned tables define, such as a nuclear medicine image, a spatial registration, or an RT image, is read, planned, and edited only so that its values are collected for its subject's search, and is never written, so that it withholds its subject's files only where its collection is incomplete, as any instance's would. Under Clean Descriptors, such an instance, like any other withheld before its descriptors are cleaned, leaves out of that collection the descriptors that cleaning would write with a kept value (D-009). This is "Collect first", which builds on "Withhold", as the maintainer decided on 6 October 2026. An instance of the subject whose values were not collected at all, because its source was refused, its SOP Class names no IOD of the pinned tables, its Specific Character Set cannot be decoded, its transform failed, or it changed during the run, withholds every file of the subject, since its values could be in them unsearched for; and where the walker sequesters an instance, the planned values it did not reach are not collected, rather than not reported. Values in an instance whose file could not be read as DICOM, which names no subject, are not pooled. File Meta Information and preamble values are not collected; the writer rewrites both, so neither is released, but a copy of one in kept text is not searched for.
    - **Search.** The gate runs the search itself, for exactly the pooled collected values, so no other search can stand in for it. The forms that the search leaves out by the rules below, such as those shorter than four characters and binary values, are its exclusions: they are listed with their reasons and never count as incomplete.
    - **Withheld.** A file is withheld where collection is incomplete for an attribute to which the pinned dictionary gives VR PN, UI, DA, DT, or SQ, whose items' content is unknown, or for a direct identifier; where its structure cannot be read to its end; and where the search finds a person name, UID, date, datetime, or direct identifier anywhere, other than a date or datetime equal to a whole date or time that a selected option keeps (D-042), or any value in the preamble, the File Meta Information, Data Set Trailing Padding, which holds no value, or bytes after the data set. Kinds are taken from the VR that the pinned dictionary gives the source attribute, whatever VR the value was collected under, and from the search only where the dictionary gives none.
    - **QC review.** A file goes to QC review where collection is incomplete for any other value, including a private value or one to which the dictionary gives no VR, or the search finds other text inside the data set that D-032 or D-042 does not exempt. Binary values are outside the search's contract, which leaves them out, and a private value that could not be collected is outside its reach, although decoded private text is searched like any other text; withholding every file with a vendor's private value that could not be collected would sequester nearly every RT file, so the residual risk is listed in the QC pack instead.
    - **Released** otherwise. A search that finds nothing is not on its own a release. The gate takes the most severe consequence of every reason, each named by an attribute path and a reason code, never a value, and has no setting that relaxes it. Release reports are to count the reasons by attribute and code, and the QC pack to list them in full (D-016, D-026).
  - **Clarification of the decisions of 1 October 2026 on content that cannot be decoded, 2 October 2026.** Where those decisions say that "the instance is de-identified" (D-010) and that values that cannot be decoded "are listed as not searched", the transformation proceeds and the values are listed as not collected; release remains conditional on the required coverage. For example:

    | Source | Before the clarification | After it |
    | --- | --- | --- |
    | Patient's Name removed, with bytes outside ISO 646 and no Specific Character Set | Read as ISO 8859-1, searched, and released after a clean search | Withheld: incomplete collection of a person name |
    | Study Description removed, with the same bytes | Released after a clean search | QC review: incomplete collection of other text |
    | Study Description kept or cleaned, with the same bytes | Instance sequestered | Instance sequestered, as before |
  - The maintainer decided on 2 October 2026 that release reports count the drops and the values not searched by attribute and reason, and that the confidential QC pack lists each one by instance and place (D-016).
  - As the maintainer decided on 6 October 2026, a residual person name found inside a retained label, such as a beam name, ROI name, or plan label, still withholds the file in the first supported release, even where it is also an ordinary word, as a surname can be in a beam name (`FIELD^MARGARET` against a Beam Name of `Field 1`). Before that changes, for example to send such findings to QC review, their effect is first measured with a list of surnames.
  - Values that the policy legitimately retains are dropped from the search, and each drop is recorded.
  - Values shorter than 4 characters are not searched, and are listed as not searched. Binary values, values of VR AS, AT, CS, DS, FD, FL, IS, SL, SS, SV, TM, UL, US, and UV, and datetimes without a full date are also listed as not searched, and are checked attribute by attribute instead (Architecture item 8).
  - Content that cannot be decoded inside a sequence that is removed is removed with it, and its values are listed as not searched in the confidential QC material (D-016). An instance is sequestered only where content that cannot be decoded would be kept or cleaned (Architecture item 2). The maintainer decided this on 1 October 2026.
  - A source value that exactly equals a constant that the engine always writes, such as a dummy date or `DEIDENTIFIED`, is not searched, and each such skip is recorded by its source attribute, never by its value. The maintainer decided this on 1 October 2026.
  - The constants for that skip are D's first and second constants, the values of D's item in Person Identification Code Sequence, and the markers' fixed values, of which the option codes are only those of the options that the engine applies, since it refuses any other and so never writes its code, and not the keyed pseudonyms or the markers' versions and method digest; a source value equals one when D would treat it as equal to its constant (Architecture item 8). The maintainer decided this on 5 October 2026.
  - A form of a source value that equals such a constant is skipped in the same way, and the value's other forms are still searched. So the family name of a person name `DEIDENTIFIED^ABCDEF` from data that was de-identified before, which is the family name of every keyed pseudonym, is not searched, and nor are the words of a family name `DE-IDENTIFIED`, but `ABCDEF` is; and the date of a datetime `19000101120000`, written as YYYYMMDD, is not searched, but the whole datetime and the date's other spellings are. The maintainer decided this on 5 October 2026.
  - Output is written to a staging area and moved to the release directory only after a clean search. The staging area is a directory beside the release directory, named for it, so on its file system, and created for the run with permissions for its owner alone where the platform has them, since it holds output that may still identify people, as QC material does (D-016). Each file is written there under a temporary name and renamed once its bytes are on disk, read back for the search, and deleted at once if it is not released. A file held for review is deleted too, and is written again by a run after its review, whose release replaces the earlier one, since the current command gives each run an ephemeral key (D-004); the maintainer decided this on 6 October 2026, for a file held for ROI name review (D-009) and for one held for a text finding of the search alike. The release directory must not exist before the run, and is published by one rename of the staged tree, so it appears whole, with only files that passed, or not at all. A run that fails deletes its staging area; one that a crash leaves holds output that may still identify people, so a run refuses to start beside it until someone has reviewed and deleted it.
- **Open questions.**
  - Whether dropping a retained value removes it from the search entirely, so that the same value removed from another attribute is not searched for either, or only skips the retained copy, is not yet decided.
  - Values that hold numbers are searched only for forms of at least 8 bytes that are not UTF-16LE, and entropy-coded data only for forms of at least 6 bytes (Architecture item 8), and the search does not list this for each value; whether release reports state these rules is not yet decided.
  - Whether clinical trial identifiers (group 0012), such as Clinical Trial Subject ID (0012,0040), are direct identifiers is not yet decided; most of them identify the trial, its site, or a time point rather than the patient.
- **Rationale.** Nothing leaks silently, at the cost of some false positives that need human review. Reporting findings only was rejected because the search would become advisory. Failing a file on any match was rejected because legitimate overlaps would make the engine unusable: a removed Study Description that reads `CHEST` is found inside a retained Body Part Examined that reads `CHESTABDOMEN`, and review, not failure, settles it. A direct identifier identifies the patient, a visit, or the study on its own, as a name does, so it fails the file although the search reports it as text. Selecting direct identifiers from the pinned tables by a rule, rather than keeping a list by hand, covers those that a new edition adds, and leaving out those that an option retains or cleans, such as device identifiers, keeps to attributes that every option removes or replaces. A constant that the engine always writes is in the output whatever the source held, so finding it reveals nothing about the source and would fail every file whose source happened to hold it; recording the skip by attribute keeps it visible without repeating the value. Content that cannot be decoded cannot be matched, cleaned, or searched. Removing it with a sequence that is removed anyway loses nothing the output keeps, listing its values keeps the gap visible to reviewers, and sequestering an instance that would keep or clean such content means that nothing unchecked is written.
- **Tests.** A residual person name, UID, date, or datetime, and any residual in the preamble, File Meta Information, Data Set Trailing Padding, or trailing bytes, sequesters the instance and leaves nothing in the release directory; incomplete collection of a person name, UID, date, datetime, direct identifier, or unread sequence withholds the file after a search that finds nothing, including where a planned value was neither collected nor reported, where the gap is a sibling instance's, and where a person name was collected as CS, OB, or LO, while incomplete collection of other text, of a private value, or of an attribute without a VR in the dictionary goes to QC review; the clarification's examples give the outcomes in its table; the search covers the pooled values; the search's exclusions are listed and leave collection complete; a file whose structure cannot be read to its end is withheld; the most severe reason decides, in either order; reasons and reprs hold no value; a residual of each direct identifier that the rule selects from the pinned tables, such as Patient ID in an item of Other Patient IDs Sequence or Accession Number in a private element, does the same, and the rule selects exactly the attributes listed above; a text finding inside the data set reaches QC review; retained values are dropped from the search, and each drop is recorded, counted in the release report by attribute and reason; before the pipeline drops retained values, a regression case shows that a legitimately retained occurrence of a value cannot conceal the same source string in the preamble or trailing bytes; values shorter than 4 characters are listed as not searched; a source value that exactly equals a constant the engine always writes is skipped and recorded by its source attribute, never by its value, while one that differs from it is still searched, as is a Patient ID equal to the code or meaning of an option that the engine refuses; a form of a value that equals such a constant, such as the family name of an earlier pseudonym, is skipped and recorded in the same way, while the value's other forms are still found; content that cannot be decoded inside a removed sequence is removed and its values are listed as not collected in the confidential QC material, and an instance that would keep or clean such content is sequestered; nothing reaches the release directory before its search is clean; the release directory appears whole or not at all; and a staging area that an interrupted run leaves stops the next run beside it.

### D-028: Private attributes without Retain Safe Private

- **Decision.**
  - Without the Retain Safe Private Option, every element of an odd group is removed, since Table E.1-1 gives Private Attributes, "(gggg,eeee) where gggg is odd", X: each private data element, whether or not its block has a private creator; each private creator, including one that reserves a block with no elements; and each element that PS3.5 Section 7.8.1 does not allow, in groups 0001, 0003, 0005, 0007, and FFFF or at (gggg,0001) to (gggg,000F) and (gggg,0100) to (gggg,0FFF), and each retired group length, (gggg,0000).
  - They are removed at the top level and in the items of every standard sequence, as E.1.1 requires for every attribute of the table. A private sequence is removed with its items and everything in them, as X requires (Table E.1-1a), so its contents are not examined.
  - A value of VR UN whose attribute the pinned data dictionary gives VR SQ, as pydicom reads a sequence that it does not know from Implicit VR Little Endian, is decoded as Implicit VR Little Endian, as PS3.5 Section 6.2.2 allows, with the character set of the data set that holds it, and its items are searched. So is every raw value of VR UN, or read without a VR from Implicit VR Little Endian, of a sequence that pydicom's own dictionary knows, which pydicom would otherwise convert to SQ without checking its bytes. A value that pydicom has not yet read, because the file was read with `defer_size`, is read from the file or buffer first, and refused if it cannot be read again or is not of its stored length. A decoded value is accepted only if items fill it (Architecture item 2) and they encode to the same bytes. Each sequence nested in those items, at any depth, is decoded and checked in the same way, with the character set of the item that holds it. Where something is removed from a decoded value, or from a sequence nested in it, the output holds its decoded items, with VR SQ; otherwise its value is unchanged.
  - A value of VR UN, or one read without a VR from Implicit VR Little Endian, is decoded only where the pinned dictionary or pydicom's gives its attribute VR SQ, and any other is left as it was read. A value of VR UN whose attribute the dictionary gives another VR holds no items, and one whose attribute the dictionary does not list is not searched, since that attribute is itself removed (Architecture item 2).
  - A standard sequence that Explicit VR Little Endian stores with a VR other than SQ or UN, such as OB, is refused, since its items cannot be searched.
  - A Specific Character Set (0008,0005) that pydicom does not map to codecs as it is given, a value that is not among its terms or several values that include ISO_IR 192, GB18030, or GBK, which allow no code extensions, or one stored with a VR other than CS, which pydicom would convert with an error that can quote it, is refused, as content that cannot be decoded (Architecture item 2), rather than read with pydicom's default encoding or one that it guesses: in an item, by the path of the sequence that holds the item, and at the top level by its own path. Where the instance transform cannot read a top-level Specific Character Set, such as one stored with a VR other than CS, the instance's scope is still decided first, from its SOP Class UID, which as UI no character set changes: an instance whose SOP Class names no IOD of the pinned tables gives its scope reason alone, and any other gives the character set's reason after any scope reason. A value that the transform reads but does not support gives the Default Character Repertoire instead (D-010).
  - A standard sequence that cannot be decoded, or a decoded UN value, or a sequence nested at any depth in its items, that does not encode to the same bytes, is refused by its path, and no copy is returned. Content that cannot be decoded inside a sequence that is removed is removed with it, so the engine sequesters the instance only where that content would be kept (Architecture item 2).
  - Elements are found by their tags, and named by their paths. No private value is decoded. pydicom's warnings and log records are redacted while it decodes, and its errors are replaced, not chained, since each can quote a value.
  - A policy that gives Private Attributes any action other than X without Retain Safe Private is refused. Under that option, the reviewed rules settle the C that the table gives them (D-035).
  - Private Data Element Characteristics Sequence (0008,0300) describes an instance's private blocks, and D-022 keeps Private Creator Reference (0008,0302) in its items, so the private creator of each block that this decision removes would survive there after every block that it describes has gone. The maintainer decided on 6 October 2026 that the sequence is removed whenever no private attribute is kept. Under Retain Safe Private it is read to find the private attributes that the instance declares safe, and then removed too, since its descriptions and the creators of blocks that are not kept can name the institution's software (D-035), so a supplementary rule gives it X/Z/D under every policy, which removes it at its Type 3 place in the SOP Common Module.
- **Open questions.**
  - The search refuses the whole data set at the first value that it cannot decode, so the engine must either remove the sequences that its rules remove before it removes private attributes, or remove the removed sequence that holds a refused path and try again. Which it does is not yet decided.
  - The search decodes sequences for itself, as it was written to run on any data set before the engine had an element reader. Whether it should use the engine's element reader, which reads UN sequences and character sets by its own rules, is not yet decided.
- **Rationale.**
  - PS3.5 Section 7.8.1 lets a de-identifier keep or remove a private creator whose block is removed. Removing every one follows the table, which names Private Attributes by their group alone, and E.3.10, which keeps only "the Private Creator IDs that are required to fully define the retained Private Attributes". A private creator can name the institution, as D-022 notes for in-house software. Keeping private creators was rejected because, without their blocks, they carry nothing useful, and no rule reviews their values.
  - The elements that PS3.5 does not allow have no defined content, so nothing shows that keeping them is safe, and the table's X covers them by their group.
  - pydicom 3.0.2 does not know some sequences of the first supported release's IODs, such as RT Assertions Sequence (0044,0110) and Dose Calculation Model Sequence (3004,0080), and E.1.1 requires a private attribute in their items to be removed too. Sequestering every instance with such a sequence that holds a private attribute was rejected, because Architecture item 2 already decodes UN values with their dictionary VR. pydicom decodes some malformed values without an error, as items that leave out part of the value, and those bytes could hold a private attribute, so a value whose items do not encode to the same bytes is not trusted: a value from which nothing is removed is written as it was read, with the bytes its items leave out. pydicom also writes the elements of a decoded item as it read them, and decodes a sequence of defined length among them only when it is read, so the check on the value that holds such a sequence does not see into it, and each is checked as it is decoded, at every depth. A sequence that pydicom decodes as it is read needs no such check, because it is written from its decoded items, and so does a raw value of VR SQ from Explicit VR, which pydicom decodes in place when it is first accessed, once its items are shown to fill it. A raw value that pydicom converts later, when it is first accessed, even of a sequence that its dictionary knows, is decoded and checked here instead, since pydicom's conversion leaves out the same bytes without an error; this also searches a sequence that pydicom cannot look up when it raises its validation errors, rather than refusing it. The cost is that pydicom's writer leaves out retired group lengths, so a UN sequence that has them in its items, at any depth, is refused: it is removed where a rule removes it or a sequence that holds it, and otherwise its instance is sequestered. The character set matters because the engine reads, and writes, the text in the decoded items.
  - Decoding every value of VR UN, or read without a VR, with its attribute's dictionary VR was rejected. pydicom checks a value as it decodes it, and can raise an error or a warning that quotes it, as it does for a value of VR US of the wrong length and for an invalid UID, which D-016 forbids; and decoding would change values that other rules act on (Architecture item 2), such as a Patient's Name stored as UN. Only a sequence can hold private attributes. pydicom's dictionary is consulted as well as the pinned one because pydicom decodes the sequences that it lists, including the retired Curve Referenced Overlay Sequence (50xx,2600) in each group of its repeating group.
  - pydicom reads the items of a sequence, with their character sets, as it decodes the sequence, so an item's character set is refused by the path of the sequence: raising its validation errors, pydicom raises its error before any item can be examined. Reading the text with another encoding than the one given was rejected because the engine reads, and writes, that text. Running the search within the engine's redaction of pydicom's diagnostics, which summarises each warning and log record only in the thread that decodes and only while it decodes, was chosen over catching warnings around each value that pydicom decodes, because Python's warning filters are process-wide and not thread-safe to change and restore, and pydicom also logs each warning.
  - E.3.10 retains the private attributes "known by the de-identifier to be safe", and Architecture item 6 records the basis for retaining each. Applying the option by removing every private attribute was rejected: the output would claim an option whose purpose, such as keeping the SUV factors of Table E.3.10-1, it does not serve. Refusing it left the option's rules to a later change, which examines private sequences as E.3.10 requires without changing removal under the Basic Profile (D-035).
- **Tests.** Every element of an odd group is removed, at the top level and in standard sequences at every depth, including private creators with and without elements, private data elements without a creator, each kind of element that PS3.5 does not allow, and retired group lengths, and each is listed by its path in the data set's order; a private sequence is removed whole and listed without its contents; nothing else changes, in the copy or the source; files in Implicit and Explicit VR Little Endian, with private sequences of defined and undefined length, keep no private attribute; a sequence that pydicom reads as UN from Implicit VR Little Endian, or that Explicit VR Little Endian carries as UN, is searched, decoded in its items' character set, and holds its decoded items only where something is removed from it, and so is a sequence of defined or undefined length nested in its items at depth 1 or 2, with items of defined or undefined length; a sequence of a repeating group is searched; a sequence that pydicom cannot look up when it raises its validation errors is searched; a UN value that does not decode to the same bytes, a malformed raw value of a sequence that pydicom knows, stored as UN in Explicit VR or without a VR in Implicit VR Little Endian, a malformed sequence nested in its items at depth 1 or 2, whether or not the sequences and items around it are of undefined length or hold a private attribute, and a sequence that cannot be decoded, are refused by path without quoting a value; a Specific Character Set that pydicom does not map as given, such as an unknown one, a Python codec name, a misspelt term, or ISO_IR 192 with a code extension, or that is stored with another VR, at the top level or in an item, in memory, in a sequence read from Implicit or Explicit VR Little Endian, or in the items of a UN value or of a sequence nested in them, is refused by path, whether or not pydicom raises its validation errors, without a warning, log record, message, or traceback that quotes it, and one that pydicom maps as given is accepted; a standard sequence that Explicit VR Little Endian stores as OB or UT, at the top level or in an item, is refused by path; a UN value whose attribute is not a sequence, or is not in the dictionary, is not searched, and one read from Explicit VR Little Endian, such as a value of VR US of the wrong length, an invalid UID, or a person name, is left as it was read, without an error or a warning, whether or not pydicom raises its validation errors; a standard sequence that pydicom left unread under `defer_size`, from a file or a buffer, is searched and its private attributes removed, a malformed one is refused by path, and one whose source has gone is refused by path; no private value is decoded; and a policy that gives Private Attributes another action without Retain Safe Private is refused.

### D-029: Runtime environments

- **Decision.**
  - Each run records its runtime environment as provenance, apart from the method digest (D-024): in Software Versions of its Contributing Equipment Sequence item (D-012), and in a runtime section of its release report, which holds the same values as structured fields rather than one display string: `pymedphys_version`, `python_implementation`, `python_version`, `pydicom_version`, and `tomlkit_version`. The section contains no key, path, or source value (D-016).
  - An execution fingerprint, such as the SHA-256 of a canonical form of the method digest and the runtime section under a format label of its own, may later be added to the release report alone, once something needs it. None is planned, and none is written to DICOM output.
  - PyMedPhys keeps declaring its dependencies as ranges, such as `pydicom>=3.0.2` (D-002), so that it installs as an ordinary library. It does not pin them exactly to keep the method digest stable, since the runtime environment is not part of the method, apart from the text finder's runtime under Clean Pixel Data (D-024).
  - The tested environment is what continuous integration runs. The de-identification tests are unit tests, which run with the versions of pydicom, tomlkit, and every other dependency that `uv.lock` locks: on Python 3.12, 3.13, and 3.14, on Ubuntu, Windows, and macOS, in the full matrix that pushes to `main`, releases, and pull requests labelled `full-test` run; on Python 3.14 on Ubuntu for merge groups and other pull requests; on Python 3.14 on Ubuntu with only the `dicom` extra installed; and on Python 3.12 with the declared minimum versions of NumPy and pandas. Wherever the unit tests run, which includes every pull request that changes the de-identification package, its tests, `pyproject.toml`, or `uv.lock`, and every release before it is published, the de-identification tests (the `test_deidentify_*` modules in `tests/dicom` and the table generator's tests in `tests/dev/test_deid_tables.py`) also have coverage on Python 3.14 on Ubuntu, with every other dependency at its locked version: with pydicom 3.0.2, the lowest release that the declared minimum allows, and with the latest release of pydicom, which continuous integration resolves from the package index each time, as the [workflow guide](workflows.md) describes. A minimum or latest version identical to the locked version reuses the normal Ubuntu/Python 3.14 run when that cell runs the complete selection without extra pytest arguments and the lock identifies one registry version. Its imported-version metadata and JUnit report must be present, both compatibility jobs record their evidence references, and the whole unit-test workflow must succeed. Other requested versions run the complete de-identification selection separately; if both roles request the same version distinct from locked, both run. A release of pydicom known to change behaviour that the engine depends on joins these runs, and another dependency is tested at other versions only where its behaviour can plausibly change the output. No other version of pydicom or tomlkit is tested, so releases of pydicom between the minimum and the latest, and releases of tomlkit other than the locked one, are supported by declaration, not by test.
- **Deferred.**
  - Warn, in a run's output and its release report, when the run uses a version of Python, pydicom, or tomlkit outside the tested environment, and refuse to run only in an environment known to be incompatible or in an explicit strict or validated mode.
  - Publish with each release a constraints file of the exact versions that continuous integration tested, so that a deployment of the command line that must be reproducible or validated can install exactly those versions, while library users keep the declared ranges.
- **Rationale.** Runtime software can change what the engine sees: pydicom's data dictionary decides whether an attribute in an Implicit VR file is read as typed values or, when the dictionary does not know it, as UN bytes, which date shifting, VR checks, and text cleaning then treat differently. That risk is managed by testing the supported environments and recording the one that ran, not by the method's identity, which would otherwise change with every upgrade (D-024). The declared minimum and the latest release are the oldest and newest versions of pydicom that an installation can resolve, and the latest is what a new installation gets unless another package constrains it, so these runs test what a new installation uses as well as what `uv.lock` pins, and resolving the latest each time tests a new pydicom release before `uv.lock` adopts it. Separate, readable fields let a custodian see what ran. Pinning the library's dependencies exactly would make PyMedPhys hard to install beside other packages, for every user, to serve the deployments that need an exact environment, which a constraints file serves instead.
- **Tests.** The release report's runtime section has each field, with the values that Software Versions records; and continuous integration runs the de-identification tests in the environments listed.

### D-030: Independent conformance validation

- **Decision.**
  - Check the output's conformance with validators that share nothing with the engine: `dciodvfy` and `dcentvfy` from dicom3tools (BSD-3-Clause), and dicom-validator (MIT), pinned in the `tests` extra and reading the DocBook source of the edition that the engine's tables are generated from (D-001). None is installed for users.
  - Validate each released instance's input as well as its output, and charge to the de-identification only what the output gives more often than its input. Each introduced finding is explained by an entry of a reviewed file of known differences, whose category is `profile`, `validator`, or `engine`, and which names any property of the output that its explanation rests on, which the comparison then checks; is not comparable, where the validator could not read the attribute that holds it in the input; or is unexplained, which fails the comparison. An `engine` entry records a defect of the output until it is fixed.
  - Keep validator output to tags, message types, and the validators' module and entity names. A message's text before its first value, and the reason after it, are kept only where they are made of strings compiled into the validator, and a module, entity, keyword, or IOD name only where the validator holds it, since a value can span lines; any other message or line is counted without its text, so the results hold no value or path (D-016).
  - Run the comparison in continuous integration over the synthetic corpus, under each preset, on every pull request that runs the unit tests, and offer the same comparison as a development command for any release with a SOP Instance UID mapping, such as the MIDI benchmark's (D-018). The [validation guide](deidentification-dicom-validation.md) describes both.
- **Rationale.** The engine's own check against the IOD before release (D-020) shares the engine's reading of PS3.3, so a misreading would pass both; independent validators do not share it. Validators also report what the input already lacks, and the synthetic corpus's own inputs give thousands of findings, so an absolute verdict on the output would charge the engine with its input's defects; the difference does not. Pinning the edition makes dicom-validator check the same standard as the tables, and Ubuntu's dicom3tools package is pinned so that a new snapshot cannot change the results unannounced, at the cost that its dictionary lags the standard; the job reports the newest snapshot so that the lag stays visible. Rejected: requiring the output to pass outright, which such inputs would fail, and reporting the validators' messages as they print them, which quotes values.
- **Tests.** Unit tests reduce validator output to findings without values and compare findings; and continuous integration runs the validators over the synthetic corpus under each preset, with the validators required.

### D-031: Answer-key checks that released files fail by design

- **Decision.** Of the checks of a MIDI-B answer key on the instances that a run releases (D-018), these fail by design, and are not engine findings:
  - Longitudinal Temporal Information Modified (0028,0303), which a run sets to `REMOVED`, as PS3.15 E.2 requires where no Retain Longitudinal Temporal Information Option is applied (D-012). The key's `text_retained` check of it, scored under its TCIA review category, expects another value, so every instance that `basic` or `basic-clean-descriptors` releases and the key checks there fails it.
  - A `tag_retained` or `text_notnull` check of an attribute that the instance's IOD requires, such as KVP (0018,0060), Acquisition Number (0020,0012), Position Reference Indicator (0020,1040), or Image Type (0008,0008), where the source lacks the attribute or its value. The engine keeps the first two and Image Type as numbers and codes (D-022), and empties Position Reference Indicator where it is Type 2 (X/Z/D); and it sequesters any instance that it would write without a Type 1 or 2 attribute that its source held, or with a Type 1 attribute emptied (D-020). So a released instance fails such a check only where its source already did, and the run reports the source's gap rather than filling it (MIDI-BP-03).
  - A `text_retained` check of Smallest Image Pixel Value (0028,0106) where the source writes it with VR US although its Pixel Representation (0028,0103) is 1, which makes it SS (PS3.3 C.7.6.3). The rule for a number keeps it (D-022), and the released instance holds the same bytes as its source, which fails the check too.
- **Rationale.** The answer keys follow TCIA's curation of each source collection where it differs from the Basic Profile (D-018), and a TCIA review category marks a check that comes from that curation alone. E.2 names `REMOVED` as the only value for a run that removes or replaces dates rather than modifying them, so writing what TCIA writes would misstate what was done. The MIDI-B challenge's report (Pei L et al., *Medical Image De-Identification Benchmark Challenge*, 31 July 2025, [arXiv:2507.23608](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.23608), Section 4.1) notes that its validation script counted non-compliance already present in the input against every participant. A value that the engine made up for an attribute that the source lacks, such as a KVP or an Image Type, would misdescribe the acquisition. Rewriting a kept value's VR or bytes to suit a reading that the source contradicts would change data that the Profile keeps; how the key's text reads such a value is not known from the run's value-free summary, so the difference is attributed to the source's encoding, not to the engine.
- **Tests.** Unit tests show the rules keep KVP, Acquisition Number, Image Type, and Smallest Image Pixel Value, and give Position Reference Indicator X/Z/D; that every released instance of the synthetic collection has `REMOVED` (PS3.15-E.2-01); and that an instance written without a required attribute that its source held is sequestered (MIDI-BP-03).

### D-032: Residual text explained by kept vocabulary

- **Decision.**
  - A text finding inside the data set, which D-027 sends to QC review, is not a reason for review where the kept value it is found in is standard vocabulary or an equipment name, in one of four ways:
    - **Standard term.** It is found in an attribute that the pinned dictionary gives VR CS, and every value of that element is an Enumerated Value or Defined Term of that attribute in the pinned PS3.3 or, for Body Part Examined (0018,0015), whose Defined Terms PS3.3 leaves to PS3.16 Annex L, in the pinned Annex L. An element with one value that is not a term is not exempt, since the search records only the first match of a source in each element.
    - **Standard code.** It is found in the Code Value, Coding Scheme Designator, Coding Scheme Version, Code Meaning, Long Code Value, or URN Code Value of an item whose Coding Scheme Designator is one that the pinned PS3.16 Table 8-1 lists, such as DCM, SCT, LN, or UCUM. A designator that the table does not list, such as a local one starting with 99, gives no exemption, so the meaning of a local procedure code that holds a description stays under review.
    - **Equipment.** It is found in Manufacturer (0008,0070), Manufacturer's Model Name (0008,1090), or Software Versions (0018,1020), which the Basic Profile keeps, and its source is a private data element or itself names equipment: one of those three, or Secondary Capture Device Manufacturer (0018,1016), Secondary Capture Device Manufacturer's Model Name (0018,1018), or Secondary Capture Device Software Versions (0018,1019). Any other source, such as Institution Name, Station Name, or a private creator that Table E.3.10-1 does not list, stays under review, since an in-house device or program can carry the institution's name (D-022).
    - **Vendor private creator.** Its source is a private creator element, (gggg,0010) to (gggg,00FF) of an odd group, or Private Creator Reference (0008,0302) in an item of Private Data Element Characteristics Sequence (0008,0300), which names one, and every value collected for the subject at that place is, without trailing spaces and NULs, exactly one of the private creators that the pinned PS3.15 Table E.3.10-1 lists, wherever in the data set it is found. A private creator that the table does not list, such as an in-house or research block's, or one in another case, stays under review, as does a finding whose source is a private data element.
  - Nothing else changes: a person name, UID, date, datetime, or direct identifier found anywhere, and any value found outside the data set, still withholds the file, and other text findings still go to review. There is no setting that adds or removes an exemption.
  - Kept values are read from the written file's bytes as ISO 646 text, never decoded by pydicom; a finding in an element with any other byte is not exempt under any of the four, even where the file declares a character set that holds it. Each exempted finding stays in the search, which the QC pack lists with its excerpt (D-016), and the release condition records why each was exempted.
  - The terms are generated from the pinned PS3.3 with the other tables (D-001): each list titled "Enumerated Values" or "Defined Terms", including "Retired Defined Terms" and lists for one value, in an attribute's row or in a section that its row refers to, and each list whose title names attributes by tag, which gives its terms to those attributes; a tag after "if" or "when" in a title is a condition. Terms are kept for the attributes of VR CS, apart from repeating groups, where VR CS can hold them. In 2026d this gives 2,549 terms of 655 attributes. Body Part Examined's terms are generated from the Body Part Examined column of Annex L's Tables L-1, L-2, and L-3, for human, large animal, and small animal use, skipping rows that give a code but no term: 347 terms in 2026d. Terms that PS3.3 gives only by reference to any other part are not generated, so findings in such attributes stay under review.
  - The maintainer decided on 11 October 2026 to exempt both standard vocabulary and equipment names, over exempting only one of them or keeping the holds with a list of reviewed texts, and to exempt the private creators that Table E.3.10-1 lists while still searching for them, over keeping them under review, no longer searching for any private creator, or exempting every Coding Scheme Designator.
- **Open question.** Whether release reports count exempted findings by exemption and place is not yet decided; the QC pack lists each one.
- **Rationale.** On the MIDI-B benchmark of 10 October 2026, residual text findings held 96% of the withheld instances in the Validation subset and 95% in the Test subset. Judged by where they were found, though not by their values, which the benchmark does not report, almost all were a removed value equal to a coded term, a defined term, or a device name that the profile keeps elsewhere, such as a removed Scatter Correction Method found in a kept Collimator Type, or a vendor's private element found in Manufacturer's Model Name. Such holds put the review effort on findings that reveal nothing. A kept CS value made only of its attribute's own terms is set by the standard's vocabulary, so a removed value found in it reveals nothing that the term does not. Exempting every CS value was rejected: sites write their own text in CS attributes, such as a procedure in Body Part Examined, which stays under review unless every value is one of Annex L's terms. D-027's example of `CHEST` found in `CHESTABDOMEN` is released, since `CHESTABDOMEN` is an Annex L term, and a removed value found inside a term reveals no more than the term does. Table 8-1 lists the schemes that the standard registers, so a code of one is shared vocabulary, while a local scheme's meanings are the site's own text. The equipment names are kept by the Basic Profile, and a vendor's private element or another equipment attribute holding the same name adds nothing to them; an institution's name in them is what the profile removes elsewhere, so that stays under review. Deciding D-027's open question broadly, so that a removed value equal to a kept one is not searched, was rejected because it would also release a local procedure code's meaning that copies a free-text description; and a list of reviewed texts, like the reviewed ROI names (D-009), was rejected for now because the benchmark's score would rest on a manual step. The residual risk is free text that a site writes as the Code Meaning of a code of a registered scheme, or in an equipment name with a vendor's private copy, which is released without review. With the three exemptions and a separate fix for MR instances, the benchmark run of 11 October 2026 released 86.7% of the Validation subset's instances and 84.0% of the Test subset's, against 16.0% and 22.3% in the run of 10 October without either; the independent validators (D-030) reported nothing unexplained in the released instances, and NCI's validation script found no failure in any HIPAA category. Exempting standard vocabulary alone or equipment names alone was estimated to release about a third of each subset, since most held instances had findings of both kinds. The private creators that Table E.3.10-1 lists are vendors' labels that the standard itself prints, so finding one reveals the vendor, which Manufacturer keeps; the benchmark found removed private creators in kept local Coding Scheme Designators, though it does not report which. In the benchmark run of 11 October 2026 on both subsets, this exemption released no further instance, so every instance that it could affect is also held by another finding, or its private creator is not exactly one that the table lists. (0008,0302) holds the same label as the creator it names, and is removed with its sequence (D-028), so under Retain Safe Private (D-035), where the creator of a block that the sequence describes is kept, it would otherwise send every such instance to review for a label the standard prints; it takes the exemption under the same condition, so a creator that the table does not list in it stays under review. Still searching for them keeps each match in the QC pack, whereas no longer searching for private creators would also stop searching for an in-house block's, which can name the institution, and exempting every designator would release a local designator that abbreviates the institution.
- **Tests.** A removed value found in a CS value whose every value is a term of its attribute is released, with the finding kept in the search and its exemption recorded, while one found in an element with a value that is not a term, or that is a term only of another attribute, or in Body Part Examined that is not one of Annex L's terms, goes to review, while one found in Body Part Examined that is, as in D-027's example, is released; a person name found in a term still withholds the file; a removed value found in a code item under a designator of Table 8-1 is released, and one under a designator that the table does not list, under one in the wrong case, or under none, and one in a code element outside an item, goes to review, as does a local designator found in a kept local designator; a removed value found in an equipment name is released when its source is a private data element or an equipment attribute, and goes to review when its source is Institution Name, Station Name, or a private creator, or when it is found in other kept text; a finding in a kept value outside ISO 646 is not exempt, by a term, a code, an equipment name, or a vendor's private creator, in a file that declares its character set; the Basic Profile keeps the equipment names; the terms generated from the pinned edition include spot terms from rows, from sections that rows refer to, and from lists that name their attribute, and none for attributes of other VRs, and Body Part Examined's terms come from PS3.16 Annex L, not PS3.3; and generation keeps only terms that VR CS can hold, gives a list that names an attribute to it rather than to the row, treats tags after "when" as conditions, leaves out lists with other titles, attributes of other VRs, and repeating groups, and fails on a page that gives no terms; and Annex L's terms are generated from each table's Body Part Examined column, skipping rows without a term, and fail where a table has no such column, a term is one that VR CS cannot hold, or no table gives a term; and a removed private creator that Table E.3.10-1 lists is released wherever it is found, while one that the table does not list, one in another case, a private data element holding the same text, and a place whose pooled values are not all listed creators go to review; and so is a removed Private Creator Reference of (0008,0300) that the table lists, found in a kept private creator, while one that the table does not list, and the same value outside that sequence, go to review.

### D-033: Option sets chosen per run

- **Decision.**
  - A run's policy is that of a preset or of a custom option set, never both. The library's `deidentify` takes `options`, the names of options of Table E.1-1 or of the Clean Pixel Data and Clean Recognizable Visual Features Options (D-011), in place of `preset`, and the command takes `--option NAME`, once for each option, in place of `--preset`; with neither, a run applies `basic`. A custom option set is composed and validated as D-011 describes, so a conflict between two of its options is rejected, including those that `tps-import` resolves, and its policy can claim conformance.
  - A custom option set is enabled only while `basic` is enabled, since every policy applies the Basic Profile, and each of its options is enabled. An option is enabled once its behaviour is implemented and validated for the documented input scope, as a preset is, including a conformance statement for the option alone that lists nothing under "Not yet described"; only an option that the engine applies can be enabled (D-011). No option is enabled yet. Enabling a preset enables none of its options, and enabling options enables no preset.
  - A run under a custom option set that is not enabled is refused before anything is read, with a message that names the Basic Profile where `basic` is not enabled, each of its options that is not, and the options that are. The command line treats a preset given with options, and an option that is none of these, as argument errors, and a policy that is not enabled, or whose options conflict, as a run that could not start; the library raises `ValueError` for a preset given with options, `TypeError` for options given as a single string, and `DeidentifyError` where the policy is not enabled or cannot be composed. The ROI name arguments apply under a custom option set exactly where it selects Clean Descriptors (D-009).
  - A run under a custom option set is marked, reported, and described as one under a preset is, with no preset: its De-identification Method names a custom option set (D-012), its release report's `policy` section gives no preset and the options, and its conformance statement is for a custom option set.
  - Nothing reaches the `pymedphys` command line before the first supported release (Scope).
  - The maintainer decided on 12 October 2026 to let a run choose its own set of options, over keeping presets only.
- **Rationale.** On 12 October 2026 the maintainer asked for every de-identification option that PS3.15 describes. Table E.1-1's ten options, two of which exclude each other (E.3.6), allow 768 sets before conflicts between options are rejected. A preset for each set that someone needs would multiply names that say less than the options do, while naming the options states exactly what is applied, as the naming principle under Presets asks of a preset named after a profile. Keeping presets only, as D-011 had it, would leave five of the ten options in no preset, and the others only in the combinations that the presets fix. Enabling each option once, rather than each set, is sound for the actions: composition rejects every attribute to which two selected options give different actions, and any conflict among several options is one between two of them, so each attribute of a set that composes takes the action of one option, or the same action from each option that gives one. In the engine as built, each item that a statement lists under "Not yet described" belongs to every policy, to one selected option, or to `tps-import` alone, so while the statements of `basic` and of each option alone are complete, so is that of any set of them that composes; the tests check each. Requiring `basic` keeps a custom option set from applying the Basic Profile before the preset that applies it alone is validated, and a set of no options is the Basic Profile under another name. Refusing a preset given with options, rather than adding the options to the preset's, keeps each policy one that its name or its list of options states in full.
- **Tests.** No option is enabled yet, and every enabled option is one that the engine applies; a custom option set of enabled options is selected, with no preset and its options in the table's order, including the empty set as the Basic Profile alone, and is refused while `basic` or any of its options is not enabled, naming each, also where a preset with the same options is enabled; a preset given with options, an unknown option, a single string, both Retain Longitudinal Temporal Information Options, and conflicting options are refused even with every option enabled; an enabled option that the engine does not apply is still refused by the engine; the command builds the transform of its options, takes the ROI name arguments only with Clean Descriptors among them, rejects a preset given with options and an unknown option as argument errors that quote nothing, exits with status 3 before loading anything when an option is not enabled, and names every option in its help; a custom option set runs end to end, with no preset in the release report and a statement for a custom option set; the library refuses a preset given with options and a single string, reads its options once, and releases the same outcomes under a custom option set of Clean Descriptors as under `basic-clean-descriptors`; and each enabled option alone has a complete statement, and an enabled custom option set's statement claims its options.

### D-034: Cleaners for C, and AE titles under Retain Device Identity

- **Decision.**
  - C is settled by reviewed cleaners, each for the attributes that one option gives C, with its method in a module of its own, and all listed in one registry (`cleaners.py`). Each entry names the option, the attributes it cleans by their tags in Table E.1-1, the VRs it reads them in, and its method, which the conformance statement gives. A run applies the cleaners of the options its policy selects to each instance's edits, wherever the attributes are in the data set, before descriptor cleaning (D-009). A cleaner that may write a value equal to its source marks the attribute as retained, so that the residual search leaves the source value out, as it does a ROI Name that descriptor cleaning keeps (D-027). Most cleaners clean each value on its own, from the value and the run's key. A cleaner whose values depend on each other or on the subject, such as one that moves an instance's dates by its subject's offset once they are in its local time, cleans the whole instance instead: it is given the source value of every attribute that it is to settle in the instance, in file order and in any VR that it reads, bytes included, with the run's key and the subject's identity, and returns, for each attribute that it settles, the values written and whether they are the source's own; an attribute that it leaves out stays pending.
  - An attribute given C that no cleaner settles stays pending, and so does one whose value was not collected or was read in a VR that its cleaner does not read. Under Clean Descriptors, descriptor cleaning cleans it where it is a free-text descriptor whose values it can clean, and otherwise gives it the action that the policy gives it without that option, where that action is not C (D-009); otherwise the instance is sequestered. Under a Retain option, writing it with the option's code would claim a retention that did not happen (D-011), and writing it without the code would claim that the Basic Profile removed values that the option keeps (D-012).
  - The first cleaner is Retain Device Identity's. Table E.1-1 gives C under that option to eleven attributes of VR AE, which name an application entity or a network: Retrieve AE Title (0008,0054), Station AE Title (0008,0055), Network ID (0008,1000), Scheduled Study Location AE Title (0032,1021), Scheduled Station AE Title (0040,0001), Performed Station AE Title (0040,0241), Selector AE Value (0072,005E), Receiving AE (0074,1234), Requesting AE (0074,1236), Originator (2100,0070), and Destination AE (2100,0140). Each value becomes a token of the run's key: `AE` followed by the first 14 characters of the base32 encoding of the value's HMAC-SHA256 in the `ae-title` domain, once the leading and trailing spaces that VR AE makes insignificant are removed, which is 16 characters, the most that VR AE allows. Each value of a multi-valued attribute gets its own token, an empty value stays empty, and an attribute with no value is written with none. Within a run, an application entity has the same token in every attribute and instance; across runs, whose keys differ, tokens are unrelated (D-004). The token's prefix and length are parameters of generated values, and its domain is one of the key's derivation domains, so the method digest covers both (D-024).
  - The conformance statement gives, for each selected option that has a cleaner, the attributes that the policy gives C and the cleaner cleans, and its method. It lists the manner of cleaning as not yet described only for an attribute given C that no cleaner cleans and that Clean Descriptors' fallback does not settle.
- **Residual findings.** Under Retain Device Identity, a station's AE title often equals its Station Name (0008,1010), which the option keeps, so the residual search finds the AE title's source value in the kept Station Name; D-042 decides when a finding inside a value that an option keeps is a reason for QC review.
- **Rationale.** E.3.8 has AE titles "Cleaned, rather than just Kept, since they may contain embedded institution or person identification information", and C replaces a value "with values of similar meaning known not to contain identifying information and consistent with the VR" (Table E.1-1a). A keyed token keeps what the option needs of an AE title, which application entity an instance names, consistently across the run's instances and attributes, such as a Performed Station AE Title that names the same entity as a Station AE Title, while it holds nothing of the value but a keyed digest, as replacement UIDs (D-003) and patient pseudonyms (D-005) do. Removing the attributes was rejected, since the option retains the device's identity; keeping them, since E.3.8 does not allow it; and one dummy value, since every application entity would then look the same. A reviewed list of AE titles to keep, as the reviewed-names list keeps ROI Names (D-009), may be added later; a token needs no review. Seventy bits of digest make a collision among a thousand distinct application entities less likely than one in 10^15. A registry, rather than a branch of the transform for each option, lets each option's cleaner be reviewed, tested, and described in the statement from one entry, and lets the options be built in parallel.
- **Tests.** A token is a valid AE value of 16 characters, derived as the conformance statement says, the same for a value with or without leading and trailing spaces, and different for another value or another key; each value of a multi-valued attribute gets its own token, and an empty one stays empty; no two cleaners clean the same attribute, each cleans only attributes that its option gives C, in the VRs that the pinned dictionary gives them, and every attribute that Retain Device Identity gives C has a cleaner; cleaners settle only the pending C of their attributes, leave one whose value was not collected or was read in another VR pending, and leave a retaining cleaner's attributes out of the residual search; a cleaner cleans single values or the whole instance, not both; a cleaner of the whole instance is given every pending value in the VRs it reads, with the key and the subject's identity, and settles only those it returns, leaving out of the search those it says are the source's own; under Retain Device Identity a plan's Station Name and Device Serial Number are kept and its AE titles are written as tokens, the same in every instance of the run, with the option's code; under Clean Descriptors as well, AE titles are cleaned before descriptors fall back, and the instance claims both options; an instance with an attribute that Retain Patient Characteristics gives C, which no cleaner cleans, is sequestered; the statement for Retain Device Identity gives the cleaner's attributes and method and lists nothing as not yet described, a preset without such an option describes no cleaner, and `tps-import` still lists the manner of cleaning its dates and patient characteristics; and changing the token's prefix or length changes the method digest.

### D-035: Reviewed safe private rules

- **Decision.**
  - The L2 file `safe_private.toml`, curated by hand for the pinned edition, gives a rule to every row of PS3.15 Table E.3.10-1 and to each reviewed addition. A rule is keyed by private creator, odd group, and element within the block, so it applies in whichever block its creator reserves (Architecture item 6). A creator matches exactly, apart from trailing spaces and NULs, as D-032 matches the table's private creators.
  - Table E.1-1 gives retained private attributes C, so each rule says how a retained value is handled:
    - `numeric`: FD, FL, SL, SS, SV, UL, US, UV, and AT values, and IS and DS values that are valid numbers of their VR, are kept;
    - `code`: CS values made only of the characters that VR CS allows are kept;
    - `uid`: UI values are replaced, as every other UID is (D-003), and a value read without its VR, from Implicit VR Little Endian or as UN, is removed, since the engine replaces only a UID that it reads as UI;
    - `text`: LO, LT, SH, ST, UC, and UT values are cleaned as descriptors are (D-009) under Clean Descriptors, as below, and removed without it;
    - `sequence`: the items of an SQ value are parsed, and each nested attribute is handled on its own merits (E.3.10), so the sequence is kept as a container only where the source holds its items;
    - `remove`: the attribute is not retained.
  - A rule takes the handling of its VR unless its note says why not, and every other VR, alternatives such as OW/OB, and a row for which the table gives no VR can only be removed. In 2026d, the 479 rows give 280 numeric rules, 65 code, 55 sequence, 44 text, 7 UID, and 28 that remove: 25 for OB, OW, OW/OB, or no VR, and 3 numeric rows that can hold absolute times, Mid Scan Time (0019,xx24) of GEMS_ACQU_01 and the two timestamp elements (0149,xx02) and (0149,xx03) of SIEMENS Ultrasound SC2000.
  - A reviewed addition, for a private attribute that the table does not list, must retain its value and cites the public document that shows it is safe, such as a vendor's DICOM Conformance Statement, with a note. The file has none.
  - TCIA's Private Tag Dictionary, the list that TCIA uses to curate its collections under this Option, is a second list that a run may choose instead of `safe_private.toml`, which stays the default. It is not a source of reviewed additions.
    - The file `TCIAPrivateTagKB-02-01-2024-formatted.csv` ships unchanged in `_tcia/`, with TCIA's attribution and the CC BY 3.0 licence under which TCIA publishes its site in `LICENSE-CC-BY-3.0.txt`, and the package's licence expression includes CC-BY-3.0. `tcia_private_tags.PIN` records its URL, length, and SHA-256, and `load_tcia_private_tags` refuses a copy that does not match. `pymedphys dev tcia-check` downloads TCIA's file and compares its length and SHA-256, and the shipped copy's, with the pin, and checks that TCIA's page still links that file and no edition not yet reviewed, and links that licence. The TCIA Edition Check workflow runs it each month, opening or updating one issue when it finds a change or cannot check, and on pull requests that change the dictionary, its loader, or the check, or that carry the `check-upstream` label, where its result is advisory for the reviewer rather than a required check. Merge groups do not run it, so TCIA's availability cannot block them.
    - Each row gives an attribute's path, from the top level down through the sequences that hold it, with its VR and a disposition. The loader reads every row: of 8,787, 3,836 keep their attribute, and 6,568 are at the top level. A creator matches as in `safe_private.toml`; rows that write a creator in upper case match only that.
    - A disposition of `k` is a basis for retaining an attribute, not a handling: a value that the dictionary keeps still passes the handling of its VR, as a rule of `safe_private.toml` would give it, so its OB, OW, and UN values are removed, and its text is cleaned only under Clean Descriptors, as below.
    - The rules of a run that chooses it are made from the rows at the top level whose creator is compared as below (`tcia_private_tags.tcia_safe_private_rules`). A row below the top level makes none, so an attribute that only such a row keeps is removed, and a rule made from a top-level row applies wherever its creator's block is, as a rule of `safe_private.toml` does. The dictionary gives an attribute a row for each VR that TCIA has seen it with, such as DS with OB and UN, so a rule is chosen by VR: an attribute written with a VR takes the rule of the row for that VR, and has none where no row gives that VR; one whose VR is not written, in Implicit VR Little Endian or as UN, takes the rule of the one VR other than OB, UN, or none that its rows give, and has none where they give several or none. A kept row's rule has a single value as its VM, or the handling and VM of the rule of `safe_private.toml` for the same creator, group, element, and VR where there is one. Any other row's rule removes its attribute, as does a VR whose rows disagree.
    - A run chooses it in place of `safe_private.toml` by `SafePrivateList.TCIA`, which the transform, the command's `--safe-private-list tcia`, and the library's `safe_private_list="tcia"` take, and which is refused without the Option. Declarations in (0008,0300) apply under either list, as below. The run's method digest and release report record which list it used (D-024), and its conformance statement names the file, its date of retrieval, and its SHA-256, gives TCIA's attribution, and lists each rule that can retain an attribute with TCIA's name for it. Benchmark results are to report the two lists separately; the MIDI-B benchmark cannot yet score this Option.
  - `pymedphys._dicom.deidentify.safe_private.load_safe_private_rules` rejects a file that covers another edition, lacks a rule for a row of the table, gives a row another VR or VM than the table, gives a handling that cannot check a value of its VR, departs from its VR's handling without a note, or adds a row of the table. So a new edition fails to load until each new or changed row has been reviewed.
  - The engine applies the rules under the Option, at the top level and in the items of every sequence that is kept (`retained_private.py`):
    - A data element's rule is the one for the private creator that reserves its block in the same data set or item. A creator is compared only where its value, without trailing spaces and NULs, is printable ISO 646 without a backslash, written as LO or without a VR, and the loader rejects a rule whose creator is outside that repertoire.
    - A rule settles its attribute only where the attribute is written with the rule's VR, or without one, in Implicit VR Little Endian or as UN, and its value passes its handling's check, with a number of values that the rule's VM allows. Any other is removed.
