    - A private creator is kept only where an attribute of its block in the same data set or item is kept, replaced, or cleaned.
    - A kept private value whose VR is not written, in Implicit VR Little Endian or as UN, is read by its rule's VR, and a kept private creator as LO, so that each kept creator, code string, and number string is among the retained strings that the QC pack lists for review (D-017), as it would be with its VR written. A sequence holds no value of its own, so it is given no VR. Kept binary numbers (FD, FL, SL, SS, SV, UL, US, UV, and AT) and kept sequences are not among the retained strings, as no binary value or sequence is (D-017): a binary value's check is only that it is a whole number of values within its rule's VM, so its retention rests on the reviewed rule alone.
    - A private sequence that no rule lists is parsed, as E.3.10 asks of one "not known to always only contain safe content", where its block's creator is compared: it is kept as a container, so that each attribute in its items, standard or private, takes its own rule at every depth, and the instance is planned again with it removed, with everything in it, where nothing in its items other than a private creator is kept, replaced, or cleaned. Its creator is then kept only for what its block keeps. A private sequence in a block without a creator that is compared is removed with everything in it, as is any other private attribute there. A kept creator that no rule names is among the retained strings that the QC pack lists for review.
    - An instance's Private Data Element Characteristics Sequence (0008,0300), at the top level in the SOP Common Module, is read for what it declares about the blocks that its items name by Private Group Reference (0008,0301) and Private Creator Reference (0008,0302), the creator compared as above, wherever such a block is:
      - An attribute that holds a value is declared safe where Block Identifying Information Status (0008,0303) is SAFE, or MIXED with the attribute listed in Nonidentifying Private Elements (0008,0304). It is declared possibly identifying where the status is UNSAFE, or MIXED without the attribute listed, or where Identifying Private Elements (0008,0306) of an item of Deidentification Action Sequence (0008,0305) lists it. Where items disagree, possibly identifying wins. An item that names a block but whose status is not one of those Enumerated Values, or whose list of attributes cannot be read, declares every attribute of the block possibly identifying.
      - An attribute declared possibly identifying is removed, even where a rule would keep it.
      - An attribute declared safe that no rule lists takes the handling of its VR, as a rule for a row of Table E.3.10-1 does: its VR as written, or, where the file does not write one, the VR that Private Data Element Value Representation (0008,030A) defines for it in Private Data Element Definition Sequence (0008,0310). It is removed where neither gives a VR, the two differ, or two definitions of it in (0008,0300), in one item or in two, disagree in VR or VM, or one names it but cannot be read, in Explicit VR as in Implicit. Its number of values must fit the VM that Private Data Element Value Multiplicity (0008,0309) defines with that VR, or a single value where (0008,0310) gives none, or gives a VM that PS3.6 has no form for, such as 1 to 7 in steps of 2. So a declared number or code string is kept, a declared UID replaced, declared text cleaned as a rule's text is, and every value of another VR, such as a date, a name, or OB, OW, or UN data, removed.
      - A private sequence is parsed as above whatever (0008,0300) declares of it.
      - The values read are those of these attributes, which describe private blocks rather than the patient. The sequence itself is removed by its supplementary rule (D-028).
    - Under Clean Descriptors too, text that a rule retains is given C where it is written with the rule's VR and with a number of values that the rule's VM allows, counted by the backslashes in its bytes; since a byte 5CH within a character of a multi-byte character set can raise that count, cleaning counts the decoded values against the VM again, and text that does not meet it takes its action without the option. Descriptor cleaning settles it as a free-text descriptor (D-009), by the reviewed descriptors list's decisions on its private attribute's key, which names the creator as compared, the group, the element within the block, and the VR; the technique vocabulary (D-036) keeps none of it. Where cleaning does not write it, it takes its action without Clean Descriptors and is removed, as is a creator that its block kept only for it, and a parsed private sequence that the policy without the option removes goes with everything in it, text that a reviewer kept included, as a cleaned descriptor in a sequence that another's action removes does. Since cleaning can so remove a creator that the plan keeps, each kept creator is collected for the residual search where the plan cleans private text, and left out where it is written, so that one removed is searched for, as a removed creator is (D-027). Text that cannot be decoded in its character set, or that is outside ISO 646 where no Specific Character Set applies, is read as removed text is, read as ISO 8859-1 for the residual search, and removed, rather than sequestering the instance. Text written without its VR, in Implicit VR Little Endian or as UN, is removed, since only the VR as written decodes it, and the writer would otherwise have to give the file a VR it did not hold. The QC pack lists each value with its key (D-016), the release report counts the values with those of the other descriptors, a kept value is left out of the residual search and listed as a retained string, as a kept descriptor's is (D-017, D-027), and the rest of what is written is searched as before.
    - Every other private attribute is removed, which E.3.10 allows in place of processing it as Deidentification Action (0008,0307) recommends.
    - The conformance statement describes how the rules are matched and applied, and lists each rule that can retain an attribute, with the meaning that Table E.3.10-1 gives it or the source of the addition; the rules for text are among them only under Clean Descriptors.
- **Rationale.** E.3.10 retains the private attributes "known by the de-identifier to be safe", and lists four ways of knowing, of which Table E.3.10-1 is one. The Note to the table calls it "a sample list of Private Attributes thought to be safe", which vendors do not guarantee, suggests retaining numeric values only, with code strings a possible exception, and calls bulk binary data particularly unsafe, since it can hold whole proprietary headers that include the patient's name. Following the Note keeps what E.3.10 names as the Option's purpose, such as CT helical pitch and PET SUV factors, while retaining nothing that cannot be checked. Retaining every row as listed was rejected because the table lists values that cannot be read or checked, such as a whole stress study file (OW) and ultrasound volume payloads (OB), which the residual search also leaves out (D-027). Code strings are retained because the Note allows them and VR CS holds at most 16 upper-case letters, digits, spaces, and underscores; a name written in one is still found by the residual search where the same name was removed elsewhere (D-027). Text is cleaned rather than kept, as the Note advises, so the descriptor cleaner's review applies to private text too. It is cleaned only under Clean Descriptors, since the reviewed descriptors list, the pooled human review that D-009 requires before free text is retained, is given to a run only with that option, and removed otherwise, as E.3.10's "removed or processed" allows. A decision names a private attribute by its creator and element within the block rather than its tag, as a rule does, since the tag depends on the block that the creator reserves, and by its VR, since a list's rules are chosen by VR; the form follows the paths of TCIA's dictionary. The technique vocabulary's words are reviewed for the standard's technique descriptors, whose meaning the standard defines, and say nothing of what a private attribute holds. Private text that cannot be decoded is removed rather than sequestering its instance, as an undecodable descriptor does, because removing it is what the Option did before it could be cleaned and loses nothing that the instance's IOD requires. The three timestamp rows depart from the table because an absolute time would carry the acquisition date and time that the Basic Profile removes, and no reviewed document shows that they are relative. Keying by creator and element within the block follows MIDI §1.17.3, since a vendor's block can be reserved at any of (gggg,0010) to (gggg,00FF). Creators are compared only in printable ISO 646 without a backslash, as their bytes, because every character set that DICOM allows reads those bytes as the same characters without an escape sequence, so no private text is decoded and no two creators that differ in their bytes can match one rule; every creator of Table E.3.10-1 is in that repertoire. Parsing a private sequence that no rule lists, rather than removing it whole, follows E.3.10's text, as the maintainer decided on 13 October 2026. It is kept only where something in it is kept or replaced, because an emptied attribute, a dummy value, and a private creator carry nothing that the Option retains, and a creator that no rule has reviewed should not be kept for an empty container; a value that is emptied or given a dummy value is still searched for by the residual search (D-027). Only a block whose creator is compared is parsed, so that every kept sequence has a creator that defines it, as E.3.10 requires of the retained attributes, and no creator is kept whose bytes might read as different characters in another character set. Honouring what an instance declares about its own private blocks follows E.3.10's first way of knowing, as the maintainer decided on 12 October 2026. A declared attribute takes the handling of its VR, because the declaration is a basis for retention as a row of Table E.3.10-1 is, and the table's Note applies to it alike: numbers and codes can be checked, while text, dates, and bulk binary data cannot, and a date would carry what the Basic Profile removes. Its VR must be known, from the file or the sequence, because a value that cannot be read by its VR cannot be checked. A declaration that an attribute may identify defeats a reviewed rule, because the attribute is then not known to be safe, and removing it is what E.3.10 asks of every other private attribute; the maintainer decided on 13 October 2026 that the file's flag wins, over letting the reviewed rule keep the attribute, at the cost of such a value as a PET SUV factor in a file that flags its block. Sequences are parsed whatever is declared of them, as E.3.10 suggests even of nominally safe ones. Only the top-level sequence is read, since PS3.3 places it in the SOP Common Module, and it applies wherever its creator's block is, since PS3.3 identifies a block by its creator rather than by where it is reserved. Taking additions only from public documents keeps every retained attribute traceable to a source that anyone can check. TCIA's dictionary is offered as a choice, not merged into the default, as the maintainer decided on 12 October 2026: it covers many more attributes than Table E.3.10-1, but by TCIA's curation rather than by public vendor documents, and it keeps bulk binary data that the Note calls particularly unsafe. Its rules are chosen by VR because its rows record the VRs that TCIA has seen an attribute with, including OB and UN for files that held it without a readable one, so only the row for the VR that reads the value says whether that value may be kept, and where the VR is not written, only a single readable VR says how to read it. A kept attribute has a single value unless a reviewed rule gives its VM, because the dictionary gives no VM, and a long array kept on its word alone would not have been checked as a reviewed rule's values are, as for a declared attribute; where a reviewed rule covers the same attribute and VR, its handling and VM are the reviewed knowledge of that value. Only rows at the top level make rules, because a rule applies wherever its creator's block is, and a row whose path runs through a sequence says nothing of the block elsewhere. Declarations still apply, because they are another way of knowing, and the maintainer's decision that the file's flag wins does not depend on the list. It ships, rather than being downloaded when used, so that a run works offline and the package fixes which list a run used; the pin and the edition check keep the shipped copy identical to TCIA's file. The check runs on a schedule and on the pull requests it concerns, not in every merge group, as the maintainer decided on 12 October 2026, so that an outage of TCIA's site cannot block unrelated changes. Its pull request result is advisory because TCIA's site does not always answer GitHub's runners: on 12 October 2026 it timed out from them twice in four hours while answering elsewhere.
- **Tests.** Every row of Table E.3.10-1 has one rule, with the table's VR and VM; each rule takes its VR's handling except the three timestamp rows, which have notes; every OB, OW, OW/OB, and no-VR row is removed; the PET SUV factors, CT pitch, and table speed are numeric; the handlings follow the Note for every VR, and only removal does not retain; a rule applies in whichever block its creator reserves, ignoring only trailing spaces and NULs, and not under another case, a leading space, another creator, another group, or another element; a lookup outside a private block is refused; a reviewed addition is read with its source and retained; the shipped file has no addition; and a file is rejected for each problem that its loader lists, including a creator outside printable ISO 646. Under the Option, the engine keeps numbers and code strings with their creators, in Explicit and Implicit VR Little Endian, in each block on its own, ignoring a creator's trailing spaces and NULs but not its case; compares only creators in printable ISO 646 without a backslash, and none written with another VR; removes an attribute written with another VR or with a number of values that its VM does not allow, a value that fails its check, text, an attribute that its rule removes or that no rule lists and that is not a sequence, one whose block has no creator, and each element that PS3.5 does not allow; replaces a UID written as UI and removes one read without its VR, and one with more values than its VM allows; parses a listed private sequence at depth 1 and 2, leaving the standard attributes in its items to their own rules, and removes one written with another VR; parses a private sequence that no rule lists, in Explicit and Implicit VR Little Endian, keeping it and its creator where a private or standard attribute in its items is kept, at depth 1 and 2, keeping each enclosing sequence that no rule lists, and removing it with its creator where its items keep only emptied or removed attributes and the creator of a nested sequence that keeps nothing; removes a private sequence whose block has no creator or one outside printable ISO 646; keeps a private block in the items of a standard sequence; writes no value that it removes; lists each kept creator, code string, and number string, and each value of one with several, among the QC pack's retained strings, at the top level and in an item, in Explicit VR Little Endian, written as UN, and in Implicit VR Little Endian, including a creator padded with a NUL, a creator written as UN, and a creator that no rule names kept for a parsed sequence; and gives the VR that reads each kept value written without one, and none to a sequence. Under the Option, an attribute that the instance declares safe, in a block marked SAFE or listed in Nonidentifying Private Elements of a MIXED block, is kept by the handling of its VR, in Explicit and Implicit VR Little Endian, with the VR that (0008,0310) defines read where the file writes none: a number, a number string, and its creator are kept, a UID is replaced where written as UI, and text and a date are removed; without a declaration, the same block is removed; an attribute is removed where its VR is unknown or disputed, including by two definitions that disagree, in one item or in two, and with an agreeing item before or after, in Explicit and Implicit VR, or its value fails its VR's check; a declared number is kept with as many values as the VM that (0008,0309) defines allows, fixed, open-ended, bounded, or with a stride, and otherwise with one, including where no item defines it, where the VM has no PS3.6 form, or where its stride is 0; an attribute that a reviewed rule keeps is removed where its block is UNSAFE, MIXED without it listed, or listed in Identifying Private Elements, where two items disagree, and where an item names its block with a status in lower case, as MIXED without its list, or with a listed element above 00FFH, and a reviewed rule still checks an attribute declared safe; a declaration for another group, a creator in another case or with a leading space, a reserved odd group, or an even group, keeps nothing; a declaration applies to its block in the items of a parsed private sequence, which it then keeps, while a (0008,0300) in such an item declares nothing and is removed; a sequence in a block marked UNSAFE is still parsed; end to end, the declared values are written, (0008,0300) is removed, no removed value is written, and the kept creator and number string are among the retained strings; and through the release gate, an instance whose declared block is a vendor's of Table E.3.10-1 is released, while one whose declared block is another's goes to QC review for the removed (0008,0302) alone (D-032). The conformance statement lists each rule that can retain an attribute, and no rule for text or removal, and describes how declarations are read, with nothing left pending under the Option; with Clean Descriptors too, it lists the 44 rules for text and describes how private text is cleaned, citing no decision. Under the Option with Clean Descriptors, private text written with its rule's VR is given C, with its key, in Explicit VR Little Endian only, and removed without Clean Descriptors, in Implicit VR Little Endian, written as UN, or with more values than its VM allows; a reviewer's decision keeps it with its creator, which leaves it out of the residual search and lists it as a retained string, or maps it, in whichever block the creator reserves; text that no decision covers is removed with a creator kept only for it, and listed in the QC pack by its key, while a creator kept for a number of its block stays; a kept value that echoes the patient is removed; text read as ISO 8859-1, and text that cannot be decoded as UTF-8, is removed without sequestering the instance; a parsed private sequence that holds only text keeps it, with both creators, where a reviewer keeps it, and is otherwise removed whole; declared text and text that TCIA's dictionary keeps are cleaned as a rule's are; a withheld instance leaves kept private text out of its collected values, but not text that no decision covers; the technique vocabulary keeps no private text; a key is refused for a group not open to private use, a creator empty, padded, or outside printable ISO 646 without a backslash, an element outside a block, or a VR other than text; the list saves and reopens private decisions as format 2, reads a list of format 1, and refuses one of format 1 with private text; the QC pack, of format 7, names each descriptor by its key or tag, and refuses another key; the release report counts one attribute in two blocks as one; and the decisions command records a decision on private text by its key. The shipped TCIA dictionary has the pinned length and SHA-256, with its attribution and licence; every row is read, with the counts of each disposition; a path is read through its sequences; a top-level attribute is found by creator, group, and element, ignoring only trailing spaces and NULs, with a creator in upper case matching only upper case; rows without a creator are read; an altered or missing copy is refused; and a file is rejected for each problem that its loader lists. Its rules keep a kept row's attribute by the handling of its VR with a single value, or with the handling and VM of a reviewed rule for the same attribute and VR, remove another row's, remove where two rows for one VR disagree, take the rule of the VR as written, and, where none is written, of the one VR other than OB, UN, or none, with none where there are several; give no rule to a row without a VR, a row below the top level, a creator outside printable ISO 646, a creator in another case, or another group; and ignore a creator's padding, so that rows for two spellings of one creator meet as one attribute's rows. A run that chooses it, in Explicit and Implicit VR Little Endian, keeps an attribute that only it keeps and removes one that only a reviewed rule keeps, reads a value without a VR by its rule's VR, removes a value with more values than its VM allows or written as OB or with another VR, keeps one written as UN, still removes a block that (0008,0300) marks UNSAFE, and releases the instance; its method digest differs from the reviewed list's and records the list, which is refused without the Option; its release report, in both forms, records `tcia`; and its conformance statement gives the file's SHA-256 and TCIA's attribution and lists TCIA's rules with TCIA's names, while the default statement does neither. The command line and the library take the choice, and each refuses an unknown list, or TCIA's without the Option, before anything is loaded or created. The edition check reports a change for a new edition not yet reviewed, a page that no longer links the pinned file or the licence, and a pinned file or shipped copy that differs from the pin, and a failure when the page or the file cannot be fetched or the page links to no edition, without reaching the network.

### D-036: Technique descriptors kept by a reviewed vocabulary

- **Decision.**
  - Under Clean Descriptors, a value of a technique descriptor that the reviewed descriptors list has no decision on (D-009) is kept as it is, without review, where every word of it is in a reviewed vocabulary of technique, anatomy, and equipment words and it echoes no identifier of the instance, as the reviewed tier checks it. The list's decisions take precedence, so a reviewer can empty or map a value that the vocabulary would keep. Every other value takes the list's decision, or is not cleaned, as D-009 describes.
  - The technique descriptors are Series Description (0008,103E), Protocol Name (0018,1030), Sequence Name (0018,0024), Contrast/Bolus Agent (0018,0010), Contrast/Bolus Route (0018,1040), Derivation Description (0008,2111), Performed Procedure Step Description (0040,0254), Window Center & Width Explanation (0028,1055), Attenuation Correction Method (0054,1101), Scatter Correction Method (0054,1105), and Reconstruction Method (0054,1103). Study Description, Requested Procedure Description, comments, histories, diagnoses, and reasons are never kept by the vocabulary.
  - The vocabulary is the L2 file `technique_vocabulary.toml`: groups of words, each with the reason its words describe technique, anatomy, or equipment rather than a person; units; the prefixes of vendors' reconstruction kernels; and the Enumerated Values and Defined Terms that the pinned PS3.3 gives selected CS attributes of acquisition and reconstruction, with the body part terms of the pinned PS3.16 Annex L (D-032), each split at `_` and spaces. It leaves out excluded words, which name titles, sex, age, dates, records, and institutions, and every common personal name that is not a reviewed exception, each of which records why the word is chiefly technique or anatomy, such as `HEAD` or `DIXON`. The common names are generated from the United States Census Bureau's 1990 census name files, which are in the public domain: every given name, and every surname held by at least 0.001 percent of the population, 22,872 names in all. A group may not hold an excluded word or a common name that is not an exception, so that each such word is a recorded decision.
  - A value is read as words, the runs of letters and digits between other characters, and kept only where it is printable ISO 646 without `^`, which separates the components of a person's name; holds no date or time, with any separators, including a clock time written with an hour unit, as in `14H30`, `14HR 30MIN`, `9H 5MIN`, or `1430HRS`, no run of five or more digits, and no digits that run on across separators from one word into the next to five or more; has at most one bare number, of at most three digits before its point and three after, other than two digits, a point, and two digits from `10.00` up, which could be a time; has every other word either a reconstruction kernel of a reviewed prefix, two digits, and at most one of the suffixes D, F, H, S, and U, as in `B70f`, or made of letter runs that are each a vocabulary word, or two or three vocabulary words of at least three letters joined, and digit runs of at most four digits before any point and three after that are not a year from 1900 to 2099, with at most one digit run in a word, so that `512X512` and `14MIN30` are held; has a single letter after digits, in a word or as the next word, only where it is a unit or the `D` of `2D`, `3D`, or `4D`, and no two single-letter words together, as initials are; holds no letter run that is a common name and not an exception, and no `MR`, `MRS`, or `MS` before an exception that is a common name; and has a vocabulary word of at least two letters. Words are matched without case. The rules are deterministic, so the same value is decided the same way in every run with the same vocabulary.
  - The SHA-256 of the effective vocabulary, after terms, names, exceptions, and exclusions are applied, is an input to the method digest (D-024), `technique_vocabulary` in its serialisation, or `null` for a run without a vocabulary, since the transform can be given another one or none, and the conformance statement describes the rules and the attributes, as PS3.15 E.3.5 requires the manner of cleaning to be described.
  - Runs apply the vocabulary with the reviewed descriptors list, as D-009 describes.
  - The maintainer chose on 12 October 2026 to keep technique descriptors by a reviewed vocabulary as well as by reviewed values, over reviewed values only, stripping words outside the vocabulary from a value, or a trained entity recogniser.
- **Open question.** Place names are not excluded by a bundled list. On 12 October 2026 the vocabulary was checked against GeoNames' cities of at least 5,000 people, the countries, and the United States' states, as the `geonamescache` 3.0.2 wheel on PyPI distributes them: 38 vocabulary words are also the name of such a place, all of them technique, anatomy, or function words, such as `SUPERIOR`, `SPLIT`, and `CENTRAL`, and they are kept. Whether to bundle a place list, whose licence would need attribution, is not decided.
- **Rationale.** Under D-009 alone, no descriptor other than ROI Name is kept without pooled human review, and review cannot run on the MIDI-B benchmark, from which only counts, codes, and digests may leave CI, so the benchmark's descriptor checks fail wherever a descriptor is expected to be kept. PS3.15 E.3.5 specifies what must be removed rather than a method, and its notes say that Series Description is usually written from a device's protocol and rarely holds identifying information, and that a word can be both descriptive and identifying. Keeping only a whole value made of reviewed words, rather than stripping the other words, keeps nothing of a value that mixes technique with anything else, such as a name or an age, which goes to review. The residual risk is a value made only of vocabulary words that is also the name of a person not recorded in the instance, such as a Series Description of only `HAND`, or a single word that reads as a kernel but is an initial, an age, and a sex, such as `B45F`; the common-name exclusion removes the common cases, the exceptions are recorded with their reasons, and the echo check holds a value that names a person of the instance. Joined words must each have at least three letters, since short words joined spell names: `WOMBAT` is `WO`, `MB`, and `AT`. A trained recogniser was rejected because its output depends on a model and its version, so it cannot be described exactly in the conformance statement, and stripping because it keeps part of a name, as `Dr. Hand` becomes `Hand`.
- **Tests.** Technique values made of vocabulary words, with units, kernels, and numbers, are kept; values with a common name, a title before a named exception, a person name's `^`, an excluded word, a word outside the vocabulary, a date or time with any separators or with hour and minute units, every hour and minute of the day in the form `TIME14HR30MIN`, with four hour units, five separators, and padded and unpadded numbers, among them, two numbers in one word, a long, split, dotted, or second bare number, a year, initials, an age or sex letter after digits, or a character outside printable ISO 646, are not; Study Description, comments, and diagnoses are never kept by the vocabulary; joined words of fewer than three letters, and of four words, are not vocabulary; the vocabulary holds no common name that is not an exception and no excluded word, and holds terms of PS3.3 and Annex L; the file is refused where a group or the kernels hold an excluded word or an unreviewed common name, a kernel prefix is longer than two letters, an exception has no basis or is not a common name of the vocabulary, or an attribute is not a descriptor; the effective vocabulary's digest changes with its words and its `repr` shows none; cleaning keeps a vocabulary value, holds one that echoes an identifier, and applies a reviewer's decision before the vocabulary; and the common-name list is refused where it is changed or is not a JSON object, and its generator keeps every given name and the surnames at or above the threshold, and refuses a source without the pinned digest.

### D-037: Clean Pixel Data by detection and review

- **Decision.**
  - Under the Clean Pixel Data Option (PS3.15 E.3.1), the engine blacks out every region of text that a text finder finds in each frame of the top-level native Pixel Data (7FE0,0010), whatever the text says, which E.3.1 Note 2 allows as "the most conservative approach of removing any and all burned in text". The finder is the text detector of the `text-detection` extra, or any object with its `find` method and a `method` that describes what decides its regions; a transform takes one exactly when its policy selects the option. Its class and a copy of its description, read once when the transform is made, are an input to the method digest of every run under the option, so the markers and the release report identify the finder: for the text detector, its model's SHA-256, the ONNX Runtime version and distributions, the NumPy and SciPy versions, its detection parameters, and its renderings (D-024). A finder that does not describe its method as a mapping that is not empty, or describes it with a value of a type that the digest does not define, is refused. Icons, in nested items, are removed by the Basic Profile.
  - Each region is set to a constant stored fill: the frame's smallest stored value for MONOCHROME2, its largest for MONOCHROME1, and zero for RGB. The fill erases the text's pattern; how bright it is displayed depends on the Modality and VOI LUT attributes that are kept, which can invert it, so it is not always black, and a rule that guarantees a black display would need its own decision. The new values are written in place of the old, byte for byte, in the source's transfer syntax, so nothing else in Pixel Data changes; in Implicit VR Little Endian the writer accepts the new value although the dictionary gives Pixel Data OB or OW, since both hold the same bytes in little endian. A frame without a region is left as it is. The file written is checked: pydicom must read the source's stored values outside the regions and the fill inside them, and every other byte of the value must be the source's; otherwise the instance is sequestered as `redaction-mismatch`.
  - Only native Pixel Data in whole bytes is cleaned: Bits Allocated 8, 16, or 32, High Bit one less than Bits Stored, and MONOCHROME1, MONOCHROME2, or 8-bit RGB. The bits above Bits Stored must hold nothing but the value's sign, since an overlay (PS3.3 Section C.9.2 before 2004) or anything else there would stay. Any other layout, and Float Pixel Data or Double Float Pixel Data, sequesters the instance as `uncleanable-pixel-data`, and compressed Pixel Data as `compressed-pixel-data`, until a later change writes redacted frames uncompressed.
  - Every instance whose Pixel Data the option cleans is held for review, as `text-blacked-out` where text was found and `no-text-found` where none was, since the finder can miss faint, small, or noisy text, as the maintainer chose on 12 October 2026 ("Rules plus detection"). The QC pack lists its regions, by frame, and previews every frame of it at full resolution, since review must see each frame for text that the finder missed: as written, in grey for a monochrome frame without regions, and, for a frame with them, in colour beside the frame as received, windowed together from it, with each region outlined in magenta along its own edge pixels, inside the region, so that the outline hides nothing beside it where text that a region only partly covered would show (D-016). A monochrome frame as written is also shown a second time, over its full range, from the smallest value to the largest of the frame as received, or as written where it has no regions, as the finder searches it, since the percentile window shows text at or near the extremes as white or black together with whatever else lies there, such as bone or air, and the full range keeps it apart. A cleaned instance claims the option, with DCM 113101 and Burned In Annotation (0028,0301) set to NO, only on a later run given a reviewer's verdict on it (D-048). An instance whose source cannot be shown beside it, because the source cannot be read or decoded or differs in its frames' shape, number, or colour, is listed as not previewed, as `source-not-shown`, rather than shown in part, and can be given no verdict, so it stays held. An instance without top-level pixel data claims the option in any run under it (D-048).
  - The option joins the options that the engine applies (D-011). No preset selects it, and it is not enabled (D-033).
- **Rationale.** On synthetic phantoms the detector covered 96 to 99% of high-contrast strings of 7 to 14 pixels, but only 55 to 82% of faint ones, so review, not detection, is what lets an instance claim the option; E.3.1 allows a process that "may require intervention of or approval by a human operator". Writing into the existing value keeps the transfer syntax and needs no transcoding, and the byte comparison makes any other change visible. Holding every cleaned instance until a reviewer's verdict is applied keeps the claim and Burned In Annotation NO tied to review, and releasing an instance whose text was found but whose pixels no one has seen was rejected for the same reason.
- **Tests.** Regions set to the fill for MONOCHROME1, MONOCHROME2, signed values, and RGB in either planar configuration, with nothing else changed; each frame searched and cleaned with its own fill; regions clipped to the frame; Implicit VR Little Endian kept; compressed and float pixel data, and every other layout, refused; unused bits that hold more than the sign refused; a written value that differs from the plan found, inside or outside a region; under the option, a CT image written blacked out, held for review, and without the code, an instance without pixel data written with it, a transform refused with a finder and without the option or without one and with it, or with a finder that does not describe its method in a form the digest defines, the finder's class and description in the method digest that each instance and the release report record, and unchanged by a later change to the finder, and the release report counting held instances by reason; the QC pack listing each cleaned instance's regions by frame, with its Rows and Columns, an entry without them or with a region past them refused, and previewing every frame, a frame with regions beside its source, through one window from the source, for MONOCHROME1, MONOCHROME2, signed values with a rescale, and RGB in either planar configuration, with the fill shown black in monochrome and nothing but the regions' own edges outlined, a frame without regions in grey alone, a monochrome frame shown again over its full range, where text that the percentile window saturates together with bone stands apart, a source kept only for an instance with regions, an instance whose source cannot be shown listed as not previewed, and a pack refused unless each cleaned instance has a preview of each frame or is listed as not previewed; and a corpus run with a finder that marks a corner of every frame, which holds every image, lists each one's regions, previews each of its frames at the expected size, and releases the rest.

### D-038: Retain Patient Characteristics

- **Decision.**
  - Under the Retain Patient Characteristics Option, the fourteen attributes that Table E.1-1 gives K are kept as they are read, wherever they are in the data set, and none is coarsened: Patient's Age (0010,1010), Patient's Sex (0010,0040), Patient's Sex Neutered (0010,2203), Patient's Size (0010,1020), Patient's Weight (0010,1030), Ethnic Group (0010,2160), Ethnic Group Code Sequence (0010,2161), Ethnic Groups (0010,2162), Pregnancy Status (0010,21C0), Smoking Status (0010,21A0), Selector AS Value (0072,005F), and Sex Parameters for Clinical Use Category Sequence (0010,0043), Code Sequence (0010,0046), and Reference (0010,0047). The attributes in a kept sequence's items take their own rules (D-022).
  - The five attributes of free text that the Option gives C, Allergies (0010,2110), Pre-Medication (0040,0012), Special Needs (0038,0050), Patient State (0038,0500), and Sex Parameters for Clinical Use Category Comment (0010,0042), are settled by the Option's cleaner, which cleans the whole instance (D-034) and writes none of their values. It settles one with no value, or whose values hold nothing but spaces or NUL padding and backslashes, which is written with no value. It leaves out one with a value, or whose value could not be decoded, so that it stays pending and its instance is sequestered. Clean Descriptors gives C to Allergies, Patient State, and the comment too; where the policy selects both options, the cleaner settles them first, and a value that it leaves pending still holds its instance, even where the reviewed descriptors list has a decision on it, since descriptor cleaning leaves such an attribute to the Option's cleaner and the action that the policy gives them without Clean Descriptors is the Option's C (D-009).
  - The conformance statement gives these five attributes and this method, so a policy whose only attributes given C, other than those that Clean Descriptors' fallback settles, are the Option's lists nothing as pending for them (PS3.15 E.3.7).
  - The measures of the patient's body that the reviewed rules remove under every option, Patient's Size Code Sequence (0010,1021), Patient's Body Mass Index (0010,1022), Measured AP Dimension (0010,1023), and Measured Lateral Dimension (0010,1024), stay removed (D-022). A kept value is not searched for (D-027), and a kept text value is one of the retained strings that `public-release` reviews (D-017), as for any attribute kept. The indicators of a possibly disclosed body weight are still reported under the Option (D-015): they are read from the source, whatever the policy, and say no more than a kept Patient's Weight does.
- **Open question.** The custodian's reviewed list of descriptor values could later hold reviewers' decisions on these five attributes too, so that a reviewed value is kept or mapped and an instance with an unreviewed one goes to review rather than being sequestered, as for ROI Names (D-009). That would need the maintainer's decision, since the list is for Clean Descriptors.
- **Rationale.** E.3.7 retains "information about age, sex, height and weight and other characteristics present in the Attributes", "as described in Table E.1-1", for applications such as standardised uptake values and measures of body composition that need them, and requires the manner of cleaning retained attributes to be described in the conformance statement. Coarsening a kept value, such as an age above 89 years, was rejected: E.3.7 does not ask for it, the practice comes from regulations, after which no preset is named (D-014), and changing a value that the Option retains contradicts it (D-011). The five attributes given C hold free text written by people, such as a list of allergies or a note on the patient's state, which can carry a name, a date, or a place. C asks for "values of similar meaning known not to contain identifying information" (Table E.1-1a), which no automatic method can know of free text, and D-009 retains no free-text value without pooled human review. Removing or emptying a value was rejected because it discards a characteristic that the Option retains, while the instance would still claim the Option (D-011), and writing the instance without the Option's code because it would claim that the Basic Profile removed the characteristics that are kept (D-012). An attribute with no value carries nothing, so sequestering its instance would discard data for no protection; and Sex Parameters for Clinical Use Category Comment is Type 2C in the Patient Study Module (PS3.3 Table C.7-4a), so it must be written with no value rather than removed where its condition holds. Allergies and Patient State are Type 3 in the Patient Study Module, which every IOD of the first supported release includes, and Pre-Medication and Special Needs are in no composite IOD's modules; how many instances each holds is not yet measured. A cleaner that leaves out the values it cannot clean, rather than a list of attributes to hold kept apart from the registry, keeps each option's handling of its attributes in one entry, which the statement describes.
- **Tests.** Every attribute that the Option gives C has the Option's cleaner, and the Option gives the other fourteen K; an attribute with no value, or whose values hold only padding and separators, is written with no value, and one with any other value, such as `NONE`, or one not decoded, is left out, stays pending in the edits, and sequesters its instance, at the top level or as the comment in Sex Parameters for Clinical Use Category Sequence; the Option keeps each kept attribute that a plan can carry, Ethnic Groups among them, with the Option's code, while the Basic Profile removes or empties each one and the sequence; a kept Sex Parameters for Clinical Use Category Sequence keeps its Code Sequence and Reference and writes an empty comment with no value; under Clean Descriptors as well, a value of Allergies still sequesters its instance, even where the reviewed descriptors list keeps or maps it, while empty values are written with no value and Patient's Weight is kept, with both options' codes; and the statement of the Option, alone and with Clean Descriptors, gives the five attributes and the method, and lists nothing as pending for them, and so does `tps-import`'s.

### D-039: Modified Dates in the engine

- **Decision.**
  - Under Retain Longitudinal Temporal Information with Modified Dates, one reviewed cleaner of the whole instance (D-034) settles every attribute that Table E.1-1 or a supplementary rule gives C under the option: in 2026d, 184 attributes, every attribute with a temporal role but Patient's Birth Date (0010,0030), Patient's Birth Time (0010,0032), and GPS Time Stamp (0016,0077), which the option removes. Each value is modified by its attribute's role (D-007): a subject-event or radiation-source DA value moves back by the subject's offset (D-006); a DT value moves its date and keeps its time, once a value with its own UTC offset is in the instance's local time with the nominal offset `+0000`; a TM value is kept, and marked as retained, so the residual search leaves it out and records the drop (D-027); and Frame Origin Timestamp (0034,0007) moves its seconds. A device, vocabulary-version, or other value takes D's constant for its VR, or the second where the source equals the first, as D writes it (D-021), so the residual search skips a source value equal to it (D-027). Timezone Offset From UTC (0008,0201) becomes `+0000`. Each value of a multi-valued attribute is modified on its own, an empty value stays empty, and an attribute with no value is written with none.
  - The subject's offset is derived from the run's key and the identity that the run resolves for the instance, its Patient ID with its issuer (D-005), so every instance of a subject in a run moves by the same offset, and runs move it by unrelated offsets, as they do UIDs and pseudonyms (D-004). With the run-scoped key, the derivation is the subject's profile, so no profile store is read or written; reading offsets from a project key's profiles comes with project keys.
  - The instance's local time is its Timezone Offset From UTC, where it has one, and otherwise the offset of its earliest DT value that has one (D-007), among the values that the cleaner writes, so a value in a removed sequence has no say. Every valid Timezone Offset From UTC in the instance must have the same value. One that is not a valid offset says nothing of the local time, so it counts as absent, and it too becomes `+0000`.
  - The cleaner leaves pending, which sequesters the instance (D-034), any value that it cannot modify so: a DA value that is not a valid date, including the ACR-NEMA form YYYY.MM.DD; a DT value without a full date, which cannot move by whole weeks (D-006); a DT value at a leap second; one whose precision cannot express its conversion to local time; a value that would move before year 1; a valid Timezone Offset From UTC that differs from another in the instance; a value read in a VR that the pinned dictionary does not give its attribute, or that its role does not provide for; and every value that moves, in an instance without a subject's identity, whose Patient's Name and Patient ID are pending anyway (D-005).
  - The conformance statement gives the cleaner's attributes and method, and, under Dates and times, how many of the attributes given C have each role, so it lists nothing about dates as not yet described (E.3.6).
  - Where two of a subject's source dates differ by exactly its offset, such as a planning CT and a follow-up a year or more later, the shifted later date equals the earlier source date, and the residual search finds that source date in the shifted element (D-027). Such a finding does not withhold the file (`residual_shifts.py`) where the run selects Modified Dates; the finding is of a date or datetime inside the data set, in an element of VR DA or DT whose attribute the option moves by the subject's offset; a value of the element, or the date of a datetime value, equals a whole value of the finding's source, or the date of a datetime; and every value of the element is a source value of the same element, pooled across the subject, moved back by whole weeks within the offset's range with the same time of day. The same source value anywhere else still withholds the file. The maintainer chose this exemption over sending such files to review or keeping the search strict.
- **Open question.**
  - MIDI-B's answer keys expect device times, such as Time of Last Calibration (0018,1201), to be kept, where D-007 replaces them with a dummy value, so those checks fail by design under this option, as D-031 records for others.
- **Rationale.** One cleaner of the whole instance, rather than one per attribute, is needed because a value depends on others: a DT value's conversion depends on the instance's Timezone Offset From UTC or its other DT values, and every shifted value on the subject's identity, which a cleaner of single values does not see. Keeping times follows D-006, since whole weeks keep the time of day: the date and time of an event move together, so the pair is modified, and the time alone identifies no one; marking a kept time as retained records it, rather than listing it as a value the search does not reach. Using D's constants as the dummy values means one fixed value per VR, which differs from the source and is the same for every subject and run, so it discloses nothing of an offset, and the residual search already skips it. Sequestering a value that cannot be moved by whole weeks, rather than writing a dummy value for it, keeps every written subject-event value consistent with the others, and such values are rare: a DT value without a full date, a leap second, or an ACR-NEMA date. Requiring one Timezone Offset From UTC per instance avoids guessing which values a nested one applies to; one that is not a valid offset cannot be applied to any value, and replacing it discloses nothing of it, so the instance need not be held for it, as the synthetic corpus, which plants a text marker in every attribute, shows. A shifted date that equals another source date of its subject is the engine's own value, and nothing in the file says that a source held it, so the match discloses only the coincidence, and nothing of the offset; a collection that holds one subject's dates on most days of several years would meet it in most of its instances. Requiring every value of the element to be a whole-week shift of the element's own source within the offset's range keeps the exemption to values that the engine wrote, so one such value cannot clear another, and requiring the whole value keeps a date found elsewhere, or inside a longer value, withholding the file.
- **Tests.** The cleaner covers exactly the attributes that the option gives C; subject dates move back by the derived whole weeks, keeping intervals and weekdays, including within multi-valued attributes and with empty values; times are kept and marked as retained; a radiation source's decay interval to treatment is kept; DT values are put in local time, from Timezone Offset From UTC or, without it, the earliest DT value with an offset, and given `+0000`; device, vocabulary-version, and other values take D's constants, the second where the source equals the first, the same for every subject and key; Frame Origin Timestamp moves its seconds and keeps its nanoseconds; offsets are the same within a run, and differ between subjects and keys; each value that cannot be modified stays pending; differing time zone offsets leave the offsets and the DT values with their own offsets pending, while one that is not a valid offset counts as absent and becomes `+0000`; without a subject only the values that do not move are settled; a shifted date, or the date of a shifted datetime, including one of several values, equal to another instance's source date is released, while the same date in an element that was not shifted, or under a run without Modified Dates, still withholds the file, and a date not moved by whole weeks in range, or in an element with another value that is not, is not exempt; an instance is written with every date modified, (0028,0303) `MODIFIED`, the option's code, no source date in the file, and a release by the gate, and one with a date that cannot be modified is sequestered; a subject moves by one offset in every instance, other subjects and another key by others, so the synthetic corpus's study and series dates that differ among a patient's instances differ as before, as dcentvfy finds; `tps-import` modifies its eleven device dates by role; and the statement of the option gives its method and role counts and lists nothing as not yet described.

### D-040: Retain UIDs

- **Decision.**
  - Under the Retain UIDs Option, the engine applies Table E.1-1's actions as it does any other option's. The 55 UIDs that the table gives K under the option are kept wherever they occur, and its five sequences, Referenced Study Sequence (0008,1110), Referenced Performed Procedure Step Sequence (0008,1111), Referenced Patient Sequence (0008,1120), Referenced Image Sequence (0008,1140), and Source Image Sequence (0008,2112), are kept as containers whose items' elements take their own rules. Every other UID is replaced with the run's key (D-003): UID (0040,A124) and Digital Signature UID (0400,0100), which the table gives U under every option, and the instance UIDs that the table omits, such as Multi-frame Source SOP Instance UID (0008,1167), Volume Frame of Reference UID (0020,9312), and Referenced Fiducial UID (0070,031B), whose `instance` role gives U whatever the options.
  - The engine still removes the four attributes of groups 0000, 0002, and 0004 that the table gives K under the option, Affected SOP Instance UID (0000,1000), Requested SOP Instance UID (0000,1001), Media Storage SOP Instance UID (0002,0003), and Referenced SOP Instance UID in File (0004,1511), as its own removals require. The File Meta Information that it writes (D-025) gives the kept SOP Instance UID as Media Storage SOP Instance UID, as PS3.10 Section 7.1 requires.
  - Output files are named by the keyed replacements of the kept Study, Series, and SOP Instance UIDs, so that under one key an instance has the same output name as under the Basic Profile, and no source UID is in a path (D-016). An instance whose kept identifier is not a single valid UID, which File Meta Information cannot hold, is sequestered as unnamed.
  - The reference graph's second pass expects each input's own UID at the identity and reference sites whose attributes the policy keeps, and its replacement at the others (D-026).
  - The residual search leaves out each UID that the policy keeps by Table E.1-1, where it is still collected: in an element removed with an enclosing sequence, or by the engine's own removals (D-027). This holds for every option's kept UIDs, so also for Device UID (0018,1002) and Manufacturer's Device Class UID (0018,100B) under Retain Device Identity.
  - The markers claim the option for every instance written under it, since its actions alone satisfy it (D-012). The conformance statement says what keeping UIDs does to referential integrity, and the release report says that kept UIDs link each released instance to its source (PS3.15 E.3.9). No preset selects the option, so only a policy applied for validation can select it until per-run option sets are enabled.
  - A reference at an attribute that the table does not keep under the option, such as Multi-frame Source SOP Instance UID or a UID (0040,A124) that names an instance, is replaced while the UID that it names is kept, so it no longer resolves. Where both instances are in the run, the replaced reference's source value is collected for the residual search, which finds it in the referenced instance's kept SOP Instance UID and in every kept reference to it, so it withholds the referenced instance and each instance that keeps a reference to it, such as a plan whose Referenced Dose Sequence names a dose that frames were extracted from. The referring instance is released, and the second pass reports that its reference names an instance that was not written. On 12 October 2026 the maintainer chose this strict reading of the table over keeping the instance UIDs that it omits through a reviewed list, which would keep such references, but goes beyond E.3.9, which retains UIDs "as described in Table E.1-1", and E.1.1, which requires the Basic Profile to protect every reference to a SOP Instance.
- **Rationale.** Keeping exactly what Table E.1-1 lists under the option is the reading that conforms to both E.3.9 and E.1.1. Its cost falls on references that the table omits, which the CT, MR, PET, and RT objects in scope seldom hold, though no collection has measured how seldom. Naming files by keyed replacements keeps D-016's guarantees: portable names within the 160-character bound, from no source value. Naming them by the kept UIDs was rejected, since a source UID of up to 64 characters breaks that bound and may not be valid, and a recipient reads the kept UIDs from the files themselves. Leaving a kept UID out of the search where its enclosing sequence is removed follows from the table, which keeps the attribute wherever it occurs, so finding its value elsewhere discloses nothing that the option does not; searching for it would withhold every subject whose removed sequences repeat a kept UID. Every UID that the option does not keep is still replaced under the run's key, so runs share no replacement UIDs (D-004); the kept UIDs link runs and sources, which is the audit trail that E.3.9 provides the option for, at the risk that its first Note describes.
- **Tests.** A policy that selects the option composes and is applied; it keeps the 55 UIDs and five sequences that the table lists, and gives U to UID (0040,A124), Digital Signature UID, and the instance UIDs that the table omits; under it, an RT Dose of frames extracted from a multi-frame RT Dose keeps its SOP Instance, Series Instance, Study Instance, and Frame of Reference UIDs and its references, while the Multi-frame Source SOP Instance UID in its Frame Extraction Sequence is replaced, its File Meta Information gives its kept SOP Instance UID, and it claims the option's code; an RT Plan's file is named as under the Basic Profile with the same key; an instance whose kept SOP Instance UID is not a valid UID is sequestered as unnamed; a kept UID in a removed sequence is left out of the residual search, while the instance's other removed values are still searched for; a run of the synthetic collection under the option releases every instance with no finding from the second pass; with that extracted dose added, the run withholds the dose that it names and the plan that refers to that dose, and releases the extracted dose, whose references the second pass reports as naming instances that were not written; the second pass reports a replacement written where a kept UID was required, and an input's UID written at a kept site is not reported as an original UID; and the conformance statement and the release report say what keeping UIDs means only when the option is selected.

### D-041: Clean Graphics by the Basic Profile's actions

- **Decision.**
  - The engine applies the Clean Graphics Option (PS3.15 E.3.3) without a cleaner of graphics: each of the four rows to which Table E.1-1 gives C under the option takes its Basic Profile action. Curve Data (50xx,xxxx), Overlay Data (60xx,3000), and Overlay Comments (60xx,4000) are removed (X), and removing Overlay Data removes every attribute of its overlay group where the IOD's Overlay Plane Module is user-optional, as under the Basic Profile (D-020). Graphic Annotation Sequence (0070,0001) takes D, which removes it from every supported IOD, since only the Presentation State IODs define it, and they are out of scope (D-010; Note 13 after Table E.1-1a). The element rules give a masked row to which the policy gives C its Basic Profile action, as they already give a sequence (D-022); only Clean Graphics gives C to a masked row.
  - Every instance written under the option claims it, with DCM 113103, since E.3.3 specifies what the option removes, and Table E.1-1 gives the minimum actions, the reading that the maintainer chose for Clean Descriptors on 6 October 2026 (D-009). The conformance statement gives each of the four rows its Basic Profile action and says why, and lists nothing about graphics as not yet described.
  - Under the option, an instance with an overlay group that has no Overlay Data (60xx,3000) is sequestered as `overlay-in-pixel-data`. PS3.3 before 2004 let an overlay lie in unused bits of Pixel Data (Section C.9.2), which the engine does not change, so removing the group's attributes would leave its graphics in the written file. The indicator is the one by which the pixel risk assessment reports such an instance for review under every policy (D-015).
  - The option joins the options that the engine applies (D-011). No preset selects it.
- **Rationale.** C allows a graphic to be kept once it is known to hold nothing identifying, but Table E.1-1's minimum is the Basic Profile's removal, so removing every overlay, curve, and graphic annotation meets the option while keeping none of them. Applying the Basic Profile's action in the element rules, rather than leaving C pending for a fallback as descriptor cleaning does, means the plan, the edits, the residual search, and the statement all see the same removal as under the Basic Profile: no value is cleaned, so nothing new is written, and every instance of the synthetic corpus is written exactly as under the Basic Profile apart from its markers. Releasing an instance whose overlay may lie in Pixel Data with the option's code was rejected, since it would claim a removal that did not happen. Releasing it without the code was rejected too: the run would drop a selected option for that instance without being asked, which D-011 does not allow, and only Clean Descriptors may go unsatisfied, besides Clean Pixel Data, which a held instance does not claim (D-012, D-048). Clearing the overlay's bit plane was rejected for now, since it changes Pixel Data, which waits for Clean Pixel Data's redaction path; that path can later clear it and release the instance. Keeping an overlay whose bitmap holds no text, which the text detector of Clean Pixel Data could decide with review, is later work.
- **Tests.** Clean Graphics gives C to exactly these four rows; the rules give each row, and each element that it covers, its Basic Profile action, with and without Clean Descriptors, and no other option gives C to a masked row; under the option a CT Image's overlay groups, curve groups, and Graphic Annotation Sequence are removed and the instance claims the option, as it claims both options with Clean Descriptors; an overlay group without Overlay Data sequesters the instance as `overlay-in-pixel-data`, even beside one with it, and the release report knows the reason, while the Basic Profile releases the instance; every instance of the synthetic corpus, whose CT, MR, and PET images have Overlay Data, is written as under the Basic Profile apart from the markers, with the option's code after the profile's; and the statement gives each row its Basic Profile action and reason, names the sequestration, lists nothing as pending, claims the option without review, and lists it among the options that the engine applies.

### D-042: Residual findings that a value an option keeps explains

- **Decision.**
  - A residual finding inside the data set (D-027) is not a reason to hold or withhold the file where a value that a selected option keeps explains it, in one of two ways:
    - **Retained text.** A text finding, which D-027 sends to QC review, is in an attribute that a selected option keeps and that the pinned dictionary gives only VRs of text (AE, CS, LO, LT, SH, ST, UC, or UT), and its source is a private data element or an attribute to which that option gives an action of its own. Under Retain Device Identity, this releases a station's AE title, which the option cleans (D-034), or a vendor's private copy of the station's name, found in its kept Station Name (0008,1010), Device Serial Number (0018,1000), or Treatment Machine Name (300A,00B2); under Retain Institution Identity, a private copy of the institution's name found in its kept Institution Name (0008,0080). Any other source, such as Institution Name or Institutional Department Name (0008,1040) found in a kept Station Name, or a private creator, stays under review.
    - **Retained value.** A finding of text, a date, or a datetime, from a source other than the element itself, equals a whole value of an attribute that a selected option keeps and that the pinned dictionary gives only VRs of dates, times, and datetimes. Under Full Dates, this releases a removed Patient's Birth Date (0010,0030) equal to the kept Study Date (0008,0020). The same value found inside a longer kept value, such as the date of a kept Acquisition DateTime (0008,002A), or in a value that no option keeps, still withholds the file. A source's values are pooled across the subject, and the search records one finding of a source in each element, so the finding is explained only where every pooled value of its source that the search could find in the element equals a whole value of it: under Retain Device Identity, a kept Calibration DateTime (0018,1203) equal to one removed Acquisition DateTime (0008,002A) does not explain another, of the same day, found there by its date. Each source value is read by the VR collected with it, as the search reads it, so a backslash in an ST, LT, or UT value is a character, and the value found across two kept times is not explained by either. Each is also looked for as the search encodes it, in each of its codecs and cases, so a value whose UTF-16LE bytes are inside a kept time is found there whatever the source's character set, and is not explained.
  - An option keeps an attribute where it gives K, by Table E.1-1 or by a reviewed supplementary rule (D-022), and the Basic Profile does not, and no other selected option gives it an action of its own other than K. Under `tps-import`, the eleven device dates and times that Modified Dates modifies (D-007) are therefore not kept.
  - The rules read the run's options, which the transform records in each instance's coverage of the residual search, rather than the tables alone, because the attributes that one option keeps can hold engine-written values under another: Modified Dates shifts the dates that Full Dates keeps, and a written date equal to its own source would mean that the shift failed. A value that an option writes by C, such as a shifted date or an AE-title token, therefore never explains a finding. A coverage with no options explains none.
  - Otherwise as D-032: a person name, a UID, or a direct identifier found anywhere, and any value found outside the data set, still withholds the file; kept values are read from the written file's bytes as ISO 646 text, and an element with any other byte explains nothing; and each exempted finding stays in the search, which the QC pack lists, with its exemption recorded in the release condition.
  - This rule replaces the questions that D-034 (an AE title equal to a kept Station Name), Retain Institution Identity (private copies of kept institution values), and the Retain Longitudinal Temporal Information Options (a removed date equal to a kept date) left for the maintainer. It leaves Retain UIDs' reading unchanged: a replaced reference whose source UID equals a kept SOP Instance UID still withholds the file.
  - The maintainer decided on 12 October 2026 to exempt findings that the option explains in both ways, over sending such date findings to review, exempting any removed value equal to a whole kept value from any source, or keeping the holds.
- **Rationale.** A Retain option keeps values that can identify, which is why `public-release` omits Retain Device Identity and the Retain Longitudinal Temporal Information with Full Dates Option. A removed value found inside a value that the run's policy keeps shows only that the removed text is in a value released anyway, so it reveals nothing that the kept value does not, and the kept value is released whether or not a removed copy of it exists. Holding such files puts review effort on findings that the policy has already decided: a station's AE title commonly equals its Station Name, vendors repeat the station's and institution's names in private elements, and dates, of which a year has only 366, often coincide. The two ways are each limited to where the coincidence explains the finding. For text, the source must be of the option's own kind or a private copy, so an institution's name found in a kept Station Name under Retain Device Identity, which shows that the station's name holds what the option does not keep, still goes to review. For dates and times, the finding must be a whole kept value, so that a kept value whose part matches is still withheld. UIDs are excluded because a UID found in a kept UID shows a reference that was replaced while its target was kept, which Retain UIDs must make consistent rather than release. Exempting a removed value equal to any kept value, from any source, was rejected because it would release an institution's name typed as a station's name without review and the Retain UIDs case; sending date findings to review instead of releasing them, because a reviewer could only confirm a coincidence; and keeping the holds, because every Retain option would then hold most files for findings that the policy explains.
- **Tests.** An option keeps what it gives K and the Basic Profile does not, including supplementary rules' attributes and not what it cleans, and `tps-import` keeps none of the device dates that Modified Dates modifies, while Full Dates keeps the Study Date that Modified Dates shifts; a coverage carries its options through a merge, and rejects them other than as a tuple of strings. Under Retain Device Identity, a kept Station Name, Device Serial Number, or Treatment Machine Name holding a value found from an AE title that the option cleans, from a private data element, or from another attribute that the option keeps is released, with the exemption recorded, and under Retain Institution Identity a private copy of the institution's name found in its kept Institution Name; the same finding with no option, one found from Institution Name, Institutional Department Name, or a private creator, and one in other kept text or in a value with a byte outside ISO 646, go to review; and a person name found in kept text still withholds the file. A removed date equal to a kept Study Date under Full Dates is released, but withheld with no option or under Modified Dates, inside a longer kept datetime, or from the Study Date itself; and a removed UID equal to a kept SOP Instance UID under Retain UIDs still withholds. Under Retain Device Identity, an RT Plan whose Station AE Title equals its Station Name, with a private copy of the name, is released with both findings exempt, and one whose Station Name holds its removed Institution Name is held for review.

### D-043: Full Dates in the engine

- **Decision.**
  - The engine applies Retain Longitudinal Temporal Information with Full Dates (PS3.15 E.3.6), which a custom option set can select and no preset does. It keeps every date, time, and datetime that Table E.1-1 gives K under the option, 169 attributes in 2026d, and the 15 that the table omits and the supplementary rules give K under it (D-023), with Timezone Offset From UTC (0008,0201) among them, so no value is shifted or otherwise modified. Patient's Birth Date (0010,0030), Patient's Birth Time (0010,0032), and GPS Time Stamp (0016,0077), to which the option gives no action, keep their Basic Profile actions. It cannot be selected with Modified Dates, which E.3.6 makes mutually exclusive with it.
  - K needs no cleaner (D-034). The markers add the option's code, DCM 113106, and set Longitudinal Temporal Information Modified (0028,0303) to UNMODIFIED, unless MODIFIED or REMOVED is already present from an earlier run (D-012).
  - Kept dates stay in the residual search (D-027), and a finding in one is explained as D-042 says. Where a source value that the policy removes or replaces equals the whole of a kept date, such as a Patient's Birth Date equal to the Study Date of a scan made on the day of birth, or a date in an item of a sequence that the policy removes, such as Request Attributes Sequence (0040,0275), equal to it, the search finds it in the kept element, and the kept value explains it, so the instance is released with the finding exempt; found inside a longer kept value, such as the date of a kept datetime, it still withholds the instance.
- **Rationale.** E.3.6 requires the dates and times that Table E.1-1 lists to be retained and the marker to say UNMODIFIED, which the composed actions and the markers already give, so the option needs only to be accepted by the engine. Such a finding discloses nothing that the option does not already keep: the output holds only the kept date, and nothing in it says that a removed attribute had the same value. The exemption is D-042's, which the maintainer decided for every Retain option, so the option adds none of its own.
- **Tests.** Under Full Dates, an instance keeps every date, time, and datetime that the option keeps, and its Timezone Offset From UTC, and none is shifted; Patient's Birth Date and Patient's Birth Time are removed or emptied as under the Basic Profile; the markers carry DCM 113106 and UNMODIFIED, and a MODIFIED or REMOVED already present is kept; the gate releases the instance; an instance whose Patient's Birth Date, or a date in an item of its Request Attributes Sequence, equals its Study Date is released, with one residual date located in the kept element and exempt as a retained value; Full Dates and Modified Dates together are rejected; and the conformance statement says that no date or time is modified, counts the attributes kept, says that a removed value equal to a whole kept one is explained by it and one inside a longer kept value withholds the instance, gives UNMODIFIED, and lists nothing as not yet described.

### D-044: Retain Institution Identity

- **Decision.**
  - The engine applies Retain Institution Identity, one of the options in Scope, as the maintainer asked on 12 October 2026 for every option of PS3.15 Annex E, alone or with any other option that it applies. No preset selects it, and nothing conflicts with it: the Option gives K only, and no other option gives those attributes another action than the Basic Profile's.
  - The ten attributes that Table E.1-1 gives K under the Option are kept as read, wherever they are kept in the data set, with the Option's code: Institution Name (0008,0080), Institution Address (0008,0081), Institution Code Sequence (0008,0082), Institutional Department Name (0008,1040), Institutional Department Type Code Sequence (0008,1041), Clinical Trial Site ID (0012,0030), Clinical Trial Site Name (0012,0031), Clinical Trial Coordinating Center Name (0012,0060), Clinical Trial Protocol Ethics Committee Name (0012,0081), and Source of Previous Values (0400,0564). The Option gives no attribute C, so it needs no cleaner (D-034). An attribute in the items of a sequence that the policy removes goes with it: Source of Previous Values is defined only in Original Attributes Sequence (0400,0561), and Institution Name, Institution Address, and Institutional Department Name are also defined in the items of the sequences that identify people, such as Operator Identification Sequence (0008,1072), and of ROI Creator Sequence (3006,004D), all of which are removed under every option.
  - Clinical Trial Protocol Ethics Committee Name is removed under the Option too, as D-020 decided, since its Type 1C condition needs Clinical Trial Protocol Ethics Committee Approval Number (0012,0082), which Table E.1-1 removes under every option. The instance still claims the Option, and the conformance statement lists the name as removed wherever it is.
  - The attributes that Table E.1-1 omits and that name other organisations, such as Coding Scheme Responsible Organization (0008,0116), Strain Source (0010,0217), Algorithm Source (0024,0202), and the issuers of identifiers, stay removed under every option (D-022).
  - A kept value is not searched for (D-027), and a kept text value is one of the retained strings that `public-release` reviews (D-017), as for any attribute kept.
  - The residual search collects the values of removed attributes and looks for them in what is written (D-027). Under the Option, such a value can also be part of a kept institution value: a private element that names the site, a Station Name that repeats the institution's name, or a Referring Physician's Address in the same hospital as Institution Address. The maintainer decided on 12 October 2026 that one rule, D-042, settles such findings for every Retain option: a finding is released where the Option explains it, because its removed value came from a private element or from one of the Option's own attributes, and any other, such as an institution's name in a removed Station Name, still goes to QC review. Person names, UIDs, and direct identifiers still withhold the file. Until D-042's rule is in the engine, every such finding goes to review.
- **Rationale.** E.3.11 retains "information about the identity of the institution in the Attributes", "as described in Table E.1-1", for applications that need the institution to analyse or interpret images, or to keep a record of it for regulatory or registry purposes. The Option keeps what the Basic Profile removes and adds nothing that conflicts with another option, so it needs only the engine's existing rules, the option's code (D-012), and its place in Scope. Clinical Trial Protocol Ethics Committee Name stays removed because keeping it, without the approval number that every option removes, would make every instance that has it invalid, as `dciodvfy` reports (D-030), and E.1.1's Note makes keeping the IOD intact the de-identifier's responsibility. The Option's own rows make that one retention impossible in valid output, so the instance still claims the Option; sequestering every instance with the name was rejected, since it would discard instances that the Option can otherwise de-identify, and leaving out the Option's code was rejected because the instance would then claim that the Basic Profile removed the institution attributes that are kept (D-012). The organisations that the reviewed rules remove are not the institution where the acquisition was performed, which E.3.11 describes, and E.1.1 lets the de-identifier protect attributes beyond Table E.1-1, so they stay removed. No exemption is built here, since one rule for every Retain option (D-042) keeps the residual search's exemptions in one place.
- **Tests.** The Option is a target option that the engine applies, alone and with each other option it applies; under it, an RT Plan keeps Institution Name, Institution Address, Institutional Department Name, Institution Code Sequence, Institutional Department Type Code Sequence, the clinical trial site's ID and name, and the coordinating centre's name, and Institution Name in an item of Contributing Equipment Sequence, with the Option's code, while the Basic Profile removes, empties, or replaces each one; Clinical Trial Protocol Ethics Committee Name is removed, with its approval number, while the instance claims the Option; Source of Previous Values goes with Original Attributes Sequence; Coding Scheme Responsible Organization is removed; and the statement of the Option lists nothing as pending, lists the ten attributes as kept, and lists the ethics committee's name as removed wherever it is, with the reason D-020 gives.

### D-046: Clean Recognizable Visual Features by face review
