- **Decision.**
  - The engine applies the Clean Recognizable Visual Features Option (PS3.15 E.3.2) by face review: it claims the option only for an instance that cannot show a face, or in which a reviewer found no recognisable face, and holds every other instance that can show one for review, so that it is never released. The maintainer chose this on 12 October 2026, from the options of reviewing first and of a second run given the verdicts. Removing the face block from CT (D-050) changes what a defaced instance holds, never whether it claims the option.
  - A run under the option is given the QC pack of an earlier run of the same files, attested with face verdicts (D-016, D-017). The attestation must bind the pack, the pack every preview, and both must be of their formats; a pack that is not attested, or whose attestation's outcome is rejected, since a reviewer who rejected the pack has not cleared what it holds, or a verdict that names a series that the pack does not list, is refused when the verdicts are read, before the run starts. Each series marked absent gives the SHA-256 of each of its source files, and each series marked present likewise.
  - An instance can show a face where it holds Pixel Data (7FE0,0010), Float Pixel Data (7FE0,0008), or Double Float Pixel Data (7FE0,0009), or is an RT Structure Set, whose contours can trace the skin surface (D-015). Such an instance satisfies the option only where the SHA-256 of its source file, as the run's first pass read it, is that of a file of a series marked absent, and the SHA-256 of the pixel data that the run writes of it is that of the pixel data that the reviewed run wrote of that file, which the pack records only where its previews show every frame of them, for an RT Dose only its dose-levels previews (D-016, D-017), and the pack showed at least as many frames of them as the run writes, as, for an RT Structure Set, is the SHA-256 of the contours that it writes, which the pack records only where its contour sheet shows them (D-017). The pixel data's digest is of the value of each of Float Pixel Data, Double Float Pixel Data, and Pixel Data that the data set holds, in that order, each after its tag and length, a value of odd length being taken with its pad byte. The contours' digest is of the number of items of ROI Contour Sequence, then, for each in order, the number of its contours, and, for each contour in order, the length and UTF-8 bytes of its Contour Geometric Type, and, for each of its Contour Data, Contour Offset Vector, and Contour Slab Thickness, the number of its values and each value as a big-endian 64-bit float, each count and length a big-endian 64-bit unsigned integer, and a data set without a contour gives the digest of no items, so it changes with any contour that the run cuts, and not with how a value is written. Otherwise it is written without the option's markers and held for review, at the stage `visual-features`, with the reason `face-present` where its file is in a series marked present, `face-verdict-on-other-pixel-data` where its file is in a series marked absent but its pixel data are not those that the reviewed run wrote, such as a CT slice defaced since the reviewer saw it as received (D-050), or one whose written file the pack does not have, or of whose pixel data its previews do not show every frame, such as an instance listed as not previewed, one with more frames than its cine strip shows, outside a volume, or one whose native Pixel Data hold more than its frames, or whose pixel data have more frames than the pack showed, or that is an RT Dose with pixel data, by its SOP Class UID or a Modality that may be RTDOSE: unreadable, not text, of several values, with a character outside printable ASCII, or RTDOSE in any case, of more frames than its dose-levels previews showed, or of any where the review gives no dose levels' counts, as only they show an RT Dose as it could show a face, `face-verdict-on-other-contours` where an RT Structure Set's file is in a series marked absent but the contours it is written with are not those that the reviewed run wrote, such as one cut by a face block that the reviewed run did not find (D-052), and `no-face-verdict` where it is in none that the reviewer judged, such as a file changed since the review, or where it is an RT Structure Set whose contours the pack could not show, since the verdict on its series was given without them. A held instance is deleted with the staging area and never published. An instance that cannot show a face, such as an RT Plan or an MR Spectroscopy instance, satisfies the option. Under Clean Pixel Data as well, an instance that the face review clears is still held for review while its pixel data await it (D-037), so a face verdict never releases an instance that another stage holds; such an instance is held for its pixel data alone, and one in a series marked present for both. A face verdict clears it only where the reviewed run blacked out the same regions, since other regions write other pixel data.
  - An instance that satisfies the option gains its code, DCM 113102, and Recognizable Visual Features (0028,0302) set to NO, as E.3.2 requires, replacing any value already present (D-012). It gains the attribute whether or not its IOD defines it, so an RT Plan, RT Structure Set, or MR Spectroscopy instance that claims the option holds it as a standard attribute of a Standard Extended SOP Class (PS3.2 Section 7.3), as the maintainer chose on 13 October 2026 over releasing such instances without the option's code or holding them. The conformance statement lists the code with those that an instance gains only after review, as it lists Clean Descriptors' (D-009), and says when an instance gains it.
  - The verdicts are matched by digest, never by path or run position, so the second run may find the same files in another order, under other names, or among others. The release report's `face_review` section gives the reviewed pack's opaque reference; the method digest is unchanged, since the verdicts decide only which instances are released, never what a released instance holds (D-024).
  - The option joins the options that the engine applies (D-011). No preset selects it, and it is not enabled.
- **Rationale.** E.3.2 requires that "sufficient removal or distortion of the Pixel Data" prevent recognition wherever there is "sufficient visual information" to allow it. Removing a face from pixel data, where the engine does (D-050), does not by itself show that recognition is prevented, so for an instance that can show a face the engine meets the option only where a reviewer judged that the condition does not hold. Holding the other instances keeps the claim true for every released instance, and releasing them without the code was rejected because the run would then drop a selected option for some instances silently. A second run was chosen over setting the attribute in the first run's held output, because a held file is deleted and a published release is never added to (D-016). Matching source digests binds each verdict, through the attestation's digest of the pack, to the exact bytes the reviewer saw previews of; a file changed since is not covered. Matching the digest of the pixel data written as well binds it to what the reviewer saw of them, and the pack records that digest only where its previews show every frame, a volume being shown by its projection and surface render, which draw each of its slices, pooling slices closer than their pixels by their maximum so that none is skipped, as a recognition system would reconstruct the face from them all, while only its dose-levels previews show an RT Dose as it could show a face (D-017), so a run under the option holds an RT Dose with pixel data, whatever its verdict, unless they showed every frame of it; so under the option an Enhanced CT, MR, or PET instance of more frames than a cine strip shows is held, unless the Clean Pixel Data Option's review showed each of its frames (D-037), a cost in what is released rather than a leak, since a verdict on a series releases only what the reviewer previewed, since a run can write other pixel data of the same file than the reviewed run did, by defacing it or by blacking out other regions, and a verdict on one is not a verdict on the other; the pair is matched, rather than the pixel data alone, so that the verdict stays one on a source file. An RT Structure Set has no pixel data, so the pack shows its contours as written on a contour sheet, drawn with the images they lie on, and the reviewer gives the verdict on its own series from that sheet (D-017); where the run defaces those images, its outline would still trace the face that they no longer show, so it is cut by the same block, or held whatever its verdict where it cannot be (D-052); its contours are digested apart from pixel data, so that the pack names what each digest covers. E.3.2 adds Recognizable Visual Features with no exception for an IOD that does not define it, and an instance that claims the option without it would not meet the option; adding it only where the IOD defines it was therefore rejected, and the validators' findings of it in the other IODs are known differences that apply only to an output holding the option's code, so the attribute added to any other output still fails the comparison. Defacing CT (D-050) does not let the option claim an instance by itself, since an instance whose face was removed still claims it only where a reviewer found no recognisable face. Its face block, the region to remove, is found from the image alone, in the patient's coordinates: everything in front of a vertical plane 5 mm behind the centres of the two eye globes, perpendicular to the line between them so that it turns with the head, and superior to the lower globe's inferior edge, lowered by a voxel or by the largest slice spacing across the globes' height, whichever is larger, for the sampling, to the edges of the field of view. The globes are found as the one pair of balls of soft tissue side by side, each with at least half of a sphere around it, and four fifths of one half of it, outside the soft tissue, as the orbital fat is behind a globe whatever touches its front, and each with bone lateral and superior to it; the head must lie behind them, and bone must enclose more above them, where the cranium is, than below. A volume without both eyes in view, with more than one such pair, with its face towards posterior, or upside down, as a patient position entered wrongly would give, with no cranium in view above the eyes, or whose slices do not lie along the patient's axes, as with a gantry tilt, is refused rather than guessed at. The region follows defaceRT's (MIT licence), which removes everything in front of the centres of the eye contours from their lowest slice up, but lies 5 mm further back and needs no ROI names or image layout; defaceRT keeps the brain and the PTVs within its region by default, which D-050 weighs.
- **Tests.** A face review gives each verdict's series' source digests, and a pack that is not attested, whose attestation rejected it, or that was changed after it was attested, gives none; attesting refuses a face verdict with an outcome of rejected; a review is checked, and its repr shows only the reference and counts; an instance with any pixel data, or an RT Structure Set, needs a verdict, and an RT Plan or MR Spectroscopy instance does not; a review is given to the transform exactly when the policy selects the option, and the option is applied but not enabled; the markers allow the option to go unsatisfied, set Recognizable Visual Features to NO with the option's code, whether or not the data set's IOD defines it, and otherwise leave it as received, and set it only to NO; the statement lists the code after Clean Descriptors' as one gained only after review, and says when; the release report records the reference and counts the held instances by reason; a second run of the synthetic collection, its files renamed into the reverse order and given an attestation that marks the CT and RT Structure Set series absent and the RT Dose series present, releases the CT instances and the RT Structure Set with the code and NO, holds the RT Dose instances as `face-present` and every other image as `no-face-verdict`, and releases the RT Plan and the MR Spectroscopy instance with the code and NO; a review gives each file marked absent with the pixel data written of it, where the pack has them, and holds as `face-verdict-on-other-pixel-data` a file marked absent whose pixel data are others, none, or unreadable; the pixel data's digest takes each element's tag, value, and pad byte; a pack lists the pixel data's digest only for a released or held instance, as 64 lower-case hexadecimal digits, and a run lists that of each such file's written pixel data, or none where reading it raises anything; each image of the compressed collection marked absent is held without its written pixel data and released with them, codestreams cut of their metadata included, its RT Dose images only where the review gives every frame as shown by their dose levels; and a corpus run given a face review that marks every file absent releases what the Basic Profile releases, its RT Dose images given every frame as shown by their dose levels, each instance with the code and NO, while every other run keeps Recognizable Visual Features as received, and the validators find nothing it introduces but Recognizable Visual Features in the IODs that do not define it, which the known differences explain only in an output that holds the option's code; and the conformance statement names exactly the supported IODs that do not define the attribute; and the same second run under Clean Pixel Data as well holds the CT instances for their pixel data and as `face-verdict-on-other-pixel-data`, since their corners were not blacked out in the reviewed run, and the RT Dose instance for its pixel data and `face-present`, and releases the RT Structure Set, the RT Plan, and the MR Spectroscopy instance with the code and NO, and with Clean Pixel Data's code and Burned In Annotation NO, since they hold no pixel data (D-048); and an analytic head's region to remove is found from its eye globes, axial head first supine, feet first, prone, and prone feet first, and sagittal, through noise, a bolus over the eyes, thick slices, coarse pixels, and a long volume, holds the upper face and the air before it, where a mask would lie, keeps the back of each globe, the nose below the eyes, the mouth, and the back of the head, and turns with a head turned 20 or 25 degrees; it is the same for slices given out of order, and a wide gap between slices far from the eyes does not move it; and it is refused for a head without both eyes, a volume that cuts through them, spheres without a bony orbit, two heads in view, a head facing posterior, a head upside down, a volume that ends above the orbits, with or without bone below them, a gantry tilt of 15 or 0.5 degrees, an oblique volume, slices sheared within their plane by more than 0.01 mm, direction cosines that are not of unit length, and two slices at one position.

### D-047: Clean Structured Content by the concept names of content items

- **Decision.**
  - The engine applies the Clean Structured Content Option (PS3.15 E.3.4) to a custom option set; no preset selects it. Table E.1-1 gives C under the option to the four sequences that hold content items. Two of them are defined by the IODs of the first supported release: Acquisition Context Sequence (0040,0555), whose items are content items, in the Enhanced and Legacy Converted Enhanced images, the PET Image, and MR Spectroscopy; and Specimen Preparation Sequence (0040,0610), whose items each hold their content items in Specimen Preparation Step Content Item Sequence (0040,0612), in the Specimen Module, which every supported image IOD allows. Content Sequence (0040,A730) and Waveform Annotation Sequence (0040,B020) are defined only by IODs outside that release (D-010), so they take their Basic Profile actions, as any sequence given C does (D-022).
  - Under the option, each of the two sequences is kept where the IOD defines it at its place, and its items' elements take their own rules, as in any kept sequence. Each content item, and each of its modifiers in Content Item Modifier Sequence (0040,0441), is looked up in Table E.3.4-1 (D-001) by the Coding Scheme Designator and Code Value of its Concept Name Code Sequence (0040,A043) and its Value Type (0040,A040), with NUMERIC, which the Content Item Macro (PS3.3 Table 10-2) uses, read as the table's NUM.
  - An item whose concept name the table lists gives its value attribute, Text Value, Person Name, Date, Time, DateTime, or UID, the action that the selected options give the concept, or else its Basic Profile action. C under Clean Descriptors, which no cleaner of content items settles, falls back to the action without that option, as D-009 has an attribute's C do where descriptor cleaning does not write it; where the remaining options give K and C, C applies, as `tps-import` resolves Retain Device Identity's K and Modified Dates' C, though no concept has both in 2026d. C under Retain Longitudinal Temporal Information with Modified Dates is settled by its cleaner, by the temporal role of the value attribute (D-034). For a reference, a code, or a number, K and D need no change: the engine already replaces UIDs or keeps them under Retain UIDs, and keeps codes and numbers.
  - An item whose concept name the table does not list keeps its codes and numbers, and its other values take their own rules: Table E.1-1 gives Person Name (0040,A123), Date (0040,A121), Time (0040,A122), and DateTime (0040,A120) D, and UID (0040,A124) U, and Text Value (0040,A160) has a supplementary rule, X/Z/D, which gives D where it is Type 1C (D-022).
  - The whole sequence takes its Basic Profile action, which empties it where it is Type 2, in place of being kept, where any of its content items cannot be kept: the table gives it X or X/D, which removes it, since no template that the engine checks requires it; its Value Type or concept name cannot be read as one Value Type and one code; or its concept name is coded in LOINC, NCDR, NCIt, or UMLS, whose rows the pin leaves out (D-001), or by a retired SNOMED designator, SRT, SNM3, or 99SDM, which PS3.16 Annex O maps to SNOMED CT codes. A concept name given only by Long Code Value (0008,0119) or URN Code Value (0008,0120) is not listed.
  - Every instance written under the option claims it, with DCM 113104. The conformance statement gives each of the two sequences C and describes the cleaning, and the other two their Basic Profile actions, and lists nothing as pending.
- **Rationale.** E.3.4 removes the information in content items "corresponding to the Attribute information specified to be removed by the Profile and any other Options specified", and Table E.3.4-1 names the concepts that carry it, so each listed item takes its row's action, as an attribute takes its row's. An item that the table does not list is treated as the profile treats any kept sequence's items, "recursively applying the Profile rules to each Data Set in each Item of the Sequence" (Table E.1-1a), so it keeps codes and numbers, as PS3.15 Notes 9 and 10 after Table E.1-1a say is usually safe, and its names, dates, free text, and UIDs are replaced as anywhere else. Removing every unlisted item was rejected because it keeps nothing that the Basic Profile's emptying does not, while the profile rules leak nothing that they do not already accept for any kept sequence. The writer keeps every item of a kept sequence, so that output items match source items for the plan, the preservation check, and every report that names a path; removing one item would change all of them, so an item that must be removed takes its whole sequence with it, which removes more than the option requires and is allowed, since Table E.1-1 gives the minimum actions. The schemes whose rows the generated table cannot hold are treated the same way, so that an item that the full table might remove is never kept by a generic rule; generating PS3.16 Annex O's mapping of retired SNOMED codes would let such items be matched, and is later work. Falling back from Clean Descriptors' C follows D-009's rule for a value that descriptor cleaning does not clean, so that a cleaner of content-item text can later settle it in its place.
- **Tests.** The engine applies the option, and Table E.1-1 gives C to the four sequences, two of which hold content items in the supported IODs; each concept takes the action that its options give, X/D removes, Clean Descriptors' C falls back, and C applies over K; the schemes never matched are those the pin leaves out and the retired SNOMED designators; without the option nothing changes; under it, an Enhanced CT Image keeps an Acquisition Context Sequence of unlisted coded, numeric, and text items, with the codes and numbers kept and the text replaced, and claims the option; a listed name is replaced; the walker gives the content items' actions with their own rule source; a listed item that the table removes, a concept coded by LOINC, SRT, or SNM3, and an item that cannot be read each empty the sequence; a long code value is not listed; modifiers are content items too; Retain Device Identity keeps a Device Serial Number, Full Dates keep an Acquisition Date and Modified Dates modify it, Retain UIDs keeps a Device Observer UID, and Clean Descriptors falls back for listed text, and for unlisted text the reviewed descriptors list does not cover; Specimen Preparation Sequence's content items are cleaned in a CT Image, and an item to remove empties it; a sequence that the IOD does not define at its place is not kept; and the statement describes the cleaning. The synthetic corpus, with acquisition context items added to its Enhanced, Legacy Converted Enhanced, PET, and MR Spectroscopy instances, runs under the option alone.

### D-048: Reviewed redactions

- **Decision.**
  - A run under the Clean Pixel Data Option may be given the QC pack of an earlier run of the same files under the option, attested with redaction verdicts (D-016, D-037), as the maintainer chose on 12 October 2026, from the options of reviewing first and of a second run given the verdicts. As for face verdicts (D-046), the attestation must bind the pack, the pack every preview, and both must be of their formats. A pack that is not attested, or whose attestation's outcome is rejected, a verdict on an instance that the pack does not list as cleaned and previewed, or lists without its source's SHA-256, and verdicts that disagree on one source file, such as identical copies given different verdicts, are refused when the verdicts are read, before the run starts. A cleaned instance that the pack lists as not previewed, such as one whose source could not be shown beside it, can have no verdict, and attesting refuses one, as it refuses any verdict given with an outcome of rejected (D-016).
  - A verdict names an instance by its run position, and the pack gives the SHA-256 of its source file, as the first run's first pass read it. It is accepted, revised, or rejected. Accepted takes the regions that the pack lists for the instance. Revised takes those and adds the reviewer's own, which the reviewer types as coordinates read from the previews, by frame index from 0, each region as `[top, left, bottom, right]` as the pack gives its own, so that together they cover every burned-in text that the reviewer saw; it adds at least one region. Rejected releases nothing.
  - An instance whose source file's SHA-256 has an accepted or revised verdict has exactly those regions blacked out, without the finder, with the fill and the check of the written value that D-037 gives a finder's regions. Under Clean Recognizable Visual Features as well, a CT slice whose series the run defaces also has its face block written as air, and the face review decides on the pixel data so written (D-050). A reviewed region is never clipped: attesting refuses one that goes past the Rows or Columns that the pack records for its instance, or lies in a frame that the pack does not count, and a run given the verdicts checks again, so one that goes past its frame's rows or columns, or lies in a frame that the Pixel Data do not hold, sequesters the instance as `redaction-mismatch`. Otherwise the instance satisfies the option, and is released unless another stage holds it. An instance whose source file has a rejected verdict is sequestered as `redaction-rejected`. Any other instance with Pixel Data is cleaned by the finder and held for review, as in the first run, so a file changed since the review is held again.
  - An instance without top-level pixel data satisfies the option in any run under it, since it holds no pixel data to clean, as an instance that cannot show a face satisfies Clean Recognizable Visual Features (D-046). The maintainer chose this on 18 October 2026.
  - An instance that satisfies the option gains its code, DCM 113101, and Burned In Annotation (0028,0301) set to NO, as E.3.1 requires, replacing any value already present (D-012). It gains the attribute whether or not its IOD defines it, so an RT Plan, RT Structure Set, or MR Spectroscopy instance that claims the option holds it as a standard attribute of a Standard Extended SOP Class (PS3.2 Section 7.3), as D-046 has Recognizable Visual Features. The conformance statement lists the code with those that an instance gains only after review, and says when an instance gains it.
  - The release report's `redaction_review` section gives the reviewed pack's opaque reference; the method digest is unchanged (D-024). It describes the run's own finder, which an instance with an accepted or revised verdict never meets, so D-024's "the regions it finds are what the option writes" holds for every other instance; the finder that proposed the reviewed regions is described by the method digest of the first run, whose release report names the same pack by its reference.
- **Rationale.** E.3.1 allows a process that "may require intervention of or approval by a human operator", and its claim must hold for every released instance; the reviewer, who saw every frame of the instance as received and as written (D-037), gives that approval, so an instance whose frames the pack could not show can have none. A reviewer who rejected the pack has not approved what it holds, so its verdicts release nothing. Applying the reviewed regions without the finder releases exactly what the reviewer approved. Running the finder again and comparing its regions was rejected, since a finder whose output differs in another runtime would hold an instance that the reviewer approved, and added regions could never match it. Revised adds to the finder's regions rather than replacing them, as the plan of 12 October 2026 has it, so that a reviewer who adds a missed region need not retype every region that the finder found, and leaving one out cannot release text that the finder found; removing a finder's region that covers no text is not provided for, since it blacks out more than is needed, which E.3.1 allows. The added regions are applied without a further review, and the summary of the attestation lists each one, beside the file in the pack that previews its frame, for the reviewer to check against that frame as they record it. A region inside its frame but misplaced, such as one whose coordinates are transposed or that is given for another frame, cannot be detected, and would leave the text that it was meant to cover while the instance claims the option; that residual risk rests on the reviewer's check of the summary. A region past its frame is refused when it is attested, from the Rows and Columns that the pack records for the instance, rather than only when the second run applies it, since the pack is attested once, and a verdict found wrong only then could not be corrected. Attesting refuses a verdict given with an outcome of rejected, since the pack is attested once and the verdict could release nothing, so recording it would only mislead. Clipping a reviewed region, as a finder's is clipped, was rejected because a transposed or mistyped region would then cover less than the reviewer meant, or nothing, while the instance claimed the option. Matching source digests binds each verdict, through the attestation's digest of the pack, to the exact bytes whose previews the reviewer saw, as for face verdicts. The verdicts and their regions are decisions about particular files, bound to their digests and recorded by the reference of the pack that holds them, whereas the method digest identifies a method that applies to any file, as the reviewed-names list does by deciding values that recur across files (D-024); so they decide which instances are released and which regions are blacked out in them, but are not part of the method. Sequestering a rejected instance, rather than holding it, records in the release report that it will not be released, whereas a held instance awaits a review. An instance without top-level pixel data holds nothing that the option cleans, since the Basic Profile removes icons (D-037), and holding it or releasing it without the code would drop a selected option for it although it meets the option. E.3.1's informative Note says that the option "is intended to apply to the Pixel Data (7FE0,0010) Attribute that occurs in the top level Data Set of an Image Storage SOP Instance", as against the icon images that Table E.1-1 removes, and does not say whether an instance of another IOD may claim it; claiming it for an RT Plan, RT Structure Set, or MR Spectroscopy instance is therefore the engine's reading of E.3.1, met because such an instance holds nothing to clean. E.3.1's normative text adds Burned In Annotation with no exception for an IOD that does not define it, and the validators' findings of it in the other IODs are known differences that apply only to an output holding the option's code, as for Recognizable Visual Features (D-046).
- **Tests.** A redaction review gives each accepted instance's pack regions, each revised instance's pack regions with the reviewer's added, and each rejected instance's source digest; a pack that is not attested, or whose attestation rejected it, a verdict on an instance that is not cleaned or not previewed, and verdicts that disagree on a source file are refused; a review is checked, and its repr shows only the reference and counts; a verdict is checked, a revised one needs a region, an attestation with verdicts is read back as written, and one of an earlier format is refused; attesting refuses a verdict on an instance that is not cleaned, or is listed as not previewed, a region in a frame that it does not hold or past the Rows or Columns that the pack records for it, though not one that reaches its last row and column, and any verdict with an outcome of rejected; reviewed regions are blacked out alone, a frame without them is left as it is, and a region in a frame that the Pixel Data do not hold, or past the frame's rows or columns, is refused; a review is given to the transform only under the option; the markers set Burned In Annotation to NO with the option's code, whether or not the data set's IOD defines it, and otherwise leave it as received; the supported IODs that do not define the attribute are those the statement names; the statement lists the code as one gained only after review, and says when; the release report records the reference; the attestation command records each verdict, lists each added region with the preview of its frame, and refuses a verdict with an outcome of rejected, an instance given two, a revised instance without a region, and a region for an instance that is not revised; a second run of synthetic files, given the verdicts on its first run's pack, releases an accepted and a revised image with their regions blacked out, the code, and NO, sequesters a rejected image as `redaction-rejected` and one whose added region goes past its frame, in a pack made to record more columns for it, as `redaction-mismatch`, holds every other image, and releases the RT Plan and the RT Structure Set with the code and NO; a second run with a finder of another method that finds nothing writes the reviewed regions all the same, never shows the finder an approved image, and releases no other image; under Clean Recognizable Visual Features as well, a revised region over more than air on a defaced CT slice holds the slice as `face-verdict-on-other-pixel-data`, and not for its pixel data; and a corpus run given a review that accepts a region in every frame releases what the Basic Profile releases, each instance with the code and NO, while the validators find nothing it introduces but Burned In Annotation in the IODs that do not define it.

### D-050: Defacing CT under Clean Recognizable Visual Features

- **Decision.**
  - Under the Clean Recognizable Visual Features Option, a run finds, after its first pass and before it writes any instance, the face block (D-046) of each series of CT Image instances, by Series Instance UID, and writes air, -1000 Hounsfield units, into the pixels of each slice of a defaced series whose centres lie in the block. Every pixel outside the block keeps its stored value, so a slice wholly outside it is unchanged. Within the block, two kinds of pixel are kept: each of Pixel Padding Value or its range, and each of the brain in the cranial cavity, which bone at 300 Hounsfield units or more encloses within the slice, more than 30 mm above the higher globe's centre, which keeps the brain above the orbits that lies in front of the block's plane. The brain is the cavity's pixels above -500 Hounsfield units that join, within the slice, such pixels behind the block's plane, and the cavity's pixels that they enclose, such as air, but never bone, which is written as air, so that the brain is never more than the cavity. So the air of a frontal sinus, which bone walls in and so encloses too, is written as air, as is any tissue lining it, where the sinus lies ahead of the plane and bone parts it from the brain within the slice: kept, the sinus's air would differ from the air written around it only by its noise, and its outline, which can identify a person, would remain. Where the sinus's back wall reads below 300 Hounsfield units in a slice, as a thin wall can, its lining can join the brain, and the sinus is kept there, as is a sinus whose lining or fluid reaches behind the plane, since any of the cavity's tissue behind the plane counts as brain. A slice with no brain behind the plane, such as a coronal slice ahead of it, keeps none. Where air's stored value is itself in the padding range, the pixels written are padding, which a viewer shows as outside the image, as the air before the face is in most scans. Where no bone encloses the cavity within a slice, the block is air to the top of the slice. The maintainer chose keeping the cavity on 14 October 2026, over removing everything in front of the plane to the top of the volume, frontal brain included.
  - The block removes the skin, fat, muscle, and bone of the forehead, brows, eyelids, and upper nose, the lens and the front of each globe, and anything resting on them, such as an immobilisation mask. It keeps the back few millimetres of each globe, behind the plane 5 mm behind their centres, the nose below the eyes' lower edge, the lips, the chin, the ears, which lie well behind the plane and which a block wide enough to take them would take the sides of the head with, and everything behind the plane. Planning loses the lenses and the front of each globe, and any dose-volume histogram of a structure in the block changes.
  - A series is defaced only where every slice can be read again from the file that the first pass read, with native Pixel Data of one frame, one sample, and whole bytes, and finite Image Position (Patient), Image Orientation (Patient), Pixel Spacing, Rescale Slope, and Rescale Intercept; where the slices share their orientation, rows, columns, spacing, and Frame of Reference UID, and the stored values of each can hold air as one stored value without clipping; and where the face block is found from the slices within 400 mm of the volume's superior end. A series without a Frame of Reference UID is unreadable, and any failure to read a slice or find its block leaves the series undefaced with a reason, never stopping the run. Any other series, such as one without a head, one that the face block's finder refuses, one of slices with more than one frame, one whose stored values cannot hold air, or one with compressed Pixel Data, which would have to be encoded again, is written as it would be without the option's defacing, and the face review decides, as for every instance that can show a face (D-046): no series is held for review because it has no face block. Enhanced CT and Legacy Converted Enhanced CT instances are likewise not defaced, and their verdicts decide them.
  - A series is not defaced, and each of its slices is held for review at the stage `visual-features` with the reason `target-in-face-block`, where a PTV, CTV, or GTV, by RT ROI Interpreted Type, in an RT Structure Set of the run in the series' Frame of Reference, or naming none, has a contour that reaches the block, or anything between its contours that does, judged by the convex hull of all its contours' points, or has a coordinate that is not finite or exceeds a kilometre in magnitude. The hull holds whatever a planning system interpolates between contours, such as between contours drawn on PET slices that lie between the CT's, or between sagittal ones, and each contour's segments and interior, so that a sagittal or oblique contour crossing the block between its vertices holds the series; it errs towards holding a target that wraps around the block's lower rear edge without entering it, such as one spanning the maxilla and the orbital apex. A series is also held where a target in another frame, whose contours cannot be placed without a registration, is in a structure set that ties that frame to the series' slices, by a contour of an ROI in that frame drawn on one of them, or by the frame's own list of contour images naming one, whatever other frames the set declares, or where a structure set searched for either cannot be read. Every structure set is searched that may be of the patient of a slice of the series, as it may be unless both give one Patient ID of printable ASCII characters alone, without its padding, and the two differ without case, whatever their Issuers of Patient ID, which planning systems write their own of or omit; a Patient ID that is absent, empty, not text, of several values, or holds any other character, such as a control character, a letter outside ASCII, or a fullwidth form, differs from none, since text that may be the same in another spelling, under Unicode normalisation or case mapping, cannot tell patients apart, and a rule of ASCII alone gives the same answer whatever Unicode version Python follows, and any other in the series' frame, naming no valid frame, or drawn on its slices, since a target whose frame is unsure may be in the series' frame whatever frames its set declares. Plain Patient IDs that differ only by a space or punctuation, such as `12-345` and `12345`, differ, so a structure set of the same patient whose Patient ID another system wrote so is passed over unless it is in the series' frame, names no valid frame, or is drawn on its slices, and a target in it whose ROI names no frame does not then hold the series. A target in another frame that the set does not tie to the series' slices, such as one of another scan, does not hold it. A contour's frame is its ROI's Referenced Frame of Reference UID; a target whose ROI gives none is taken to be in the series' frame and in each frame that its structure set declares, so it holds the series where its hull reaches the block or its set ties any of those frames to the series' slices. Malformed input is taken the same way, so that it fails safe: an ROI's or a declared frame of more than one value, or one that is not a UID, and an ROI's frame that its set does not declare as one UID in one item, which PS3.3 C.8.8.5 requires it to, is taken as naming no frame and as each label it gives, so that a declaration of several values may name a frame but never makes an ROI's frame certain, so that a label that its set ties to the series' slices still holds the series, and an ROI Number repeated with different frames is taken to be in each of them as well.
  - A series is likewise not defaced, and each of its slices is held for review with the reason `eyes-disagree-with-rois`, where an eye of a structure set searched for targets, an ROI in the series' Frame of Reference or naming none, a malformed frame counting as none as for a target, whose ROI Name names an eye, a globe, or an eyeball, by its words, alone or with its side, and not a lens, a cornea, a ring, or an avoidance region, has a contour point more than 25 mm from the nearer centre of the globes from which the block was found, or one that is not a number, or where such a set cannot be read again. A name's words are those that an underscore, a space, other punctuation, a digit, or a change of case parts, such as `Eye`, `L`, and `PRV` in `Eye_L_PRV05`. A target in the block is the reason before the eyes. An eye in another frame, which cannot be placed without a registration, and an ROI that the set names otherwise, such as in another language, are not checked.
  - Defacing never claims the option: a defaced slice claims it only where its source file is in a series that the face review marked absent and the pixel data written of it are those that the reviewed run wrote (D-046), so a verdict on a series seen as received does not release it defaced, and the slices that defacing changes are held as `face-verdict-on-other-pixel-data`. A run under the option needs a face review, so the first run to deface a series is given a review with no verdict on it, which holds it, and its QC pack previews the slices defaced; the verdicts on that pack release the next run's, which writes the same pixel data. Defacing CT thus costs one run more than a review of it as received. Under Clean Pixel Data as well, regions of text revised after the review change the pixel data written, so the slices are held until their faces are reviewed again.
  - Beyond CT Image instances, the run cleans the same region of an RT Dose, set to zero there (D-051), and of an RT Structure Set's contours and a Positron Emission Tomography image's activity, cut or set to zero there (D-052). Any other RT Structure Set, RT Dose, or Positron Emission Tomography, Enhanced PET, or Legacy Converted Enhanced PET instance in a defaced series' Frame of Reference keeps its contours and pixel data, so it is held for review at the stage `visual-features` with the reason `undefaced-in-defaced-frame`, whatever its own series' verdict, as is one of these that names no Frame of Reference in a run that defaces any series, or that refers to a defaced slice, as a structure set's Contour Image Sequence does, whatever frame it declares. The hold goes by frame and slice references, not by what the instance shows, but an RT Dose without pixel data, holding only dose-volume histograms, shows no face and is not held (D-051). The QC pack lists each with that reason, and a verdict of absent on its series does not cover it in a later run, whatever that run defaces, since the reviewer judged it beside the defaced images. A structure set's outline can trace the face, and its contour sheet shows its contours as the run wrote them, uncut where the run holds it, drawn on the defaced images (D-017); RT Dose's low-dose envelope and PET's body outline can show the face too, and a reviewer may judge them beside the defaced CT. They are released only where the run cleans them (D-051, D-052). Any other instance in that frame, such as an MR series or a CT series that is not defaced, keeps its own verdict, which its own previews, of the pixel data it is written with, support. Structure sets are searched for targets in the block of each series, and a target in another frame holds a series whose slices its structure set ties to that frame, as above. A later step renders each defaced series' surface for the reviewer.
  - The release report's `face_review` section counts, in `ct_series`, the CT series that the run defaced and those it did not, by reason: a face block finder's refusal (`not-along-patient-axes`, `no-eyes`, `ambiguous-eyes`, `head-not-behind-eyes`, `cranium-not-above-eyes`, or `too-large`), `unreadable`, `compressed-pixel-data`, `not-one-stack`, `air-not-stored`, `target-in-block`, or `eyes-disagree-with-rois`, and names none of them.
  - The finder samples to cubic voxels of 1.5 mm or more and at most 2^24 in all, refusing (`too-large`) a volume that would need voxels larger than 2.5 mm, and takes the distance transform in slabs of 32 voxels along the slices, overlapped by the depth it needs, so that the memory a series needs is bounded whatever its length.
- **Rationale.** Removing the face from CT is meant to let a reviewer find no recognisable face in head CT that would otherwise show one. Of the CT defacing methods tested against face recognition, defaceRT is the one built for radiotherapy data, and its code is permissively licensed: with one recogniser (FaceNet512 through DeepFace, at the cosine distance that maximised Youden's index on the same pairs), frontal body-contour renders of 70 head-and-neck patients' planning CTs matched their replanning CTs in 68 cases before defacing and 3 after, against 258 of 2,415 pairs of different patients (O'Sullivan-Steben and others, Med Phys 2025;52:e70160, preprint arXiv:2508.13914, sections II.C and III.B). For other defacing methods, built without radiotherapy data in mind, CT matches fell from 78% to 5% (Schwarz and others, NeuroImage 2022) and from 93% to 6% (Selfridge and others, J Nucl Med 2023, whose 7% is its PET figure, which defaceRT's paper quotes for CT). defaceRT's result is for its default, which keeps the brain and the PTVs within its block (section II.B), so keeping the cavity follows the tested method. The cavity is kept only above the orbits since an orbital roof can be thinner than a millimetre, so bone in a slice can enclose an orbit, or fail to seal it from the cavity, and an eye kept in front of the plane would undo the block; the 30 mm above the higher globe's centre is the largest globe radius the finder accepts, 15 mm, and as much again, about 18 mm above an adult eye's top, a placeholder, like the 5 mm margin, to be set by validation, and the reviewer's previews remain the backstop. The brain carries no face, although brain anatomy can match scans of the same person, a risk under either choice since nearly all of the brain lies behind the plane. A target in the block holds its series rather than being removed, which would lose it for planning unseen, or kept, which would leave part of the face; 4.9% (39 of 793) of defaceRT's defaced CTs had a PTV overlapping its initial crop (section III.C.2). defaceRT places its block from the eye contours themselves; this block is found from the image alone, so a structure set's eyes, where it has them, check it: an eye drawn away from the globes found means that the finder took something else for a globe, or that the set's eyes are not where the image shows them, and either way the block cannot be trusted to hold the eyes, so the series is held rather than defaced. The 25 mm is the largest globe radius that the finder accepts, 15 mm, and 10 mm more for an eye drawn with a margin, such as a planning organ-at-risk volume, or a globe found off its centre, a placeholder to be set by validation. Lenses, corneas, rings, and avoidance regions are left out since a name that holds an eye's word can name them, and a ring or avoidance region need not lie near a globe. The check can find a block placed wrongly, never show one placed rightly, and the reviewer's previews remain the backstop. Writing air in place keeps every byte outside the block, and refusing compressed Pixel Data keeps the engine from encoding pixel data again. A series without a block is not held for that alone, as most series in a run show no head, and the review decides. Stored values that cannot hold air would need clipping, which writes another density, so such a series is not defaced. Searching only the 400 mm below the superior end bounds memory; a scan with the arms raised above the head may then show no eyes, which costs yield, never safety.
- **Tests.** The face block is found from DICOM slices of an analytic head, per series; no head, a slice that cannot be read again, slices of another size, unsigned stored values with no intercept, and RLE Lossless Pixel Data leave a series undefaced with their reasons; a PTV, CTV, or GTV with a point in the block, or a structure set that cannot be read, holds the series, as do a sagittal target triangle with no vertex in the block whose segment crosses it and two axial target contours, neither reaching the block, whose hull does, while two behind the plane, or two whose hull passes below the block's corner, do not, and a point too far to place, or not finite, holds it, while an organ, an outline, a target behind the plane, an ROI without a type, and a target in another Frame of Reference do not; slices beyond reach of the superior end are not searched; the outcomes are counted by reason; the air written lies within the block, covers the front of each globe, keeps the brain above the orbits and the padding, and is the stored value of -1000; bone enclosing an orbit within a slice keeps no part of the eye, which it would without the floor; a slice below the block has nothing written, and one whose air cannot be stored is refused; the pixel writer writes a mask's value, sign included, after the text regions, frame by frame, refuses a mask of another frame or shape, of a value outside storage, or of colour data, and finds written pixel data that differs from the mask; the block found with the distance transform taken in slabs is the block found from the whole volume's, its geometry must be finite, and a volume that would need voxels too large is refused; a slice without Image Position (Patient), with five direction cosines, non-finite spacing or position, a rescale too small to store air, a short Pixel Padding Value or limit, truncated pixel data, colour, or a series without a Frame of Reference UID leaves the series undefaced with its reason, never an error; a target whose ROI names no frame holds the series; the brain is written as air 27 mm above the globes' centres and kept 33 mm above, measured from the higher globe; a skull open within a slice keeps no brain, and padding that reads as bone closes no ring; a frontal sinus walled in by bone ahead of the brain, of air or lined with tissue, is written as air, which it was not before, in one slice and in every slice of a head with one, while the brain beside it is kept; a calcification within the kept brain is written as air, as bone always was; and the synthetic collection with its CT slices replaced by the analytic head's, its CT series alone marked absent in the pack of a first run under the option, which previews them defaced, is released by a second run with each head slice carrying the option's code and NO, defaced, with the eyes air, nothing outside the block changed, and the brain above the floor kept, while the Enhanced PET and Legacy Converted Enhanced PET instances in the same frame, also marked absent, are held as `undefaced-in-defaced-frame`, and without a verdict, since the first run held them for the same reason, and one series is counted defaced; in a second run without the head's slices, which defaces nothing, those instances are held without a verdict, since the pack held them for the defaced frame; RT and PET instances that name no frame, or refer to a defaced slice from another frame, are held, while one in another frame, and MR and CT instances, are not; a defaced series records its Frame of Reference UID and no other does; a structure set that names no frame is searched for targets, and a structure set in another frame drawn on the series' slices holds the series if it has a target, in the block or behind the plane, and not for an organ alone; a target behind the plane in another frame, in a structure set with an organ in the series' frame drawn on its slices, holds the series where its own contour, another ROI's in its frame, or its frame's list of contour images names one of the series' slices, and not where none does, even with its contour in the block; a set naming only another frame and tied only by that frame's list of contour images holds the series; a PTV whose ROI names no frame, by an empty or absent value, in a set naming only another frame and drawn on the series' slices, or tied only by that frame's list of contour images, holds the series though its hull misses the block, as does one in a set naming the series' frame and another so tied; a PTV in the block holds the series where its ROI's frame has two values, is not a UID, or is repeated under its ROI Number with another frame, and where its set names only frames that are not UIDs; a PTV in the block, in a set declaring only another frame and not tied to the series' slices, holds the series where its frame is empty, absent, not a UID, of two values, repeated with another frame, or a valid UID that the set does not declare, or declares only among other values or twice in one value, while one declared alone as well leaves it defaced, and not where the set gives another Patient ID, while a Patient ID absent, empty, of several values, holding a control character, inside it or after it, or spelt in other Unicode forms, such as composed and decomposed letters, fullwidth letters, or a dotless i, on the set or on one slice of the series, padding, case, or issuers, given on one side or differing, do not keep it from being searched, and a set of the Patient ID of either half of a series whose slices give two is searched; a PTV behind the plane whose ROI and set give the same label that is not a UID holds the series where that frame's list of contour images names one of its slices, and not otherwise; a QC pack entry without its reasons gives no face review; marked absent in the pack of a run under the Basic Profile instead, each slice that defacing changes is held as `face-verdict-on-other-pixel-data`, and each it leaves unchanged released; a PTV in the block holds every slice as `target-in-face-block`, and with no series defaced the RT and PET instances marked absent are released; an ROI is an eye by its name's words in its usual forms, such as `Eye_L`, `LEye`, `Rt_Eye`, `Globe_R`, and `Eye_L_PRV05`, and not as `Lens_L`, `Eye_L_Lens`, `Cornea_R`, `Eye_L_ring`, `Eye-Avoid`, `Eyelid_L`, or `Orbit_L`; an eye with a point beyond 25 mm of the nearer globe, above, beside, or ahead of it, or not a number, disagrees, while eyes at the globes, both in one ROI, and with a 5 mm margin agree, and an eye in another frame, or another ROI, is not checked, while one naming no frame is, as is one whose frame is not a UID, has two values, is given twice, has a component with a leading zero, or is not one that its set declares; on the analytic head, eyes at its globes leave it defaced, an eye 40 mm below its globe holds it as `eyes-disagree-with-rois`, a target in the block is the reason before such an eye, an eye in another frame does not hold it, while one naming no frame in a set of another frame, searched as the same patient's, does, and a structure set that cannot be read again for its eyes holds it; in the defacing runs, a structure set with the left eye drawn 40 mm below its globe holds every head slice as `eyes-disagree-with-rois`, counted as a series not defaced, and the structure set is released as received; and under Clean Pixel Data as well in both runs, each slice is held for its pixel data alone.

### D-051: Setting RT Dose to zero in a defaced CT series' face block

- **Decision.**
  - Under the Clean Recognizable Visual Features Option, an RT Dose with pixel data whose Frame of Reference UID is that of exactly one defaced CT series (D-050) has the stored value 0 written into each voxel of its grid whose centre lies in that series' face block, less the voxels that the CT keeps: a voxel is kept where the nearest pixel of the nearest slice of the series that the block reaches, within half the slices' smallest spacing (half the largest pixel spacing for a single slice), is one that defacing keeps as cranial cavity. A voxel beyond the slices is not kept, so its dose is set to zero. Dose is never recalculated, and every stored value outside that region is written as received, as is each frame's, and Dose Grid Scaling.
  - The grid is placed as PS3.3 C.8.8.3.2 defines: by Image Position (Patient), Image Orientation (Patient), and Pixel Spacing, and for each frame by Grid Frame Offset Vector, relative to the first frame where its first offset is 0, or, only where Image Orientation (Patient) is (1, 0, 0, 0, 1, 0) and the first offset is Image Position (Patient)'s z within 0.001 mm, as each frame's z. A single frame without the vector lies at Image Position (Patient). A grid placed otherwise, whose Image Orientation (Patient) is not two orthogonal unit vectors, as PS3.3 C.7.6.2.1.1 requires, within 0.0001 in each vector's length and in their dot product, with a spacing, or a coordinate of a corner of a frame, of magnitude above 1,000,000 mm, or with an attribute that cannot be read, of more than one sample, with compressed Pixel Data, or with a negative stored value, as an RT Dose of Dose Type ERROR can hold, is not set to zero and stays held as `undefaced-in-defaced-frame` (D-050).
  - Where the greatest stored value that would be set to zero is more than 5% of the grid's greatest stored value, nothing is set to zero, and the RT Dose is written as received and held for review at the stage `visual-features` with the reason `dose-in-face-block`. The 5% is a placeholder, to be set by validation. As for `undefaced-in-defaced-frame`, a verdict of absent on an RT Dose that the reviewed run held for this reason does not cover it in a later run (D-046), since the reviewer judged it beside the defaced CT.
  - An RT Dose that names no Frame of Reference, or is in a frame with more than one defaced series, or whose series' slices can no longer be read, is not set to zero, and is held as D-050 holds it. So is one that refers to a defaced slice of a series in another frame; one in the frame of a series set to zero is not held for referring to its slices. An RT Dose without pixel data, which holds only dose-volume histograms, shows no face, and is not held for its frame.
  - Dose-Volume Histograms, in this and any other RT Dose, are kept as received, so they describe the grid before any of it was set to zero, and no longer match a grid that was. A set-to-zero RT Dose claims the option, like a defaced slice, only where its source file is in a series that the face review marked absent and the pixel data written of it are those that the reviewed run wrote (D-046), which the QC pack records for an RT Dose only where its dose levels, drawn from the grid as written, set to zero, show every frame (D-016, D-017).
- **Rationale.** An RT Dose's low-dose envelope follows the skin wherever a beam enters or leaves, so a grid over the head can outline the face that defacing removed from the CT, and the defaced CT beside it would otherwise be held for nothing. Setting dose to zero in the CT's region, on the grid's own geometry, removes the same anatomy from both, and keeps every voxel outside it. The kept cavity follows the CT's own, looked up on its slices rather than recomputed, so that the dose follows what the CT keeps, and a voxel beyond the slices, which nothing kept, is set to zero. The tolerance on the direction cosines accepts cosines written to six decimal places; it bounds the vectors, not the voxels, whose error grows with their distance from the grid's first voxel, to about 0.1 mm for one 500 mm along the normal, since the two vectors' length errors compound in it. A grid with a spacing, or a coordinate of a frame's corner, of magnitude above 1,000,000 mm is refused, since finite but huge geometry could put the block anywhere on the grid. Dose there matters for planning where it is a target's, and RT ROI Interpreted Type is Type 2, so a target may be unmarked: a grid whose dose in the region is a material fraction of its maximum is held rather than cut. The fraction is of the grid's maximum rather than of the prescription, since only an RT Plan, which may not be in the run, holds the prescription, and Dose Grid Scaling multiplies every stored value alike, so the stored values are compared. Only one defaced series per frame is used, since two series' blocks and cavities could disagree, and placing a grid by anything but the offsets that PS3.3 defines would guess. Dose-volume histograms are kept since they show no face, and recomputing them from a grid set to zero would describe no real plan.
- **Tests.** A grid's voxels in a fixed block are set to zero, less those whose nearest slice and pixel the block keeps, with relative offsets in each of four orientations, absolute offsets on an axial grid, and a single frame without offsets; voxels beyond the slices' reach are not kept, a single slice reaches half its largest pixel spacing, and the nearest slice and pixel decide; a grid is held as `dose-in-face-block` only where the dose set to zero exceeds the fraction of its greatest, dose in the kept cavity not counting, and as `undefaced-in-defaced-frame` for a negative stored value; a grid with absolute offsets in another orientation or elsewhere than its position, without its spacing, position, orientation, or offsets for more than one frame, with too few offsets, zero or non-finite spacing, a short position, five direction cosines, zero, non-unit, or non-orthogonal direction cosines, cosines that are not numbers, huge spacing, offsets, or position, a frame placed beyond the bound, or colour, is refused, and oblique cosines rounded to six decimal places are accepted; a defaced series gives its frame a block that keeps the brain above the floor and not the eyes, and no block is given without an RT Dose in the frame, for one naming none, for a frame with two defaced series, for a series not defaced, or where a slice can no longer be read; a grid set to zero is still held where it refers to a defaced slice of another series, and not where it refers to one of its own; the face review leaves out a verdict of absent on a file held as `dose-in-face-block`; and in the defacing runs, the synthetic collection's grids behind the block are released unchanged, a grid over the head with low dose in the block is released with the option's code, set to zero in the block below the floor and around the kept brain and unchanged outside the block, a grid with high dose in the block is held as `dose-in-face-block` and without a verdict, grids with zero, non-unit, or non-orthogonal direction cosines, or huge spacing, offsets, or position, are held as `undefaced-in-defaced-frame`, an RT Dose without pixel data or the Image Plane and Image Pixel modules is released with the option's code, and in a run without the head's slices, the grid set to zero in the reviewed run is held as `face-verdict-on-other-pixel-data`.

### D-052: Cutting RT Structure Set contours and setting PET to zero activity in a defaced CT series' face block

- **Decision.**
  - Under the Clean Recognizable Visual Features Option, an RT Structure Set whose only Frame of Reference, of its own and its ROIs', is that of exactly one defaced CT series (D-050) is written with its contours cut by that series' face block, as D-051 places an RT Dose in it. A contour is written as received where none of its segments enters the block at a point whose nearest pixel, of the nearest slice of the series that the block reaches within half the slices' smallest spacing, is one that defacing writes as air: a pixel in the block, other than one of the cranial cavity that defacing keeps. Each segment is sampled within the block at half the smallest pixel spacing of those slices, and a point with no such slice counts where it lies in the block. So a contour behind the block, below it, or within the kept cavity alone, as the brain's is above the orbits, is kept whole. The closed contours of an ROI that lie in one plane, each contour's plane being the one that fits its points best, so that it is found for either winding and for a contour that crosses itself, such as a bow tie whose lobes cancel in its signed area, and two contours lying in one plane where every point of one lies within 0.15 mm of the other's plane, which allows for coordinates written to 0.1 mm and for a small hole whose own plane is uncertain, any two such contours joining their groups whatever their order, bound one region together, as a hole's contour does within its outer one, whether by exclusive or, as PS3.3 C.8.8.6.3 defines it for `CLOSEDPLANAR_XOR`, or as nested `CLOSED_PLANAR` contours are commonly read, so where one of them reaches the face, each of them is cut by the same region, as below, and a hole is never left as a region that its outer contour no longer surrounds.
  - Such closed contours lose only what defacing writes as air, as the CT does, as the maintainer chose on 19 October 2026, where they can: where each is simple, neither crossing nor touching itself once a point that repeats the one before it is dropped, as where a contour's first point is written again at its end, all lie at one z above the block's axial plane, and every point within them has one nearest slice of the series within half the slices' spacing, whether or not it lies within the slice's pixels, as their points nearest and farthest along the slices' normal show, an axial one, whose unit normal lies within the alignment tolerance of the patient's z axis, its z component within 10⁻⁴ of 1 in magnitude, or about 0.81°, the normal being scaled to unit length since direction cosines may differ from it by as much, with pixels that defacing keeps in the block. Since face finding already refuses a series whose slices' direction cosines lie more than 10⁻⁴ off the patient's axes, the tilt check in practice refuses only the slices of a sagittal or coronal series. Each is then written as its part behind the block's vertical plane joined with its part ahead of that plane within the squares of that slice's pixels that defacing does not write as air, those of the kept brain and those whose centres lie outside the block, each square being the points whose nearest pixel it is, so that its part beyond the slice's pixels counts as in the block. The part within the squares is found exactly, by clipping to the bounds of each row's runs of such pixels, and the pieces are joined along the edges that they share into rings, outer rings winding as the source contour does and holes the other way, so that a reader that fills by the nonzero rule still finds a hole where the source's hole winds against its outer contour, each its own contour of the same type. A ring of no more than 10⁻⁹ mm², such as one of three points in a line that joining points within 10⁻⁹ mm of a pixel's corner can leave, is dropped, and rings that meet only at points, around a gap, are written as they are, the gap lying outside each, so it is a hole of the region they bound for either type though no ring winds around it. `CLOSEDPLANAR_XOR` contours combine by exclusive or (PS3.3 C.8.8.6.3), so a hole there is written as a contour of its own, but a reader may fill each `CLOSED_PLANAR` contour alone, which would fill the hole again, and PS3.3 encodes such a hole only by a keyhole, which this step does not draw. So where a `CLOSED_PLANAR` contour's rings would bound a hole, as a pixel written as air within the kept cavity, such as bone that the cavity encloses, makes one, its pieces are split along the edge between two columns of the slice's pixels at the lowest column of the holes' points, or the first such edge beyond it, and each side is joined again, until no ring bounds a hole. A hole that the edge crosses or runs along becomes a notch in the rings beside it, and none lies wholly on the edge's lower side, so each split leaves at least one hole fewer, except where joining takes points within 10⁻⁹ mm of each other as one, which can leave as many holes or more; there the limit of 256 splits, below, still ends the splitting. The rings are then simple, wind as the source contour does, overlap nowhere, and together bound what the rings with holes would, so a reader that fills each alone, or that combines them by exclusive or or by union, finds the same region; an edge that two of them share lies between two columns of pixels, so it passes through no pixel's centre. Where, for any of the plane's contours, the rings' signed areas do not sum to the pieces' or a ring cannot be closed, or the edge beyond a hole's lowest column misses it, as it can where the plane, not a pixel's edge, bounds the hole there, or 256 splits do not free a `CLOSED_PLANAR` contour of holes, or one of its rings is not simple, as joining points within 10⁻⁹ mm of a pixel's corner can leave one crossing itself, every closed contour of the plane is cut by the whole block instead, as below. A contour whose region loses no more than 10⁻⁹ mm² by this is written as received. A contour that is cut loses every square within it that defacing writes as air, such as one of bone that it encloses, while one that is not cut, since none of its segments reaches such a square, keeps the squares it encloses, as above. The squares of padding pixels in the block, which defacing leaves as they are, are not kept, so a contour that is cut loses them though the CT keeps them.
  - Any other contour that reaches the face is cut by the whole block, the kept cavity included, so that what is written of it lies outside the block or on its boundary. A closed contour, of Contour Geometric Type `CLOSED_PLANAR` or `CLOSEDPLANAR_XOR`, is written as each polygon of its part behind the block's vertical plane and of its part ahead of that plane and not above the block's axial plane, each its own contour of the same type; an open contour, `OPEN_PLANAR` or `OPEN_NONPLANAR`, as each piece of it outside the block; and a `POINT` contour in the block not at all. A part is found by clipping the polygon against one plane at a time, and the pieces into which a plane cuts it are separated by pairing the points at which its edges cross the plane, in order along their line, as a simple polygon's pair; where they cannot be paired, as for a polygon that crosses itself, the part is written as one polygon joined along the plane, which still lies outside the block. Each piece keeps its source contour's other attributes, such as its Contour Image Sequence, with its Contour Data, written as decimal strings of up to 16 characters, and its Number of Contour Points; the first keeps its Contour Number, and the others have none, since a number names one contour of its ROI. An ROI left with no contour is written without its Contour Sequence, which is Type 3, and keeps its other attributes.
  - A structure set is cut only where each of its ROIs names the defaced series' frame, each ROI Contour Sequence item refers to an ROI that it declares, and each contour has a known Contour Geometric Type and Contour Data of finite coordinates, in threes, none above 1,000,000 mm in magnitude, and no Contour Offset Vector or Contour Slab Thickness, which would place it elsewhere or give it depth, and, in an ROI of which a contour is cut, each closed contour lies within 0.15 mm of one plane, so that it can be cut with those it bounds a region with. Any other, and one that names no frame, more than one, or a frame with more than one defaced series, stays held as `undefaced-in-defaced-frame` (D-050), as does one that refers to a defaced slice of a series in another frame.
  - A Positron Emission Tomography image in such a frame, of native Pixel Data, one frame, and one sample, placed by Image Position (Patient), Image Orientation (Patient) of two orthogonal unit vectors, as D-051 checks them, and Pixel Spacing, with its corners within 1,000,000 mm, a Rescale Slope finite and positive, and a Rescale Intercept whose zero activity, the stored value nearest -intercept / slope, can be stored, has that stored value written into each pixel whose centre lies in the block, other than one that defacing keeps, by the same nearest slice and pixel as D-051, and one of Pixel Padding Value or its range. Any other PET image, and every Enhanced PET and Legacy Converted Enhanced PET image, stays held as `undefaced-in-defaced-frame`.
  - A cut structure set, or a PET image set to zero, claims the option, like a defaced slice, only where its source file is in a series that the face review marked absent and the contours or pixel data written of it are those that the reviewed run wrote (D-046), so a verdict on one seen as received is held as `face-verdict-on-other-contours` or `face-verdict-on-other-pixel-data`.
  - The QC pack shows the reviewer each RT Structure Set's contours as the run wrote them, drawn with the surface of a volume of its frame where the run wrote one (D-017), and records the SHA-256 of a structure set's written contours only where it shows them, so a verdict on a structure set releases only the contours so shown, and one whose contours the pack cannot show is held as `no-face-verdict`, since its series' verdict was given without them. This meets the condition, set with this decision, that the Clean Recognizable Visual Features Option stay not enabled (D-033) until the pack did so; enabling the option is still a decision of its own.
- **Rationale.** An outline of the skin traces the profile that defacing removed from the CT, and a PET image's uptake at the skin outlines it too, so each would otherwise keep the defaced CT beside it held for nothing. Cutting the contours by the CT's own block and pixels, rather than recalculating them from the defaced CT, keeps every point of them out of what the CT writes as air, and a contour that the block does not reach is written as received, so that the brain, the targets that D-050 already keeps out of the block, and every structure behind it keep their contours exactly. The plan for this step was to clip contours to the block less the kept cavity. Cutting every contour that reaches the face by the whole block was built first, since two planes cut exactly and remove more rather than less, but the outline then lost the front of the brain that the CT, RT Dose, and PET keep, leaving kept tissue and dose outside the body, and a brain contour drawn on the inner table, as segmentations commonly are, reached bone that defacing writes as air and lost its frontal lobe on those slices, for almost no gain in privacy, since the released CT shows the inside of the skull anyway. So a contour loses what the CT loses, as the maintainer chose. Its edge ahead of the plane follows the pixels' squares, tracing the inside of the skull in steps, as the CT shows it. The squares are those of the nearest-pixel rule by which a contour is judged, so a contour keeps nothing that the CT writes as air, though it can keep up to half a pixel within the block beside a pixel whose centre lies outside it, which the CT keeps too. The cut is computed on the contours' own polygons with NumPy alone, as the maintainer decided, rather than with a polygon library, and only where it is well defined, for simple contours in an axial slice's plane; elsewhere the whole block, which removes more, cuts them. A contour is judged by the CT's own pixels, as defacing writes them, so that a brain contour that crosses the plane within the cavity is not taken to reach the face. Splitting a `CLOSED_PLANAR` contour around a hole that the cut makes was chosen over cutting its plane by the whole block, as the maintainer chose on 20 October 2026, since bone within the kept cavity, such as a crista galli or a calcification of the falx, would otherwise cost the outline and the brain all that the cut spares on those slices: in a model of the analytic head with both added, 5 of the 17 slices with pixels kept in the block, and about 46% of the area spared there. The split runs along an edge between pixels rather than through the hole's centre, so that no pixel's centre lies on an edge that two rings share. Cutting a plane's closed contours together keeps the region that they bound exact, since cutting each by the same region cuts their exclusive or, or their union, by it, whereas a hole within the cavity kept whole inside an outer contour that was cut would become a filled island. Pairing crossings along the plane separates a polygon's pieces without the bridges that a single clipped ring would draw along the plane, which would put contours on the block's boundary that the source never had. An eye's contour keeps only the back of the globe, behind the plane, as the CT does, and a lens, wholly in the block, is removed, so planning loses them as it loses the front of each globe from the CT. A target is never cut, since a target in the block holds the series. PET is set to the stored value of zero activity, the background of air, rather than to a background measured from the image, which would vary between slices and could itself outline the body. A PET image is placed by its own geometry, not by its series, so each slice is set to zero alone. Enhanced PET is left held, since its frames are placed by functional groups, which this step does not read. When this decision was made, a verdict of absent on a structure set released contours that the reviewer had not seen drawn, since the QC pack previewed pixel data, not contours, and an RT Structure Set's verdict was given by its series without a view of its contours (D-046). The contour digest bound the verdict to the contours that the reviewed run wrote, so a set whose contours a later run writes otherwise, cut or not, was held rather than released on it, but it could not show the reviewer a face that a contour traces. So the option was not to be enabled until the pack rendered the written contours for the reviewer with the images they lie on, which its contour sheets now do, with every surface that an ROI hides behind its own and every mark that it would hide, to within two voxels, at most 4 mm, of its edge, and a set that a sheet cannot show records no contour digest, so that its verdict releases nothing.
- **Tests.** A contour that reaches no pixel written as air, behind the block, below it, or within the kept cavity, is kept whole, and one wholly in the face is removed, as is a `POINT` in it; a closed contour that reaches the face keeps its part in the squares of the pixels that defacing does not write as air, kept pixels that a mirror image of them would not match, for `CLOSED_PLANAR` and `CLOSEDPLANAR_XOR`, as 200 random star-shaped polygons on such a slice do exactly, by an independent polygon library, or are kept whole only where they reach no pixel written as air but at a graze, and as a rectangle does with its first point written again at its end or a point repeated within it, and on slices whose direction cosines fall 6 × 10⁻⁵ short of unit length, while a triangle near the
