midpoint between two slices tilted within face finding's tolerance, its one corner within their pixels nearest one and points within it nearest the other, is cut by the whole block, so that it keeps no pixel that the other writes as air, and around a pixel written as air within the kept cavity, as a hole of its own for `CLOSEDPLANAR_XOR`, and for `CLOSED_PLANAR` as rings split along the edge before the pixel's column, as it does around holes in one column and in another, and as 150 random star-shaped polygons of each winding do around random pixels written as air, each ring simple, winding as the source does, overlapping no other, and meeting another along a line only at an edge between columns of pixels, their union exactly the contour's part in those squares, by an independent polygon library, while a `CLOSED_PLANAR` contour that the limit on splits leaves with a hole is cut by the whole block, as is an L-shaped one whose inner corner lies 10⁻⁹ mm from a corner of a pixel written as air on a grid turned 133.1° where its split rings would cross themselves, and where joining would leave a ring of no area, none is written, and two rings that meet at points around gaps are written as they are for either type, while contours of a plane at more than one z, a sagittal contour, a polygon that crosses itself, open types, and every closed contour of a plane where the pieces of one cannot be joined into rings are cut by the whole block; a cut contour keeps its winding, so that a clockwise or anticlockwise outer contour and a hole within the kept cavity that winds against it bound the same region by the nonzero rule as by exclusive or; the pieces of a polygon within chosen pixels, and the rings they join into, are exactly its part in the pixels' squares, by sampled points, for 200 random star-shaped polygons on grids turned, flipped, or along the axes; pieces that tile an L, meet at a corner, surround a gap, or share an edge split unevenly join into the region's rings; a contour below the cavity's floor, or beyond the slices, is cut where it enters the block; an edge that crosses the block between its vertices is cut; a hole within the kept cavity, or crossing the plane, inside a `CLOSEDPLANAR_XOR` or nested `CLOSED_PLANAR` contour that reaches the face is cut with it, so that the region they bound is its part outside the block, as it is for 200 random holes and for either winding of each contour, either order, and the same contours and block translated below the origin, while a contour and its hole in another plane, within the cavity alone, are kept whole, and contours in parallel planes either side of the origin are not taken for one plane, while 300 oblique contours and holes of 0.3 to 2.5 mm, far from the origin and written to 0.1 mm, are, and a contour 0.5 mm beside them is not; a small contour half a millimetre below a contour and its hole, turned so that its plane passes through the outer contour's centre, leaves the hole grouped and cut with the outer contour in every order; a hole within the kept cavity in a bow tie's lobe, for either winding of each and either order, stays excluded, nothing in the block is included, and the rest is as it was, by counting crossings; a closed contour with a point 1 mm from its plane refuses an ROI that is cut, and is kept in one that is not; a U-shaped contour cut in two is written as two, numbered once, and a polygon that crosses itself is written outside the block; 200 random star-shaped polygons near the plane are each cut to exactly their part outside the block, by an independent polygon library, or kept whole only where they enter the block no deeper than its nearest pixels; a sagittal contour is cut by both planes; open contours are written as their pieces outside the block; cut contours are written in their ROIs' order; a structure set with an ROI in another frame or none, an undeclared ROI, an unknown type, Contour Data not in threes, not finite, or too far, a Contour Offset Vector, or a Contour Slab Thickness is refused; PET is set to zero activity in the block less the kept cavity, as the stored value of zero activity, with padding kept, a slice behind or below the block has no mask, and one that cannot be placed or stored, or compressed, is refused; a verdict releases only the contours that the reviewed run wrote, and the contours' digest changes with a contour's values and type, with contours moved to another ROI or split otherwise, and not with how a value is written, and is null for contours that cannot be read; and in the defacing runs, the analytic head's structure set is released with the option's code, its outline and eyes below the kept brain's floor exactly their parts outside the block, its outline above it, and a brain drawn out to the skull, exactly their parts outside the squares of the pixels that the released CT holds as air in the block, and more than the block's plane leaves them, its brain and target unchanged, and a lens wholly in the block without contours, its two PET slices are released set to zero activity in the block, wholly below the cavity's floor and around the kept brain above it, and unchanged outside it, and in a run that reviewed them as received, or without the head's slices, they are held as `face-verdict-on-other-contours` and `face-verdict-on-other-pixel-data`.

### D-053: Recording an external statistical assessment

- **Decision.**
  - The statistical gate's evidence is, for now, an assessment made outside the engine, recorded beside the QC pack of a run of the collection assessed, as `risk-assessment.json`, a JSON document labelled `pymedphys-deid-risk-assessment/1` (`pymedphys._dicom.deidentify.risk_assessment`). It is QC material: it can describe the cohort and names its assessor, so it is written owner-only, never overwritten, and kept with the pack (D-016). A release report records only its opaque reference, `R-` and 32 random hexadecimal digits, and the gate's outcome.
  - The record holds what D-017 requires the assessment to state, each field required and none defaulted: the model's name and version and its implementation's name and version; the attacker's assumed knowledge, and whether they know who is in the collection; either the reference population with its sampling assumptions, or why the assessment is within the release cohort, never both; the matching variables, at least one, each once, and how characteristics derivable from the pixel data were considered (MIDI-BP-07); the generalisation, the handling of missing data, the uncertainty, and the exclusions; how linked longitudinal records and earlier linked releases were accounted for; whether keeping machine identity would raise the risk above the threshold, which justifies the policy omitting or keeping it (MIDI-BP-04); whether re-identification from faces reconstructed from the images was among the channels assessed; the number of distinct subjects assessed, at least 1; the threshold, 0.09 or 0.05; the maximum per-subject re-identification probability, from 0 to 1; and who assessed it, and when, in UTC.
  - The record binds the assessment to the QC pack, by its reference and the SHA-256 of its file; to the collection, by the SHA-256 of the sorted SHA-256 digests of the source files that the pack records, so that it depends on which files the run read, and how many copies of each, but not on their paths or order; to the policy, by the SHA-256 of the canonical JSON of the run's release report's `policy` section and its `method` section without the method digest and the keyed digests of the reviewed lists; and to the reviewed ROI names and descriptors lists that descriptor cleaning applied, by an HMAC-SHA-256, under 32 random bytes that the record keeps, of the SHA-256 of each list's canonical form, or of null for a list not given. Recording takes the lists as their custodian holds them, and refuses a list given where the release report records none, or not given where it records one; a release report that is not of its format or names another pack; a pack that records no source file, or a released, duplicate, or held instance without its source file's SHA-256; and a pack that already has a record. Reading refuses a record that is not of its format, names a member twice, names another pack, or no longer matches the pack's file or collection.
  - The statistical gate of a run given a record fails, by the first that holds, for no record (`no-assessment`), another collection (`other-collection`), another policy (`other-policy`), other reviewed lists (`other-lists`), fewer or more subjects than the distinct subjects of the instances that the run releases (`fewer-subjects`, `more-subjects`), or a maximum probability above the threshold (`above-threshold`), and passes otherwise, a probability equal to the threshold passing. The threshold is the one that the assessment states; nothing yet selects the stricter one for a collection, so whoever reviews the record checks that the threshold suits the collection's sensitivity. Its outcome is a reason code, never a value. No run reports the gate yet: the release report's readiness of a `public-release` run, which combines it with pixel and face review and the attestation, builds on it, supplying the run's collection, policy, lists, and subjects.
- **Rationale.** D-017 enables no estimator until one is selected and validated, and accepts a documented external assessment meanwhile, so the engine records and checks one rather than computing one. Binding the record to the collection and the policy is what makes "any change requires reassessment" checkable: a run of other files, or under another engine, policy, vocabulary, text finder, or list of safe private rules, finds another digest and fails the gate. The engine's files and the text finder's runtime are inputs of the method digest, so a new version of PyMedPhys, or of a library that the text finder names, needs a new assessment too, which is deliberate: either can change what is released. The keyed digests of the reviewed ROI names and descriptors lists differ under each run's key, as D-024 sets, so a later run given the pack could never match the method digest of the run assessed. The lists are bound instead by their own digest, which any change to a decision changes. They hold source names and values, so that digest is salted, kept only in the confidential record, and compared in constant time, and no release report carries it; a release report's format is unchanged. Requiring the count of subjects to equal the run's, rather than to be at least it, catches an assessment that counted images, frames, or visits, which D-017 rules out. Recording the record beside the pack it assessed, rather than giving it to a run as a file of its own, keeps it with the confidential material that it describes and binds it to the pack as the attestation is bound. Whether faces were assessed is recorded so that an assessment that covers them can serve as the risk assessment reference that D-017 allows in place of a face verdict. The record carries the assessor's statements without judging them: the engine checks that each is given, not that it is adequate, which is the assessor's responsibility.
- **Tests.** A record is written beside its pack, owner-only, with every field, and read back equal; each record has a new reference; a pack without one has none; reprs show no statement; each statement, each part of the model, the attacker's knowledge and membership, and the population, given as both, neither, or half of a reference, is required; matching variables are at least one, each once, ignoring case and surrounding spaces; subjects are a whole number of at least 1; the threshold is 0.09 or 0.05 and the maximum probability a number from 0 to 1; the time is in UTC and not in the future; the gate passes a bound record within its threshold, at the threshold itself for both thresholds, and fails above it, without a record, for a collection with a copy fewer or more, a changed file, or none, for another preset, for another decision or value in a list, a list left out or given empty, or an edited salt, for fewer or more subjects, giving the first failure, and passes the same lists read again; the lists' digest is the specified HMAC, differs for another salt, and tells each list's absence, emptiness, and content apart; the collection digest ignores order and counts copies; the policy digest is the same for another pack, the method digest, and any keyed digests of the lists, and differs for any other component, such as an engine file; a report of another pack or not of its format, a list given where the report records none or not given where it records one, a pack that binds no source file or has a released or held instance without its digest, and a second record, are refused, naming no path; and a record for another pack, of another or an earlier format, with a field missing, added, or named twice, or of a pack changed since, is refused.

## Roadmap

Milestones group outcomes; they are not a sequence of pull requests or releases. Each pull request ships its own tests, documentation, and traceability, and M6 consolidates the evidence. The first supported release needs only the parts of M1 to M5 that its scope uses.

| Milestone | Outcomes | Done when |
| --- | --- | --- |
| M0 Legacy hygiene | Diagnostics free of identifying values and paths; limitation warnings and notices (D-019); pydicom minimum (D-002) | Each change has regression tests and documentation, and remaining disclosure channels are listed in the tracking issue. Hygiene does not make the legacy tools conform. |
| M1 Standard | Table generator and generated tables, including the PS3.3 Types of every composite IOD with its Functional Group Macros, generated for all but the three real-time IODs, which also need PS3.6 Table 9-1 (D-001); requirements register; monthly edition check (D-001) | Source provenance and table coverage are verified, and the generator and generated data are reviewed separately. |
| M2 Primitives | Keys and subject profiles; UID replacement; patient pseudonyms; date offsets; VR and VM validators and dummy values (D-021); rule layers, including the reviewed rules for the text, dates, times, and other attributes that Table E.1-1 omits (D-022, D-023), and preset composition; method digest (D-024) | Primitives and policy conflicts are validated before integration. Hypothesis is added with the first property tests that use it. |
| M3 Engine | Dataset transformation, with actions resolved by Type (D-020) and markers (D-012); file container (D-025); private attributes (D-028); descriptor cleaning; bitstream metadata; dataset-level API | Each component has tests, API documentation, and conformance evidence for explicitly supported input. A dataset-level API does not establish collection integrity or readiness to release. |
| M4 Pipeline | Discovery and writing through a staging area; reference graph with graded consequences (D-026) and two-pass verification; residual search (D-027); reports; risk detection; QC pack and attestation; statistical assessment; end-to-end preset validation | Graph and subject consistency, the separation of reports, QC material, and state, and every preset gate are verified. |
| M5 Interfaces and migration | Command line using the same engine as the library, for the first supported release, including the nomenclature converter commands that run as `python -m pymedphys._nomenclature` until then, with the test that keeps the tool's commands out of the `pymedphys` command line changed to require them, both in the pull request of the first supported release (M6); the app, after its own design for keys and QC material, in a later release (Scope); migration guides; deprecation warnings when D-019 allows | Only supported behaviour is exposed, with user documentation. |
| M6 First supported release | `basic` and `basic-clean-descriptors` enabled; how-to guide, generated conformance statement, requirements-to-tests matrix, and benchmark results (D-018) for the release scope | Evidence is published for the shipped coverage and exclusions are documented; the evidence is refreshed for each later release. |
| M7 Legacy removal | Removal of both legacy interfaces | The deprecation window in D-019 has passed. |

On 10 October 2026 the maintainer postponed the first supported release until the tool is more comprehensive and its public validation much more comprehensive, as public and reproducible as possible, and decided that the release pull request opens only once its release criteria hold, rather than taking them as blockers inside it. The criteria are:

- every internal error that the MIDI-B benchmark runs report is fixed;
- the MIDI-B score is high, with its target set once the benchmark's triage shows what is achievable;
- every answer-key failure on a released instance is fixed, or recorded, with MIDI-B evidence, as a deliberate difference (D-018);
- the MIDI-B yield and each category of unreleased instance are published in the documentation, with the code that reproduces them;
- the independent validators run in continuous integration and fail on any finding outside the reviewed known differences (D-030);
- the fuzz testing of the engine's parsers of untrusted bytes has merged; and
- the maintainer's pilot on a representative local export is summarised by reason counts.

The pilot stays a criterion because the MIDI-B collection holds no RT Structure Set, RT Plan, or RT Dose, and a curated benchmark, whose clinical images carry injected synthetic identifiers, cannot show how often a department's own exports meet the conditions that withhold a whole subject (D-027) or stop a run (D-026).

Later work needs its own design, tests, and conformance review: accompanying spreadsheets pseudonymised with the same key, the Encrypted Attributes Sequence for controlled sharing (D-013), keeping a source ICC profile's colorimetry with its identifying content removed (D-021), and the unsupported objects and options listed under Scope. Generating a table in M1 does not enable the corresponding option or support the corresponding IOD.
